CHAPTER I

On the Superiority of Rudra Over Viṣṇu

སྐལ་བྱིན་

भवता कथितं सूत महदारख्यानुक्तमम् ।

कृष्णाय चरितं दिख्यं सर्वपातकनाशम् ॥ १ ॥

सन्देडोउ महाभाग वासुदेवकधानके ।

जातवे नः प्रोच्यमाने विस्तरणे महामते ॥ २ ॥

The Rsis said: “The great legendary story, the life of Śrī Krṣṇa, supremely divine, destructive of all sins, has been narrated by you, O Sūta! But, O Blessed One! You, though highly intelligent, have dwelt on it not at great length; hence many doubts are cropping in our minds.

चने गत्वा तपस्तवं वासुदेवेन दुष्कम् ।

विष्णोश्वातरेण शिवत्यागाधनं कृतम् ॥ ३ ॥

वरप्रदानं देया च पार्वत्य यकृतं पुरः ।

जनमान्तुङ्ग पूर्णायाः श्रीद्योआं अंशपूर्वता ॥ 4 ॥

ईंध्रोगपि कृष्णोन कुतस्ती सम्पूर्वजतौ ।

न्यन्ता वा किमसत्यस्य तद्वे संगरो मम ॥ 5 ॥

A very difficult tapasyā was performed by Vāsudeva, part incarnate of Viṣṇu, who had to go to forest to worship Śiva. Next, it has been known that the Devi Pārvati, the part incarnate of the Great Mother, the Mother of the universe, the Supreme, and Perfect offered boons to Śri Krṣṇa. How did it then come to pass that Śri Krṣṇa, being himself the God, had to worship Pārvati and Mahādeva? Is it that Śri Krṣṇa was inferior to Mahādeva and Pārvati? This is our doubt."

सूत उवाच

श्यूघ्रं कारणं तत्र मया व्यास श्रुतं च चत् ।

प्रवंतीम महाभागाः कथां कृष्णगुणानिताम् ॥ ६ ॥

ब्रुतान्तं व्यासतः श्रुत्वा वैरादीमुताजस्त्वा ।

पुनः पप्रच्छ मेधावी सन्देहं परमं गतः ॥ ७ ॥

Sūta spoke: “Hear then, the reasons, O noble Rīsi! that I heard from Vyāsa; I will now sing before you those meritorious deeds of Śrī Krṣṇa.” The son of Pariksit, the intelligent Janamejaya had also the same doubts that you now have, when he heard the story before from Vyāsa; and he asked the same questions that you now ask.

जनमेव उवाच

सप्यक्सत्यवतीसूने आुतं परमकारणम् ।

तथापि मनसो वृत्ति: संशयं न विमुईति ॥ 8 ॥

कृष्णोनाराधितः शम्भुस्तपतत्वात्रिदाकाणम् ।

विसयोउं मद्राभाग देवदेवेन विष्णुना ॥ 9 ॥

Janamejaya said: “O son of Satyavatī! I have heard from you much about the Supreme Goddess, the Highest Cause; still the doubts are not leaving me. O Fortunate One! Kṛṣṇa the Deva of the Devas, the Viṣṇu incarnate, worshipped Sambhu and had to perform dire penances; this is my great wonder!

यः सर्वात्मासि सर्वैश: सर्वसिद्धप्रद: प्रशु: ।

स कर्व कृताव्योरे तप: प्राकृतबद्धरि: ॥ 10 ॥

He is the soul of all the Jivas, the One Ruler and Lord of this world and He is able to confer all

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

the Siddhis; how is it, then, that the Lord Hari had to perform very difficult asceticism like an ordinary mortal.

जनकर्तूं क्षम: कृष्णस्वधा पालयितुं क्षम: ।

संडुर्गायि कस्माल दारुणं तप आवर्त् ॥ १ १ ॥

He who is able to create this universe, moving and un-moving, He who is able to preserve and destroy it, why did He practise such a terrible penance."

व्यास उवाच

सत्यमुक्ते त्वया राजनासुरेदो जनादीनः ।

शमः सर्वेषु कार्येषु देवानां दैत्यसूदनः ॥ १२ ॥

Vyāsa said: “True it has been said by you that Vāsudeva, the Janārdana, is the destroyer of the Daityas and He is able to create and preserve the Devas and do all other acts for them.

तथापि मानुवं देह्माश्रभ: परमेश्वः ।

कृतवान्मनुषान्यावल्याणीप्रस्मापाश्रितान् ॥ 13 ॥

But the Great Lord assumed a human body; therefore he had to perform his duties like a man and observe the Varna and Śārama Dharmas pertaining to human beings.

युद्धानां पूर्नं चैव गुरूपादाभिवन्दनम् ।

ब्राह्माणां तथा सेवा देवतातारधनं तथा ॥ 14 ॥

गोके शोकाधिपोग्रह हर्ष हंस्यमुवर्ति: ।

दैनं नानप्रवादाश्र ऋषीष कामोपसेवनम् ॥ 15 ॥

Respecting the elderly persons, worshipping the spiritual teachers, doing service to the Brāhmanas, adoring and propitiating the Devas, feeling sorrow at times of sorrow, feeling pleasure at times of happiness, feeling dejection or expressing censure or scandal, or having sexual intercourse with women, in other words, to feel lust, anger, greediness and other passions when their proper time arises.

कामः क्रोधस्तथा लोधः काले काले भवन्ति है ।

तथा गृणमये देहे निर्णणत्वं कर्ध भवेत् ॥ १ ६ ॥

All these are natural to all human beings; how can, then, Śri Kṛṣṇa though intrinsically of pure qualities, become Nirguṇa (devoid of human

qualities) when he assumed a human body which is Saguna, i.e., with qualities.

सीब्तीशपजाइघातथा ब्राह्मणशपजात् ।

निधनं याद्वानां तु कृष्णदेहस्म योचनम् ॥ 17 ॥

हरणं तुरुन्तं तद्धान्त्वनीनां नराधिय ।

अजुन्स्यास्वमोक्षे च कर्तीबाव्त तस्करेषु च ॥ 18 ॥

अज्ञलं हरणे गेहातन्यधूनानिकृद्धोः ।

एवं भानुपदेशे3स्मिनानुपं खतु चेहितम् ॥ 19 ॥

O Ruler of men! The extinction of the Yādava race by the curse of Gāndhāri, the daughter of Subala, and the curse of a Brāhmin, Krṣna's leaving his human coil, the stealing away of his wives, the robbing of their wealth on the way by the dacoits of the Ābhīra tribe, Ajuna's becoming powerless to hurl any weapons on those dacoits, Krṣna's not knowing anything about the stealing away of Pradyumna and Aniruddha from his Dvārakā palace, these all correspond verily to exertions and failings appropriate to human bodies.

विष्णोरंगशतारोरंस्मातायमानुनेलथा ।

अंगजे वासुदेवेउत्र किं चित्र शिवसेवने ॥ २० ॥

स हि सर्वेशुते देयो विष्णोपरि च कारणम् ।

सुयुतस्थाननाथ: स विष्णुना च प्रयुक्ति: ॥ 21 ॥

Again the Rsi Nārāyaṇa is the part incarnate of Viṣṇu, and Vāsudeva is the part incarnate of the Rsi Nārāyaṇa; hence what wonder is there, if Vāsudeva be seen to adore and propitiate Śiva as the God of gods; and He is the Lord of all the causal bodies that exist in the state of Sushpti (deep sleep). In this respect, Śiva is the creator of Viṣṇu and Viṣṇu worships Him in this light.

तदोर्भपूता कृष्णाइास्नैः कर्ण न स पुरस्ते ।

अकातो भगवान्बहाय्याकार: स्याद्धि: स्वयम् ॥ 22 ॥

मकातो भगवानुश्रीउपधर्माता मेहक्षी ।

Rāma, Krṣna and others are all part incarnations of Viṣṇu; so there is no wonder if they worship Śiva. The letter A is Bhagavān Brahma; the letter “U” is Bhagavān Hari; the letter “M” is Bhagavān Rudra and the half letter m is Maheśvarī, the Supreme Mother of the universe.

Book V Chapter I

उत्तरोतरभावेनाप्युतमचं स्मृतं बुद्धे: ॥ 23 ॥

उतः सर्वेषु शाखेशु देथी वसंतांनमा स्मृता ।

अधमात्रा स्थित तिनया यानुच्चायां विशेषतः ॥ 24 ॥

Therefore, consider Viṣnu superiter to Brahmā; they again consider Rudra superior to Viṣnu and Maheśvari (Turiya State) again superior to Rudra. The speciality of the half letter is that it can never be uttered; it is the symbol of the Eternal Devi. In all, therefore, the superiority of the Devi is established.

विष्योपधिको रूदी विष्युस्ते बहणोर्धिकः।

तस्मार संशयः कार्यः कृष्णेन शिवपूजने ॥ 25 ॥

इच्छया बहणो वक्यात्रद्वाराथयुद्धभी ।

Visnu is superior to Brahmā; Rudra is superior to Visnu. Therefore no doubt can arise in Krṣna's worshipping Śiva. It is through the will of Śiva that a second Rudra originated from the forehead of Brahmā to offer boons (i.e., to Brahmā).

मूलहन्धस्यांभृतो महनामा ब्रितीयकः ॥ 26 ॥

सोर्पि पूर्णोर्सित सर्वेभी मूलहन्य का कथा ।

देवीतच्चस्य साझिश्यानुतमचं स्मृतं शिवे ॥ 27 ॥

This second Rudra is venerable and entitled to all worship; what to speak of the First Rudra? O King! It is through the proximity of the Devi that the importance and superiority of Śiva is thus established.

अवतारा हरेरेखं प्रभवन्ति चुंगे युगे ।

योगमायाप्रभावेण नात्र कार्य विचारणा ॥ 28 ॥

Thus the incarnations of Hari arise in yugas after yugas through the intervention of the Yoga Māyā; so there is no discussion on this point.

या नेरपक्षपरिसडृतनेन सम्यविश्वं

सुज्ज्वाति हन्त निगुहभावा ।

सैया कृतोत सततं ईहिषण्यूतोग-

त्रानावतारकलने धरिभ्यमानान् ॥ 29 ॥

Why to Acyuta alone, to Brahmā and Śiva also She gives troubles for getting involved into incarnations. She the Yoga Māyā who is indirectly, with the twinkings of Her eyes, creating, preserving and destroying this universe.

सूतीगृहाद्वजनमध्यनया नियुक्ते

संगीतितश्व भवने यपुणालराः ।

संप्रापितश मधुर्ग विनिवीतितश

श्रीहारकाप्रणयने ननुं भीतिधतः ॥ 30 ॥

It is the Yoga Māyā that caused Krṣṇa to be transferred from his lying-in-chamber to the village Vraja and then protected him in the house of the cowherd Nanda; afterwards took him to Mathurā for the destruction of Kaṇisa, whence he was again out of Jarāsandha's fear to the city of Dvārkā.

निर्माय षोडगसहस्णताधिकासता

नार्योघ समतताः स्वकलासमुचाः ।

तासां विलासवर्गगं तु विधाय कामं

दासीकृती हि भगवाननयापनन्तः ॥ 31 ॥

It is She that created from Her Ownself the eight Naikās (the leading mistresses) and also sixteen thousand and fifty women for the pleasure and enjoyment of Krṣna Bhagavān, the incarnation of Ananta (Viṣṇu Bhagavān); thus Krṣna Bhagavān was made completely subservient to them just like a perfect slave.

एकासिप बन्यनिवधी युवती समर्था

पुंसो यथा सुद्वलोहमयं तु दाम ।

किं नाम घोडागसहस्रशताध्रकाश

तं स्वीकृतं शुकामिवातिविन्तन्यन्ति ॥ 32 ॥

साजाजितीक्षणतने मुदान्यतेन प्राप्त

सुरैप्रवर्न हरिणा तत्वानीम् ।

कृत्वा भूयं भयवता विह्नासल्णागीश:

प्रियासदनभूषणतां य आप ॥ 33 ॥

When a young woman, though she is alone, can bind a man down by the network of Māyā, like a strong iron chain, what wonder is there that the sixteen thousand and fifty women would make Krṣṇa play in their hands like a Śuka bird and make him an instrument to serve any purpose that they liked. Śrī Krṣṇa got himself so much under the control of Satyabhāmā that He went gladly under her commands to Indra's heavens to get the Pārijāta flowers. There he had to fight with Indra

and subsequently stole away the Pārijāta tree and gave it to Satyabhāmā as a very valuable ornament to be kept in her room.

यो भीमर्ग है हृतवाक्षिरुपालकादीझुला

विधि निविलतधर्मकृती विधित्युः ।

जग्रह तां निजबलेन च धर्मपन्तीं

कोउसी विधि: परकललहृती विज्ञाना: ॥ 34 ॥

Behold! The same Kṛṣṇa, by His own prowess, defeated Śiśupāla and others for the preservation of religion and then stole away Rukminī, the daughter of Bhima and afterwards married her as his legal wife; where is the rule, then, observed that it is a sin to take away another's wife?

अहंकारशः प्राणी करोति च रुभाणुषम् ।

विमूढो महाङ्गलेन तत्कृतेनासिपतिना ॥ 3 5 ॥

Thus all embodied beings get themselves subdued by Aharinkāra and do acts, good or bad, confounded and deluded by the network of Moha that always drags one down below.

अहंकाराद्धि संजातिमदं स्थावरजघूम् ।

मृत्नाद्धिरहादीनानुप्राप्तुकृतिसभ्यन्ति ॥ 36 ॥

अहक्रापरिस्तची यदा भवति पधजः ।

तदा विमुक्तो भवति नोचेत्सोराकर्मकृत् ॥ 37 ॥

From the Mūlā Prakṛti are born Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Hara and from the Tāmasik Ahanikāra of Prakṛti is created this whole cosmos, moving and non-moving. The lotus-born Brahmā becomes free when he is free from Ahanikāra; otherwise He becomes engaged in this world affairs.

तनुक्तनु विमुक्ती हि बढ़मतद्धगतां गतः ।

न नारी न धर्न गेहं न पुत्रा न सहोदरा: ॥ 38 ॥

बन्थन प्राणिनां राजनहंकारसनु बन्यकः ।

अर्ह कर्ता मया चेरे कृते कार्यं बलैरासः ॥ 39 ॥

When freed from this Ahanrikāra, all the Jivas become free; and their houses, wealth, wives, sons and brothers are quite powerless to tie them down; but when bound by Ahanrikāra, the Jivas come under their control.

कर्णियामि करोयेवं स्वयं कंपतालि प्राणभूत ।

कारणेन विना कार्य न सम्भवति कर्हिंघित् ॥ 40 ॥

O king! This Aharikāra is the cause of bondage to all the beings; “I am the doer, this work is done by my power; or this I will do myself” thinking thus, the embodied beings bring themselves under this bondage.

मथा न दृषयते जातो भूर्यिडेन विना घटः ।

विषयु: पालातिवा विश्रयराहंकारसमन्ति: ॥ 4 1 ॥

An earthen pot cannot be made without earth; no effects can be visible without a cause; consequently Viṣnu is preserving this universe, because of this Ahanṅkāra (imposed on him Prakṛti).

अन्यथा सर्वदा चिन्तांबुधी मानः कर्वं भवेत् ।

अहंकारविमुक्तसु यदा भवति मानवः॥ 42 ॥

The human beings are always drowned in their cares and anxieties simply because they are bound by this Aharīnkāra; when they become free from this Aharīnkāra, their cares and anxieties at once vanish.

अवतारप्रावहैषु कर्थ मज्जेच्छुभाशयः ।

मोहमूलमहंकारः संभारतस्तस्युद्धः ॥ 4 3 ॥ १

अहंकाराविद्वीनानां न मोहो न च संमृति: ।

विविधः पुरुषः प्रोक्तः साचिक्यो राजसस्तथा ॥ 4 4 ॥

तामसन्तु महात्राज ब्रहाविष्युगिशावितु ।

विविध्यास्त्रि राजेन्द्र काजेशादितु सर्वदा ॥ 4 5 ॥

Moha (delusion) comes out of Ahrikākāra; world and the enjoyments thereof come out of Moha; otherwise how can it be accounted for, that Hari and others, the mine of all good and auspiciousness, take their several incarnations in various wombs? Neither Moha nor this world comes to those that are bereft of Ahrikāra. Men are of three kinds, Sāttvik, Rājasik, and Tāmasik; O king! Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva are sprung respectively from the Rājasik, Sāttvik, and Tāmasik Ahankāras,

अहूक्रारः सदा प्रोक्तो युनिभिस्तत्वदर्शिधि: ।

अहूक्रारण तेनैव बढ़ा एते न संशयः ॥ 46 ॥

मायाविमोहिता मन्तः प्रवद्धिन मनीषिणः ।

क्रोति स्वेच्छया विष्णुव्रताननेकः ॥ 47 ॥

Book V Chapter II

मन्तोप्रि दुःखगहने गर्भवासेस्तिसढ़हे ।

न क्लोति माति विद्यान्कयं कुर्मास चक्रभूत् ॥ 48 ॥

In these three, the three Aharinkāras are always to be found, so the Munis, that have realised the Real Essence, declare. They are all bound by this Aharinkāra; there is no doubt in this. The Paṇdits of dull intellect, and deluded by Māyā declare that Viṣṇu takes various incarnations out of his own free will; for when it is seen that men of even intellect do not entertain any desire to enter into the wombs, painful and terrible; how will Viṣṇu, then, the Holder of the discus, like to come into this womb!

कीसत्यादेवकामर्षे लिहुपालसमाकुले ।

स्वेच्छया प्रवर्त्यद्धा गतो हि मभ्युसून: ॥ 49 ॥

वैकुण्ठसदर्तं व्यक्ता भर्षायो सुखं नु किम ।

चिताकोटिमसूथाने दुःखदे विषयमिति ॥ 50 ॥

तपस्त्वा क्रनूकृता दत्वा दानान्यनेकशः ।

न वाईति यातो लोका नभवासं सुखुःखदम् ॥ 51 ॥

The slayer of Madhu, the Vaiṣṇavas say, entered all at once into the wombs of Kausálya and Devakī, full of faeces and other dirty things, of His own free will. But you must think out what happiness can Madhusūdana, quitting his Vaikuntha Heavens, attain in this womb, full of so many troubles, and where arise, like poisons, hundreds of cares and thoughts to torment an

individual! Especially when it is seen that human beings perform asceticism, sacrifice Yajñas and do various charities, that they would avoid thus entering in wombs, which is very painful and terrible.

स कर्ष भगाविष्णु: स्ववगीडज्ञनिदानः ।

गर्भवासरुचिर्याद्वैरल्वगता यदि १५२ ॥

जानीति त्वं महाराज योगमायावरो जगत् ।

बह्ञादिस्तंवपर्यन्ते देवमानुषितियम् ॥ ५३ ॥

मायातन्मीतिबब्धा ये बह्वाहिष्युतरयः ।

भ्यन्ति बन्धमायाति लीलाया घोर्णनाभ्यत् ॥ ५४ ॥

इति श्रीमद्वीभाग्याते महापुराणे पत्रमकन्ने

प्रभोत्रयाय: 11 1 11

How can Bhagavān Viṣṇu be called independent? If so, He would never have yielded to enter into various wombs. Therefore, O king! Know this that this whole universe is under the control of Yoga Māyā; the Devas, men, birds, what more everything from Brahmā down to a blade of grass are all under the control of Yoga Māyā. Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Hara all are bound by the rope of Her Māyā. So they roam easily by Her Māyā from womb to womb like a spider.

Here ends the First Chapter of the Fifth Book on the superiority of Rudra over Viṣṇu in the Mahā Purānam of Śrimaddevibhāgavatam by Maharsi Veda Vyāsa, consisting of 18,000 verses.

Book V Chapter II

CHAPTER II

On the Birth of the Dānava Mahiṣa

यात्र सच्च

योगेश्वरा: प्रभावोउरं कथितश्रातिविस्तरात् ।

ब्रृहि तचार्वितं स्वामिच्छ्योतुं कौतूहलं मम ॥ १ ॥

महादेवीप्रभावं वे श्रोतुं को नाभिवांशति ।

यो ज्ञानाति जगत्मखं तदुत्पयंचे चराचर्म ॥ 2 ॥

The king said: “Lord! You have described fully the glory of the Mahā Māyā Yogēsvari; now describe Her Life and Character; I am very eager to hear them. This whole universe, moving and non-moving, has been created by Maheśvarī; who is there that desires not to hear Her Glory!”

ध्यास उवाच

श्रुणु राजन्यवश्यामि विस्तरेण गहामते ।

श्रद्धायानय शानात्य न ब्यासल तु मन्दधीः ॥ 3 ॥

Vyāsa spoke: “O king! You are very intelligent; I will describe in detail all this to you; whoever does not describe Her Glory to the peaceful and faithful, is certainly low-minded?

पुरा युद्धमभूद्वारे देवदानवसेनयो: ।

पृथिव्यां पृथिव्याणल महिषाण्ये महीर्णते ॥ 4 ॥

In days of yore, a terrible battle ensued between

Devas and Dānava forces on this earth when Mahisāśura was the Ruler of this world.

महिषो नाम राजेन्द्र चकार तप उत्तमम् ।

गत्वा हेमिपती चोर्धे देवविस्यकाकतम् ॥ 5 ॥

O king! Mahisāśura went to the mountain of Suneru and performed a very severe and excellent tapasyā, wonderful even to the gods.

वर्षाणामयुतं पूर्ण चित्यनन्द्रिदे देखताम् ।

तस्य तुही महाराज बझा लोकपितामहः ॥ 6 ॥

O king! Meditating on his Iṣta Devatā (the deity for his worship) in his heart, elapsed full ten thousand years, when Brahmā the Grandfather of all the Lokas, was pleased with him.

तत्रागत्याब्वीढ़ावं हंसाक्वश्रतुर्भुंखः ।

वं वरय धर्मानन्दादिम तव वांछ्ठनम् ॥ 7 ॥

The fourfaced Brahmā, arrived there on his vehicle, the swan, and asked Mahisāsura “O One of virtuous soul! Ask from me what is your desired object; I will grant thee boon.”

महिष उवाच

आमत्वं देवेदेव वाज्छाभि द्रुहिण प्रभो ।

यथा मृत्युभयं न स्वात्तथा कुक चितात् ॥ 8 ॥

Mahiṣa said: “O Lord, Lotus-eyed! I want to become immortal! therefore O Thou, the Grandfather of the Devas! Dost thou do for me so that I have no fear of death.”

बह्योवाच

उत्पनस्य ध्रुवं मृत्युईं जन्म मृतस्य च ।

सर्वेधा मणीतचती सर्वेधां प्राणिजां किल ॥ 9 ॥

नाश: कालेन सर्वेधां प्राणिजां द्विपुंगव ।

महामहीधराणां च समुद्राणां च सर्वेधां ॥ 10 ॥

Brahmā said: “O Mahīsa! Birth must be followed by death, and death must be followed by birth; this is the eternal law of nature. Then know this as certain that when one is born, one must die; and when one dies, one will be born. O Lord of Dānavas! What more to say than this, that high mountains, vast oceans, and all the beings will die when time will come.

एकं स्थानं परित्यज मणयस्य महीपते ।

प्रबृहि तं वरं साधो यसे मनसि वर्ते ॥ १ १ ॥

O Ruler of the earth! You are virtuous; therefore ask any other boon than this immortality; I will grant that to you."

महिष्य उवाच

न देवामानुबाहैत्यामरणे मे पितामह ।

पुरुषाच च मे मृत्युर्षीया मां का हीन्यति ॥ १२ ॥

तसामाने मरणं नूनं कामियाः कुरु पयज ।

अवला हन मां केनुं कथं शक्ता भविष्यति ॥ 13 ॥

Mahisa said: “O Grand Sire! Grant, then, that no Deva, Dānava, nor human being of the male sex can cause my death. There is none among women who can cause my death. Therefore, O Lotus-eyed! Let women be the cause of my death; how can women slay me! They are too weak to kill me!”

ब्रह्मोवाच

यदा कदापि दैलेन्द्र नायांतसे मरणं धुवम् ।

न नरेषो महाभाग मृतिले महिषासुर ॥ 14 ॥

Brahmā said: “O Lord of the Dānavas! Your death will certainly occur, at any time, through a woman; O Highly Fortunate One! No man will be able to cause your death.”

व्यास उवाच

एवं द्रत्वा वरं तत्मी ययौ बह्जा निजालयम् ।

सोउपि दैत्यवरः प्राप्त निजं स्वानं मुतुवित्व: ॥ 15 ॥

Vyāsa said: “Thus granting him the boon, Brahmā went to his own abode; the lord of the Dānavas, too, returned to his place, very glad.”

राजोबाच

महिषः कस्य युप्रोउसी कर्वे जातो महावत्नी ।

कक्षे न माहिषं रूप प्राप्तं तेन महात्मना ॥ १ ६ ॥

The king said: “O Bhagavān! Whose son was this powerful Mahisāsura; how his birth took place? and why, too, did he get a body of a buffalo?”

Book V Chapter II

यास उवाच

दनोः पूत्री महाराज विद्यातों हिंतिमपड़ले ।

रामश्रीव कर्मभश्र द्वावास्तां दानवोतमौ ॥ 17 ॥

Vyāsa said: “O king! Rambha and Karambha were the two sons of Danu; these two Dānavas were far famed in this world for their pre-eminence.”

तावपूजी महाराज पूजार्थे तेनपुस्तपः ।

बदर्भर्पिणाण-कामा पुर्णे पछाने जले ॥ 18 ॥

“O king! They had no issues; hence, desirous of issues, they went to the sacred banks of the Indus (Pañca Nada) and there performed severe asceticism for long years.”

करामस्तु जले मान्यछाकार परमं तपः ।

यूक्षं रसावटं प्रायः रम्भोउनिम्मेववत् ॥ 19 ॥

पश्रामिनासधानासक्तः स रंभसून यदापवत् ।

जात्वा शर्षीपतिडुःखमुच्यो दानवो प्रति ॥ 20 ॥

Karamba got himself submerged in water and thus began his severe tapasyā; while the other, Rambha, had recourse to a juicy peepul tree (haunted by Yakṣinīs) and there began to worship Fire. Rambha remained, engaged in worshipping the Five Fires; knowing this, Indra, the Lord of Saṃ, was pained and hurried thither, being very anxious.

नाचा पड़नंदे तन ग्राहरूपं चकार है ।

वासवस्तु कारभं ते तदा जगाद पावयेः ॥ 21 ॥

Going to Pañcha Nada, Indra assumed the form of a crocodile and caught hold of the legs of the wicked Karambha and killed him.

निजधान च तं तुर्दं कारभं वूरमुद्नः ।

भातरं निरहतं शूला रम्भः कोपं परं नातः ॥ 22 ॥

स्वर्गीर्ष पावके होतुषेच्छच्छता केगे ।

केशणशे गृतीलास२५शु वामेन क्रोधसंयुतः ॥ 23 ॥

दक्षिणेन कर्णगर्भ गृहीला खद्वागुन्तामम् ।

छिन्ति शीर्ष तावाद्विहिना प्रतिविधितः ॥ 24 ॥

Hearing of the death of his brother, Rambha got very much enraged and wishing to offer his own head as an oblation to the Fire, he wanted to cut off his own head; he, being infuriated, held

the hair of his head by his left hand, and, catching hold of a good axe, by his right hand, was on the point of cutting it, when the Fire gave him knowledge, desisted him from this act and spoke thus:

उक्तछु दैत्य मुर्खोंजीस स्वर्गीशं उतुचिष्छसि ।

आतमह्यापतिछुःसाधाय कर्थं ले कर्तुमुहतः ॥ 25 ॥

वरं वरथ भई ते यत्मे मनसि वर्तते ।

मा विषयस मुनेताथ किं ते कार्य भविष्यति ॥ 26 ॥

"You are stupid; why have you desired to cut off your own head; killing one's own self is a great sin; and there is no means of deliverance from this sin. Why are you then ready to execute it?" Do not seek your death now; what end will that serve you? Rather ask boons from me; thus you will get your welfare."

व्यास उवाच

तच्छूला वचनं रमः पावकस्य सुभाषितम् ।

ततो3बबीडुचो रामस्थलवा केशकलापकम् ॥ 27 ॥

यदि तुढो3स देहेगा देहेि मे वारिड्ठतं वरम् ।

त्रैलोक्यविजयी पुः: स्यातः परवलातः ॥ 28 ॥

Vyāsa said: “O king! Hearing thus the sweet words of Fire, Rambha quitted the hold of his hairs and said: “O Lord of the Devas! If thou art pleased, grant my desired boon that a son be born unto me, who will destroy the forces of my enemy and who will conquer the three worlds.”

अजेब: सर्वथा स्याहेद्वतनयमानविः ।

कामस्पी महावीर्वः सर्वलोकामिवस्ति: ॥ 29 ॥

"And that son be invincible in every way is the Devas, Dānavas and men, very powerful, assuming forms at will, and respected by all."

पावकस्तं तंत्याहूष पविष्यति तवैप्रिस्तम् ।

पुत्रस्तव महाभाग मरणाहिमयानुना ॥ 30 ॥

Fire said: “O highly Fortunate! You will get your son, as you desire; therefore desist now from your attempt of suicide.”

यस्यां चित्तं तु रम्पा त्वं प्रमदानं कार्यस्य।

तस्यां पुत्रो महाभाग विध्वयित् वताधिकः ॥ 31 ॥

O highly fortunate Rambha! With any female

of whichever species, you will co-habit, you will get a son, more powerful than you; there is no doubt in this.

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्तो वहितां रभ्यो वचनं चितरऊनम् ।

भुत्वा प्रणाय्य प्रययी वहितं ते द्रावलोभमः ॥ 32 ॥

यक्षे: परिवृतं स्थानं परमाणिं शिव्यान्यितम् ।

सूद्वा चडेते तदा भावं महिष्यां द्रावलोभमः ॥ 33 ॥

Vyāsa said: “O king! Hearing thus the sweet words of Fire as desired, Rambha, the chief of the Dānavas, went, surrounded by Yaksas, to a beautiful place, adorned with picturesque sceneries; when one lovely she-buffalo, who was very maddened with passion, fell to the sight of Rambha.

मताचां रूपणुर्णां विहायान्यां च योषितम् ।

सा समागच्छ तसा कामवन्ती मुद्रान्ति ॥ 34 ॥

रामो5पि यानं चके भवित्यगुणिति: ।

सा तुु भर्षितां जाता महीधी तस्य वीयतेः ॥ 35 ॥

And he desired to have sexual intercourse with her, in preference to other women. The she-buffallo, too, gladly yielded to his purpose and Rambha had sexual intercourse with her, impelled as it were by the destiny. The she-buffalo became pregnant with his semen virile.

तां गृहीतावंथ पालातं प्रविश्य मनोह्मम् ।

महिषेध्यन्त्र तां रक्षिन्याममुन्ता किल ३ ३ ६ ॥

The Dānava, too, carried the she-buffalo, his dear wife, to Pātāla (the lower regions) for her protection.

क्रताधिन्यिष्ठान्य कामान्तैस्तामुपाद्वल ।

स्वयमान्य तं हेतुं दानवः समुपाद्वत् ॥ 37 ॥

On one occasion, another buffalo got excited and wanted to fall upon the she-buffalo. The Dānava was also ready to kill him.

स्वरक्षां समान्यल महिंं समताड्यत ।

सोर्पि नं तिज्ञानायुं शुभाप्यां कामाभेहितः ॥ 38 ॥

The Dānava came hurriedly and struck the buffalo for the safety of his wife; whereon the excited buffalo attacked him with his horns.

ताडितस्तेन तीक्षणान्यां शुभाण्यां हृदये भुणम् ।

भूमौ प्यात ततसा ममार च विमूर्हितः ॥ 39 ॥

The buffalo struck him so violently with his sharp horns that Rambha fell down senseless all of a sudden and finally died.

मुते भर्तीर सा दीना भयातां विद्युत भुणम् ।

सा वेगातं वटं गाय्य व्यक्षाणों शरणत ॥ 40 ॥

Seeing her husband dead, the she-buffalo quickly fled away in distress and, with terror, she quickly went to the peepul tree and took refuge under the Yakṣas.

पृढ़तस्तु गतस्तत्र महिषः कामपीडितः ।

कामयानस्तु तां कामी बलवीर्यमोद्धतः ॥ 41 ॥

But that buffalo, excited very much and maddened with vigour, ran in pursuit of her, desiring intercourse with her.

रुदती सा भुशं दीना दृषा यद्येष्यातृना ।

धायानं च तं वीक्ष्य यक्षास्तुं समाययुः ॥ 42 ॥

On seeing the miserable plight of the weeping she-buffalo, distressed with fear, and seeing the buffalo in pursuit of her, the Yakṣas assembled to protect her.

युब्द्रं समभवब्दोरं यक्षाणां च ह्वायरिण ।

त्रोग ताडितस्तूर्णी पचात धरणितले ॥43 ॥

मृतं रभं समानीय यक्षान्ते परभं विद्यम् ।

चितायां तोयामामुस्तस्य देहस्यु शुक्रदे ॥44 ॥

A terrible fight ensued between the buffalo and the Yakṣas, when the buffalo, shot with arrows by them, fell down and died. Rambha was very much liked by the Yakṣas; so they cremated his dead body for its purification.

महिली सा पति हृद्धा चितायां रोपित तदा ।

प्रवेषु सा मर्ति चक्रे पतिना सह पावकम् ॥ 45 ॥

The she-buffalo, seeing her husband laid in the funeral pyre, expressed her desire to enter also into that fire.

वाय्योपार्थि यक्षेः सा प्रविशेष शुताशनम् ।

ज्ञात्मानालुकले साधी पतिमदाय वतलभगम् ॥ 46 ॥

The Yakṣas resisted; but that chaste wife quickly entered the pyre along with her husband.

Book V Chapter III

महिष्यनु चितामध्यात्समुन्त्रधौ महावलः ।

रभोयव्यविद्युः कृत्वा नि:सूत पुरवस्तलः ॥ 47 ॥

When the she-buffalo died, the powerful Mahisa rose from his mother's womb from the midst of the funeral pyre; Rambha, too, emerged from the fire in another form out of his affection towards his son.

रक्रीकीजोपयसी जातो महिषोपि महावलः ।

अधिधिस्त्रु राज्येउसी ह्वारिसुरोतमे: ॥ 4 8 ॥

Rambha was known as Raktabīja after he had changed his form. His son was thus born as a very powerful Dānava and became famous by the name of Mahisa.

एवं स महिषो जातो रक्तबीजश्छ खीयंवान् ।

अवध्यस्तु सुरैर्दैल्मानवैश्व रुणेतम ॥ ४९ ॥

The chief Dānavas installed Mahisa on the

throne. O king! The very powerful Raktabīja, the Dānava Mahisa, thus took their births and became invincible of the Devas, Dānavas and human beings.

इत्येतत्क्रियां राजझ्म तत्थ महात्मतः ।

वरप्रदानं च तथा प्रोक्ते सर्व सविस्तम् ॥ 50 ॥

इति श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणी पद्धमस्कन्धे

महिषासुरो्य्पतिनीम द्वितीयंडधायः ॥ 2 ॥ 11

O king! I have now described to you the birth of the high-souled Dānava Mahiṣa and his getting the boon, all in detail.

Here ends the Second Chapter of the Fifth Book on the birth of Mahiṣa Dānavā in the Devi Bhāgavatam of the Mahāpurāṇaṃ composed of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter III

CHAPTER III

On the Daitya Armies Getting Ready

यास उवाच

एवं स महिषो नाम दानवो वर्दर्पितः ।

प्राय एकं उनसर्व वं जोके महाबलः ॥ १ ॥

पृथिवी पालवामास सागरानां भूजार्जिताम् ।

एकच्छत्रा निरारतंका वैरिवर्गविवर्जिताम् ॥ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: “The very powerful Asura Mahisa, puffed up with vanity on his getting the boon, obtained sovereignty and brought the whole world under his control! He, being of paramount power, began to protect the sea-girt earth acquired by the power of his own arms, over which he had the sole sovereignty, there being no other rival to the king nor any cause of the slightest fear.

सेनानीश्रिक्षतस्य महावीर्णं मदोकटः ।

भान्यक्षन्धता ताभः सेनाशुतममावृतः ॥ 3 ॥

His Commander-in-Chief was then the very powerful Cikṣura, maddened with pride; and Tāmra was in charge of the Royal Treasury, guarded by many soldiers.

असिलोमा तयोदकों बिडालाख्य शाकपतः ।

त्रिजोउध तथा कालबन्धको बलदर्पितः ॥ 4 ॥

There were, then, many gencrals Asilomā, Vidāla, Udarka, Bāskala, Trinetra, Kāla, Bandhaka and others, very proud, and each in charge of his own corps respectively and occupying this sea-girt earth.

एते सै-ययुता: सर्वे दानवा मेदिति ताद ।

आयुल्य संशित्या: काममुख्यो सागगमेखलाम् ॥ 5 ॥

कराद्धि कृता: सर्वे भूमिपाला: पुरातना: ।

निहता ये बलोद्वाः क्षानप्रभ्यविधिताः ॥ 6 ॥

O king! The powerful kings that reigned before were made subservient and tributary; and those, that fought vaunility befitting the Kṣattriya line, were slain by Mahiṣa.

ब्राह्मणा वरणा जाता यज्ञापासमर्पका: ।

महिषय्य महाराज निखिले श्रितिमण्डले ॥ ७ ॥

The Brāhmaṇas over the earth becama subservient to Mahiṣa and gave their Yajña offerings to him.

एकातपधे तत्राम्यं कृत्वा स महिषासुर: ।

स्वर्गे जेतु मनघक्रे वरादानेन ग्रविंतः ॥ ८ ॥

When that Mahisāsura got the sole sovereign

Šrnuaddevibhāgavatam

sway of this world, he proud of his boons, desired to conquer the Heavens.

प्राणियाँ प्रश्वासाम श्यातिरस्तु शनीपतिम् ।

स सन्देशहं श्रीप्रभादूयोबार इत्यतर् ॥ ९ ॥

Then Mahiṣa, the Lord of the Daityas, desirous to send an envoy to Indra, the Lord of Śacī, instantly called for the messenger and spoke to him thus:

गच्छ बीर महाबाही दुत्तलं कुक मेउनय ।

बृहि ग्राके दिवं नाला नि:गंकः सुरासिर्धी ॥ 10 ॥

Go, O hero! O valiant one! to Heaven." Act as my messenger and tell Indra fearlessly thus:

मुंघ खर्याँ सहसाक्ष ययेघं गच्छ माधिमम् ।

सेवां वा कुक देवेश महिषय साहात्मनः ॥ 11 ॥

स त्वां संस्थयेयूनं राजा शरणमागतम् ।

तस्मार्चं शरणं याहि महिषय सर्चीपते ॥ 12 ॥

नोघद्रजं गृहाणगु युद्धाय बलसूदन ।

पूर्वॉजितोस चास्माकं जनामि तव पीक्षम् ॥ 13 ॥

"O thousand-eyed one! Quit the Heavens; go anywhere you like, or offer your service to the highshould Mahisa! He is the lord; and if you take refuge unto him, he will certainly protect you. Therefore, O Lord of Śacī, better seek the protection of Mahisa. If, O Balasūdana! Not willing to do so, wield your Vajra at once; we know your powers; you were, in days of yore, conquered by our ancestors."

अहत्याजार विज्ञातं बलं ते सुर्यंघ ।

युध्यस्व जत्र वा कामं यत्र ते रमेत मनः ॥ 14 ॥

O chief of the Sūras! You are the paramour of Ahalyā; your strength is well-known, give battle or go anywhere you like."

व्यास उवाच

तच्छूला घवर्न तस्य शक्र: क्रोधासम्वितः ।

उवाच तं नृपश्रेश सिमतपूर्वे घचस्तदा ॥ 15 ॥

न जाने३हं सुमन्दात्मन् यतस्तं मद्दर्पितः ।

चिकित्सां सूक्ष्त्रियामि रोगस्याय प्रभोतरा ॥ 16 ॥

Vyāsa said: “Hearing the messenger’s words, Indra became very indignant and laughed and said: “I did not know, O you stupid, that you were

maddened with vanity; I will shortly give medicines for your master's disease."

अतः परं क्रियामि मूलस्याय निकृतनम् ।

गच्छ दुत तथा बृहि तस्यो मम भाषितम् ॥ 17 ॥

शिष्टैदूनान हन्तव्यात्मसाचां विशुबामवहम् ।

युद्धच्छा चेत्समागच्छ त्वितो महिषीमुत ॥ 18 ॥

हयोर न्वढृतं ज्ञातं शुणास्तवर्न जडाकृति: ।

श्रगयोते कारीव्यामि सुरुर्धं च शगासनम् ॥ 19 ॥

"Now I will extirpate him by the roots; wise persons do not slay messengers; I therefore let you go. Better go and tell him what I say: "Son of a buffalo! If you are willing to fight, better come and do not delay." "O Enemy of horse! (Buffaloes and horses are always at war with each other) Your strength is well known to me; you are a grass eater and your appearance is stupid, idiotic; out of your horns I will make a good bow." "You depend on your horns for your strength; that I well know.

दर्प: श्रंगवालान्तेस्ति विवितं कारणं मया ।

विषाणे ते परिस्थित्या संहित्यामि तदूलम् ॥ 20 ॥

यद्रतेनातिपूर्णस्वं जातोस्ति वलदार्थितः ।

कुशलस्वं तदायाने न युद्धे महिषाधम ॥ 21 ॥

You are clever in striking with your horns; you don't know anything about warfare; therefore I will cut off your both the weapons and render you powerless. You are very much puffed up with vanity due to that.

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्तोउसी सुरो-द्रेण स दुतस्वचितरी गतः ।

जगाम महिषं मन्ते प्रणाय्य प्रज्युवाच ७ ॥ 22 ॥

Vyāsa said: “Indra having spoken thus, the messenger quickly returned to his haughty master Mahisa and saluting, spoke:

दूत उवाच

राजन्देवाधिष कामां न त्वा विगणायत्यसौ ।

मयते स्ववर्त पूर्णं देवसैन्यसामावृतः ॥ 23 ॥

The messenger said: "Indra counts you not even a fig, as he is surrounded by his Deva forces and considers himself quite sufficient.

Book V Chapter III

यदुर्फं तेन मुखैंग कथमन्यदूबवीभ्यहम् ।

प्रिय सलं च वक्त्वयं भूत्वेन पुतः प्रभो: ॥ 24 ॥

If ought one's servant to speak true and pleasant before one's master, how can I utter the words before my master, that are spoken by that brute Indra."

प्रिय सलं च वक्त्वयं प्रभोरो शुभेच्छुना ।

इति नितिमहात्राज जागर्ति शुभकारिणी ॥ 25 ॥

Whereas the well known maxim reigns in my mind withal that I am your well-wishing servant and I ought to speak truth before you, my master, and that truth is to be pleasant to hear also.

केवलं चेत्रियं ब्याज ते कार्य भाविष्यति ।

परभं च न वक्त्वं कदाचिच्छुभमिच्छता ॥ 26 ॥

If pleasant words I speak only, then I fail in my duty; at the same time, harsh words ought not to be spoken by me, your sincere will-wisher.

यथा रिपुभुवाद्वारः प्रसरन्ति विषयोपाः ।

तथा भृत्यमुखाग्राव नि:सरन्ति कर्थ गिरः ॥ 27 ॥

यादुशानीह वाक्यानि तेनोक्तानि महीपते ।

तादुशानि न मे विज्ञा वतुर्महति कहिंघित् ॥ 28 ॥

My Lord! The cruel poison-like words that come from the mouth of an enemy, how can I, a servant of yours, utter those harsh sayings! O Lord of the Earth! I will never be able to utter those rude sayings that Indra has spoken.

व्यास उवाच

तच्छुत्वा वचनं तस्य हेतुगर्भं तृणाशनः ।

भूगं कोपपरीताता बभूव महिषासुरः ॥ 29 ॥

समाह्याववारीह्यानकोधसंरक्तलोचनः ।

तर्णूलं पृड्डरेगे च कृत्वा मूर्ष परित्यजन ॥ 30 ॥

Vyāsa said: “Hearing the messenger’s words full of meaning, the grass cater Mahisā Dānava got very angry and, waggling his tail behind his back, passed urine; then his eyes reddened with anger, he called the Dānavas before him and said:

भे भो दैवाः सुरैंडोजी युद्धकामोप्रेति सर्वथा ।

बलोद्योगं कुर्वयं वै जेतव्योजरी सुरायमः ॥ 31 ॥

“O Dānavas! The Lord of the Devas is firmly

resolved on battle; therefore collect your forces; we will have to conquer that devil, the chief of the Suras.

मद्यो को भवेच्छूरः कोटिगश्छेनाशविधाः ।

न विभेम्येकतः कामं इनिध्याय्यदा सर्वथा ॥ 32 ॥

Who can stand for him as my rival here! If hundreds and thousands of warriors like Indra come, I do not fear any of them at all; O Dānavas, we will thoroughly put an end to him.

शूरः शान्तेचस्पी नूनं तपस्विष्य बताधिकः ।

बलकर्ता हि कुहको लैप्टः पर्वात्रत् ॥ 33 ॥

His heroism is before only those that are peaceful and quiet, before the ascetics that have become lean and thin by penances; he is licentious and can only seduce other's wives by craftiness and arts.

अपरोबलसम्पत्नपोचिनकाः खलः ।

छिद्रप्ररण: यापो नित्य विश्वासयातकः ॥ 34 ॥

He is a thorough rogue and hypocrite, vicious and fault-finding; otherwise why does he put obstacles before others, depending for his strength only on the beauties of the Apsarās or heavenly prostitutes.

नापुचिनिरुक्तो येन कृत्वा सन्धि दुरावमना ।

नषायान्विवियानादौ कृत्वा भौतिन छघना ॥ 35 ॥

He is treachrous to very core; therefore he, being afraid at the very outset, took oaths, and entered into agreement with the high-souled Namuci; afterwards, when his time turned favourable, that villian broke his treaty and treacherously killed him."

विष्यानु कमटाचारः कुटकः शयाकाः ।

नानासपरः कामं बलकुर्भ्यपिडतः ॥ 36 ॥

Again the powerful Viṣṇu is a thorough master of treachery and hypocrisy, quick in taking oaths and can only show his vanity and is expert in that. He can assume many forms at will by his Māyic power."

कृत्वा कोलाप्तिकी येन हिरण्याक्षो निपातितः ।

हिरण्यक्रिशपुष्पैन् नृसिंहेन च पातितः ॥ 37 ॥

For these very reasons Viṣṇu had to take the form of a boar and kill Hiranyāksa; and again he had to take up a man-lion form to kill Hiranya Kaśipu.

नाहं तद्धगो नूनं भवेयं दनुनन्दना: ।

विश्वासं नैव गच्छापि देवानां कुक कहिंघित् ॥ 38 ॥

O Dānavas! Never shall I surrender myself to Viṣṇu, for I never place my trust in the words or deeds of Viṣṇu and his Devas.

किं कसिवाति मे विद्याप्रौत्रो वा वलवस्तः ।

रूजे वापि न मे शक़ः प्रतिकर्तू रणांगणे ॥ 39 ॥

What can Indra or Viṣṇu do against me, when the most powerful Rudra is not able to fight against me in the battlefield!

त्रिविष्टं प्रतिष्यामि तिजेन्द्रं वरुणं यमम् ।

धनदं पाककं चैव चन्द्रपूर्वीं विजित्व च ॥ 40 ॥

वझभाभपुज: सर्वे पशिव्यामोउढा सोभाप: ।

त्रिवा देवसमूहं च विहित्यामि दानवैः ॥ 41 ॥

I will instantly defeat Indra, Varuna, Yama, Kubera, Fire, Sun and Moon and get possession of their Heavens. On our conquering the Devas, we all shall get our share of Yajñas and we along with other Dānavas drink the Soma juice and enjoy ourselves in Heaven.

न मे भयं सुरोभ्युं खरादानेन दानवाः ।

मरणं न नरोभ्युं नारी किं मे कारिष्यति ॥ 42 ॥

O Dānavas! I have got the boon; what do I now care for the Devas. My death is not from men too. What can a woman do to me?

पातालपवैतैभ्यघ स्माहुव राजनारन् ।

द्रावनावाम सै-योगात्कुवंतु त्वलिताश्रर: ॥ 4 3 ॥

O my emissaries! Call without any delay the chief Dānavas from the nether regions and the mountains and make them my generals?

एकोडे सर्वदेवेशगतिविजतुं दानावा: शमः ।

शोभाषं वः समाहूय नयामि सुरसुझमे ॥ 4 4 ॥

O Dānavas! I can alone conquer all the Devas; only to make war arrangements look nice, that I am taking you to defeat them.

श्रृगायां च खुतायां च हनियेड़र्ग सुगतिकल ।

न मे भयंं सुरेय्यछु खरतानप्रभावतः ॥ 45 ॥

There is no fear of mine from the Devas, consequent on the boon conferred on me. I will kill them by my hoofs and horns.

अवध्योड़ं सुरगणैरसुरैमान्वैस्तथा ।

तस्मालज्ञा भवन्त्यद्वे देवलोकजयाय दै ॥ 46 ॥

I am not to be killed by Suras, Asuras, as men; therefore get yourselves ready to conquer the Devas.

त्रिला सुरालयं दैत्वा विकृत्तियामि नन्दने ।

मन्दारकुभुमपीडा देवयोषित्यमित्राने ॥ 47 ॥

O Dānavas! After conquering the Heavens we will be garlanded with Pārijāta wreaths and we will enjoy the Deva women in the Nandana Garden.

कामधेनुपयोसितभा: सुधापानप्रमोदिता: ।

देवान्यर्वर्गीतादितून्यलायमसपिततः ॥ 48 ॥

We will drink the milk of the heavenly mulching cow (the cow that yields all desires) and, intoxicated with the heavenly drinks, we will hear and see the music and singing the dancing of the Gandharvas there.

उद्वीगी मेमका राभा घृताभी च तिलोत्ताम ।

प्रमद्धरा महासेना मिश्रक्षेगी महोल्कटा ॥ ४ १ ॥

You will all be served there with various bottles of wine by Urvaśī, Menakā, Rambhā, Ghṛtāci, Tillottamā, Pramadvarā Mahāsenā, Miśra Keśi, Madotkatā.

विद्यचितिप्रपृतवी नृत्यनीतिविशपराद: ।

रूत्रविष्यन्ति वः सर्वांत्रानसविनिवेशीः ॥ 50 ॥

Vipracitti and others. Then be all ready at once for the auspicious occasion to march to Heaven and fight there with the Suras.

सर्वे सज्जा घर्वेल्हा रोत्नां गमनं दिवि ।

संग्रामार्थ सुरैः साधुं कृत्वा मुकूलपुत्तमम् ॥ 51 ॥

स्थूलार्थ च स्वर्वं घानांरं घुनिस्तमम् ।

समाहूच च संपूज्य स्थाय्य चेत्रे गुरुं परम् ॥ 52 ॥

And be pleased to call that pure-souled Muni Sukrācārya, the son of Bhrgu and the Guru of the Daityas and worship him and tell him to perform sacrificial ceremonies for the safety and victory of the Dānavas."

Book V Chapter IV

<table><tr><td>व्यास उवाच</td></tr><tr><td>इति सन्निर्यय दैत्येन्द्रान्माधिष: पाघपीस्तदा ।</td></tr><tr><td>जगाम त्वितीता राजन्मेवनं स्यं मुदात्यितः ॥ 5 3 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे ततुर्भरकन्मे दैत्यसैन्योहोगे नाम तृतीयोउतयान: ॥ 3 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>Vyāsa said: O king! Thus, ordering the chief</td></tr></table>

Dānavas, the wicked Mahiṣa went to his abode, with gladness.

Here ends the Third Chapter of the Fifth Book on the Daitya armies getting ready in Śrimaddevibhāgavatam, the Mahāpurānam by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa of 18,000 verses.

Book V Chapter IV

CHAPTER IV

On the War Counsels Given by Indra

व्यास उवाच

गते दुते सुरेन्द्रोप्रि समाहूय सुरान्य ।

यमावायुभान्यक्ष्मणानिवमूचिवान् ॥ 1 ॥

महिषो नाम दैवेन्द्रो रमभूत्रो महावलः ।

वरदर्भमेदो-मन्तो मायाशतविश्वक्षणः ॥ 2 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The messenger of the Dānavas having departed, Indra, the lord of the Devas, Yama, Vāyu, Varuna, and Kubera and other Devas, called an assembly and addressed thus: "O Devas! the most powerful Mahisa, the son of Rambha, is now the king of the Dānavas; he is particularly expert in hundred of Māyās (māyic) and has become haughty on the strength of his boon."

तस्य दृतोडा सम्यालतः प्रेषितसेन भोः सुरा: ।

स्वर्गकामेन लुब्धिन मायुवारोदूर्ण वचः ॥ 3 ॥

O Devas! Mahiṣa has sent his messenger; he wants to take possession of the heaven; he come down to me and spoke thus:

स्वज देवालयं शक्र यदेष्छं जज वासव ।

सेवां वा कुरू दैत्यस्य महिषस्य महात्मनः ॥ 4 ॥

“O Indra! Quit your this heaven and go any where you like, or be ready to pay your homage to the high souled Mahisāsura, the Lord of the Dānavas.”

दयावादानवेत्रोउसी से तुक्ति विधायती।

से तेषु भृत्यभूतेषु न कुण्यति कदाचन ॥ 5 ॥

The Dānava Chief never becomes angry with his opponent who becomes submissive like a servant; if you surrender and serve him, he will, out of mercy, grant an allowance to you.

नोचेबुद्धाय देवेश सेनोहोांगं कुर स्ववम् ।

गते माय स दैल्येन्द्रस्वित्तः समुपेथति ॥ 6 ॥

O Lord of the Devas! If this does not behave you, then collect your forces and be ready for fight; no sooner I return, the Lord of the Dānavas will come here at once ready to give battle to you.

इत्युक्त्वा स गतो तृती दानवस्य दुरात्मनः ।

क्रिं कतन्यमतः कार्य चित्तपत्थं सुरोतमाः ॥ ७ ॥

Thus saying, the messenger of that wicked Dānava departed. Now what are we to do? O Devas! Think on that.

दुबलेतीपि न चोधेक्यः शुब्बैलवता सुरा: ।

विशोधेण सदोद्योगी बलवान्बलदार्यितः ॥ 8 ॥

O Devas! Even a weak enemy is not to be overlooked by a powerful opponent, especially when the enemy is powerful by his own powers and is ever energetic, never is he to be overlooked.

उद्यम: कलि कर्तेणी यथा बुद्धिद्यां बलम् ।

दैवाधीनी भवेघूनं जयो वाउध पराजयः ॥ 9 ॥

It is always incumbent on us to make our efforts, as best as we can, both by our body and mind as far as lies in our power; the result, victory or defeat depends entirely on Fate.

सचिव्योगो न चागसित खले सचित्रिनिर्वकः ।

सर्वथा साधुभि: कार्य विचार्य च पुन: पुनः ॥ 10 ॥

It is useless to make treaty with a deceitful and dishonest person; we therefore never should make treaty with this person; you are all honest; that Dānava is dishonest; therefore ponder and ponder deeply and ponder again; do you that which is proper.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

यानमध्यून नैव कत्लेंव सहसा पुनः ।

प्रेशकाः प्रेषणीयाशु शीर्भगाः सुप्रेशकाः ॥ १ १ ॥

It is not advisable to go out at once for fight when we are unaware of our enemy's strength; let us therefore send spies truthful, honest, motiveless, quick, to ascertain their strength, those who can easily enter amongst our enemies and yet who have no relation, nor any interest with them.

ईक्तिज्ञान्थ निसज्ञा निस:सुखा निस:सुखा सत्यबालिनः ।

सेनापियोगं प्रव्यानं बलसेंख्यां यथावीतः ॥ 12 ॥

The arrangements of their forces, their movements, their numbers, they will ascertain correctly; who are their generals, what is their number and what is their strength, they will thoroughly examine and return here quickly.

वीरणांच च परिज्ञानं कृत्वा यान्तु त्वारपिततः ।

त्राला दैत्यपतेतस्य सै-नस्य च बलाबलम् ॥ 13 ॥

First, we will ascertain the strength of the force of our opposite party and then we will decide at once whether we will start for battle or seek protection within forts.

कौर्यामि ततस्तूर्णे याता वा दुर्गसंग्रहम् ।

"विषायं खतु कतैल्यं कार्य बुद्धिपता सदा ।"

सहसा विहितं कार्य दुःखदं सर्वधा भवेत् ॥ 14 ॥

Wise persons always consider before they act; any act done rashly leads in all respects to many troubles, and anything done after mature prejudgments leads to happiness; so the wise do.

तस्मादिपूग्य कतैलं सुखदं सर्वथा बुधैः ।

नात्र भेदविधिन्यान्यो दानवेषु च सर्वथा ॥ 15 ॥

The Dānavas are all one in their heart and mind; therefore it is not advisable, in any way to apply the principal of Bheda (sowing principles of discord).

एकशित्रेषु कार्येस्सिमस्तमाचारा जन्तु वै ।

ज्ञाला बतालतं तेषां पश्ञाजिति विचार्य च । । १ ६ ॥

Let our spies go there, ascertain their strength, return and inform us; we will then judge what principle is proper and apply to the expert Dānavas.

विधेधा विधिचित्जैस्तेषु कार्यपेषु च ।

अन्यथा विहितं कार्य विव्यतिफत्पत्रम् ॥ १ ७ ॥

सर्वथा तद्वेक्नूनमज्ञातपोषधं यथा ।

Any act done contrary to policy and expediency will undoubtedly produce effects contrary in every way just like a medicine which we have not tried already.

व्यास उवाच

इति संचित्य तै: सर्वे: प्राणिधं कार्यवेदिन् ॥ १ ॥

प्रेयवामास देवेऋः: परिज्ञानाय पार्थिवः ।

दूतस्तु चित्तीत गत्वा समागम्य सुर्गाधिधम् ॥ १९ ॥

निवेदयामास तदा सर्वे सैन्यवलावनम् ।

Vyāsa said: O king! Thus counselling with the Devas, Indra, sent expert spies to ascertain the true state of affairs. The spies, too, went into the abode of the Daityas, with no delay and made their searches thoroughly into every nook and corner and returned and told Indra all the strength of the Dānava force.

ज्ञाना तद्वलपुत्रोगे तुरापाडतिविसितः ॥ 20 ॥

देवानचोदयनुर्ण समाहूय पुरीहिनम् ।

मन्नं मन्नविंशं श्रेष्ठ चकार विदशेष्यः ॥ 21 ॥

उवाचांगिरासश्रेष्ठ समासीने वासने ।

Indra was very much startled to know, then, of their arrangements. He immediately bade all the Devas be ready for battle and called for his High priest Bhraspati, expert in giving advices and began to consult with him how to carry on the warfare with that indomitable enemy, the Lord of the Asuras. On Bhraspati, the best and famous of the Aginā family, taking his excellent seat.

इन्र उवाच

घो भो देवगुर्गे विद्धिकं कत्थं वद्यव नः ॥ 22 ॥

सर्वैज्ञोसि समुपवे कार्यं त्व गतिच्छ नः ।

Indra thus said: "O Guru of the Devas! O Learned! Please say what are we to do now in this critical juncture? You are omniscient; today you are our guide.

दानवो महिषो नाम महार्थीवर्ग मदोत्तिथ : ॥ 23 ॥

योढ़ुकामः समायाति बहुभिद्वनिवैवृतः ।

Book V Chapter IV

The Demon Mahisa has become very powerful, very haughty; surrounded by Dānavas he is now coming to fight with us.

तत्र प्रतिक्रिया कार्यं त्वया मन्त्रविद्धपुना ॥ 24 ॥

तेषा शुक्रतथा त्वं मे विन्तहतां सुसंयतः ।

You are expert in mantras; find out the remedy for us. Śukrācārya is the remover of all obstacles on their side; and that you are our safe guard is well known to us.

व्यास उवाच

तच्छुत्वा वचनं प्राह तुलासहं बृह्यसितः ॥ 25 ॥

विविच्य मनसा कामं कार्वसाधानतपर: ।

Vyāsa said: Hearing these words of Indra, Brhaspati, who is always ready to effect the Deva's purposes, thought intently on the subject, said very shortly thus :

गुरुक्षवाच

स्वस्यो भव सुरेन्र त्वं धीयमोल्ब्य मारिष १ 2 6 १।

यवमने च समुचये न त्याज्यं धीयागु वै ।

Brhaspati spoke: O Lord of the Devas! O Venerable One! Be peaceful; have patience; when a difficulty comes, one should not, all on a sudden, lose one's patience.

वयाजयी सुराध्यक्ष देवार्थिनी सदैव हि ॥ 27 ॥

स्वातयं दीयमातंव्य तस्मादुबुद्धिमता सदा ।

O Chief of the Immortals! Victory or defeat is completely under the control of destiny; therefore intelligent ones should always be patient.

भवित्यं भवत्येव जानत्रेव शतक्नो ॥ 28 ॥

उद्यम: सर्वेथा कायों यद्यापौरभयामना ।

O Śatakratu! What will unavoidably be done must come to pass; knowing this as certain, one would always be an enthusiast and exert one's powers.

मुनयोपि हि मुक्नवयंमुधामैकरता: सदा ॥ 29 ॥

दैवधार्थि च जानन्तो योगाध्यानपरायणा: ।

तस्मासदैव कर्तैवो स्वहारोदितोदाय: ॥ 30 ॥

सुखं भवतु या मा वा दैव का परिवेदना ।

विना पुरुषकारेण कदाचिशसिद्धमानुयात् ॥ 31 ॥

अन्यवत्पडूवत्कामां न तथा मुदामाबहेत् ।

कृते पुरुषकारोंपि यदि सिद्धिनं जायते ॥ 32 ॥

Everything is guided by Fate. Knowing this, the Munis devote themselves at all times solely filled with energy in their meditation and Yoga practices for their final liberation. Therefore, to show one's energy, according to the rules of the daily practices, ought to be indespensably done; and one should not repent or feel pleasure on failure or success; for that is under Fate. Success sometimes comes without the exercise of one's own powers, as seen in cases of the lame and the blind; and that is not the reason why one should be very glad.

न तत्र दूषणं तस्य दैवाधिने श्रौरिपि ।

कार्यसिद्धिर्नं सैनैर्जस्ति न मनेन न च मज्यो ॥ 33 ॥

न रथे नायुधे नूनं दैवाधीनासुरापि ।

The embodied beings are all under Daiva (Fate); therefore, even if success be not attained, though one's own powers are exercised thoroughly, no one is to blame for that. O Lord of the Suras? What to say of forces, mantras, or advices, what if chariots or weapons, nothing is to lead to success; It is Daiva, and only Daiva that makes one successful.

बलवान्वलेषाग्यानोति निर्विलः सुखम्यनुते ॥ 34 ॥

बुद्धिमान्थ्यितः शेने निर्विद्धभोगवान्ययेत् ।

कातारो जययान्तोने शुग्रे याति पराजयम् ॥ 35 ॥

धवाधिने तु संसारे कामं का परिदेवना ।

उद्यो योजयेनुं भवित्वयं सुराधि ॥ 36 ॥

दुःखदे सुखदे वार्थि तत्र तो न विधितंयेत् ।

This whole universe is under Daiva; it is, therefore, that we see powerful persons suffering pains, and weak ones getting happiness; the intelligent ones sleeping without any food and fools enjoying merrily; distressed persons getting victory and powerful ones suffering defeats; what cares, then, ought one to entertain in this. O Lord of the Suras! Whatever is inevitable to come to pass, be it success or failure, one will lead one's

Šimaddevbīhgāvatam

energies to that end; therefore one needs to consider beforehand whether one's energies will be successful or not. In times of distress, one sees distress too much; and in times of pleasure, one seeks pleasure too much; one's self, therefore one should not surrender to one's enemies pleasure and pain.

कुर्ये दुःखाधिकान्यपेसुरेषे पशेस्युखाधिकम् ३ 37 ॥

आमानं हर्षीकोकायां सुब्याधीमव नार्येत् ।

धीयमेवावगानलंहं हर्षीकोद्वे वृष्टे: ॥ 38 ॥

अर्थयोद्याणुरं कुं त्र न दुर्धेंयित तादुपग् ।

दुर्लंभं सहन्तवे वै समये सुखदुःखयो: ॥ 39 ॥

हर्षीकोद्वीय तय न यवेवेतुद्विन्दिश्यात् ।

किं कुःखं कस्य वा कुःखं निरुणोड़े सदाजययः ॥ 40 ॥

चतुर्बिशातिरित्तोउस्मि किं मे दुःखं सुखं च किम् ।

प्राणस्य कृष्णविपासे हैः मनसः शोकमूच्छैप ॥ 41 ॥

जगमूच्यगुरीरस्य चकुर्भिरिक्तः शिवः ।

Pain and suffering is not felt so much in patience as is felt when impatient; therefore one must practise patience when pain or pleasure comes. Indeed, it is very difficult to bear oneself up in distress or happiness; therefore wise persons try not to let these feelings crop up at all from the very beginning. “I am always full, undiminishable, I am beyond Prākrtiç qualities. Who is there to suffer? What is suffering?” Thus one ought to think at that moment. I am beyond the twenty four Tattvas; what pleasure or pain can, arise to me? Hunger and thirst are the Dharma of Prāna; pain and insensibility is the Dharma of mind, old age and death belong to this physical body. I am free from these six diseases; I am Śiva.

गोकामोही शरीरस्य गुणी किं मेउत्र चिंतते ॥ 42 ॥

शरीरं नात्यमयात तत्सव्यनी न चायह्म।

सपैकेकोडगादिष्यो विभिन्नोड़ं सदा सुखी ॥ 43 ॥

प्रकृतिर्विकृतिराई किं मे दुःखं सदा पुनः ।

इति मत्वा सुरेश त्वं मनसा भव निर्ममः ॥ 44 ॥

Grief and delusion are the qualities of this body; what then do I care for them? “I” am not the qualities of the body nor “I” am the soul pertaining

to that. I am beyond the seven transfigurations, change, e.g. Mahat, etc., I am beyond this Prakrti, Nature, and beyond the sixteen changes wrought out by Prakrti; I am therefore eternally happy, I am beyond Prakrti and its transformation, then why am I to suffer pain always? O Lord of the Suras! Think on these and be without any passion.

उपायः प्रथमोउन ते दुःखनागे शतक्रतो ।

ममता परमं दुःखं निर्ममत्वं परं सुखम् ॥ 4 5 ॥

सन्तोबादपरं नास्ति सुखस्थानं शबीपते ।

अथवा यदि न जानं ममत्वनाग्ने किल ॥ 4 6 ॥

ततो विवेकः कर्तेबो भवित्ये सुराधिप ।

प्रारक्षकर्मणां नागो नाभोगतलस्थवे किल ॥ 4 7 ॥

यद्धालि तद्धलयेव का चिन्ता सुखुख्यवो ।

O Śatakratu! This attachment is the root of all miseries; and non-attachment is the source of all happiness; non-attachment, therefore, is the chief means of the extirpation of all your troubles. O Lord of Śachi! Nothing can be happier than contentment. In case you do find it difficult to practise dispassion, apply, then, discrimination and think of Fate, that what comes inevitably to pass. O Lord of the Suras! Actions already done cannot die out without their effects being enjoyed.

सु: सर्वै: सहायैर्वां बुद्धा वा तल सत्ता ॥ 48 ॥

सुर्ज क्षयाय पुण्यस्य दु:खं पायस्य मारिति ।

O Best of the Suras! Let all your intelligence be brought to action, let all the Devas lend their helping hands to you; what is inevitable must come to pass; what then can you care for your happiness or pain?

तस्मात्सुखक्षये हर्ष: कतन्व्य: सर्वेबा बुधै: ॥ 49 ॥

अश्वा मन्त्रलाड़ कुरु यन्तं यथाविषि ।

कृते यन्ते महाराज भवित्वयं भविष्यति ॥ 50 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे पढ़मकन्के

चतुर्धोध्याः ॥ 4 ॥

O King! Happiness is felt for the expiation of good deeds and pain is felt for the expiation of bad deeds; therefore wise persons get thoroughly delighted when their punya ends. O King! Judge

Book V Chapter V

and hold a council today; then try your best. But what is unavoidable will come to pass, even if you try your best.

Here ends the Fourth Chapter of the Fifth Book on the counsels given by Indra in the Mahā Purānam Śrimaddevibhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharsi Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter V

CHAPTER V

On the Defeat of the Dānava Forces of Mahisā

व्यास उवाच

इति श्रुत्वा सहसाधः पुनराह बृह्यस्तिन् ।

युद्धोर्णं करिष्यामि ह्यानेनिशनाय वे ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: The thousand-eyed Indra, hearing this, again asked Brūṣapati that he would make preparations for war against Mahisāsura.

नोद्योन विना राज्यं न सुखं न च वै यशः ।

निरुद्धायं न शंसिति कातता न च सोहायः ॥ 2 ॥

Without effort kingdoms are not attained; no nor happiness, nor fame, nor anything; those who are weak, they exol effortlessness; but the powerful never praise that.

यतीनां भृपणं ज्ञानं सन्तोषो हि ह्विजनमान्।

उद्यम: श्रत्रुकनं भूषणं भूतिमच्छताम ॥ ३ ॥

Knowledge is the ornament of the ascetics and contentment is the ornament of the Brāhmanas; but those who desire lordship over powers, effort and prowess to destroy one's enemies are their excellent ornaments.

उद्यमेन हतल्याष्ट्री नपुचिबल एव च ।

तर्वेनं निर्हिन्यापि मदिषं नुप्सितस्म ॥ 4 ॥

O Muni! I will kill this Mahisāsura by my heroism as I had, of old, destroyed Vṛtta, Namuci and Balāsura.

वलं देवगुरुस्तं पे घड़ामायुधतुमम् ।

सहायस्तु हरितूनं तथोपापतित्वयः ॥ 5 ॥

You are the Deva Guru; therefore you and my thunderbolt are my strength. The immortal Hari and Hara also will help me in this.

रक्षोटानयट मे साधो करोग्यदा समुदायम् ।

स्वरैन्यार्थिनियं च महिंत्र प्रति मानद ॥ 6 ॥

O Guru! Preserver of my honour and prestige! Now recite the mantras calculated to remove all

the obstacles towards my victory. I, too, am making preparations and raising up my own forces to wage up war against that Dānava Mahiṣa.

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्तो देवराजेन वायस्यतिकावाच ह ।

सुरेन्द्रं युद्धसरांक सिमतपूर्वं वचलसाद ॥ ७ ॥

Vyāsa said: On hearing Indra's words, Brāhspati smiled and said: "O Lord of the Devas! I see you are bent on fight. I will neither stimulate you to fight nor shall I make you desist from your the purpose. The issue is doubtful.

ब्रह्मसतिकावच

प्रेयामि न चाहं त्वां न च निवर्तेयामदम् ।

सन्दिष्योज जये कामं युध्यतश्र पाजाये ॥ 8 ॥

There may be defeat or there may be victory. O Lord of Śacī! You are not to blame at all in this matter; what is written in the Book of Fate will come to pass, be it victory or defeat.

न तेउन दूषणं किकड्ज्द्वितन्त्ये जशीयते ।

सुखं वा यदि वा दुःखं द्विक्तं च भविष्यति ॥ १ ॥

I am not aware of the future in this respect. O Child! You know already what an amount of suffering I had to endure in times gone by when my wife had been stolen.

न मया व्यस्तिर्ज्ञां भावि दुःखं सुखं तथा ।

यद्वायहिरणे प्राप्तं पुगा सामव वेसि सि ॥ १० ॥

O Destroyer of the enemies! My wife had been stolen by Moon who turned out my enemy; living in my stage of an householder, I was put to all sort of miseries, deprived of all my happiness.

शशिना में हता भार्यों निश्चेगामिकर्जन ।

स्वाभ्रमश्चेन सम्याप्त दुःखं सर्वसुखापहम् ॥ १ ॥

Šrimaddevibthāgavatam

ब्रुद्धिमान्सर्वलोकेषु विवितोर्जे सुराधिप ।

क्व मे गता तता बुद्धिदंदा भार्यां हता कवतात ॥ 12 ॥

O Lord of the Suras! I am renowned in all the worlds as a man of much wisdom and intelligence. Where then was my intelligence, when Moon carried away, perforce, my wife.

तमायुपायः कत्नोये बुद्धिमद्धिः सदा सरे: ।

कार्यसिद्धिः सदा नूनं देवधीनीय सुरापीध ॥ 13 ॥

O Lord of the Suras! To my mind, success or failure depends entirely on destiny; yet intelligent ones should always resort to efforts and be energetic.

3 यास उवाच

तच्छुवा वचनं सत्यं गुरोः सार्थि श्रवीपितः ।

ब्रहाणां शरणं पत्या नत्वा वचनमब्रवीत् ॥ 14 ॥

विनामहं सुगाध्यक्ष दैत्यो महिषर्शकाः ।

ग्रहीतुकामाः स्वर्ग में वक्तोषांगं करोलत्यम् ॥ 15 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! On hearing the words of Brhaṣpati, pregnant with truth, Indra went with him to Brahmā, took his refuge and saluting him said: O Grand Sire! The Dānavas are collecting a big army, and wants to conquer and take possession of the Heavens.

अन्ये च तयावा: सर्वे तत्सैन्यं समुपस्थिता: ।

योत्पुकामा महावीधीः सर्वे युद्धविशारादः ॥ 16 ॥

All the other Dānavas have enrolled themselves in the list of his army; they are eager to fight and they are all very powerful and skilled in arts of warfare.

तेनाहं घीतभीतोउभि्य तलसकाशमिहारतः ।

सर्वज्ञोसि महाप्रात साहाययं कत्नुमहिंसि ॥ 17 ॥

I am therefore very afraid and have come to you. You know everything; please help me in this matter.

ब्रहोवाच

गच्छामः सर्वे एवाहि केलासं स्विता वयम् ।

शूक्तं पुस्तः कृत्वा विष्णुं च बलिनां वयम् ॥ 18 ॥

Brahmā said: We all will go today to the Mount Kailāsa and take Śaṅkara with us and go to Viṣṇu.

ततो युद्ध प्रकर्त्व्य सर्वैः सुरगणैः सह ।

मिलिता मन्यमाधाय देशं काला विधित्य च ॥ १९ ॥

There all the Devas, assembled, will hold a council and consider the time and place, when it will be settled whether it is proper or not to fight.

बलाबलमवित्रतं विवेकमपहाय च ।

साहसं तु प्रकृत्यागी नरः पतनमूच्छिति ॥ 20 ॥

For one who dares to do any act without considering one's strength and without any judgment, certainly courts his own downfall.

व्यास उवाच

तश्रिण्य सहस्वाक्ष: कैलासं निर्जीगाम है ।

ब्रहाणं पुतः कृत्वा लोकपालसमविन्तः ॥ 21 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing this, Indra with the other Lokāpalas and Devas, headed by Brahmā, went to Kailāśa.

तृषाव शड़हं नरा वेदन-नैमेहंधम् ।

प्रसर्च पत: कृत्वा यद्यै विद्युपुरं प्रति ॥ 22 ॥

Then they came to Śaṅkara and sang vedic hymns to him. Maheśvara became very much pleased and they taking Him went to Vaikuntha, the abode of Viṣṇu.

स्तुत्वा तं देवदेवेशं कार्य प्रोवाच चामनः ।

महिषात्मद्धं चोर्धं वरदानमदोढ़तान् ॥ 23 ॥

Indra saluted Viṣṇu and sang hymns to him, and told about his errand thus: “Mahīṣa has become very haughty on account of the favour bestowed on him and therefore we are very afraid (and therefore ask your help to relieve us from this danger).

तत्करणं घयं तस्य विज्युद्वितानुवाच ह ।

क्रतिथायो वयं युद्ध हनिष्यामस्तु दुर्जनम् ॥ 24 ॥

Viṣṇu, then, hearing the cause of fear, told them: "We all will fight and kill that Demon."

व्यास उवाच

इति ते निशयं कृत्वा ब्रह्धिष्युहीक्षा: ।

स्वानि स्वानि समासष्ठ वाहतानिय क्युः सुः ॥ 25 ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! Thus settling the questions, Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and Hari and Indra and the other

Book V Chapter V

Devas riding on their own Vāhanas (means of conveyance) respectively dispersed.

बहा हंससमाको विष्णुगंडवहानः ।

गंकते वृषभाक्तो वृत्रहा गरसंश्रितः ॥ 26 ॥

मयूरवाहनः क्वन्तो यमो महिषवाहनः ।

कृत्वा सैन्यसमायोगं घावते निर्वयुः सुरा: ॥ 27 ॥

तादहैर्यवलं प्राप्तं चूनं महिष्यपालिनम् ।

ततभूतभूतनं युद्धे देवदानवसैन्ययो: ॥ 28 ॥

While Brahmā on his vehicle Swan, Viṣṇu on his Garuda, Śaṅkara on his Bull, Indra on his elephant Airāvata, Kārtika on his peacock, and Yama, the god of death on his Vāhana, the Buffalo, were on the point of going with the other Deva forces, the army of the Dānava Mahisa met them on their way, all fully equipped with arms and weapons. A dreadful fight then ensued between the Devas and the Dānavas.

वागी: खड़रीत्या प्रासैमुलेष्ते पघधोः।

गदाधि: पद्ट्रीश: गृत्योक्षेक्षे शक्तितापे: ॥ 29 ॥

मुढैर्धिन्यपालैंह छटीक्षातिदाकणी: ।

अन्यैक्षि विविधरखैर्निजनुले पस्पमम् ॥ 30 ॥

Arrowas, axes, Prāsas, Mūsalas (clubs), Paraśus (pick axes), Gadās (clubs), Pattisās, Sūlas (tridents), Cakras (discus) Śakti (weapons), Tomaras, Mudgaras, Bhindipālas, Lāngalas, and various other deadly weapons appeared on the scene with which they fought against one another.

सेनानीश्रिक्षुरतस्य गजार्को महावलः ।

मथवन्तं पशुधभसैः सायकैः समताड्यत् ॥ 31 ॥

The Commander-in-Chief of Mahisa, the very powerful Ciksura, shot five sharp arrows at Indra.

तृग्याड़ी पतिछला वाणीबाणांस्वात्रिलतः ।

हदये चाधधन्नेण ताउवामास ते कृती ॥ 32 ॥

The ever-ready and light-handed Indra, too, with his arrows cut off all of them and struck at his heart heavily with his Ardachandra (half moon) arrow.

बाणाहतसु सेनानीः प्राण मूच्छा गजोपये ।

वर्णं वज्ञायनेस स ज्ञान करे ततः ॥ 33 ॥

तद्ध्रापिछातो नागो भवनः सैन्यं जगाम ह ।

दृष्ट्वा ते दैव्यगद् कुब्दो विज्ञालास्मधावर्जीत् ॥ 34 ॥

गच्छ वीर महावाहो जहाँई मदार्यादिनम् ।

वरणाधीर्यान्दोबान्द्वासप्राव्यमान्तिकम् ॥ 35 ॥

The Commander-in-Chief, struck by this arrow fell senseless on the back of his elephant. Indra, then struck the trunk of the elephant with his Vajra (thunderbolt); the elephant then severely struck with the Vajra fled away into the Dānava's forces, The Lord of the Dānavas seeing this, got very angry and addressed the general Vidāla "O Hero! You are very powerful; go then and kill first that haughty Indra; then kill Varuna and other Devas and come back to me."

व्यास उवाच

तच्छुला वचनं तस्य विज्ञालायो महावलः ।

आहकही वाकणं मर्त जगाम विद्याधिपनम् ॥ 36 ॥

Vyāsa said: The very powerful Vidāla, on receiving the order came up at once to Indra, mounted on a very furious elephant.

वासवस्तं समावानं नृद्वा क्रोभस्पनितः ।

ज्ञान विगिष्ठतस्तीक्षणीरागविषयमप्रधे: ॥ 37 ॥

Seeing him coming, Vāsava shot at him angrily with very terrible and most powerful arrows that looked like deadly snakes.

स तु त्रित्ता शतस्नुर्णं स्वरगैक्षापनि: सुतेः ।

पश्ठागर्द्रजक्रौभागृ आसचं च शिलीमुर्षे: ॥ 38 ॥

But the Demon, too, cut off those arrows at once with his excellent arrows and quickly shot at Vāsava fifty arrows, sharpened on stones.

तर्पेज्ञपि च तत्बाणिछित्वा कोपसमित्ति: ।

ज्ञान विज्ञास्त्रीक्षीरात्रिविस्वमप्रधे: ॥ ३१ ॥

स तु चित्वा शरांसृर्ण स्वरोंश्रापनि: सुतेः ।

गद्वा तात्यामास गजं तस्य क्रोपेरि ॥ 40 ॥

Indra cut off all those and, being infuriated, shot again sharp deadly serpent like arrows at him, and cutting off again all his enemies' arrows by arrows discharged from his bow, struck the elephant's trunk with his Gadā (club).

स्वकरे निर्तहो नायश्रकारात्रैवरं मुहु: ।

परिवृत्य जयानागु दैत्यसैन्यं भयातुर्म् ॥ 41 ॥

The elephant, being thus struck on his head, cried aloud in a distressed tone and being afraid turned back, thus killing the Dānava forces as he fled away.

दानवस्तु गर्ज बीक्ष्य परावूल्य तत गर्ता ।

स्मार्विष्य रद्ये रच्ये जगामाशु सुर्गन् रणे ॥ 42 ॥

General Vidāla, seeing the elephant fleeing away from the battlefield, mounted on a beautiful chariot and instantly appeared before the Devas to fight with them.

तृगधादि तं वीक्ष्य रथस्यं पुनरागतम् ।

अहनद्रिशिजैस्तीक्ष्योगशिविमसप्रमर्धे: ॥ 43 ॥

Seeing the Dānava coming again on a chariot, Indra shot at him sharp arrows after arrows like venomous snakes.

सोप्रि कुब्द्रशकायोरी याणवृष्टि महावल ।

बधूव तुयुले युद्धं तयोसत्र जयैषिणीः ॥ 44 ॥

The powerful Dānava, too, infuriated hurled at terrible arrows; then a sharp conflict ensued between Vāsava and the Dānava.

इस्ट्रस्टु बलिनं दूध्वा कोपेनाकुलितेंत्रिय ।

जयन्तमग्रतः कृत्वा युवुघे तेन संयुतः ॥ 4 5 ॥

Finding the Dānava powerful, Vāsava's senses were confounded with anger; he then took his son Jayanta before him and began to fight.

जयन्तसु शितिवाँगीलेयं जयान सनानते ।

पड़धिः प्रवलक्रुहैरुर्ग मवर्याविंन ॥ 4 6 ॥

Jayanta stretched his bow tight and shot at the breast of the Dānava swollen with pride, five sharp arrows with his full strength. Thus shot at by the network of arrows with his full strength.

स वाणाधिरस्तावत्रियपत श्वोरिप ।

अतिनाहा रथे मुतो निजांगमा रणाजितरात ॥ 47 ॥

Thus shot at by the network of arrows, the Dānava fell unconscious on the chariot; the charioteer then fled away with his chariot from the battle-field.

तम्मिन्विनिर्ते दैते विडालाखेउव मूर्छित ।

जगगळो महानासीद्वनुभौनां च पि:स्वतः ॥ 48 ॥

Thus on the Dānava Vidāla becoming

unconscious and being taken away from the field, the Dundubhīs (drums) of the Devas were resounded and great aclamations of “Victory to the Devas” were heard.

सुग प्रभृदिता: सर्वे तुहुवुस्तं ज्ञाचीपतिम ।

जनुर्गधर्वेषतयो ननृतृधायसोपेगणः ॥ 49 ॥

The Devas were very glad and sounded hymns before Indra; the Gandharvas began to sing and the Apsarās began to dance.

चुकोप मधिष: शुत्वा जयग्वल सुरैः कृतम् ।

प्रेयायास तदेव तापं परमदायकम् ॥ 50 ॥

O king! Hearing the loud acclamations of victory to the Devas, Mahiṣa became very angry and ordered the Dānava Tāmra, the destroyer of enemy's pride, to go to the battle-field.

तामस्तु बहुधिः सार्यं समायाल्य रणातिजे ।

शराष्ट्री चकाशगृ ततिह्वानिव सागरे ॥ 51 ॥

Tāmra appeared in the battle, and, coming face to face with many Deva warriors, hurled on them showers of arrows.

वक्षण: पाणिणुच्य जगाम स्वरितस्तदा ।

यमश्र महिषारूको दर्षदामादाय निर्वर्षी ॥ 52 ॥

Varuna appeared with his Pāśa weapon and Yama, mounted on his buffaio, appeared with his Dāndā (staff).

तत्र युदमभूष्ठौरं देवदानवयोर्षय: ।

बाणीः खद्यौषु मुसलेः शक्तिश्विष्य पर्षधे: ॥ 53 ॥

A terrible fight then ensued between the Devas and Dānavas and the weapons, arrows, axes, Mūsalas, Śaktis and Paraśuṣ guittered in the fields. दर्जकेन निहतसाधो यमहत्नोहोने च ।

न चचाल महाबाहु: संग्रामांगणतस्तदा ॥ 54 ॥

वापमाकृय योगे मुक्लवा तीवार्षिद्वरीमुखान ।

इन्द्रादीनहन्तृष्टा तापसतमिश्रणारिजे ॥ 55 ॥

Yama raising his Dānḍa with his hands struck at Tāmra; but the powerful Tāmra, though severely struck, was not at all moved and remained firm in his place in the field. On the other hand, Tāmra, violently drawing his bow, hurled a mass of sharp arrows at Indra and the other Devas.

Book V Chapter VI

तेपि देवा: शरीद्वैर्दर्दिशितेश शिलाशितः ।

निजनुद्रनवानकुन्द्रांतिरूल तिष्ठेति चुकुशुः ॥ 56 ॥

The Devas got angry and shot at the Dānavas multitudes of divine arrows sharpened on stone, and frequently called aloud “Wait, wait.”

निहतसर्ः: सुरैद्वेयो मृष्ठ्रांमाय रणांगणे ।

हाहाकारो महानासीदैत्यसैये भयातुरे ॥ 57 ॥

श्रिति श्रीमदैशीभगवते महापुराणे पत्रमकन्चे दैनसैन्यपराजयो

- नाम पड़गोउध्याः 11 5 11

The Dānava Tāmra thus shot at by the arrows of the Devas, fell unconscious in the battle-field; the Dānava forces got afraid and a cry of universal consternation and distress arose.

Here ends the Fifth Chapter of the Fifth Book on the defeat of the Dānava forces of Mahisa in the Mahāpurānam Śrimadlevibhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣī Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter VI

CHAPTER VI

On the Deva Dāinava Fight

व्यास उवाच

तापेउध मृचिते दैत्य महिष्यः क्रोधसंयुतः ।

समुधस्य गरांं गुर्वीं देवानुपरणसाग मह ॥ १ ॥

तितंत्वच सुरा: सर्वे इन्यदं गदया किल ।

सर्वे बलिषुजः कामं बलहीनाः सदैव ॥ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! On the Daitya Tāmra becoming unconscious, Mahisa became very angry and, raising his Gādā (club), came up before the Devas and said: "Devas! O Ye powerless like crows; wait; with one stroke of Gadā, I will kill you."

इत्युक्नवाउंगी नगान्हलं समप्राय मद्मार्वितः ।

ज्ञान गद्या तूर्णं बाहुधूले महापुर्षः ॥ ३ ॥

Thus saying, the powerful Mahisa swollen with pride, seeing Indra before him mounted on his elephant, instantly struck him on his arms.

सोप्रि वङ्गण धोगेण विच्छेद्वणु गदां च ताम् ।

प्रहृकौमास्वतीतो जगाम महिंश प्रति ॥ ४ ॥

Indra, again lost no time, and struck violently with his thunderbolt and cut the Dānava's Gādā into pieces, and came up very close, wanting to strike at him.

हयारिति कोपेन खड्गमादाव सुप्रभम् ।

ययचिन्द्र महावीर्वं प्रद्रत्यिज्ञ्वांतिकम् ॥ ५ ॥

Mahisa, too, becoming very angry took up his lustrous sword and came to Indra to attack him with this weapon.

बधूव च त्योयैंदूं वर्षलोकपयावहम् ।

आयुधैर्विंविद्धेस्त्र सुनिविसमयकारकम् ॥ ६ ॥

A fight then occurred between the two, terrible to all the Lokas and wonderful to the Munis, where various weapons were showered from both the sides.

चक्रानु तादा दैवो मायां मोहकर्ता किकल ।

शाभार्थी सर्वतोकन्धी भूनीनामपि मोहितैन् ॥ ७ ॥

The Demon Mahisa spread then his Sāmbhari Māyā, destructive to all the worlds and fascinating to the Munis.

कोटिओ गो महिष्यास्त्र तनुष्यास्त्याकमाः ।

ददृषु: सायुधा: सर्वे निर्णंतो देवाहितीम् ॥ ८ ॥

Hundreds and hundreds of powerful buffalo-like appearances resembling Mahisa became, then, visible on the battle-field; they all began to kill the Deva forces with weapons in their hands.

माथवा विल्यास्तत्र दृष्टवा तां दैत्यविमितिम् ।

बभूपातिनघयोढ़िनो मायां मोहकर्ग किल ।। ९ ।।

वरणोपि सुसंग्रस्तलथेव धन्यानवकः ।

यमो हुताशनः सूर्वः शीतरिणमर्थायतुः ॥ १ ० ॥

Seeing this majic of the Dānava, Indra became thunderstruck and very much confounded with terror. Varuna, Kubera, the Lord of wealth, Yama, Fire, Moon, Sun, and other Devas all fled in terror. Indra then, being surrounded by the network of magic, began to call Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Maheśa in his mind.

पलायनयरा: सर्वे बध्धुर्मिहिता: सुरा: ।

ब्रह्मविष्णुमदेशानां स्मरणं चकुकृत्या: ॥ १ १ ॥ ॥

तत्राजमुख काजेशा: सुतमात्रा: सुगोतमा: ।

इसताख्यैवृषाकडाखतुकामा वरायुधा: ॥ 12 ॥

At the instant when they were called in mind, Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Maheśa riding on respective conveyances Swan, Garuda, and Bull, came up there with best weapons in their hands for Indra's protection.

शीरितां मोहैर्नी दृष्टा सुदर्जनयोज्जलम् ।

मुगोच ततेजसैव माया सा विलयं गता ॥ 13 ॥

Visnu seeing the play of that fascinating magic hurled his bright discus, Sudarśana; and caused the magic to vanish at once.

शैक्ष्य तान्योषसतर सृधिश्वल्प्त्तलिणि: ।

योयुकामः समादाय परिघं समुपाद्रवत् ॥ 14 ॥

Seeing the three, the Creator, the Preserver, and the Destroyer, the Dānava Mahisa came up there with his Parigha (a club tipped with iron) weapon, desirous to fight with them.

महिषाण्डो महावीरः सेनानीशक्षुरस्तथा ।

उयायश्छीप्रवीर्थ शुद्वुर्वुद्धकामुकाः ॥ 15 ॥

असितेमा जिनेश्वं बाक्लतधक एव च ।

एते चान्ये च बह्वो योद्धकामा विविनेयुः ॥ 16 ॥

Then the general Cikṣura, Ugrāśya, Ugravirya, Asilomā, Trinetra, Vāskala, Andhaka and other warriors came up fight.

सन्द्रध दृतचापासे रथाकडा मदोद्धता: ।

परिवहु: सुगत्सर्वान्यका इव सुवत्सकान् ॥ 17 ॥

Those proud Dānavas, clad in armour and mounted on chariots with bows in their hands besieged the Devas, like a tiger attacking an heifer.

बाणपृष्टि ततश्रकादनिवा मदार्थविताः ।

सुगशापि तथा चकुः परस्परजिधासेवः ॥ 18 ॥

Then those Dānavas swollen with pride began to shower on arrows after arrows; the Devas, too, began to do the same, desiring to extirpate them.

अत्यको हरिसासाघ पत्रबाणापिऊलशितान् ।

मुपोच विषसपिद्धधान्कणीकृष्टामहावलम् ॥ १९ ॥

The General Andhaka, coming up to Hari, drew his bow with great force up to his ear and shot at him five sharp arrows tipped with poison.

वासुदेवोउपसम्प्रापतान्विशिज्ञानगृहीस्तदा ।

चिच्छेदे तानपुनः पड्न मुमोन सिंगुनानः ॥ 20 ॥

Vāsudeva, the Destroyer of the enemies, cut off those arrows no sooner they came up before him; and He shot at the Dānavas five arrows.

तयोः परस्यं सुद्धं बभूव हरिदैत्ययोः ।

बाणासिचकमुसलरोगदाराक्षिपश्धये: ॥ 21 ॥

Then Hari and the Dānava struck each other with various weapons and arrows, disēus, Mūsala, clubs, Śakti, and Paraśu.

महेशोध्यकायोर्दुंदं तुगुर्न लोमहर्भणम् ।

पडाणरिद्नपर्यर्त वधूच च परस्यम् ॥ 22 ॥

Here, on the other hand, the fight lasted for fifty days between Maheśa versus Andhaka; and it was a very close conflict, causing horripilation.

इन्द्रबाष्कलयो तद्धन्सिद्धासुरक्रदयो: ।

यमत्रिजेयोस्तद्नसहहानुधनेशयो: ॥ 23॥

Thus severe fights ensued between Vāskala and Indra, Mahisa and Rudra, Trinetra and Yama, Mahā Hanu and Kubera, Asilomā and Varuna.

असित्मोवकरणायोर्पुंद परमदृतकर्णम् ।

गरुडं गटया दैत्यो जयान हरिवाहनम् ॥ 24 ॥

स गदापातिखाज्ञानै नि:श्रसत्रवित्रहिति ।

शौरितं दक्षिणेनागुं हतेन परिसांत्वयम् ॥ 25 ॥

स्थिरं चकार देवेशेगे वैनंतय महावलम् ।

समाकृष्य धनुः शणई मुगोच विशिष्ठान्बून् ॥ 26 ॥

The Dānava Mahiṣa struck Garudā, the conveyance of Hari, with his club; Garuda, being very much distressed with the blow, sat down, gasping. Viṣṇu then comforted the powerful Garuda, the son of Vinatā and made him calm and quiet.

अत्यंकोपरि कोपेन हनुतकामो जनादनः ।

दायावेपि च तान्वाणांश्छिद्धे स्वर्गे: शितः ॥ 27 ॥

Wanting to kill Andhaka, Janārdana became infuriated, and, drawing his bow made of horn, call Śārṇga, shot at him arrows after arrows.

पडाणिद्वर्दरी कोभाज्ञान च शिलाशिति: ।

वासुदेवोपि नासूनुर्णे वंचविख्या शरोतमान् ॥ 28 ॥

The Dānava cut off all those arrows to pieces

Book V Chapter VI

with his own mass of arrows. Then, becoming very angry, he shot fifty sharp arrows at Hari.

झकं मुगोच बेगेन सहसारं मुडर्गन् ।

त्यन्के सुर्दर्गनं दुरात्स्वचेकम प्यबारयत् ॥ 29 ॥

Vāsudeva quickly made all those arrows useless and hurled Sudarśana Cakra with thousand spokes on the Dānava with great violence.

ननाद च महाराज देवतन्मोहयत्रिव ।

दूध्वा तु विफलं जातं चढ़े देवस्य शाईपुः ॥ 30 ॥

Andhaka thwarted this with his own discus and shouted aloud with such a great force that all the Devas became confused and confounded. Viṣnu's cakra being baffled, the Devas became distressed with grief and the Dānavas got elated.

जम्भु: शोकं सुरा: सर्वे जहरुदैरनावसता ।

वासुदेवोकि रासा दूर्धा देवावछूष्ठा उज्ञान । 31 ।

Seeing the Devas thus grieved, Viṣṇu held alotī his Kaumodaki Gādā (club) and came hurriedly before the Dānava.

गदां कोमोदकीं धृत्वा दानवं सम्पात्रवत् ।

तं ज्ञानातिवेगेन मूर्टिन मायावित्र नहीं: ॥ 32 ॥

स गदाउभितो मूमी निपयातातिमूचितः ।

तं तथा पतितं वीक्ष्य ह्वायरितिकोपानः ॥ 33 ॥

आजगाम यानाथे ज्ञायव्रतिराजितैः ।

वासुदेवीपि तं सुखा समायानं क्रामतिवतम् ॥ 34 ॥

Hari stuck then with his Gādā on the Dānava's head, whereon he fell senseless on the ground. The hot-tempered Mahisa, seeing Andhaka senseless, bellowed aloud and, terrifying Hari, came up there Seeing him there, Vāsudeva made such a thundering noise with his bow string that the Devas became highly glad.

वापस्वानितदं चोर्धे चकार नद्यन्सुरान् ।

शरब्धरि चकारायु भगवान्महिषपरि ॥ 35 ॥

सोप्रि चिच्छेद बाणौधैस्ताऊरानगभरेत्तान ।

तयोरुंद्रमभूरतज्ञयरस्यभगवाहम् ॥ 36 ॥

Then the Bhagavān shot showers of arrows on Mahisa; and Mahīṣa, too, cut those arrows while they were seen in the air. O king! Then a very close fight ensued between the two. Keśava struck on the head of the Dānava with his club.

गदया ताड्यामास केशवो मस्तकोपरि ।

स गदाधिरहो भूर्भि पपानोत्यां सूमूर्हितः ॥ 37 ॥

Thus struck, he fell in a swoon on the ground and a general cry of distress arose amongst the Dānavas.

हाहाकागो महानासीसैन्ये तस्य सुदारणः ।

स विहाय चवधां दैनो मुक्तितुंदितः पुनः ॥ 38 ॥

In a moment the Dānava got up again, free from trouble; he then struck again on Viṣnu's head with his Parigha (a club mounted with iron, a mace).

गृहीत्व परिघं शीर्षं जयान मयुर्मुदनम् ।

परिश्रेणाहत्सेन मुर्जीमप जनदानं: ॥ 39 ॥

Struck by that mace, Janārdana lay senseless; Garuda seeing him thus unconscious, immediately took him away from the battle field.

मृद्धितं तमुबाहाणु जगाम भकडो रणात् ।

परावृते जगत्राधे दे्वा इन्द्रपुरोगमाः ॥ 40 ॥

भयं प्रगुः मुडुःखातांश्रुकृषुश रणात्रों ।

कंदरमानासुरान् वीक्ष्य संकाः: शुलभून्ता ॥ 41 ॥

When Vispu thus fled, Indra and the Devas were much distressed with fear and began to cry aloud. Hearing the Devas cry, Śaṅkara became wrathful and, quickly coming before Mahisa, struck him with his trident (Śūla).

महिषं तत्सास्थेय त्राहरोषसंयुतः ।

सोर्धि शर्फि मुणोचाथ शंकस्योरिसि क्रुद्यम ॥ 42 ॥

The wicked Mahisa made his weapon ineffectual and bellowed aloud as he struck on the breast of Śaṅkara with his Śakti (a kind of missile).

ज्ञर्जे स च वुहात्या वंधविता शिशुलकम् ।

शक्रौरोषि तदा पीक्षां न प्रणोरस तीक्षितः ॥ ४३ ॥

तं ज्ञान श्रिगृतेन कोपादरुगतोचनः ।

संलनं शंकरं दृष्ट्या महिषेश्न दुरात्मा ॥ ४४ ॥

Thus wounded in his breast Śaṅkara diś not feel any pain; rather, with his eyes red with anger, He struck him again with Triśūla. Seeing Śaṅkara engaged with Mañṣa, Hari becoming conscious came again on the battle-fields.

आजगाम दहरितवचकत्वा मुहां प्रहारजाम ।

महिषस्तु तदा वैश्य संग्रगते हरिगंकारे ॥ 4 5 ॥

Śrimaddevithāgavatam

युद्धकामी महावीर्वीं चक्रगुल्धरी वरी ।

कोपयुक्तो बंभावासी दूद्या ती समुयागती ॥ 46 ॥

Seeing the two powerful Deva chiefs, Hari and Hara, in the battle-field Mahisa became very much angry; he then assumed a buffalo body and wagging his big tail to and fro came in front of them with a desire to fight.

जगाम संमुखसतावल्संधामार्थ महापुबः ।

माहिषं वपुरास्थाय धुवन्युच्छं समुकटन ॥ 47 ॥

चकार चैपरं नई आसयवमतरनपि ।

धु-वढ्छंगी महाकायो दाकणो जलदो यथा ॥ 48 ॥

That terrible Mahisa of a huge body shook his horns and bellowed so deep like a thunder cloud that even the Devas got frightened. He began to hurl the huge mountain peaks with his two horns.

भृगभयं पर्पताट्यूढ़ग्रशिक्षेय प्रयामुकटन ।

द्वृता ती तु महावीर्वीं दानवं देहवसनों ॥ ४ ९ ॥

The two powerful Devas Hari and Hara, began to shoot at the Dānava deadly arrows after arrows.

चक्रतुबाणवृष्टि च दानवोपरि दाफाम् ।

कुबाणीय बाणवृष्टि तो दूद्या हरितही हरि: ॥ 50 ॥

Seeing these two gods shower arrows upon him, Mahiṣa began to hurl mountains on them by his tail.

चिवेष प्रिरिश्रंगां तु पुच्छेनावूल्य दाफणम् ।

आपतन्ते प्रिंरि वैश्य भपावान्सावतां पति: ॥ 51 ॥

Visņu cut off those mountains into hundred

pieces by his arrows and struck at him instantly with his Cakra.

विशिष्टोः शताया चेके चक्रेणाणु जयान तम् ।

हरिचक्रान्तः संखेय मूच्छांमाप स दैत्यवर्ध् ॥ 52 ॥

Struck thus by Cakra, the Lord of the Dānavas fainted, but he instantly rose up with a human body.

उतस्थी च क्षणाङ्नं मानुपं वयुराधितः ।

गदायामिमहीघोगे दानवः पर्वतोपयः ॥ 53 ॥

The mountain-like terrible Dānava with a club in his hand frightened the Devas and uttered grave sounds like those of rumbling rain clouds.

मेघवादं ननादोच्यैपरिचयमगनायि ।

तच्छूदा भगवानिष्युः पोचजस्यं समुञ्जलम् ॥ 54 ॥

Hearing that, the Bhagavān Viṣṇu sounded a more terrible sound with his Pañcajanya Śaṅkha (conchshell).

पूरयामास तरसा शब्दं कर्तुं खरस्वम् ।

तेन शब्देन शंख्यय भवमताश्र दानावाः ।

बषव्युप्टिता देवा ऋषयश तपोधनाः ॥ 55 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे पत्रमकन्ने भटोःशायः ॥ 6 ॥ 11

Hearing the sound of that conchshell, the Dānavas were struck with terror and the ascetic Rṣis and Devas became exalted with joy.

Here ends the Sixth Chapter of the Fifth Book on the Deva Dānava fight in Śrimadevibhāgavatam the

Mahā Purānam of 18,000 verses

by Maharṣī Vedā Vyāsa.

Šrimaddevībhāgavatam

CHAPTER VII

On the Going of the Devas to Kailāsa

व्यास उवाच

असुतामहिषो भूद्यि विषणपन्नसत्त्वा ।

त्यक्त्वा तन्माहिषह रूपं बचुप मृगगडसी ॥ १ ॥

कृत्वा नादं महापोरं विस्तार्यं च महासटान् ।

घपात सुरसेनायां जासयत्रखदर्शनैः ॥ 2 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Mahiṣa, seeing the Dānavas afflicted with grief, quitted his buffalo appearance, assumed a lion form and spreading his long mains began to roar aloud and fell amidst

the Deva forces; then the Devas were terrified on seeing his sharp nails.

मार्डे च नखापातैः कृत्वा कृधिरवितृम् ।

जयान च धुवे लिणुं नख्यापातेन केक्षेरी ॥ ३ ॥

That lion-form Mahisa first attacked so severely the Garuda with his nails, that his whole body was besmeared with blood; then he attacked Vişnu's arms with his nails.

वासुदेवो5पि तं दूधका चकमुध्यय वेगवान् ।

हन्तुकामो हरि: काममवायगु कुश्यान्वितः ॥ 4 ॥

Book V Chapter VII

Seeing the Dānava, Vāsudeva Hari raised his discus in anger and attacked him with great force to kill him.

यावद्ध्यरियुं वेगान्चक्रेणाणिबज्ञान तम् ।

तावलोरोतिजलः शुड़ीं शुर्णायां न्यह्नद्धरिण ॥ 5 ॥

Just when Hari struck the Dānava violently with his Cakra, the powerful Dānava quitted immediately his lion-form, assumed the buffalo form and struck Hari with his two horns.

वासुदेवो विषाणायां ताडितोरिस विद्धलः ।

पलायवपरो वेणाजजगाम पूर्वनं निजम् ॥ 6 ॥

Vāsudeva, thus pierced in his breast with the horns, became confounded and fled away as best as he could till he reached his own abode, Vaikuntha.

गतं दूद्वा हरिं कामं संक्रोरूपि भयान्यन्तः ।
