अवध्यं तं परं मत्ता ययी केतामयर्वतम् ॥ 7 ॥

Seeing Hari thus fleeing away, Śaṅkara, too, thought him invulnerable and fled to his Keilāśa mountain with fear.

हद्धारपि च निथ धाम त्वरितः प्रययो भयात् ।

भयावा वज़्मालंब्य तस्थावाजौ महावलः ॥ 8 ॥

Brahmā, too, fled to his own abode with terror; but the powerful Vāsava took patience and remained steady in the battle.

वकणः शक्तिमालंब्य धैर्यमालंब्य संस्थितः ।

यभोर्षि दणघमादाय चत: समतत्वरः ॥ 9 ॥

Varuna taking his Sakti waited patiently for battle. Yama, too, with his staff remained there ready to fight.

ततो यक्षाधियः कामं बधूव रणतयरः ।

पाबकः शक्तिभादाय तताभ्युब्द्रमानसः ॥ 10 ॥

Kubera, the Lord of the Yakṣas, remained very busy in close fighting with the Dānavas; Fire, taking Śakti, also waited.

नक्षत्राधियपितः सुर्यः स्मवेति श्वितावृषी ।

बीक्ष्य तं दानवश्रेष्टे युद्धाय कृतनिश्रशी ॥ १ १ ॥

The Sun and Moon, the Lord of the stars, both remained in firm resolve to fight Mahişa, Lord of the Dānavas.

एतमिचलेते कुब्दे देत्यसेन्यं समभ्यगत् ।

विशुजबाणजालानि कृगहिसुशानि च ॥ १ २ ॥

O King! In the meanwhile, the Dānavas forces got angry and attacked them on all sides, shooting at the enemies a mass of dangerous serpent-like arrows.

कूला हि महिषं रूपं भृपति: संस्थितस्तदा ।

देवदानवयोधानां निनादस्तमुतोप्रभत् ॥ १ ३ ॥

The Lord of the Dānavas, Mahisa, too, assuming the buffalo appearance, reigned supreme in the middle.

न्याघात्रु ततल्यातो मेघनादसमोर्भभवत् ।

संग्रामे सुमहायोरे देवदानवसेनयोः ॥ १ ४ ॥

At this moment fierce sounds of the warriors on both the sides were heard. During the sharp contest of the Devas and Dānavas, the sounds from the bowstrings and the clippings of the hands were heard like the roarings of thunder.

श्रुभाध्यां पवतत्वरूपांशिक्षेय पर महावलः ।

जयान सुरसंधांश दानवो महावर्षितः ॥ 15 ॥

The powerful Dānava, then swollen with pride, began to hurl the mountain tops with his horns, thus killing the Deva forces.

सुरापाततैल्वा देवान्युच्छस्य धामणेन च ।

स जयान रूपाधिछा महिषः परमादूतः ॥ 16 ॥

Some by hoofs and some by the lashing of the tail, that angry Mahisa, very wonderful to behold, sent to the region of Death.

तते देवाः सर्पिधर्वा भयमाजमुकझाताः ।

मघवा महिंशे दूद्वा पलायवनरोभवत् ॥ 17 ॥

Then the Devas and Gandharvas became very much frightened; so much so, that Indra fled away once on the sight of Mahisa.

संगरं संपरित्यज्ञ नगे श्रेक शचीपती ।

यमो धनाधिः पाशी जमुः सर्वे भयानुतः ॥ 18 ॥

महिषोर्पि जयं मंता जगाम स्यगृहं ततः ।

रेरातं गजं प्राय त्यचार्मिदेश गच्छता ॥ 19 ॥

तवोच्चे:श्वसं भानो: कामधेपुं पश्वस्वीम् ।

स्वरैय-त्मसंतूनर्णे स्वर्ग गन्तुं मनो दो ॥ 20 ॥

Indra thus retreating from the field, Yama,

Kubera, and Varuna all quitted the battle-ground with fear. Indra fled away quitting his Airāvata elephant and Uccaísravā horse; so Mahiṣa got the possession of the elephant and the horse, as well the heavenly cow of the Sun. So the Dānavas considered themselves pre-eminently victorious and returned to their abodes.

तस्या देवसदनं गत्वा स महिषासुरः ।

जग्रह सुरारज्यं वै त्यक्तं देदेश्यातुरैः ॥ 21 ॥

Next they wanted to go, as early possible, to the Heavens, with all their forces. In no time Mahisa went to the abode of Indra, deserted by all the terror-stricken Devas and got the possession thereof.

इत्रासने तदा रथे दानवः समुपाविष्ठान ।

द्रानवान्स्याययामास देवानां स्थानवेकु सः ॥ 2 2 ॥

Then taking his seat on the beautiful throne of Indra, he made the other Dānavas occupy the several seats of the other Devas.

एवं वर्षेगतं पूर्णं कृत्वा युद्धं सुदारुकाण् ।

जयार्थैप्रदरं कामं दानवो मदार्थिकं: ॥ 23 ॥

Thus fighting full one hundred years, the Dānava Mahiṣa, puffed up with pride, acquired the seat of Indra, his desired object.

निर्जी निर्मिता नाकानेने सर्वेजनीतीझता: ।

एवं बहुिन वर्षाणि बध्मुर्गिरागूरं ॥ 24 ॥

He banished the Devas from the Heaven; the Devas, thus tormented began to wander in the caves of hills and dales for a period of good many years.

श्राता: सर्वे तदा राजनबहाणों शरणं ययुः ।

प्रजापति जगजाधं राजोकर्ण चतुर्मुद्धम् ॥ 25 ॥

O King! The Devas, at last, were quite tired and took the four-faced Brahmā, the Creator's refuge.

यद्यासनं वेदभर्भे सेवितं मुनिनिः स्वजैः ।

मतीचिपुप्रषुः शान्तैर्वंदेवतूद्वपारः ॥ 26 ॥

किन्ने: सिद्धान्यवैक्षारणोरागवर्षेः ।

गुरुद्वर्भेधनीताने देवेवंदे जगतुतुम् ॥ 27 ॥

At that instant, the Lord of the world, the Rajas

incarnate, the Originator of the Vedas, was seated on His lotus seat; surrounding Him were standing his mortal sons Marici, etc., with their passions subdued, mind calm and beyond the sphere of the Vedas and Vedāngas; there were also Siddhas, Gandharvas, Kinnaras, Čāranas, Uragas, and Pannagas, the terrified Devas then began to praise and chant hymns to Brahmā, the Lord of the world.

धातः किमेत्तदिखलातिर्हारांबुजन्म

जन्माधिवीक्ष्य न दया कुरुषे सुगान् यत् ।

सम्पीडितानप्रणजितानसुराधिघने

स्थानच्युतानिरगुरुहाकृतसश्रिवासान् ॥ 28 ॥

The Devas said: “O Creator! O Lotus-born! O Thou, the Remover of the pains and afflictions of all this world! How is it that you are not moved with pity towards the Devas, seeing that we are defeated by the lord of the Dānavas and have been banished from our abode; what more shall we say, our troubles are now indescribable, as we are living in the caves of hills and dales.

पुत्रमिता किमपराधानतेः समेतानंत्यज्य

लोभग्रहितः कुकतेजितुःस्थान् ।

यस्त्वं सुरांतव पदावुज्ञभिन्युक्तानान्

दैत्यादितांध कुपणान् यदुपेक्षसेपेध ॥ 29 ॥

O Creator! A son may be a hundred times guilty of offence; is it, then, that the father, devoid of any feeling of covetousness, deserts his sons and gives them trouble! We are oppressed by the Dānvans, we who are wholly devoted to your lotus-feet, why are you today showing signs of indifference towards us!

अमभयुवनारज्य तेन भूक्ते नितानंत

मसूडविरपि योग्य ब्राह्मणीयदाति ।

सुरत्कवरपूर्ण यंसेवतउसी दुरात्मा

जलनिधितिधिभृतां गामयी सेवते ताम ॥ 30 ॥

That wicked Dānava is thoroughly enjoying today the Heaven of the Devas, is forcibly taking their share of the oblations of clarified butter in the Yajñas (sacrifices) from the Brāhmanas; is

Book V Chapter VII

enjoying the Pārijāta tree and also the heavenly milching cow, the jewel of the ocean.

किं वा गृणीमः सुराकार्यदम्भृतं

ज्ञानासि देवेश सुरारिचेहितम् ।

ज्ञानेन सर्वे त्यमश्रेषकार्यवित्त-

स्मात्यफो ते प्रणातःस्म पादादोः ॥ 31 ॥

What more shall we describe to you the strange doings of the Asuras; O Lord of the Devas! You are perfectly aware of all that they strive and execute; for, by your knowledge, you know everything of this world; therefore. O Lord! We lie prostrate at your feet.

यशापि कृत्रापि गतान्युग्नासी

नानावरिः: खलु पायमानसः ॥

पीडा कोतेवेच स दृष्ट्येढितैस्त्रातासि

देवेश विधेहि रां विधो ॥ ३२ ॥

That vicious Dānava, of wicked character and full of mischievous actions, gives us troubles in various ways wherever we go; O Lord of the Devas! Thou art our only Protector; therefore, O Lord! Do what is good to us.

नो घेढ़यं दावमहातिन्मीवितान:

क शान्तिकतारियन्तेतेजसम् ।

याम: प्रजेशं गरणं सुरेषं

धातारमाद्यं परिभूच्य के शिवम् ॥ 33 ॥

Thou art the Awarder of the desires of the Devas. Thou art the First Creator of the world, and Preserver; therefore if Thou dost not do us our good, to whom else shall we take refuge, when we are so severely oppressed as if we are burnt in a forest conflagration! Who else is more lustrous, more beneficent and more peace giving Governor?

शूनि सुत्वा सुग: सर्वे प्रणेपुन्ते प्रजापतिन् ।

बद्धांशिलपुटा: सर्वे विश्वणवद्वना भूगण् ॥ 34 ॥

तांस्वा पीक्षितान्दृष्टा तदा लोकपितामहः ।

उवाच रलक्षणया वाचा सुखं संजयनप्रिद्धि ॥ 35 ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! All the Devas, praising Him thus, bowed down to the Lord of creation with folded hands and saluted him, with their faces very heavy, overladen with deep sorrow. The Grand

Sire of all the Lokas, seeing the plight of the Devas, consoled them with sweet words and made them happy.

ब्रह्मोवाच

किं करोमि सुरा: कामं दानवो वरादर्पितः ।

स्वीरयोउसी न पुंवयो विधेयं तक किं पुः न । ३ ६ ॥

Brahmā said: O Suras! What shall I do? The Dānava has become exceedingly haughty on account of his getting boons; he can be killed by females only; He is invulnerable by any male.

प्रजामोउदा सुरा: सर्वे कैलासं पवर्तोनाम् ।

शकूरं पुरतः कृत्वा सर्वक्राविशारादम् ॥ 37 ॥

ततो प्रजाम वैकुंट यत्र देवो जनादनैः ।

मितिलावा देवकायं च विश्रामो विश्रतः ॥ 38 ॥

What remedy is there now? Therefore, O Suras! Let us all go to Kailāśa, the best of all the mountains; thence we will take Śankara, the expert in doing the works of Gods, and go to Vaikuntha, where Visnu, the Deva of the Devas resides. There we all will unite and hold a counsel and decide what is best to do, to serve the purpose of the gods.

इत्युत्वना हंसभारकु बढ़ा कार्यममूच्चये ।

देवांगे पृथतः कृत्वा कैलासाधिषुखो यदी ॥ 39 ॥

ताविच्छोयोरित तस्मा जाला ध्यानेन पचबम् ।

आगच्छनं सुरैः सार्यं निरनतः स्वगृहाद्विः ॥ 40 ॥

Thus making out the programme, Brahmā riding on his Hamsa went to Kailāśa, accompanied by all the Devas. At the same time Śiva came to know out of his introspection about the coming of Brahmā and the other Devas and soon came out of his dwelling abode.

दृष्ट्या परमर्ते तौ तु कृताधिवादनी भृणम् ।

प्रणती च सुरैः सर्वैः सन्तुष्टी संव्यपुन्तः ॥ 4 1 ॥

When they met each other, they saluted each other and felt very glad. The Devas then bowed down to them.

आसानपि पुधवत्या देयेभ्यो मिरित्यपति: ।

उपविष्टि तेयेव निवसादासने स्वके ॥ 42 ॥

Seats were given to the Devas; and when they

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

sat respectively on their Asanas, the Lord of Pārvati also took his own seat.

कृत्वा तु कुशलप्रश्नं बढ़ाणं वृषभध्वजः ।

पग्रष्ठ कारणं देवात-कैलासागमाने विषुः ॥ 43 ॥

Śiva asked the welfare of Brahmā and the Devas and asked the reasons of their coming to Kailāśa.

शिव उवाच

किम्रागमानं ब्रहान्कृतं देवे: स्वासर्वे: ।

भवता च महाभाग बृहि तत्कारणं किल १ ४ ४ १ ॥

O Brahmā! What has caused you to come here along with Indra and the other Devas? O highly fortunate one! Please mention it.

ब्रहोयाच

महिषेण सुरेशान पीडिता: स्वर्निवासिन: ।

प्रभन्ति गिरिरुंगेषु भयजस्ताः स्वासबाः ॥ 4 5 ॥

Brahmā said: O Deva of the Devas! The Dānava Mahisa is oppressing all the Devas in the Heavens; they therefore terrified are wandering hither and thither in the caves and hills with Indra.

यजभुड्महिषो जातस्तशप्रे भूराशयः ।

पीक्षिता लोकपालाश्व त्वायमा शरणं गताः ॥ 46 ॥

Mahīsa and the other Dānavas are now accepting their share of Yajñas; the Lokapālas, being oppressed have come today and are now taking shelter of Thee.

मया ते भवर्न संभो प्रापिताः कार्यंगीरयात् ।

ययुक्ते तिद्धपल्यदा सुरकायं सुरेश्य ।। 47 ।।

वलिये भारोस्ति सर्वंशी देवानां भृतभावान ।

O Sambhu! Considering the situation serious, I have taken them with me here; therefore, O Deva, do that which is reasonable and by which the purpose of the Devas can be carried out. O Bhūta Bīśvana! (The creator of the world) The whole charge and responsibility of all the Devas devolves on Thee.

व्यास उवाच

इति तद्विनं शुचा संकर: प्रहसचिद्य। ॥ 48 ॥

वचनं रलक्षणाय वाचा प्रोवाच पघानं प्रति ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing thus, Śankara

smiled a little and spoke charming words to the Lotus-born in the following manner:

श्रव उवाच

घवतैव कृतं कार्यं वर्यानात्युरा विभो ॥ 49 ॥

अनर्थैं च देवानां किं कत्न्यमतः परम् ।

ईयुगे बल्पाच्यूः सर्वेदेवभयमूदः ॥ 50 ॥

O Bibhu! It is You that gave before this boon to Mahisa and therefore it is you that have wrought this mischief. The Dānava has become so strong a hero that he has caused terror to all the Devas even.

का समर्थ बरा नारी ते रन्तुं मदर्दर्षिनम् ।

न मे भाषां न ते भाषां संग्रामा नन्तुर्महिति ॥ ५१ ॥

Now where can we get such a noble woman who becomes able to kill that Dānava, elated with pride. My wife nor your wife ought to go to battle; even if, the good ladies go, how will they be able to fight?

गत्वैव ते महाभागो युवुघाते कर्व पुनः ।

इन्द्राणी च महाभागा न युद्धकुशलाति हि ॥ 52 ॥

The fortunate wife of Indra, too, is not expert in the art of warfare; where else there is another lady who can kill this demon, blinded with pride.

कान्या हनूं सामर्थान्ति ते पाणे मवदर्पिन् ।

ममेदे मतमदीय गत्या देखें जनादेन्म् ॥ 53 ॥

I, therefore, propose this; let us all go today to Visnu and, praising him with hymns, engage him quickly to this cause of the gods.

सूत्या तं देहवाक्याय प्रेत्याम: सुस्तवम् ।

सोस्तिषुद्यमां श्रंशो विष्यु: सर्वांशसाधने ॥ 54 ॥

यिलाला वासुदेवे वै कत्वेलं कार्यवित्नम् ।

प्रपड़ेच च बुद्ध्वा स संविद्यास्यति साधनम् ॥ 55 ॥

Visnu is foremost amongst the intelligent; therefore it is highly advisable to execute all actions after duly consulting with him. He, by dint of his high intelligence, will find out means and effect our purpose.

व्यास उवाच

इति रूक्षच: शुत्वा ब्ब्याहा: सुरसत्तमा: ।

उपस्थितासते तेथ्युक्त्वा शिवेन सह सत्वा: ॥ 56 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Brahmā and the other

Book V Chapter VIII

Devas heard Rudra and approved heartily and saying, "Be it so" instantly rose up.

स्वकीयविंहनः सर्वे चयुविंथणुर्ग प्रति ।

भुक्ताङ्ठकानन्यूधा कार्यसिद्धिकाराङ्कुमान् । ५७ ।।

At the time, seeing all the auspicious signs concerning the success of the gods, they all became glad; and, riding on their respective vehicles, drove towards the abode of Viṣṇu.

ववुर्विता: शुभा: शान्ता: सुगन्धा: शुभशंसिन: ।

पहिषण्ड शिवा वास्तस्तोचुः पथि सर्वशेनः ॥ 58 ॥

Favourable fragrant winds, pleasant to touch, began to blow gently, birds began to chant hymns

of praise and signs of success were seen all along their way.

निर्मलं चाधावक्रौभो विश्राक्ष त्रिवात्तशा ।

गमने तत्र देवानां सर्वं शुभमिवाभवत् ॥ ५९ ॥

श्रिति शीमदैवीयाणवते महापुराणे पद्धस्कन्चे

स्वामोउध्याः ॥ 7 ॥ 11

The sky was clear and the quarters became free; in short, everything showed favourable all along their way.

Here ends the Seventh Chapter on the going of the Devas to Kailāsa in the Fifth Book of Śrimaddevi-bhāgavatam, the Mahā Purānam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter VIII

CHAPTER VIII

On the Description of the Origin and the Form of the Devi

व्यास उवाच

तरसा ते5थ संग्राम्य वैकुंद विद्युवल्पभम् ।

ददुगु: सर्वशोगसहा विद्यगृद्विवारज्ञिन। 1 1 11

सरोवारीसरित्रिक्षा संयुतं सुखदं शुभम् ।

हंससारसचकाढ़ेः कुजार्द्रिक्ष शिवाजिनम् । 2 11

चंयकागोककक्रामन्यारव्यकुलावरे: 1

मल्लकातिलकाप्रातयुतेः कुरबकादिभि: 11 3 11

Vyāsā said: Soon the Devas reached Vaikuntha, protected by Viṣṇu; they at once began to look at the exquisite indescribable beauty of the place. At intervals they saw nice lovely divine houses, shining and appearing very splendid; pools and lakes were seen in front of them beautified with Kalhāra lotus flowers. They began to see, at other places, rivers flowing; swans, cranes, Cakravākas and other aquatic birds were swimming there easily and warbling lovely sounds.

कोकितारावसादोः शिशडेंनूरतिष्ठतः ।

भ्रमरावरम्येश दिव्येकपवनेयुन्ति ॥ ४ ॥

At other places again, beautiful gardens came to their sight adorned exquisitely by Campaka, Asoka, Mandāra, Bakula, Āmrataka, Tilaka, Kurubaka and Mallikā and various other flower trees, the cuckoos were seen cooing melodiously,

bees humming gently and peacocks dancing beautifully.

सुनन्दान्वनाडीध पार्वैर्भक्तित्वरः ।

संसृवाईद्धूर्त भक्तान्यभव्यतिभि: ॥ 5 ॥

प्रासदै तन्तब्धितः काऊनैश्रिभागिष्ठतः ।

अभितिरहैरिजाद्धः संयुतं शुभमसचके: ॥ 6 ॥

There were Viṣṇu's Pārṣadas or attendants, Sunanda, Nandana, and others; they were so much devoted to their master that there hearts never become attached to any other thing; so they were devotedly singing His praises and chanting His hymns with undivided attention. In the centre was situated the golden palace of Hari, towering to heavens, the rooms and quadrangles were all charming; at places, they were bedecked with gems and jewels and adorned with various paintings. There was the Divine Seat in the centre, composed wholly of gems and jewels; and Viṣṇu was occupying this place.

नायाद्रिद्वैरान्यर्वैर्यन्द्रिसरोगणीः ।

राज्ञत क्रिनरै: गश्छत्ककदोनोहैः ॥ ७ ॥

There were dancing the Apsarās (celestial nymphs) and the Devas, Gandharvas, and Kinnaras were singing in melodious tunes.

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

सुनिभिश्र तथा शान्तैर्वैदपाठकृतादौः ।

सुर्जाद्धः श्रुतिसूर्जेश्ठ मणिडतं सदनं होः ॥ 8 ॥

Those who love the chanting of the Vedas, such calm-tempered Munis were reciting the Vedic Sūktas and thus highly extolled Him.

ते च विद्यगृहं प्राय्य द्वारपाली सृष्णाकृतीं ।

वीधेमोचुर्जनिविजयो हेमयहिष्टयी त्रिचिति ॥ 9 ॥

The two lovely gate-keepers Jaya and Vijaya were waiting at the entrance gate with golden sticks in their hands; the Devas coming nigh the city of Viṣṇu caught sight of them and said:

गतैकी3प्युष्योमीये निवेदयुतं सगतान् ।

द्वारस्यान्वहान्द्वारीनील्याणुदर्सनेलालतान् ॥ 10 ॥

"Any of you may go and inform Viṣṇu that Brahmā, Rudra, and the whole host of gods are waiting at His door to see Him."

व्यास उवाच

विवसन्तुचः शुत्वा गावत्ध विणुपसित्रों ।

सर्वान्समगतान्देवान्पगम्योवाच सत्वर ॥ १ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! Hearing their words, Vijaya went away at once to Viṣṇu; and, saluting Him, informed Him of the arrival of the Devas.

विजय उवाच

देवंदेव महाराज रमाकान सुरारिह्न ।

समगातः सुरा: सर्वेत्रारि निष्ठिति वि विषो ॥ 12 ॥

Vijaya said: O Lord! Thou destroyest the enemies of the gods; hence Thou art the most worshipped of them. O Lord of Rama! The whole hosts of gods have come and are waiting at Thy door.

ब्रह्मा रुद्रलयंद्रिक्ष वर्जण: पावको यमः ।

सुर्बनि ने देवावस्यैरल्यामसरा दर्जानिधिनः ॥ १३ ॥

O Bibhu! Brahmā, Rudra, Indra, Varuna, Fire and Yama and other gods, anxious to see Thee, are all praising Thee by proper hymns.

व्यास उवाच

तच्छुवा खचनं विद्याप्रिवजस्य सपापति: ।

निर्विगाम गृहानुर्ण सुरान्समिधिकोस्वः ॥ १४ ॥ ॥

Vyāsa said: Hearing Vijaya's words, Viṣṇu, the

Lord of Ramā became very anxious and soon went out of his room to see the Devas.

गत्वा बीध्य हर्पिंद्वान्त्रास्थान्युकमक्रिणतान् ।

प्रीतिप्रवाणया वृद्धया प्रीणवामास दुःवितान् ॥ 15 ॥

प्रणेपुले सूरा: सर्वे देवदेवे जनादनम् ।

नुयुद्धुः सुरारिध्ने वारिभर्देविनिश्रितम् ॥ 16 ॥

देवा ऊचुः

देवदेव जगाधाय सृष्टिश्वयन्तकाकर ।

द्वासिस्मो महाराज जाहि नः शरणभगतम् ॥ 17 ॥

Hari came up to them and seeing the Devas waiting at the doors very morose and tired, cheered them up by casting a favourable glance full of affection and love. The gods bowed down and praised hymns to Jagannātha the Deva of the Devas, the enemy of the Daityas and revealed in the Vedas. O Deva of Devas! Thou art the Creator, Preserver, and the Destroyer of the worlds; Thou art the ocean of mercy and the sole refuge of this Universe; O Lord! We have come to Thee as our Great Refuge; therefore dost Thou save us from the present difficulty. Thus praised by the gods,

विष्यान्वाच

विश्नान् निजांना: सर्वें कुर्जानं कवयंतु वः ।

आसमेषु क्रियर्व वै मितितानः समुपानतः ॥ १ १ ॥

Viṣṇu said: O Immortals! Take your respective seats and speak how are you all? Why have you all in a body come here?

चितानुतः कर्व जाता विष्णणा दौन्यानसतः ।

ब्रह्मडेण सहिताः कार्य प्रवृत् सत्वम् ॥ १९ ॥

Why are you so much depressed and worn out with cares? Why do you look so melancholy? Say soon for what purpose you with Brahmā and Rudra have come here.

देवा ऊचुः

महिषेण महाराज पीडिताः पायकर्माण ।

असाध्योनातिदुढेन वरतूदेतने पायिना ॥ 20 ॥

The Devas said: "O Lord! The Asura Mahiṣa is very cruel and wicked; always addicted to vicious acts; now that most sinful Dānava has

Book V Chapter VIII

become very much puffed up with pride and is tormenting us always."

यजभागासी धुंकने बाह्योः प्रतियादितान् ।

अभसा मितिरुर्भु घमन्ति च भयानुतः ॥ 21 ॥

What more shall we say than this, he is appropriating to himself the share of the Yajñas performed by the Brāhmins; we are, therefore, terror-stricken and are wandering in mountains and fastnesses.

वरतूनेन धातुः स दुर्जयो मधुसूदन ।

तमाच्चां राणेंण प्राणा ज्ञाला तत्कार्योपरिब्य ॥ 22 ॥

O Destroyer of Madhu! He has become unconquerable due to his being granted the boon; considering, therefore, the gravity of our situation we have taken refuge unto Thee.

समर्पेसि समुद्रुतै दैत्यमायाविशारत ।

कुक कृष्ण कथोपायां तस्य दानवमदाने ॥ 23 ॥

O Kṛṣṇa! Thou art acquainted with all the tricks and Māyā of the Daityas; therefore Thou art capable to kill them.

धाता तसै वरी दते ह्वायधोत्रिस नरै: कित ।

का कृती लेवेथिति बाला या ह्यन्ता गर्भे एने ॥ 24 ॥

Therefore Thou alone art able to deliver us from the present difficulty; be pleased, therefore; to devise means for that purpose. The Creator Brahmā has granted him this boon that the demon could not be killed by any man; therefore we are asking you where can we get a female who will be able to kill that hypocrite in battle.

उमा मा वा जयी विदा का समर्थन्य धानते ।

महिष्यसातिदुर्ध्य  वरतदानकलादपि ॥ 25 ॥

Mahīsa has turned out very wicked on the strength of that boon; say, therefore, who amongst Umā, Laksmī, Śacī, or Vidyā or any other woman will be able to kill him.

विधिन्य बुद्धा यत्सर्वे मरणमायाय कारणम् ।

कुरु कार्य च देवानां भक्तवलस्त पृधार ॥ 26 ॥

Therefore, O Gracious One to faithful worshippers and attendants! Thou art the Preserver of this world; now devise specially the cause of his death and carry out the purpose of the gods."

व्यास उवाच

श्रुत्वा तद्वचनं विध्यास्नानुवाच हसचित्व ।

युद्धं कृतं पुरास्माथिसतथापि न पृते होसी ॥ 27 ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! Viṣṇu, on hearing their words, spoke smiling “we fought before; but this Asura could not at that time be killed.

अष्ट सर्वसुगणां वे तेजोपी रूपसमयदा ।

उत्पत्रा चेहतुरोहा सा द्यन्ता एगे वन्ता ॥ 28 ॥

Hence if some beautiful female Deity be now created out of the collective energy and form of the Śaktis of each of the Devas, then that Lady would be able easily to destroy that Demon by sheer force.

ह्यारि वरतूनं च मायाशतविशगदम् ।

हन्तु योग्या भवेत्रागी सक्योर्गेनिंर्मिता हि नः ॥ 29 ॥

The Lady Deity then sprung from the collective energy of ours, would at once be able to destroy that Mahisa, elated on his getting the power, though he is skilled in hundreds of Māyās (magics).

प्रार्थिन्तु च तेजौगान्यवीयेरमाकं तथा पुनः ।

उत्पर्शैतेक्षे तेजौगैरेजेजारीर्षभवैशया ॥ ३० ॥

आयुधानि वयं द्वा: सर्वं म्हडुपोगमाः ।

तस्यैं सर्वांणि दिव्यानि विगुतावीति याचि ॥ ३१ ॥

Therefore ask ye now all, with your wives respectively, boons from that portion which resides in you all in the form of Fiery Energy, that the collected energy thus manifested may assume the from of a Lady. We will then offer unto Her, all the Divine weapons, the tridents, etc., that belong to us.

सर्वायुधधरा नारी सर्वतेज:सम्यित्ता ।

हनिष्यति दुरान्यानं ते पार्थ मदार्थविंन्म ।। ३२ ॥

That Deity, then, full of energy and with all the weapons in Her hands would kill that wicked Demon, vicious and swelled with vanity.

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्तविति देवेशे बढ़ाणो बदनाततः ।

स्वयमेवोद्धपी तेजोराशिष्ठातील दुःसहः ॥ 33 ॥

Vyāsa said: On Viṣṇu, the Lord of the Devas, saying thus, came out spontaneously, at once, of the face of Brahmā, the brilliant fiery energy, very difficult to conceive.

त्क्तवर्णं शुभाकां पदारागमिणिपुम् ।

किंघिष्ठतीततवाणेणं मरिफाजलमिफितृदान् ॥ 34 ॥

That energy looked red like gems and pearls, hot, at the same time, a little cool, having a beautiful form, and encircled by a halo of light.

निःसृतं हरिणा दृष्टं हेण च महात्मना ।

विमित्ती तो महाराज कंप्यतुरुककमो ॥ ३५ ॥

O King! The high-souled Hari and Hara, of mighty valor, were astonished to see this Fire, emitted from Brahmā.

शूक्रस्य शरीगत् नि:सुतं महद्धूतम् ।

तीयवर्णिभूतीजं दुर्दर्ग भटानं म्हत् ॥ ३६ ॥

Next came out of the body of Śankara, His fiery spirit, quite in abundance and very wonderful to behold; it was silvery white, terrible, unbearable, and incapable of being seen even with difficulty.

भयंकरं च दैत्यानां दैत्यानां विस्मयप्रदम् ।

घोरकरं निरिग्रष्टं तमोगुणमिवापरम् ॥ 37 ॥

ततें विश्वगुरीतृ नेजोराशिमिवापगम् ।

नीलं सत्त्वगुणोमेत प्रादुरास महाद्वुति ॥ 38 ॥

It extended like a mountain and looked horrible as if the incarnation of the Tamo Guṇa like another Tamo Guṇa (Śiva is the incarnation of Tamo Guṇa that destroys everything). It was very surprising to the Devas and very fearful to the Daityas. Next a dazzliug light of blue colour emanated from the body of Viṣṇu.

ततश्रैस्त्रशीरानु चित्रकरूपं दुरासदम् ।

आविरासीतुसंवृतं तेजः सर्वेगुणात्मकम् ॥ 39 ॥

The light that came out of the body of Indra was hardly bearable, of a beautiful variegated colour, and comprised in itself the three qualities.

कुर्वयमद्धीनां शरोरिथः: सर्वतः ।

निश्छाकाम महतैजो वक्णस्य तथेच च ॥ 40 ॥

Thus masses of lights came out respectively from Kubera, Yama, Fire and Varuna.

अन्येधो देव देवानां शरीरभेधोस्तिषायम् ।

निरितं तन्महतेजो राशिरासीन्होज्जलः ॥ 41 ॥

तं दृष्टवा विमताः सर्वे देवा विद्यापुर्णोगाः ।

तेजोराशि महाद्विद्धे महापचलमिवापगम् ॥ 42 ॥

The other Devas, too, gave their shares of fiery lights, very lustrous and splendid. Then these all united into a great Mass of Fire and Light. Like another Himālayan mountain shone full their lustrous Divine light; Viṣṇu and the other Devas were all extremely surprised to see this.

पश्चतां तत्र देवानां तेतःपुड्वसमूद्वा ।

बभूवातिवारा नारी सुन्दरी विस्मयपदा ॥ 43 ॥

While the Devas were thus looking steadfastly on that Fire, an exquisitely handsome Lady was born out of it, causing excitement and wonder to all.

विरुगना सा महातलबीः सर्वदेवगरीरा ।

अष्टादशुपत्रा रम्या त्रिवर्गा विश्यामौहिने ॥ 44 ॥

This Lady was Mahā Lakṣmī; composed of the three qualities, of the three colours, beautiful, and fascinating to the universe.

छेतानान कृष्णेनत्र सेरत्धारयपल्ला ।

ताप्यपीलातन कान्ता दिव्यमूषणभूषिता ॥ 45 ॥

Her face was white, eyes were black, her lips were red and the palms of her hands were copper-red. She was adorned with divine ornaments.

अष्टादिषुना देती सहसधुजमण्डिता ।

सम्पृतासुरनायास तेजोराशिसमुद्वा ॥ 46 ॥

The Goddess was now manifest with eighteen hands, though She had a thousand hands (in Her unmanifested state). Now She became manifest out of the mass of fire, for the destruction of the Asuras.

जनमेवय उवाच

कृष्ण देव महाभाग सर्वत्र मुनिस्तम ।

विस्तं बृहि तस्यास्वं शरीरस्य समुद्धम् ॥ 47 ॥

Janamejaya said: O Best of the Munis! O Kṛṣṇa! You are highly fortunate and you are all-knowing. Kindly describe, in detail, the birth of Her body.

एकीभृतं च सर्वेयं तेकः किं वा पृथविस्वलम् ।

अज्ञानि चैव तस्यास्तु सर्वतेजोभयानि च ॥ 48 ॥ ॥

O Deva! Please say whether the energies of all the gods united into one or remained separate? Whether Her body and Her limbs were all luminous.

Book V Chapter VIII

भिवभाणविधानेन जातान्यंशानि यानि तु ।

भुखनासक्षिभेदेन सर्वैकभवानि च ॥ 49 ॥

Was it that Her face, nose, eyes, etc., and all other parts of Her body were created out of the different fires respectively or whether was it that those limbs were fashioned when the different fires blended into one huge mass?

खृष्टि तहित्तरं व्यास शरीग्रभुगुद्वम् ।

वधूव यस्य देवस्य तेजसोड़ं यददुन्ति ॥ 50 ॥

आयुधाभरणादीनि दत्तानि वैयंथा यथा ।

सत्वरं श्रौतकामोप्रिय तव्याद्वंजिनितम् ॥ 51 ॥

Describe, in detail, the origin of the body and the several limbs thereof; also inform me the limbs that were produced out of the corresponding Deva's fiery part; as well tell me the several ornaments and several weapons given by the several Devas respectively. I am very desirous to hear all these from your lotus-like mouth.

न हि तृत्याद्यई बहन-सुधायसर्वे पिचन् ।

चरितं च महातल्य्यास्त्वमुखाधोपजिन:सुतन् ॥ 52 ॥

O Brāhmaṇa! Hearing from your lotus-like mouth the life and doings of Mahā Lakṣmī, the sweet juice as they are, I am as yet not satiated (and am desirous to hear more).

सूत उवाच

इति तस्य वचः शुत्वा रात्रः सत्यवतीसुत ।

उवाच मधुरं वावयं प्रीणयाद्वितः भूषितम् ॥ 5 3 ॥

Sūta said: Veda Vyāsa, the son of Satyavati, hearing his words addressed him in the following sweet words:

व्यास उवाच

शृणु राजन्महाभाग विस्तरेण इवीमि ने ।

यथामिति कुरक्षेष्ठ तस्या देहसमुद्वम् ॥ 54 ॥

"O Best of Kurus! Very fortunate you are. I will describe in detail, to the best of my understanding, the origin of Her body.

न ब्रह्मा न हरि: साहित्य मूर्तो न च चायसः ।

याथानाथेन तृप्तं वर्फुभीगः कदाचन ॥ 55 ॥

Even Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Mahēśa and Ir.īra are

never competent enough to describe Her form properly.

कर्ष जानाम्यहं देल्या यदुर्ण यायुर्णं चतः ।

वाचारम्पणामांत ततुव्येति ब्रवीमि यत् ॥ 56 ॥

As I already told you that She sprung at the instant the word was spoken, how then can I ascertain the form or likeness of the Devi.

सा निवा सर्वदैवासते देवाकार्यांसिद्धये ।

नानाक्या तेकरका जायते कार्यांगीतात् ॥ 57 ॥

She is constant, She is always existent; though She is one, yet She assumes different forms for the fulfilment of the Deva's ends, whenever their positions become serious.

यथा नटो रंगगती नानाक्पो भवत्यसौ ।

एकल्पव्यभावोशि लोकैरान्वेतेचे ॥ 58 ॥

तथैषा देवकायांधीम्वापरि स्वलीला ।

क्रोतीत बहुकूपणि निरुणा सुगणानि च ॥ 59 ॥

Though the actor is one, yet for the entertainment of the spectators, he assumes different forms in the stage, so the Nirgunā Devi, though formless, assumes in Her pastime, many different forms of Sāttvik, Rājasik or Tāmasik qualities, to fulfill the Deva's purposes.

कार्यकर्मानुसारेण नामानि प्रवर्तिन हि ।

धात्वर्विणुपुक्तानि गौणानि सुबहृद्वपि ॥ 60 ॥

There are various names given to Her, according as the works done by Her vary immensely in their natures, just as the meanings of one root vary, some being principal and some secondary, according to the meanings and objects they convey.

तदै बुद्धानुसारेण प्रबंधीयि नराधिप ।

यथा तेज:सुद्धुभूत रूपं तत्या मनोहतम् ॥ 61 ॥

O King! I will now describe to you, as far as my knowledge goes; the Excellent Form that came out of that mass of Celestial Light.

शुक्रस्य च यतेजसेन तन्मुखपूजम् ।

शुतिचर्षे शुभाकामजावतं महत्तम् ॥ 62 ॥

Her grand beautiful white lotus-like face was created out of the fiery energy of Śaṅkara.

केशातस्यास्त्वासिन्मधाय्यनेतेज्ञापवम् ।

वक्राग्राशितिरीधों वै भेवरणों मनोहरा: ॥ 63 ॥

Śrimaddevatbhāgavatam

Her glossy black beautiful hairs of the head, overhanging to the knees, were formed out of the light of Yama; these all came to a fine pointed end.

नयन्नितियं तस्या जज़े पाचकतेजसा ।

कृष्णा रन्के तथा श्रेते वर्णत्रयविष्यिष्मि ॥ 64 ॥

Her three eyes came out of the energy of Fire; the pupils of those eyes were of a black colour; the middle parts were of a white colour and the ends were red.

वके सिचो कृष्णवर्णे संन्यायेलेलजा घुबो ।

जाते देवया: सुतेजस्के कामस्य धनुषीव ते ॥ 65 ॥

The two eyebrows of the Devi were black and came out of the spirit of Sandhyā (twilights); they were nicely curved and were looking spirited, like the bow of the Cupid and they were shedding, as it were cooling rays.

वायोधु तेजसा शस्ती श्रवणौं संवभूवतुः ।

नाति दीर्घों नातिहुस्यो दोताविघ मनोभुचः ॥ 66 ॥

From the light of Vāyu (air), Her two ears were created; they were not very long, nor very short, beautiful like the swinging seat (rocking chair) of the God of Love.

तततुष्यमसाकारा नासिका सुमनोहरा ।

संज्ञात सिन्यधरणों वे धनदस्य च नेसना ॥ 67 ॥

Her nose was fashioned out of the fire of Kubera, the Lord of wealth; it looked like the til flower, glassy and exquisitely charming.

दन्ता: शिखारिणः रूक्षणा: कुन्द्यासदूषा समाः ।

संज्ञान सुभा राजन्जागपर्येन तेजता ॥ 68 ॥ ॥

O King! Her pointed rows of glossy and brilliant teeth, looking like gems, came out of the energy of Dakşa; they looked like the Kunda flowers.

अधश्रातिरात्कोअस्या: संज्ञातोउक्तपतेसा ।

उत्तोहस्तथा रच्यः कार्तिकेयस्य तेसना ॥ 69 ॥

Her lower lip was deep red and it came out of the fire of Aruna (the charioteer of the Sun); Her beautiful upper lip came out of the energy of Kārtika.

अष्टादिपुर्जाकारा बाहवो विधानुतेसना ।

वसूनां तैससंगुल्यो रत्कवर्णास्तधारभव् ॥ 70 ॥

Her eighteen hands came out of the Tejas of Viṣṇu and Her red fingers came out of the Tejas of the Vasus.

सीयेन तेससा जातं सतनयोयुंममुत्तमम् ।

रेट्रेशणास्यास्तधा मयं जांत विवतिसंमुन्तम् ॥ ७ ॥

Her breasts came out of the energy of Soma and Her middle (navel) with three folds was created out of the spirit of Indra.

जंघोरू वकणस्याय तेजसा सम्बध्वतुः ।

त्रितंबः स तु संजातो विपुलत्नेसा भवः ॥ ७ २ ॥

Her thighs and legs were from Varuna and Her spacious loins came out from Earth.

एवं नारी शुभाकाता सरूपा सुस्या भृपाम् ।

समुच्या तथा रात्वसंज्ञाराशिमस्पूद्वा ॥ ७ ३ ॥

तां दृष्ट्वा सुवु सर्वांगी सुदर्ती चाक्लोचनाम् ।

मूदं प्रपु: सुगा: सर्वे महिषेण प्रपीडिताः ॥ ७ ४ ॥

O King! Thus from the various Tejas, contributed by the Devas, that Heavenly Lady came out. Her body and the several parts thereof were beautiful; Her form was incomparably graceful and the voice was exquisitely sonorous and lovely. The Devas, oppressed by Mahisāsura, became overpowered with joy seeing this well decorated Devi, having beautiful eyes and teeth, and charming in all respects.

विष्णुस्त्वाह सुरान्सर्वान्पूषणान्यायुधानि च ।

प्रवच्छुं तृष्णायर्य देहा: सर्वांगी साम्यान् ॥ 75 ॥

स्वायुधेभ्यः समुभाघ तेजोयुक्तानि सत्त्या: ।

समपर्यन्तु सर्वेउघ द्वेये नानायुधानि वे ॥ 76 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे पद्रमलक्ये देवय:

स्वरूपोद्वये नागायुभोउद्यायः ।। 8 ।।

Viṣṇu then addressed all the Devas to give all their auspicious ornaments and weapons, He said: "O Devas! Better give, all of you the various arms and weapons, endowed with strength, created out of your own weapons and give them all to day to the Devi.

Here ends the Eighth Chapter of the Fifth Book on the description of the origin and the form of the Devi in Śrimaddevibhāgavatam, the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter IX

CHAPTER IX

On the Worship by the Gods to the Devi

व्यास उवाच

देवा विष्णुवचः श्रृत्वा संर्वे प्रमृदितासन्तव ।

ददुश्ध प्र्यरणान्याणु वस्त्राणि सवायुधानि च ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: On hearing Viṣṇu’s words, the Devas became very glad and presented immediately their own weapons, ornaments and clothing.

श्रीरोद्धशंद्र विद्ये रतेके सूक्षे तधाउजरे ।

निर्मलं च तथा हारं प्रीतस्तद्ये सुमाणिदुक्ति ॥ 2 ॥

The Ksīroda (Milk) Occan presented to Her gladly, the well fitted necklace, clear as crystal, and a pair of divine cloths, of a red colour, never becoming old and very fine.

ददौ चुदामणि दिव्यं सुर्योकौटिममुभम् ।

कृष्ण्डले च तथा शुष्के कटकानि भुजेषु वे ॥ ३ ॥

केनूरात्करणान्दियाज्ञानालयिकाज्ञान।

ददौ तसे विश्वकामां प्रसर्जेत्रियमानासः ॥ 4 ।

Vísvakarmā was very much gratified in his heart and presented a divine jewel to be worn in Her diadem or crest, blazing like hundreds of suns; white earrings, bracelets for Her wrist, bracelets for Her upper arm, and other bracelets decked with various gems and jewels and anklets brilliant like gems, of a clear Sun-like lustre, decked with jewels, and tinkling nicely.

नूपुरी सुस्वरी कान्ती निर्मतीं रत्नभूषिति ।

दूदी सूर्यप्रीकाणी लखा तव्ये सुप्रादयोः ॥ 5 ॥

तथा त्रीयकं रयं दूदी तत्वे महापाँवंः ।

अदूलीयकरत्नारि तेजोबलित च सर्वेशः ॥ 6 ॥

The architect of the gods, the ocean of intellect, Visvakarmā gave Her as offerings beautiful ornaments also for the neck, all very beautiful, as well as for the fingers decked with gems and jewels, all shining splendidly.

अत्नापजूँजां मालां शीघाड्यां धामगुभाम।

तथेव वैजवन्ती च वर्णः संप्रथळ्त । ७ ।।

Varuna gave for Her head a garland of lotuses,

never fading away, of such a sweet fragrance as bees constantly hover round them and the Vajjayanti garland for Her breast.

हिमवानय सन्तुषो रत्नानि विवियानि च ।

ददी च गहानं सिंहं कनकाभं मनोहाम् ॥ 8 ॥

The mountain Himalaya gladly offered Her various gems and a beautiful lion, of a golden colour for Her conveyance.

भूर्णीभूषित तिद्वे: सा राज वरा रुषा ।

सिंहाकडा वरारोहा सर्वेलक्षणयेयुत ॥ 9 ॥

Then that beautiful Lady, having all the auspicious signs, wishing welfare to all, and decorated with the divine ornaments began to look grand and splendid, mounted on Her conveyance, the Lion.

विष्यधुकात्मसुचाधा ददावख्ये रथांगकम् ।

सहचारं सुदीर्णं च देवारिगिरासां हन् ॥ 10 ॥

Visnu then created another thousand spoked discus (Cakram) from His own Cakra, capable to take off the head of any Asura, and offered it to Her.

स्वयिगूलातमयुभाथा शड़़ः: शूलमुत्तमम् ।

ददी देखे सुर्गीर्णां कृतेनं भयनाशनम् ॥ १ १ ॥

Śaṅkara created another excellent Triśūla from his own Trident, terrible and demon-killing, and offered it to the Devi.

वर्णाधा प्रसतात्या ददी शंखं सपुज्जलम् ।

घोथवन्तं स्वशंखानु सम्यताय सुमतलम् ॥ १ २ ॥

Varuna created another bright conch from his own conch and offered it gladly to the Devi.

हुताशनस्तथा शर्फिं शतन्यीं सुमनोजाम् ।

प्रायच्छतु प्रसत्रात्मा तब्ये दैत्यविनाशिनीम् ॥ 13 ॥

Fire offered Her a weapon named Sātaghni which kills violently the demons, as if that is another god of death.

शुध्धि बाणपूर्णं च चापं चाळ्ठतर्शनम् ।

मात्तो दत्तांसत्ये दुराकर्षं खरस्यम् ॥ 14 ॥

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

Maruta (wind), the chief of the gods, offered Her a wonderful bow and an arrow case filled with arrows. The bow can be drawn with great difficulty and emits a very harsh sound.

स्वज्जाद्रप्रमुतयाद द्वाद्विऋ3स्तितारुणम् ।

घंटामैरावततृष्णु सुग्ब्वी चातिसुन्द्वाम् ॥ 15 ॥

Indra created another dreadful thunderbolt from his own thunderbolt and gave it at once to the Devi; as well the beautiful sonorous bell that used to hang from the elephant Airāvata.

ददी दण्डे यमः कामं कातदण्डसमुढ़वम् ।

येनानं सर्वभृतानामकरीकाल अगते ॥ 16 ॥

Yama, the God of Death, created another beautiful staff from his own sceptre which takes away when time comes, the life of all beings.

बह्मा कर्मबंर्तु दिव्यं गंगाविरप्रपूर्तिन् ।

ददावस्य मुवायुक्तो कमण: पाणमेव च ॥ 17 ॥

Brahmā gladly gave Her a divine Kamandalu, filled with the Ganges water; and Varuna offered Her a weapon called Pāśa.

काल: खदुरा तथा चर्म प्रावछन्तु नराधिय ।

परगूं विश्वकर्मा च तीक्षामास्टे द्वावव ॥ 18 ॥

O King! Time gave Her an axe and a shield and Viśvakarmā gave Her a sharp Paraśu.

धनदसु सुगपूर्णे पानघांसु सुवर्णम् ।

पृज्ञं वक्षणश्रादोद्वैये चिदयं मनोहम् ॥ 19 ॥

Kubera, the Lord of wealth, gave her a golden drinking cup, filled with wine; and Varuna offered Her a divine beautiful lotus.

गदां कोमोदर्की नत्हा घंटगणनिरनादीम् ।

अदात्तयै प्रस्नात्या सुरगनुविनाशिणीम् ॥ 20 ॥

अक्षापयेनकल्पाणि तावाउभेघं चे उंगनम् ।

ददौ तत्हा जगनाये निजगभीन्दिकाः ॥ 21 ॥

Vísvakarmā became very glad and gave Her the Kaumodakī gadā, capable to kill the enemy of the gods and whence hundreds of bells are hanging, an impenetrable armour and various other weapons. The Sun gave to the Divine Mother his own rays.

सायुधां भूषणैयुक्तो बृद्धता ते विस्ययं गतः ।

गुरुवुस्तां सुरा देवीं तैनेल्व्यमोहैर्नी विगवाम ॥ 22 ॥

The Devas, seeing Her adorned with ornaments and weapons, began to praise and chant hymns to that most Auspicious Goddess, the Great Enchantress of the three worlds.

देवा कबुः

नमः शिवायै कल्याण्ये शास्तै पृष्टे नामेनः ।

भगवल्ये नमो देद्ये क्रतूणये सत्तं नमः ॥ 23 ॥

The Devas said: “Salutation to Śivā, Salutation to the Most Auspicious; Thou art peace and nourishment; we salute again and again to Thee. Salutation to Thee, the Bhagavati Devi; Thou art the Goddess Rudrāni (the terrible), we always salute again and again to Thee.

कालतार्ये तर्वावार्ये इन्हारणे ते ननोमतः ।

मिल्दीं घुब्दीे तथा वृद्धों वैश्याल्ये ते ननोमः ॥ 24 ॥

Thou art the Kālarātri (the night of destruction at the end of the world); Thou art the Indrānī. Thou art the Mother, we salute again and again to Thee; Thou art the success, Thou art the intelligence, Thou art the growth, Thou art the Vaisnavī; salutation again and again to Thee.

पृथिव्यां या शिवता पृथव्यां न ज्ञाता पृथिवी च या ।

अनं:शिवता यमयिति वन्दे तामीक्षीं पराम् ॥ २५ ॥

Thou art within the earth; yet the earth does not know Thee. Thou art again the inmost of the earth and controlled the things within this earth; we offer our salutations to that Supreme Cause, he may have God.

मायायां या श्विता ज्ञाता मायायां न च तमाजाम् ।

अन्त:स्थिता प्रेयति प्रेयित्री नुम: शिवाय् ॥ 26 ॥

Thou art within this Maya (the unborn) yet the Maya does not know Thee. Thou resident again within the innermost of the Maya and directest that Unborn One, the Maya, we salute again and again to that Supreme Cause, the Great Directress, the Siva (the most auspicious).

कल्याणं कुरु भो मातस्ताहि नः श्रुतापितान् ।

जहि पारं द्वाराि त्वं तेजसा स्थेन मोहितम् ॥ 27 ॥

O Mother! Do what is good to us; we are oppressed by our enemy, dost thou protect us; by

Book V Chapter IX

Thy own power dost Thou overpower and kill that Mahisa.

खलं मायाबिनं घोरं क्रीवध्यं वरद्यपिंन् ।

दुःखदं सर्वदैवानां नानारूपधरं शक्न् ॥ 28 ॥

That demon is vulnerable by woman only, he is deceitful, cunning, dreadful, and swollen with pride on his having got the blessing; he assumes many forms and torments the Devas.

त्वमेका सर्वदैवानां शरणं भक्तवत्सले ।

पीडितान्दानवेनाद्य जाहि दैव नमो3तु ते ॥ 29 ॥

O One, devoted to the Bhaktas! Thou art the only refuge of all the gods; O Thou art the supreme goddess, we are very much harassed and oppressed by the Dānava; therefore dost Thou now protect us; we bow down to Thee.

व्यास उवाच

एवं सृत्ता तदा देवी सुरैः सर्वसुखादया ।

तानुवाच माठदौषी सिमातूर्वे शुभं चवः ॥ 30 ॥

Vyāsa said: When the Devas had praised thus, the Highest Goddess, the Giver of all happiness, then smilingly said in the following auspicious terms:

देव्युवाच

भयं त्वजुत् गीर्वाणा महिषामन्दोचेतसः ।

हनिष्यामि रणोईव वरतून विमोहितम् ॥ 31 ॥

“O Devas! Today in the battle group I will overpower what wicked Mahisa, of cruel disposition and take away his life.”

व्यम उवाच

इत्युक्लवा सा सुरान्देवी जहासातीव सुस्यम् ।

चिचमेतच्च संसारे घममोहतुर्जत जन्त । ३२ । ॥

Vyāsa said: Speaking thus in a melodious voice, the Supreme One smiled and again said: "Thus world is all full of error and delusion.

ब्रहाविष्यामुळेशादा: सैराङ्ठाये सुगतस्तथा ।

कमययुक्ता भयवस्ता वर्तन्ते महिषात्कल ॥ 33 ॥

Really, it is very wonderful that Brahma, Vispu Maheśa, Indra and other gods are all shuddering out of fear from Mahisa Dānava.

अहो दैवबलं घोरं दुर्जंय सुरस्तम्भः ।

कालः कतप्रस्ति दुःखानां सुखानां प्रभुशिः : ॥ 34 ॥

The power of Destiny is exceedingly great and terrible; its influence cannot be overcome even by the best of the Devas.

सृष्टिपालनसंहिते समर्था अपि ते यदा ।

मुह्यिति कलेशसन्तमा महिषेण प्रपीक्षिति: ॥ 35 ॥

O king! The Time is the Lord of happiness and pain; Time is, therefore, the God. The wonder is this that even those who can create, preserve and destroy this world, they are being overpowered and tormented by Mahiṣa.

इति कृत्वा सितं देवी साउटुहासं चकार ह ।

उच्छे: शब्दे महापोरं दानवानां भपप्रदम् ॥ 36 ॥

The Devi, thinking thus, smiled; then laughed and laughed very hoarsely; it seemed that a roar of laughter then arose.

चक्रमे वसुभा तत्र श्रुत्वा तलळ्यद्धानुम् ।

चेलुध शवतो: सर्वे चुक्षोपाधिश्र श्रीवयान् ॥ 37 ॥

And the Dānavas were struck with terror at that very dreadful sound. The earth trembled at that extraordinary sound; the mountains began to move and the vast oceans that remained calm began to be agitated with billows.

मेक्रश्याला शब्देन दिशः सर्वा: प्रपृत्तिनः ।

घयं जमुस्ताद शुवा टानवास्तत्वयं महत ॥ 38 ॥

The uproar filled all the quarters and the mountain Meru trembled. Then the Dānavas, hearing the tumultuous uproar, were all filled with tremendous fear.

जय पाहिति देवात्सापुचः परमहर्षितः ।

महिषोपि स्वयं शूला चुकोय पदार्तिः ॥ ३१ ॥

The Devas became very glad and said thus: "O Devi! Let victory be Yours; save us. The intoxicated Mahisa, too, hearing those words, became very angry.

किमेत्तिति तन्दीत्यायप्रछ स्वनाशिकृतः ।

गच्छन्तु वारिता दूता ज्ञानु शब्दसमुद्वय् ॥ 40 ॥

Mahisa, struck with terror at those words, asked the Daityas “O Messengers! Go and ascertain how has originated this sound.

कृतः केनायमत्युः शब्दः करण्यायकाः ।

देवो वा दानवो वापि यो घबेत्ववनाकाः ॥ 4 1 ॥

Who has made this harsh sound? Bring that devil who has made this hoarse noise, be he a Deva, Dānava, or anyone else unto me, and I will kill that roaring villain, who, it seems, has been puffed with egoism and vanity.

गृहीता तं दुगात्मानं मत्सीपं नर्यत्थत् ॥

ह्निष्यामि दुराचारं गर्जन्तं सम्यदुमंदम् ॥ 42 ॥

The Devas are not making this noise, for they are vanquished and terror-stricken; The Asuras are not doing so, for they are my subjects; then, who is the stupid fellow that has done so?

क्षीणायुयं मन्दमर्ति नवामि यमसादनम् ।

पराजिता: सुरा: कामं न गर्जित भवानुग: ॥ 43 ॥

Surely he is of very little understanding; his days are numbered; and I will carry him to the home of Death.

नासुरा मम वश्यासते कमयेदं मुद्धरेष्टितम् ।

त्विता मामुपायातु जात्वा शब्दस्य कारणम् ॥ 44 ॥

अहं गत्वा हनिययामि तं पायं वित्तवश्रमम् ।

Go you, ascertain the cause of sound and come back to me; then I will go there and destroy that wretch who made this noise to no purpose.

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्तास्तेन ते दुता देर्वी सर्वांकुष्यदीयम् ॥ 4 5 ॥

अष्टादिषुर्जा दिव्यां सर्वांभरणभूरिताम ।

सर्वलक्षणास्यम्बों वरायुध्यरां रुभाम् ॥ 4 6 ॥

द्यर्तीं चरकं हरले पिबन्ती च मुहुभेंम् ।

Vyāsa said: No sooner the messengers heard these words of Mahiṣa, than they at once went to the Devi and saw that Her body and the several parts thereof were all very beautiful; She had eighteen hands, She was decorated completely with various ornaments all over Her body, all the auspicious signs were being seen in Her body and that She was holding excellent divine weapons. That auspicious Goddess beautiful, was holding in Her hands, the cup and drinking wine again and again.

संवैध्य भयभीताते जमुक्तसा: सुर्णिकता: ॥ 47 ॥

सकाशं महिष्यायु तमयु: स्वनकारणम् ।

Beholding Her this form, they were afraid and fled at once to the Mahisa and informed him the cause of that sound.

दूत ऊयु:

देवी दैनेश्व श्रंड दूण्यते कार्विदंगना ॥ 4 8 ॥

सर्वोईभूषण नारी सर्वत्लोषणीघात ।

न मानुषी नासुरी सा दिव्यस्पा मनोहता ॥ 49 ॥

The passengers said: “O Lord! We have seen one grown up woman; whose whereabouts we are quite ignorant. The Devi is decorated with jewels and ornaments all over Her body; She is not human nor Asurit but Her form is extraordinary and beautiful.

सिंहासूकडेपुथाप्रा चाहार्यकार वरा ।

सा नादं कुकते नारी लक्ष्ये मदार्थिति ॥ 50 ॥

सुरापानरता कामं जानीमो न सम्भृतं ।

अन्तरिक्षिता देवासां सुवर्ति मुदाविता: ॥ 51 ॥

That noble Lady is mounted on a lion, holding weapons on all Her eighteen hands and is roaring loudly; She is drinking wine; so it seems that She is puffed up with liquor. It is quite certain that She has no husband.

जयेति पाहि नक्षिति जहि शत्रुमिति प्रभो ।

न जाने का वरोगोहा कव या सा परिश्रहः ॥ 52 ॥

किमर्थमाता चात्र किं विधकीपिति सुन्दरी ।

The Devas are gladly chanting praises from the celestial space that Let Victory be to Her side and that She save the Devas, O Lord! We don't know at all who is that handsome woman? or whose wife is she; why has she come there? and what is Her motive?

उलुं नैव समर्षा: समस्ततेज: परिधर्षिति: ॥ 53 ॥

भूगारवीतासाभिका रौत्रादुतरासाधिता ।

Sentiments of love, heroism, laughter, terror and wonder are all fully shining in Her; therefore we are very much overpowered by the halo emitted from Her; and we could not even see Her well.

Note: Rasas means sentiments. The rasas are usually eight. Śrīngāra, Hāṣya, Karunā, Raudra, Vira,

Book V Chapter IX

Bhayānakab, Bibhatsādbhū tasangān, Cetyaṣṭau, Natyan, Rasab smṛtab but sometimes Śāntarasa, is added thus making the total number nine; sometimes a tenth, Vatsalyarasa is also added.

दृष्टिवैंविधो नारीमममाथ्य समागतः ॥ 5 4 ॥

वयं त्वदान्ना राजन्कि कर्तव्यमतः परम् ।

O King! In compliance with your order, we have come back to you no sooner we had seen the Lady, without even addressing Her in any way. Now order us what we are to do.

महिष उवाच

गच्छ चीर मावाहिष्टी मन्न्रश्रेशु कलावित्वः ॥ 55 ॥

सामादिसभगवैत्सवं समानव शुभाननाम् ।

नवाति यदि या नारी तिथि: सामादिपिबित्व ॥ 56 ॥

अहत्वा तां वशरोहां त्वामानय मपातितकम् ।

कर्मणि यदुगहिंगी तां मारलभुवं मुदा ॥ 57 ॥

Mahisā said: “O Best of ministers! O Hero! Under my command, go there with all the forces and use the means, conciliation, etc., and bring that woman, having a beautiful face (like the Moon), to me. If that Lady do not come even when the three policies, Sāma (conciliation), Dāna (making gifts), and Bheda (sowing dissensions in an enemy’s party and thus winning him over to one’s side, one of the four Upāyas or means of success against an enemy) are adopted by you, then apply the last resort Dāna, (or war) in such a way that Her life be not destroyed and bring that beautiful woman to me. I will gladly make Her, of black curling hairs, my queen-consort.

प्रीतिपुत्ता समायाति यदा सा मुगलोचना ।

रसभंगो यथा न स्थताया कुक मेमोपैतम् ॥ 58 ॥

श्रवणान्मोहितोरम्यद्य तत्या कल्पस्य सम्पत् ।

In case that deer-eyed one comes gladly, then do my desires without causing any unpleasant feeling; (a cessation of sentiment). I am enchanted on hearing about Her beauties and wealth.

व्यास उवाच

महिषय स्व च: शुत्वा पेगलं मन्थिसतम: ॥ 59 ॥

जगाम तरसा कामं गजाश्रधस्येतुः ।

Vyāsa said: The prime minister, on hearing the words of Mahisa, took with him elephants, horses, and chariots and hurriedly went to the desired place.

गत्वा दुर्तर्ग स्थत्वा तामुवाच मनस्विनीम् ॥ 60 ॥

विवयावन्तः रलक्षणं मन्त्री मधुरया गिरा ।

On coming near to the Devi, the minister began to address Her in sweet words from a sufficient distance in a very humble and courteous way.

प्रधान उवाच

कांसि तवं मधुरतावे किमजागमं कृतम् ॥ 6 1 ॥

पृष्ठति तां महाभागे मन्युवेन मम प्रधुः ।

स ज्ञाते सर्वदैवानामवध्यन्तु नौः किल ॥ 6 2 ॥

O Sweet speaking! Who art Thou? What has caused Thee to come here? O Highly fortunate! My master has asked through me these questions. My master cannot be killed by all the Devas and men; he has conquered all the Lokas (worlds).

बहाणो बरादोने गर्वितशुकालोचने ।

दैत्येश्वीरोंजी बलवात्कामरूपधर: सदा ॥ 63 ॥

श्रुत्वा तांं समुपायानांं चाक्चेयं मनोहमाः।

प्रधुमिच्छति राजा मे महीचो नाम पार्थिवः ॥ 64 ॥

मानुर्ष रूपभादाय त्वत्सीपं समेध्यति ।

O Beautiful-eyed! On account of getting his boon from Brahmā, the Lord of the Daityas has become very powerful; and consequently being very proud, assumes different forms at will. He, our King-Emperor Mahisa, the lord of the earth hearing about Thy beauty and dress, has expressed a desire to see Thee. O Beautiful one! Whether he will appear before Thee in a human form?

यथा कष्टेत चार्विङ्ग तथा नयामहे वयम् ॥ 65 ॥

तहिंहि मृगशगवांसि समीपं तस्य धीमतः ।

He will do whatever Thou likest. O Deer-eyed One! Be pleased now to go to that intelligent King. In case Thou dost not go, we will bring the King, Thy devotee, to Thee.

नो चैहिद्वानयद्येने राजानं भफित्तलयम् ॥ 66 ॥

तथा कशोम विशेष यथा ने मनसोयितम् ।

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

<table><tr><td>वशणोउसी ततात्यर्थ कृपसंश्वरणान्ति १ 6 7 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>क्रमोफे वदागु त्वं संविधेयं मया तथा ॥ 6 8 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>इति श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणो वैवीमाहात्ये</td></tr><tr><td>नवमोउध्याः 11 9 11</td></tr></table>

O Lord of the Devas! Our King has heard of Thy beauty and grandeur and has become very much submissive to Thee. We will therefore do exactly what Thou desirest. Therefore, O Thou

having thighs thick and round like those of a young of an elephant! Be pleased to express what Thou likest and we will do quickly as Thou desirest.

Here ends the Ninth Chapter of the Fifth Book on the worship offered by the gods to the Devi and the weapons offered by them in the Mahā Purānam,

Śrimaddevibhayatarat of Vas, 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

[Unreadable]

Śrimaddevībhāgavatam

CHAPTER X

On the Messenger's News to Mahiṣa

व्यास उवाच

इति तस्य वचः श्रुत्वा ग्रहस्य प्रमदोत्तमा ।

तमुवाच महाताजे मेघामार्थीर्या गिरा ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: The Māyā, that Excellent Lady, hearing thus the words of the prime minister of Mahisa, laughed and spoke with a voice, deep like that of a cloud, thus:

देव्यवाच

मत्थवर्षे सुराणां वै जननीं विविड्मां मां किल ।

महालक्ष्मीमिति ख्यातां सर्वैत्यनिपृष्टिनीम् ॥ २ ॥

“O Minister-in-chief! Know Me as the Mother of the gods; my name is Mahā Laksmī. It is I that destroy all the Daityas.

प्राधितानई सुरैः सर्वेर्मधिस्य वधाय च ।

पीक्षितैर्वर्णनवेत्रेण यज्भागवहिष्ठकृतेः ॥ ३ ॥

I am requested by all the Devas to kill the Dānava Mahisa; they have been oppressed and deprived of their share of Yajña offerings.

तस्मादितागतास्म्यघ तद्धार्थ कृतोष्णा ।

एकाकिकी न तै-येनो वंसुता मन्सिसमा ॥ 4 ॥

यच्चवार्डं साम्यवृं कृत्वा स्वागतमादात् ।

उक्ता मधुरया वाचा तेन तुहासमि तेनच ॥ 5 ॥

Therefore I have come here today alone, without any army, to take away his life. O Good One! I am pleased with your sweet words of welcome, in showing me marks of respect.

नोदेब्द्विम मृथा त्वां वे कालात्पिनसमया किल ।

कस्य प्रीतिकरं न स्यानाम्युवैवचनं खलु ॥ 6 ॥

Had you not behaved thus, I would have certainly burnt you to ashes by my fiery sight, which is the universal conflagration at the break up of the world. O Minister! Who is there that gets not pleased with sweet words!

गछ्त तं महिषं पारं वद मढ़चानादिदम् ।

गछ्त पातालमयुना जीवितेछा यदास्ति ते ॥ 7 ॥

Go you to Mahisa and speak to him the following words of mine: "O Villain! Go down to Pātāla (the nether regions) at once if you have any desire to live.

नोचेकृतागसं उदुं हनिष्यामि रणाङ्गो ।

मद्राणधृष्णपोहरूर्न नगतार्सि यमसादनम् ॥ 8 ॥

Otherwise, I will slay you, the wicked one, in the battle-field; you will have to go to the house of Death, pierced by my mass of arrows.

दयालुत्वं ममेदं त्वं विदित्वा गछ्छ सत्यम् ।

हते त्वयि सुरा मुह स्वर्गी प्राप्यक्ति सत्यम् ॥ 9 ॥

तस्माद्छस्य त्वलतेको मेढ़िणी च समागम् ।

पातालं तरसा मन्द यावद्धाना ने भेउपन्त् ॥ 10 ॥

O Stupid One! Know that this is merely kindness shown unto you, that I have told you to go soon to Pātāla and that the Devas get possession of their Heaven, with no delay, “O One of weak intellect! Therefore dost Thou leave possession of this sea girl earth and go alone without any delay to Pātāla, before my arrows are shot at you.

युद्धेच्छा चेन्मनसि ते नहोहि चलितोसुर।

वौरैर्हबालै: सर्वैन्यायी यमसादनम् ॥ १ १ ॥

Book V Chapter X

O Asura! Or if you desire to fight, then come at once with your powerful warriors; I will destroy all of them.

युगे युगे महामूद हतास्त्वसूशा: किल।

असंख्यातास्तथा त्वाँ वे हनिव्यायि पराणोंने ॥ 12 ॥

O One of dull intellect! I will kill you in battle, just as I killed before in yugas after yugas countless Asuras like you.

साक्ष्यं कुक श्रशाणं धारणे तु श्रभोट्यथा ।

तद्धुद्वस्य मया सार्थं समरे स्मरीक्ति: ॥ 13 ॥

O Passionate creature! Better shew that your efforts in holding weapons have been crowned with success by your being engaged in battle against Me; otherwise they will all be useless.

मा गर्व कुक दुहामन् यन्येजित ब्रहणो वरः ।

सीकाय्यले तथा मूढ़ पीक्षिता: सुरसामना: ॥ 14 ॥

O Stupid! You thought that you would be vulnerable alone to women hence you oppressed the Devas entitled to worship; O wicked one! No longer show your pride on the strength of your getting the boon from Brahmā, that you would be vulnerable only to the females.

कर्तेब्ये चर्चनं धातुस्तेनाहं त्वामुपागता ।

श्रीरूपमतुलं कृत्वा सत्यं हन्तुं कृतगामस् ॥ 15 ॥

यथेच्छं गच्छ वा मुंद पातालं पत्रमावृतम् ।

हिला भूसुसद्धाद्य जीवितेच्छा यदास्ति ते ॥ 16 ॥

Thinking it advisable to observe the words of the Creator, I have assumed this incomparable Eternal Female appearance and I have come here to slay you, O wicked one! O stupid one! If you have any desire for your life, then quit this Heaven and go to Pātāla, infested with snakes, or anywhere else you like.

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्तः स तातो देल्य मन्निश्रेष्ठो वर्तानितः ।

प्रत्युवाच निशण्यासी वचनं हेतुर्गर्भितम् ॥ 17 ॥

Vyāsa said: Hearing these words of the Devi, that minister, surrounded by forces replied in reasonable words thus:

देवि क्षीसरूर्ण वाक्यं बूँपे त्वं मदगार्विता ।

कवासी वक्त त्वं कंये युद्रमर्शेण्यविद्धि कित १ १ १ ॥

एकाकिनी पुनर्वालिंग प्रायध्यवीवन मुचु: ।

महिषोस्ती महाकायो दुर्विभाव्यं हिं संगरम् ॥ १९ ॥

"O Devi! You are speaking in words befitting a woman and puffed up with pride. You are a woman; the lord of the Daityas is a hero; how can a battle be engaged between you two. It seems to me impossible. Your body is delicate, a girl in full youth; especially you are alone and Mahisa is of huge body and powerful; so the fight comes next to impossibility.

सैन्यं बहुविधं तस्य हस्तयश्रयसंकुलम् ।

पदातिणगर्भविद्धं नाना उद्युविराजजन् ॥ 20 ॥

कः. अम करिराजस्य मालतीपुथमदर्दे ।

मारणे तव वामोक महिषस्य तथा रणे ॥ 21 ॥

He has elephants, horses, chariots, infantry, etc., and countless soldiers all armed with weapons. Therefore, O Beautiful One! He will find no difficulty in killing you in battle as an elephant finds no difficulty in treading over the Mālati flowers.

यदि त्वा परभं वाक्यं ब्वीमि यवल्यमादून ।

शूगारे तिड़कूदे हि रसभूज्ञाद्धिभ्यमूँ ॥ 22 ॥

Rather, if I utter anything harsh to you, that would go against the sentiment of love with you; therefore I cannot speak rudely to you out of my fear not to interrupt the above feeling.

राजप्रेमाके सुरपिटुर्षतैत त्वयि भक्तिमान् ।

साममेव मया वाच्छं दानयुक्ते त्वा वचः ॥ 23 ॥

True, that our king is enemy of the gods; but he has become extremely devoted to you. Therefore it is wise to speak words full of conciliation or generosity.

नोचेद्वन्दमहादेव खाणेन त्वां मुचावदाम् ।

सिध्यास्थिमानचतुरां रूपदयीवनगर्वितम् ॥ 24 ॥

Were it otherwise. I would have shot arrows at you and would have killed you in as much as you have thus boasted in vain and spoken so dire a falsehood, resting merely on the strength of your youthful pride and cleverness.

स्वामी मे मोहितः श्रुत्वा रूपं ते धुवनातिभम् ।

तनिग्रामर्थि प्रियं कामां चकच्छ्यं त्वयि यन्यम् ॥ 2 5 ॥

राज्यं तत धरने वर्द सदासमे महिषः क्रिकल ।

कुक भावं विशालात्रिक्य त्वक्ना रोषं भूतिप्रदम् ॥ 2 6 ॥

My master has become fascinated on hearing your extraordinary beauty hardly to be seen in this world; it therefore behoves me to speak sweet words to you for the sake of pleasing my master. O Large-eyed! This kingdom and the wealth thereof are all yours; in fact, Mahisa will be your obedient servant; therefore, better forsake your anger, leading to your death; and cultivate friendship with him.

पतापि पाद्योलोर्डं चक्तिभावेन भामिनि ।

पुत्गाती महागतो भव शीघं शुचित्मिते ॥ 27 ॥

O Sweet Smiling One! I am falling at your feet; you better go to him and become at once his quenconsort.

त्रैलोक्यविघर्व सर्वे प्राय्यसि त्वमनाविलम् ।

सुखं संसारजं सर्वे महिष्यय परिश्रहान् ॥ 28 ॥

O Handsome Woman! No sooner you become the queen of Mahisa than you will get at once all the pure wealth of the three worlds and the unbounding happiness of this world.

देव्यवाच

शृणु मन्न्यव्ययामि वाक्यानां सारमुत्तमम् ।

शाखदुईने माणीं चातुर्मैननुंचित्य च ॥ 29 ॥

The Devi said: “Minister! I now speak what is pregnant with goodness and wisdom to you, according to the rules of the Śāstras, keeping in view also the cleverness that you have shown in using your words.

महिष्य प्रधानस्वं मया ज्ञातं त्रिया किल ।

पगुबुद्धिर्बभावो.सि ववनातल संग्रतम् ॥ 30 ॥

मनिजानस्वानुशा यस्य स कर्वं बुद्धिमाभ्यन्ते ।

Now I come to understand from your talk, that you are the chief secretary of Mahisa; and therefore your nature and intelligence are like those of a beast. And how can he be intelligent, whose ministership is occupied by a man of your nature!

उभयो: सरुगो योगः कृतोरयं विधिना किल ॥ 31 ॥

यदुक्ते कृसीव्यावासिर्स निद्वाराय मूद किम ।

पुभात्राई तत्सवाघावासं भयं क्रीवेधधारिणी ॥ 32 ॥

Nature has ordained connection between two persons of like nature. O Stupid One! Did you think a little beforehand the meaning of your words when you told me of my feminine nature? Though I am not apparently a man, yet my nature is that of the Highest Puruṣa (Man); I shew myself simply in a feminine form.

याचितं मरणं पूर्व तिच्या तत्प्रभृणा यथा ।

तस्मामन्ये3तिमुखों3सौ न वीरासितमः ॥ 33 ॥

Your master asked before from Brahmā that he would prefer death, if possible, at the hands of a woman; therefore, I consider him quite illiterate and ignorant of the sentiment, worthy of a hero.

कामिन्या मरणं कर्तीबारितरं शूरदुःखदम् ।

प्रार्थितं प्रभुणा तेन महिषेणातम्बुद्धिना ॥ 34 ॥

Because to die at the hands of a woman is very painful to one who is a hero; and this is gladly welcome to one who is a hermaphrodite.

तस्माल्डीक्रपादाधाय कार्यं कर्तुयुपाता ।

क्रवं विषयेच त्वद्वावैधंमीशाखविरोधकः ॥ 35 ॥

Now see that your master Mahisa has shown his intelligence, when he courted his death from the hands of a woman. For that very reason, I have come here in the shape of a woman to effect my purpose; why shall I fear, then, to hear your words, contradictory to those of the Śāstras.

विरपरितं यदा दैवं तृणं बजुसमं भवेत् ।

विधिधोत्पुरुः कामं कृलिंगं तृलवत्ता ॥ 36 ॥

When Fate goes against any one, a grass comes like a thunderbolt; and when fate goes in favour of anyone, a thunderbolt becomes a soft as a bundle of cotton.

कि सै-योगयुध: कि वा प्रपड़ेरुंगसेवनै: ।

मरणं सांगृतं यस्य तस्य सै-यैसृतु किं फलम् ॥ 37 ॥

What does it avail even when one possesses an extensive army or various weapons in abundance, taking shelter in a wide extending fort? What will

Book V Chapter X

his soldiers do to him, whose death has come close at hand?

यतउयं देहस्पबन्धो जीवस्य कालयोगतः ।

तदेव लिखितं सर्वे सुखं कुःखं तथा मृति: ॥ 38 ॥

Whenever, in due time, the connection of the Jiva (the human soul) with this body is brought about, then his pleasure, pains and death are written.

यस्य येन प्रक्रोगे मरणं दैविनिर्मितम् ।

त्यव तेनैव जातेन नान्यक्षेति विनिश्रय: ॥ 39 ॥

Know this as certain, very certain, that death will come to him in the manner as written by the hands of Fate; it will never be otherwise.

बहादीनां यथा काले नाशोत्पनी विनिर्मितं ।

तबेव भवतः कामं किमन्येषां विचार्यो ॥ 40 ॥

As the birth and death of Brahmā and other gods are ordained, your death had been similarly ordained; no, there is no need of taking the example further than this.

ये मृत्युपिंगपतैंशं वरतानेन दर्पिताः ।

मारियामो न मयवंते ते मृहा मन्द्वेतेसः ॥ 4 1 ॥

Those who are tied up by the hands of death are surely fools and of extremely blunt intellect, if they think simply on the strength of their getting some booms “that they would never die.”

तस्मादछ्न नृप बृहि वचनं मम सत्मम् ।

यदानापयते भूपसत्कर्तैलं त्वया किल ॥ 4 2 ॥

Therefore go quickly to your king and speak to him what I have said; you will then surely obey what he commands you to do.

मतया स्वर्गमाप्नोतु देवा: सन्तु हक्षियुंः ।

यूयं प्रयात पातालं यदि जीवितुमिच्छथ ॥ 4 3 ॥

If he wants his life, he, with his retinue, would at once go down to Pātāla; let Indra and the other Devas get possession of the Heavens and their share of Yajñas.

अन्यथा चेभातिर्वेर महिषयरु तुरात्मनः ।

त्वुध्यस्व मवा सार्थ मरणाय कृतात्रः ॥ 44 ॥

If he holds a contrary opinion, let him be eager

to go to the house of Death and come and fight with Me.

मन्यसे संगरे भग्ना देहा विद्युपुतेगमाः ।

दैर्व हि कारणं तत्र वर्दानं प्रजानेः ॥ ४ ५ ॥

If he thinks that Vishnu and the other Devas have fled from the battle-fields, he has nothing to boast of; for he has not shown his manliness at all even then; for his victory is solely due to his having got the boon from Brahmā.

व्यास उवाच

इति देया वच: शूला चिन्तयामास दानव: ।

किं कतिथ्या मया युद्धे नान्तवं वा नृपं प्रति ॥ 46 ॥

Vyāsa said: Hearing these words of the Devi, the Dānava began to think “whether I ought to fight or to go to Mahisa?”

विद्यार्थीमहात्रानो राजा कामातुर्ण वे ।

तत्कर्वे विरसं कृत्वा गत्क्छेन नृपसक्तिधी ॥ 47 ॥

The King has become very enamoured and has sent me hither to negotiate for marriage; how then will I be able to go to him if I make this affair unpleasant and interrupted in the middle in its course of harmony."

इयं बुद्धिः सभीदीना यद्धजामि कलि विना ।

यथाउजातं तथा शीर्भ ग्रांत सेवेदयाम्यदूम् ॥ 48 ॥

Now it is wise for me to go to the King without fighting; let me then go as early as possible in this way and inform him about this whole affair.

स प्रभाणं पुनः कार्यं गताम मितमांतं वरः ।

क्रतिथित विचार्यैव सचिवैर्निपृष्णे: सह ॥ 49 ॥

The King is exceptionally intelligent and experienced; he will consult with his other experienced ministers and do what is best.

सहसा न मया युद्रं कतल्ममनया सह ।

जये पराजये वाथि प्रप्तोष्रियं भवेत् ॥ 50 ॥

Therefore I ought not to fight here rashly; for victory or defeat would alike be distasteful to my monarch.

यदि मा सुंदरी हन्यादहं या हन्म तां पुनः ।

येन केनायण्यायेन स कुवोर्याधिवः किल ॥ 51 ॥

Šrimaddevīthāgavatam

Whether this Lady kills me, or I kill this Lady, the king will be angry in either case.

तन्यात्रवैर गत्वार्ड बोधीय्यामि तं पुम् ।

यवाउढ़ाथिहितं देय्या यवास्त्रिच करोतु सः ॥ 52 ॥

I will therefore go now to the king and tell him what the Devi has said; he will do whatever he likes.

व्यास उवाच

इति संचित्य मेधारी जगाम नृपसिद्धधि ।

प्रणाय तमुवाचैदं कृतांजलिसायाकः ॥ 53 ॥

Vyāsa said: Thus that intelligent son of the minister argued and went to the king. Then, bowing down before him, he began to say thus:

मन्युवाच

राजन्देवी वरागोहा सिंहस्योपरि संस्थिता ।

अष्टाविशुभ्जा नया वरायुधधरा पत्ता ॥ 54 ॥

O King! That excellent woman, fascinating to the world, the beautiful Devi is sitting on a lion with weapons in all her eighteen hands. O King! I told her:

सा मयोत्ता महाराज महिंषं भज भाविता ।

महिषी भव राजसर्व तैगेलकायिपतेः पिया ॥ 55 ॥

“O Beautiful Lady! Be attached to Mahisāsura; you will become, then, the queen-consort of the king, the lord of the three worlds.

पुताजी तर्वेवास्य भविता नात्र संशयः ।

स तत्ज्ञाकरो जातो क्यवर्ती भविषयिति ॥ 56 ॥

You will certainly then be his queen-consort; he will pass his life, ever obedient to you like an obedient servant.

त्रैलोक्यविषयं भुक्त्वा चिरकालं वरानने ।

महिंशे परित्यासाठ्य योचितां सुभगम भव ॥ 57 ॥

"O Beautiful One! If you choose to make Mahisa your husband, you will become fortunate amongst women and will enjoy ever all the wealth of the three worlds."

इति महदानं भूत्वा सा स्यावोदगोर्भिता ।

मातुबच चिशालाड़ी सितमुर्बमिति वचः ॥ 58 ॥

Hearing my these words, that large-eyed

woman, puffed up with egoism, laughed a little and said thus:

महिषीर्षभिसम्पूतं पशुनामधरं किल ।

खलि दास्याम्बहं देखे सुगणां हितकाघमया ॥ 59 ॥

"Your king is born of a buffalo and is the worst of brutes; I will sacrifice him before the Devi for the benefit of the gods. Is there any woman in this world so stupid as to select Mahisa as her husband?

का मुका कामिनी लोके महिंवे वे पतिं भबेत् ।

मातृषी मन्त्वदुके किं पयुभावं भवेनिद्ध ॥ 60 ॥

महिषी महिंदा नावं सर्शगा श्रगसंयुतम् ।

कुकते कन्दमाना वे नाहं तत्सदृषी शका ॥ 61 ॥

O You stupid! Can a woman like me ever indulge in bestial sentiments! A female buffalo has got horns; she, being excited with passion, may select your Mahiṣa with horns as her husband and come to him bellowing. I am not stupid nor like her so as to make him my husband.

कतिच्छेड़े मुधे युद्धे हिचेल वांं सुरापिष्यम् ।

गछ्ध वा दृष्ट पातालं जीवितेच्छा यदित्ते ते ॥ 62 ॥

O Villain! I will fight and destroy the enemies of the gods in the battle-field. Or if he desires to live, let him flee to Pātāla.

परन्तु तृ तथा वाक्यमित्युक्तं नृय मतया ।

तच्छूलाड़ं समावातः प्रविष्टित्य पुनः पुनः ॥ 63 ॥

O King! Hearing those rough words uttered by Her in a moment of madness, I have come to you, thinking also how to redress this wrong.

सभंगं विविच्वैव न युद्धे तु मया कृतम् ।

आज्ञां तिवा तावाल्यन्ते ककं कुर्गा युंचोघामम् ॥ 64 ॥

O King! Only I feared not to interrupt in your love sentiment; and therefore I did not fight with Her; especially, without Your command, how can I engage myself in useless excitement?

साउसीच च कलोन्मत्ता वर्तते भूप भामिनि ।

शवितळं न ज्ञानाभि किं वा भावि भविष्यति ॥ 65 ॥

O Lord of the Earth! That handsome woman rests maddened on Her own strength; I do not know what is the womb of future or whatever is destined to happen, will surely come to pass. You

Book V Chapter XI

are the sole master in this matter; I will do whatever you order me.

கார்பீரியைல் प्रमाणं नो मन्योरितील वुरासदः ।

युद्धं पलायनं श्रेयो न जानेउहं चिन्धिश्यम् ॥ 6 6 ॥

इति शीमदैर्षीभागवते महापुराणे पड़सदकन्थे

दशगोर्धायाः 11 10 11

The matter is very difficult to be reflected upon; whether it is better to fight or it is better to fly away, I cannot say definitely.

Here ends the Tenth Chapter of the Fifth Book on the messenger's news to Mahisa, in Śrimaddevī-bhāgavatam, the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter XI

मामामान रसा यावा.

CHAPTER XI

On Tämräs Fight with the Devi

ཡབས བབས

སྒྱི་ཆེ་སྟན་ཆེ་སྟན་ཆེ་སྟན་ཆེ་སྟན་ཆེ་སྟན་ཅེ་སྟན་ཆེ་སྟན་ཅེ་སྟན་ཆེ་སྟན་ཅེ་བྱིན་ཆེ་སྟན་ཅེ་བྱིན་ཆེ་སྟན་ཅེ་བྱིག་ཅེ་བྱིག་ཅེ་བྱིག་ཅེ་བྱིག་ཅེ་བྱོག་ཅེ་བྱིག་ཅེ་བྱིག་ཅེ་བྱིག་ཅེ་བྲུག་ཅེ་བྲུག་ཅེ་བྲུག་ཅེ་བྲུག་ཅོ་བྲུག་ཅོ་བྲུག་ཅོ་བྲུག་ཅོ་བྲྲུག་ཅོ་བྲུག་ཅོ་བྲུག་ཅོ་བྲུག།

diseases, so true words, though appearing unpleasant, lead to beneficial results. Those that are simply pleasant, are generally injurious as to their effects.

सत्यस्य श्रोता मन्ता च तुरंभ पृथिवीयते ।

वक्ताउपि तुरंभ: कामं बह्वश्रतुभाषका: 11 6 11

O Lord of the Earth! The bearers and approvers of truth both are very rare; truth speakers also are very difficult to be seen; laudatory sycophancy is found in a great measure in this world.

कथं बुभोउत्र नृपते विवारे गहने तिहह ।

गुर्णे बाउपयुर्णे बार्थि को बेथिं घुवनजये ॥ 7 ॥

O King! Nobody in the three worlds knows what will be good or what will lead to inauspicious results? How can we then definitely pronounce our judgment in this difficult matter?

राजोवाच

त्यव्यमान्नुसारेण बुवंलब्धा पुथक्यथक् ।

येवां हि याङ्गो यावस्तच्छूता चिन्तयायमूम् ॥ 8 ॥

The King said: Let each of you say separately, according to his own intellect, what is his opinion; I will hear them all consider for myself.

बहूतां मतमात्राय विवार्यं च पुनः पुनः ।

यच्छेयस्तद्धि कर्तव्यं कार्यविचक्षणैः ॥ 9 ॥

Clever persons should hear the opinions of several persons, then judge for himself what is the best and then adopt that as what is to be done.

व्यास उवाच

तर्यैवं वच्नं शुत्वा विक्सप्यो महावलः ।

उवाच तरसा वाक्यं रंजवम्पृथिवीपतिम् ॥ 10 ॥

Vyāsa said: Hearing his words, the powerful Virūpāka same out foremost of all and began to say pleasant words to the King.

विकल्पा उवाच

राज्ञरी वराकीय सा बृते मदार्विता ।

विभीषकामाजिरिंदं जातल्यं वचनं तथा ॥ १ ॥

Virūpāka said: O King! Please take for certain, what has been spoken by that ordinary woman, swelled with vanity, as words simply to scare you.

को विभोत सिक्यो वास्यैटुकेनै: रणतुर्दैः ।

अन्ततं साहरं चेति जानराशिविधेत्ति ॥ 12 ॥

The efforts and courage of a woman are known to all; who will be afraid therefore, to hear abusive language from a woman, praising her ownself in matters of warfare?

त्रिला विभुवनं राजत्रढा कान्तापघेन वै ।

दीनतेउदयशे नूनं वीरस्य घुवने भवेत् ॥ 13 ॥

O King! You have conquered the three worlds by your own heroic valour; now if you acknowledge your inferiority, out of fear to a woman, you would be subject to very much disgrace in this world.

तस्माधायमेकाकी युद्वाय चपिड़ां प्रति ।

हनिये तो महाराज निर्भयी घव साम्यतम् ॥ 14 ॥

Therefore, O King! I will go alone to fight with Candikā and I will kill Her. You can stay here now without any fear.

सेनावृतोर्ध नाला तां शलालीस्विविधः किल ।

निपुर्दयामि दुर्मर्गे चाधिकांப் चाप्रदिक्रमम् ॥ 15 ॥

बट्टा सर्मवै: पाशैरानायिधे त्वांतिकम् ।

वशगता तु सदा ते सत्यस्य राजन्बलं मय ॥ 16 ॥

O King! Seemy prowess now; I am just now going with my army and I will kill that violent Candikā, maddened with pride, or I will tie Her down by a coil of snakes and bring Her before you; then that Lady, seeing Herself helpless, will become quite submissive to you; there is no doubt in this.

व्यास उवाच

विरूपाधावच: श्रुत्वा दुर्भीयो वाक्यमब्रवीत् ।

सत्यमुक्ते वचो राजन्विरूपाधेशण धीमता ॥ 17 ॥

Vyāsa said: Hearing these words of Virūpākṣa. Durdhara said: O King! Virūpākṣa is very intelligent; what he has said just now is all reasonable and true.

मापसी घचनं रूक्षण श्रोतल्यं धीमता रव्या ।

कामातुरैया सुदृती लक्ष्यतेउ्यनुमानतः ॥ 18 ॥

O King! You are intelligent; hear my words full of truth also. As far as I think, I consider that woman with beautiful teeth as passionate.

भवत्येवयिता कामं नायिका रूपर्पति ।

भीषयिता वराहात् तां वशे कर्णैयच्छति ॥ 19 ॥

For that woman of broad hips has expressed a desire to bring you under control by making you fearful; the mistresses, proud of their beauty generally use such words when they become passionate.

हावोर्ध मानिनितां वे ते वेति रसवितान: ।

वक्रोत्केश कामिन्या: प्रियं प्रत पशवाणम् ॥ 20 ॥

When they behave in this way, people call these amorous gestures. These crooked words of mistresses are the chief causes in attracting dear persons unto them.

वेति को3पि नरः कामं कामशास्त्रविध्वणः ।

यदुर्फे नाम बालीस्वां वधिष्ये रणामूर्पिति ॥ 21 ॥

Those who are skilled in the art of love affair, some of them can know these things thoroughly well. O King! That woman has said, "I will pierce and kill you by arrows, face to face, in the battlefield."

हैतुणर्भिर्यं वाययं जातव्यं हेतुवितपेः ।

बाणासु पानीनीयं वे कटाङ्गा एव विश्रुतः ॥ 22 ॥

The sense of this is different. The wise persons that are clever and experienced in the art of finding out the cause, declare that the above sentence is pregnant with deep and esoteric meaning.

पुर्णाजलियायाश्रान्ये व्यंयाति वचनाति च ।

का शफिन्तन्यबाणांां प्रेणे लविय पार्थिव ॥ 23 ॥

You can easily see that the handsome women have no other arrows with them; their side-glances are their arrows. And their words carry their hidden

Book V Chapter XI

meanings, and, expressing their desires, are their flowers.

तातुशीना न सा शफित्बंड्वविष्णुराविष्यु ।

ययोक्ते नेत्रबाणीस्वां हनिषेय मन्द पार्थिव्यम ॥ 24 ॥

O King! Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Maheśa even have no powers to shoot arrows at you; how can, then, that helpless woman, who appears so passionate, dart real arrows at you? O King! That lady said: "O Stupid! I will kill your King by my arrow-like eye-sight.

विरपरितं परित्ञतं तेनारसिवहा किल ।

पतार्थिच्यापि श्याच्यां रणमयां पत्ति तब ॥ 25 ॥

विपरिततितकीआभाषणं जेमेवत तत् ।

"But the messenger was wanting in that power to appreciate; so he, no doubt, understood her words in their contrary sense." The saying of that lady "I will lay your lord in the death-bed in the battlefields" is to be taken in the light of inverted sexual intercourse, where woman is above the man.

कीचेय विमाताणं यतुकं कचनं तथा ॥ 26 ॥

वीर्य प्राण इति प्रोकं तद्धिहीनं न चान्यथा ।

Her utterance “I will take away the vitality (life) of your lord is also significant.” The semen virile is known as the vitality (life). Therefore the above expression means that she will make you devoid of your virility. There can be no other meaning.

वंयायधिखेन वावकेन वरवत्यनाम गृध ॥ 27 ॥

तदे विचाराते जेयं रसप्राथविशक्षणैः ।

O King! Those women that are excellent shew by too much of their covert expressions (inuedos) that they select and like very much their beloved. The experts only in these amorous affairs will be able to appreciate these things.

इति जाता महाराज कतव्य रससंयुतम् ॥ 28 ॥

सामदानवृदं तथा नान्योपायोरिति घृपते ।

Knowing thus, dealings ought to be made with Her so that the harmony in amorous sentiments be not broken. O King! Sāma (conciliation) and

Dāna (gifts) are the two means to be adopted; there is no other way.

रुहा या गविला वासि यशमा मानिनी भवेत् ॥ 29 ॥

तादूर्शीपूरेवावैर्येनाविष्ये त्वान्तिकम् ।

किं बढ़ूक्तेन मे राजकर्ताया यशपार्तिनी ॥ 30 ॥

गत्वा मवाउघुनैवयं किंककीय सदैव ते ।

By these two, that Lady, whether she be proud or angry, is sure to be brought under control; I will go not and bring Her before you by such sweet words. O king! What is the use of my talking too much? I will make Her submissive to you like a slave girl.

व्यास उवाच

इर्थे निशम्य तुढालयं तास्तस्त्वविश्वण: ॥ 31 ॥

उवाच चवनं राजक्षिगामय मयोदितम् ।

हेतुपद्वर्मसहितं रसयुक्ते नवान्यितम् ॥ 32 ॥

नैषा कामातुरा बाला नानुर्जक विश्वक्षण ।

व्यंभाविन नैव वाक्यानि त्योक्तानि तु मान्द ॥ 33 ॥

Vyāsa said: Hearing those words of Durdhara, the Dānava Tāmra, who was very experienced in finding out the real nature, said: 'I am telling you what is sanctioned by virtue and is at the same time full of sweet amorous feelings pregnant with deep meanings. "O Giver of honour! This intelligent woman is not at all passionate nor devoted to you; nor has that woman used any covert expressions to you."

चित्रमत्र महावाहो यदेका वरर्गिनी ।

निरलाव्बा समायाति चित्रकरा मनोहरा ॥ 34 ॥

“O Great Hero! This is strange indeed that a Lady, beautiful, handsome, and of strange feature, at the same time alone and helpless, has come here to fight.

अशदारभुजा नारी न शुता न च वीक्षिता ।

केनापि विश्र लोकेषु पराक्रमवतीं शृणा ॥ 35 ॥

आयुधान्यपि तावर्ते घृतानि बलवित्ति च ।

विपरीतिंमदं मन्ये सर्वं कालकृतं नृप ॥ 36 ॥

A good-looking woman, powerful, and having eighteen hands is never heard of, nor ever seen by me in these three worlds. She is holding in each

Šrimaddevibhagavatam

of Her hands powerful weapons. O king! All these seem to be the contrary actions of Time.

स्वपानि वुर्निमितानि मग रूषानि वे निशि ।

तेन जाताम्यहं नृते वैशसं समुपगानम् ॥ 37 ॥

O King! I saw ominous dreams during the night; and I conclude, therefore, that great dangers are over our heads.

कृष्णावरधरा नारी रूटती च गृहांगे ।

दृष्टवा स्वपेयुष:काले विंतितल्यस्तदायः ॥ 38 ॥

Early in the morning twilight, I saw in my dream that a woman, wearing a black raiment, was weeping in the inner courtyard; that some inauspicious events are forthcoming can be easily judged from the above.

विकृता: पक्षिणो राजी रोकवन्ति गृहे गृहे ।

उरपाता विविधा राजम्पभविति गृहे गृहे ॥ 39 ॥

तेन जानाभरहं नूनं कारणं किकिष्ठदेव हि ।

यत्वां प्रार्थयेत बाला युद्धाय कृतनिश्राय ॥ 40 ॥

O King! The birds were screaming hoarsely in every house and various calamitous events were seen in various houses; at this time that woman, firmly resolved, was challenging you to fight; it, therefore, seems to me that there is something very serious in this matter.

नैवान्त्रित मनुषी नो वा गाधर्वीं न तथायुरी ।

देवै: कृतेय ज्ञात्मा यामाभोहकरी विभो ॥ 4 1 ॥

O Lord! This woman is neither human, nor a Gāntharvī, nor the wife of any Asura. Only to cause delusion to us, she, this wondrous Māyā has been created by the gods.

काततत्वं न कतवेंय मेपित्तमतिपयल्यम् ।

कर्तैवं सर्वथा युद्धं यद्धानं तद्धविषयित् ॥ 4 2 ॥

O King! In no case, weakness is to be resorted; it is wise by all means to fight as best as possible; what is inevitable will come to pass; this is my opinion.

को बेद देवक्तियं शुभं वास्यगुभं तथा ।

अरतल्य घिया दीर्घ स्थानतयं वै: विश्वभौः ॥ 43 ॥

No one is able to unriddle the doings of the Devas, whether they would be auspicious or

inauspicious. Therefore intelligent ones should weigh pros and cons carefully and remain patient and steady.

जीवित मरणं पुंसां दैवधीर्न नराधिप ।

कोर्पि नैवान्यथा कर्तु समर्थं घृतनगरे ॥ 44 ॥

O King! Life or death is at the hands of destiny; Nobody, therefore, can do it otherwise."

गच्छ ताप महाभाग युद्धाय कृतनिश्रयः ।

ताभानय वरगोहां जिल्या धर्मेण मानिनीम् ॥ 45 ॥

Hearing this, Mahisāsura said: "O Highly fortunate Tāmra! Better, then, stand for fight, fully resolved and go that Lady, beautiful, and conquer Her according to rules of justice and bring Her before me.

न भवेद्रागा नारी संग्रामे यदि सा तव ।

हन्तवा नान्यथा कामं माननीया प्रधत्नतः ॥ 46 ॥

"In case She does not come under your control in fight, kill Her; but if She comes round, then shew Her honour; do not kill Her.

वीरस्वमसि सर्वत्र कामगशाखिशापादः ।

येन केनाव्युपायेन जेतक्यो वरवर्शिणी ॥ 47 ॥

O All knowing! You are a great hero and at the same thoroughly conversant with Kāma Śāstra (science of love); therefore conquer that Fair One by any means you can.

त्वरन् वीर महाबाहो सैन्येन महता वृत: ।

तत्र नगला त्वया झेय विचार्यं च पुनः पुनः ॥ 48 ॥

O valiant Tāmra, of mighty prowess! Go then with a mighty force and ponder over again and again and find out Her intention.

किमर्थयाता चेयं तात्यां तिद्धि कारणम् ।

कामात्रा वैरभावाच्च माया कर्येवियातृम् ॥ 49 ॥

Is She prompted by passion or by real inimical feeling or by any other motive? Try to find out whose Māyā is this?

आदौ तक्रिश्रयं कृत्वा जातवयं तिच्चाकीर्भिन् ।

पश्रातुघूं प्रकर्तेल्यं यथायोग्यं यथाबतम् ॥ 50 ॥

Know all these beforehand; then find out the remedy; next fight with Her according to your strength and prowess.

Book V Chapter XI

क्रातरखे न कतिथ्य निद्रेयलं तथा न च ।

याजूर्ग हि मनस्तस्या: कतिथं ताजूर्ण तथा || 51 ||

Weakness should not be shown nor merciless behaviour is to be resorted; you should behave with Her according to the bent of Her mind.

व्यास उवाच

इति तद्धाविं शृत्वा तायः कालवर्ग नतः ।

निर्भात: सैन्यसंयुक्तः प्रणाय्य महिष्चं नृपम् ॥ 52 ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! Thus hearing the King's words, Tāmra coming as if under the sway of Death, saluted the king Mahiṣa and marched away with his army.

गच्छमार्गे दुरावास्तीशकुनावीश्य दरभान्न ।

विव्यवं च भयं प्राप यमभागप्रदर्शकान् ॥ 53 ॥
