That wicked Dānava, who on his way, began to see all the fearful inauspicious signs, indicative of Death, became surprised and was caught with fear.

स पत्ता तां सामालीक्य देवीं सिंशोषी स्थितान् ।

सूद्यमानां सुरै: सर्वे: सर्वायुधविष्णवितान् ॥ 54 ॥

When he arrived at the spot he saw the Devi standing on a lion, while She was decorated with all the weapons and instruments, and all the Devas chanting hymns to Her.

तात्याच विमिता: सन् वाक्यं मधुरया सिता ।

सामभावं समाश्रित्य विनयावन्तः स्थितः ॥ 55 ॥

Tāma, then bowed down before Her with humility and modesty and addressed Her with sweet words, according to the rules of the policy of conciliation.

देवि दैतेवधरः श्रुंगी त्वद्वपुणामोहितः ।

सूष्ठां कोतित महिषस्त्वाणिश्रद्धाया च ॥ 56 ॥

“O Devi! Mahiṣa, the lord of the Daityas, has become enchanted on hearing Your beauty and qualifications and has become desirous to marry You.”

भार्यं कृक विशालास्थि तत्सम्रमरतुर्णे ।

पाति तं प्रायय मृदुर्गिन नन्दने विहराद्धते ॥ 57 ॥

O Beautiful One! You would better be graciously pleased with that conqueror of the Immor-

tals, the Mahisāsura; O Thou of delicate limbs! Make him your husband and enjoy all the exquisite pleasures of the Nandana garden as best as you can.

सर्वागसूनरं देई प्राय सर्वसुखास्यद् ।

सुखं सर्वसेन्या ग्राहं दुःखं हेयमिति स्थिति: ॥ 58 ॥

The end and aim of attaining this human form, beautiful in every respect and the abode of all bliss, is to enjoy, in every way, all the pleasure of human existence and to avoid the sources of all troubles. This is the rule.

करभोक किमर्थे ते गृहीतान्यायुधान्यम् ।

पुष्पकन्दुकीयागते कारा: कमलकौमतः ॥ 59 ॥

O Thou of beautiful thighs like those of the young of an elephant! Your soft and delicate lotus-like hands are fit to play only with nice balls of flowers; why then are You holding in Your hands all the weapons and arrows?

ब्रुचाये विद्यानेपर विधृया किं प्रजोजनम् ।

कटाक्षा विशिष्ठा: सन्ति कि वाणीनैण्यवोनैः ॥ 60 ॥

What is the use of holding ordinary arrows, when those two eyebrows like bows, are existing with You?

संसारे कुःखदं युद्धे न कतकैलं विज्ञानता ।

लोभासक्ता: प्रकृतीत संग्रामं च परस्पत् ॥ 61 ॥

What need have you to take ordinary arrows when you are graced with that piercing eyesights, your arrows.

पृष्येपि न योद्धल्यं किं पुनर्निरिति: शरैः ।

घेदनं निजगगाणां कस्य तज्जायते पुंदे ॥ 62 ॥

War is exceedingly painful in this world; those who know thus ought never to fight. It is only those human beings that are prompted by greed, that fight with each other.

तस्मात्मपरि तर्वयि प्रसादं कतुर्महसि ।

भतरं भज मे नाथं देवदानवपूजितम् ॥ 6 3 ॥

What to speak of those sharpened arrows, one ought not to fight with flowers even; O Devi! You can well say who is that feels pleasure, when one's own body is pierced?

Śrimaddevibhaigavatam

स तेउर वाछित्त सर्व कारित्यति मनोराथ।

तं पुभुमिकी राज: सर्वथा नात्र संपर: ॥ 64 ॥

Therefore, O Delicate One! Gladly you can worship Mahiṣa, the lord of the world and the object of worship of the Devas and Dānavas. Then he will satisfy all your desires. What more to say, you will no doubt be his queen-consort.

वचनं कुक मे देखि प्राययसे सुखमुत्तमम् ।

संग्रामे जयसेंटुः कहं प्राय न संग्रशः ॥ 6 5 ॥

O Devi! If one tries one's best, it is doubtful whether one would be crowned with success; therefore keep my this request; you will surely get all the best pleasures.

ज्ञानासि राजनीति लं यथावदुर्वर्णिनि ।

भूव्र राज्यसुखं पूर्णे वर्षाणामयुतायुतम् ॥ 6 6 ॥

"O Beautiful! You are well acquainted with

all the politics; therefore you better enjoy thoroughly the pleasure of the kingdom for full many years.

पुत्रसे घसिता क्रांत: सोअपि राजा भविष्यिति ।

योजने क्रीष्टिवान्ते वार्विये सुखाग्यस्विं ति 67 ॥

इति श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणे पक्षमकन्धे

एकावरशोउकायः ।। ११ ।।

"And if you marry Mahisa you will have beautiful sons and those sons again will be kings; and enjoying the pleasures of your full grown womanhood, you will no doubt, be happy in your old age."

Here ends the Eleventh Chapter of the Fifth Book on the appearing of the Dānava Tāmsra before the Devi in Śrimaddevibhagāvatam, the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses, by Maharsī Veda Vyāsa.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER XII

On the Cabinet Held by Mahiṣa

व्यास उवाच

तिश्राम्य चचस्तस्य तात्यस्य जगद्धिक्का ।

मेयाम्भीरा वाचा हस्तनी तमुवाच् च ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: The World-Mother, hearing Tāmra's words, spoke laughing a little and with a deep voice like that of a rumbling thunder cloud.

देख्यवाच

गच्छ ताप यतं बृहि मुपुषु मन्दोतसम् ।

महिंचं चातिकामातं मूदं ज्ञानविवर्जितम् ॥ २ ॥

The Devi spoke: “O Tāmra! Go and say to your Lord Mahisa who, it seems, is stupid, whose end is nigh, who has become very passionate, and who is void of knowledge of what is proper and what is improper.

यथा ते महिषी माता प्रीक्षा यवसभक्षिणी ।

नाहं तथा शृगवती लम्बपुच्छा महोदरी ॥ ३ ॥

I am not like your grown up mother, the she-buffalo, having horns, eating grass, with a long tail and a big-belly.

न कामपेउई देवेशं नैव विद्युं न शक्रिम् ।

धनदं वर्णो नैव ब्रह्माणं न च पावकम् ॥ 4 ॥

I do not like to have even Visnu, the god Śaṅkara, Brahmā, Kubera, Varuna, or Fire.

एतान्देवाणादित्य पगुं केन युणेन वै ।

युणोम्यहं यूथा लोके गहीणां मे भवेदिति ॥ 5 ॥

How then can I select a beast? If I do so, I will be an object of much censure amongst the several worlds.

नाहं पतिवरा नारी वर्तते मे पति: प्रभु: ।

सर्वकर्ता सर्वसाधी ह्यकर्ता निःसृष्टः स्थग: ॥ 6 ॥

I am not desirous of any more husband; my Husband is existing; though He is the Lord of all, Witness of All, yet He is not the Actor; He is without any desires and He is calm and tranquil.

निर्गुणो निर्मांतीउनन्तो निरालम्बो निराश्रयः ।

सर्वैन: सर्वैन: साक्षी पूर्ण: पूर्णाशय: जिज: ॥ 7 ॥

He, the Śiva, is devoid of any Prāktric qualities, without any attachment, the Great Infinite, without anyone to rely on, without any refuge, omniscient, omnipresent, the Great Witness, the Full, and the scat of the Full, the Auspicious.

Book V Chapter XII

सर्वावास: क्षम: शान्त: सर्वद्वसर्वभावन: ।

तं चक्सना माहियं मन्दं कवं संवितुपुलहो ॥ 8 ॥

He is the abode of all, capable to do all, the peaceful, capable to create everything and He is seeing everywhere. How can I then leave Him and try to serve dull, stupid Mahisa?

प्रशुध्य युध्यतां कामं कारोमि यमावहान् ।

अथवा मनुजानां वै कार्ये जलवाहकम् ॥ ९ ॥

Let him come and fight with this understanding that he will be defeated and be made the conveyance of Yama, the God of Death or the carrier of water of the human beings.

जीवितेष्ठप्रति देवाया गळछ पालालमाशु वै ।

समसरीर्विवैयुक्तत्वव्या हृन्मि संगरे ॥ १० ॥

And if that impious heretic desires to live let him fly at once to Pātāla with all his demon comrades; else I will slay him in battle.

कामं सदूषायोर्गे: संसारे सुखदो घबेत् ।

अन्यवा तुःखदो घ्यावत्नानादिर कल्मितः ॥ १ १ ॥

See! combination of similar substances leads to happiness; and if out of ignorance, the connection takes place between things entirely different in their nature, it becomes at once the source of all pain and troubles."

मुर्जल्मवसि यदचूषे पर्ित मे भज आधिनि ।

क्याहं क्य महिषः शूत्री सवचः कीरुणो इयोः ॥ 12 ॥

You are a thorough by illiterate when you ask me to worship your lord; do you not see me endowed with exquisite beauty? and what is your Mahisa? A buffalo with horns; how can then connection become possible between us?

गच्छ सुध्यस्य वा कामं हनिष्येहं सवान्यवम् ।

यद्भागरं देवतलोके नोतेच्यकता सुखी भव ॥ 13 ॥

Better fly away or fight if you like; I will kill you and your friends, and if you leave the region of Heaven and the share of Yajña, then you will become happy."

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्त्वा सा तात द्वेो वेगर्भ शुभामूद्तम् ।

कल्यान्तसूर्ण नावं चक्रे दैत्यभयावहम् ॥ 14 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus saying, the Devi howled and roared so loudly that it appeared strange and it caused a great terror to the Dānavas who took it as the great dissolution of the universe at the end of a Kalpa.

चक्षेय वसुधा चेलुस्तेन शब्देन भूधराः ।

भाधिशु दैन्यपत्नीना सर्वसुर्गर्पित्तस्वन्ता ॥ 15 ॥

The earth and the mountains trembled: the wives of the Dānavas had miscarriage at that terrific noise.

ताघः शुत्वा च तं शब्दं भयसत्मानस्ताद ।

पलायनं ततः कृत्वा जगाम महिष्योतिकम् ॥ 16 ॥

Tāmra hearing that sound was terrified; his mind became unsteady and he at once fled to Mahiṣa.

नगरे तस्य वे दैत्यास्तेपि चित्तमायानुवत् ।

वधिरीकृतकर्णगीछ पलायनपरा नृप ॥ 17 ॥

O King! The Dānavas present in the city became deaf; they fled and became very anxious and were absorbed in the thought whence and how that sound came.

तदा कोर्टेन सिंहोरूपि ननाद भुशमूलस्टः ।

तेन नाटेन दैतेवा भयं जगमूरिय स्कुटम् ॥ १ ॥ ॥

तापं समागतं दूद्या ह्वारीरिंग मोहितः ।

चिंतयामास सचिवः किं कल्यमतः परम् ॥ १९ ॥

The lion, too, enraged and, raising up its manes, roared so loud that the Daityas became very much terrified. Mahisa too, became confounded to see Tāmra returning; he then held a council with his ministers on what ought to be done next?

दुर्गग्राहो वा कतव्यो युद्ध निरर्गत्व वा पुनः ।

पलायने कृते श्रैयो भयेड़ा द्वान्वोतमाः ॥ 20 ॥

Mahisāsura said: "O best of the Dānavas! Shall we now take our shelter within the forts? Or shall we go out and fight? Or will it be favourable to us if we fly away?

बुद्धिमन्तो दुताधर्षी: सर्वे शास्त्रविशगरातः ।

मनः खतु प्रकल्पः सुगुप्तः कार्यसिद्धो ॥ 21 ॥

You all are intelligent and versed in all the Śāstras and unconquerable by your foes; therefore

ought you all to consult over the matter in utmost privacy for our success at the present moment.

मन्त्रभूलं स्मृतं राज्यं यदि स स्यात्सृक्षितः ।

मन्त्रभिक्षा सदाचारित्विधेयः सर्वेधा चुयेः ॥ 22 ॥

The root of Kingdom lies in the council in the secret place (cabinet) and Statesmanship; if this counsel be kept well preserved in secrecy, then that Kingdom is also kept entire; therefore it is good and virtuous.

मन्त्रधेते विज्ञा: स्याज्ञस्य प्रपतेलया ।

तस्माद्रेवपयात्युतः कतवीय भूतिभिछ्ठता ॥ 23 ॥

If the plan be out, then destruction comes both to the King and his Kingdom; hence the plan must be kept secret by those wanting glory, lest it be taken advantage of and rendered ineffective by other persons.

तद्व मत्निषिबाँच्छं वचनं हेतुपादितून ।

कालदेशानुसारेण विविधेन नीतिनिवर्धियम् ॥ 24 ॥

या योषात्र समावाता प्रवला देविनिमिता ।

एकाकिनी निरालम्बा कारणं तद्धिव्यताम् ॥ 25 ॥

O Ministers! Now declare, taking due consideration of time and place, after duly discussing and ascertaining what is the best course to adopt, what would be beneficial and full of reason and intelligence. First find out the cause why this powerful woman, created by the Devas has come here alone and helpless?

युद्ध प्रार्थयेत बाला किमाध्ययेत: परम् ।

श्रेयोत्र विवर्गितं वा को वेति भुवनत्रये ॥ 26 ॥

That woman is challenging us to fight. What more wonder can there be than this? Who can say in the three worlds what the result will be, whether it will be good or otherwise?

न बहूनां जयोउपत्यित नैकस्य च परावयः ।

दैत्यापीनी सदा जेगेयु युद्रे जयपरावजी ॥ 27 ॥

Victory comes not to many persons nor defeat comes to a single individual; therefore victory or defeat lies at the hands of the Luck and Destiny.

उयायवादितः प्रगृदैर्व कैन कैन वीक्षितम् ।

अनुश्मिति यजाम प्रवर्तिन मनीषिणः ॥ 28 ॥

तस्तचेडपि प्रमाणं किं कततराणावलंबनम् ।

Those who plead for place, policy, statesmanship, they say what is Fate? Is there anyone who has seen Fate? (Adṛṣṭa) No one has seen His appearance." It may be argued that there may exist such a thing as Fate; to which it might be replied, what proofs are there for such an existence?

न समर्थजनानां हि दैर्व कुजापि लक्ष्यते ॥ 29 ॥

उद्यानो दैवलमेते हि शूरकातायोर्मन्ति ।

विधिन्याथ विधा सर्वी कतैल्यं कायमादातृम् ॥ 30 ॥

Thus the weak persons alone hold it out as their only hope; nowhere are seen energetic persons who can fulfil their ends by their own efforts, by those who enrol themselves under Fate. Therefore “effort,” “energy” are the words of the heroes and “Fate” is the word of cowards. You should all consider today these subjects fully and intelligently and then decide what are we to do?

व्यास उवाच

इति राजी वचः शुत्वा हेतुगर्भ महायाशा: ।

विद्वाताख्यो महाराजमिव्युवाच कृतांजलितः ॥ 31 ॥

राजेशेय शिवगालाशी ज्ञातया यतन्तः पुनः ।

किम्बर्धिंहि सभ्यापता कुतः कस्य परिश्रहः ॥ 32 ॥

Vyāsa said: Thus hearing the King, the famous Vidālākṣa with folded hands spoke thus: "O king! First it should be definitely ascertained as to whose wife is she, this woman possessing large eyes? Whence and for what purpose has she come here; next what ought to be done should be decided.

मरणं ते परिश्राय सिक्वा: सर्वानिमा सुरैः ।

प्रेशिता यदपत्राक्षी समुचणा स्ततेजास ॥ ३३ ॥

It seems to me that the Devas, knowing that your death will ensue from the hands of a woman, have created very carefully this lotus-eyed woman out of their own essences.

तेपि छातः स्थिताः खेउत्र सर्वे युद्धिदूक्षतः ।

समयंेःस्थाः महायासे भविष्याति युयुसासः ॥ 34 ॥

And they are lying in wait, unknown to anybody in the celestial space with a desire to see the battle

Book V Chapter XII

but really to fight with you. In due time, they will undoubtedly help this woman.

पुस्त: कामिनी कृत्ता ते वे विद्यापुरोगमाः ।

विध्यन्ति च नः सर्वान्सा त्वं युद्धे हनिषयिति ॥ 35 ॥

When the war will ensue, Viṣṇu and the other Devas will put this woman in front and slay us all. Whereas this Devi will slay you.

एतचिक्वीर्तिते तेनां मवा ज्ञातं नराधिता ।

भवितत्यस्य न ज्ञानं वर्तेत मम सर्वेथा ॥ 36 ॥

This is their earnest desire. O king! I have come to know this beforehand; but what will be the actual result I cannot say.

योद्योपं न तव्याडोति नाहं वचुः क्षमः प्रभो ।

प्रमाणं त्वं महाराज कार्येज्ञ देविनिमिते ॥ 37 ॥

I cannot say also whether it is advisable for You to fight now; therefore it would be better if you consider yourself well on this matter of the gods and do accordingly.

त्वर्धेंउमाधिरनिणं मतंव्य कार्योगरावत् ।

विद्रतैंत्य तवा साधीमेव धर्मेउनुजीविनाम् ॥ 38 ॥

Our duty, the duty of your servants lies in this: That we should sacrifice at any moment our lives for the preservation of your prestige; and to enjoy with you whenever you are enjoying.

विद्यारोउन महान्सित यदेका कामिनी नृप ।

यूद्र प्रार्थवेउसामभि: ससै-यैर्वलिरपिंतेः ॥ ३ ९ ॥

But, O King! It is extremely advisable to ponder over this very carefully when we see that this woman, though alone, is challenging us to fight who are armed with powerful soldiers.

दूर्यैल उवाच

राजन् युद्रे जयं नोउष चपिता वैव्यई किल ।

पलायनं न कर्तव्य यगोहानिकरं नृगाम ॥ 40 ॥

Durmukha said: "O king! I know for certain, that we will not get victory in this battle; still we ought not to shew our back; for that would lead us to sheer disgrace.

इत्रादीनां संयुप्रेपि न कृतं यज्ञगुषिसतम् ।

एकाकिनी सिधंग प्राय को हि कुर्पत्पलयात्यम् ॥ 41 ॥

Even in our encounter with Indra and other Devas, we did nothing hateful and blameable; then how can any of us fly away when we come face to face with a helpless woman?

तस्मादुर्द प्रकर्नियं मरणां वा रणे जयः ।

यद्धालि तद्वलेवेक काऊत्र चिन्ता विषयश्न: ॥ 42 ॥

Therefore fight we must; that is certain; let whatever happen's happen. What is inevitable, must come to pass. Thus considered, what need we care for the result?

मरणोत्र यशःप्रापिरिजीचे च तथा सुखम् ।

उभयं मनसा कृत्वा कतिथ्यं युद्धमष्ट वै ॥ 43 ॥

If we die in the battle, we will get name and fame; if we be victorious, we will get happiness. Thus thinking both the cases, we must fight today.

पलायने यशोहामिरणं चायुषः क्षये ।

तस्माछोको न कर्तव्यो जीक्षिते मरणे बुबा ॥ 44 ॥

Death is inevitable when our longevity expires; our prestige will suffer if we fly away; therefore we ought not to spend uselessly our time in thus expressing our vain regret for life or for death.

व्यास उवाच

दुर्भूतय स्व च: शुत्वा बाक्लते वायवमब्रवीत् ।

प्रणतः प्रांजलिभूत्वा राजानं वाक्यकोविदः ॥ 45 ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! Hearing thus Durmukha's words, Bāskala, the eloquent speaker, thus spoke to the king, with clasped hands and his head bowed down.

वाक्पल उवाच

राजौक्षिता न कतव्या कार्येत्रीयकातरप्रिये ।

अहमेको हनियापि चपईी चंचल्लोचनाम ॥ 46 ॥

“O king! You need not think thus in agony upon this unpleasant affair; alone I will kill that Caṇḍikā, of unsteady eyes.

उत्ताहतु प्रक्रतिथः स्थायीभावो सस्य च ।

भयानको भवेहौरी वीरस्य नृपसतम ॥ 47 ॥

O Best of kings! To be always prompt and energetic indicates that one is steady in one's heroic valour, to consider one's enemy as dreadful

Śrīmaddevibhāgavatam

is contrary to above; so we ought not to take recourse to heroic valour.

तस्मायत्ववा भयं घूप कारिष्य युद्धमद्धुतम् ।

नत्यथामि नरेन्हाई चपिड़कां यमसावन् ॥ 4 8 ॥

O King! Therefore I will discard fear altogether and fight out valiantly; I will no doubt, send Candikā in the battle field to the abode of Death.

न विधेमि यमादिद्राकुधेवाढ्गणादि ।

वायोवैहैस्तथा विष्णो: शुक्राछ्डिशनो रेवे: ॥ 49 ॥

एकाकिनी तथा नारी किं पुर्नमेदारविता ।

उहं तां निद्विण्यामि विशिषेक्छे शिलापिति: ॥ 50 ॥

I fear not Yama, nor Indra, nor Kubera, nor Vāyu nor Agni, nor Viṣṇu, nor Śaṅkara, nor Moon nor Sun; I do not fear any of them; what fear can I, then, entertain of that vain arrogant woman, who has got none to support her. I will kill Her with these arrows, sharpened on stones.

पश्य बाहुबलं मेउं हितरस्य यथासुरखम् ।

भवतात्रन न नत्वयं संग्रामे उपवन्या समम् ॥ 51 ॥

You can today see the prowess of my arms and enjoy peace; you will not have to go to battle any more to fight with Her."

एवं बृवति राजेन्द्रं वाक्कते मदगविंते ।

प्रणाय नृपति तन दुर्धीते वाक्यमवीत् ॥ 52 ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! Bāskala having said thus to the lord Mahisa in a haughty spirit, Durdhara bowed down and said thus:

दुर्धर उवाच

महिषहा विजेचायामि देर्वीं देवाविनिर्मितम् ।

अश्रदाशुजां रायां काराणाच्च समागताम् ॥ 53 ॥

“O Lord of the earth! Let the purpose be whatsoever, with which the beautiful Devi with eighteen hands, the creation of the gods, may come hither, I will vanquish Her.

राजभीयवितुं त्वां वे प्रपोषा तिमिता सुरैः ।

विधीपिकेयं विज्ञाय त्वज मोहं मनोगतम् ॥ 54 ॥

O king! I think, it is simply to terrify you, as the Suras have thus created this Māyā woman; therefore, do you forsake your delusion by knowing this merely as a scare.

राजनीतिरिंच राजन्मन्त्रिकृचं तथा श्रणु ।

साचित्का राजसा: कैरितानमासून तवापरे ॥ 55 ॥

O King! Such is the statesmanship; now hear about the workings of the ministers. Ministers in this world are of three kinds: (1) Sāttvik; (2) Rājasik and (3) Tāmasik.

मन्विणस्त्रिविधा लोके भविते दानवाधिप ।

सात्चका: प्रपुक्रायणीय साध्यपत्यि सखातिफिंभि: ॥ 56 ।

Those ministers in whom the Sattva quality is predominant, they perform their Master's duties according to their own strength.

आत्मकृत्य प्रकृवर्नित स्वामिकार्यविरोधतः ।

एकवित्ता धर्मपरा मन्त्रागत्कशियगरादः ॥ 57 ॥

The Sāttvik Mantri (ministers) are well versed in their Mantra Sāstras (the policies and statesmanship), virtuous and one-pointed in their thoughts, they never do any injury to their king and they fulfil their own purpose.

राजसा भिप्राचिता श्रु कव्यापिताना: सदा ।

कदाचित्यसामिकार्य ते प्रकुर्षिति यद्धच्या ॥ 58 ॥

The Rājasik Mantrīs are of different sorts; they are always after own interests; at times; whenever they like, they do the State duties.

तामसा लोभनितता: स्वक्यर्नितता: सदा ।

प्रभुकायं विनार्यैव स्वकर्ग साध्यान्ति ने ॥ 59 ॥

The Tāmasik Mantris always look of their own interests out of their greedy nature; they serve their ends even by ruining the regal interests.

समयं ते विधिन्धते पेस्टु परिवडिता: ।

स्विच्छर्ने शुण्रधीयानिर्दिशित गृहस्थितः ॥ 6 0 ॥

कार्यपेटकार नित्य कोशगृणताविसल्पा ।

संग्रामे5 थ समुस्परे औषयन्ति प्रभुं सदा ॥ 6 1 ॥

It is the Tāmasik Mantris that are influenced by the bribes from the enemies, become separated at their hearts from their own masters and give out the secrets to the enemies, while staying in their homes. They always advise alienation policy like the sword ensheathed in a scabbard; and when the time of war comes, they always frighten their masters.

Book V Chapter XIII

विश्वाससून न कतव्यस्तेषां राजकन्दाचन ।

विश्वास कार्वहीन: स्थानमन्दारिन: सदैव हि १ ६ २ १

खला: कि किं न कुर्जलि विश्वस्ता लभतवराः।

तामसा: पापनिरता बुद्धिहीनाः शठासस्था ॥ 6 3 ॥

Therefore, O King! Never put your trust on ministers; if you do so, they will always hinder you in your actions and counsels; what harm cannot be done by those ministers that are treacherous, greedy, deceitful and void of any intelligence and always addicted to vicious acts, when they are trusted!

तस्माकार्यं करिष्यामि गतावड़हं रामसतेक ।

चित्ता तव्या न कर्तेव्या सर्वेथा नृपसत्तम ॥ 64 ॥

गृहीत्वा तां दुराचारामागमिष्यामि सत्वः ।

पश्य मेउडा बलं दीर्घं प्रभुकामों स्थशफिततः ॥ 65 ॥

इति श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणे पहामकद्भ्ये

ब्राहणोउध्याः: 11 12 11

Therefore, O king! I will go myself to the battle and serve your purpose; you need not be at all anxious in this matter. I will soon bring before you that vicious woman; I will do your actions by my own strength and powers. Let you be calm; and look at my strength, fortitude and valour.

Here ends the Twelfth Chapter of the Fifth

Book on the holding of counsel by Mahisāsura in

Śrimaddevibhāgavatam the Mahā Pruānam, of

18,000 Ślokas by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter XIII

CHAPTER XIII

On the Killing of Bāskala and Durmukha

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्ता ती महाबाहू दैत्यों बाक्यलनुंगुंढ़ी ।

जगमनुर्मदिद्धगांगी सर्वशास्त्राकोदिति ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The two powerful Dānavas Bāskala and Durmukha, well-versed in arts of warfare, went out for battle, maddened with their prowess.

तौ गत्वा समरे देवीमूचतुर्वचंन तदा ।

दानवी च मदोन्मत्ती मेघमाम्पीत्या शिरा ॥ 2 ॥

The two Dānavas, elated with vanity, went to the battle-field and began to address the Devi in voice deep as the rumbling of a cloud.

देवि देवा जिता येन महिषेण महात्मना ।

वाय त वं तरातोरे सर्वैत्यापिधिं पुर्णम् ॥ 3 ॥

O Beautiful Devi! You better choose and worship the Lord of the Daityas, that high-souled Mahisāsura who has conquered all the Devas.

स कृत्वा मानुषं रूपं वर्मलक्षणसंयुतम् ।

भूषितं भूषणैदिख्यैस्त्वामेध्यति रहः किल ॥ 4 ॥

He will come before you in privacy in a human shape, with all auspicious signs and adorned with beautiful ornaments.

त्रैलोक्यविभयं कामं त्वमेध्यस गृधचिमिते ।

महिषे परं भयं भाकं कुरु कानते मनगेतगम् ॥ 5 ॥

O Sweet smiling One! Better place your highest feelings of love on the lovely Mahisa as your husband, and you will get all the pleasures of the three worlds as you desire.

कृत्वा पर्लिं महावीरं संसामसुखमढ़तम् ।

त्वं प्राप्यसि चिकालाये योचितां खलु वांक्षितम् ॥ ६ ॥

O Sweet speaking! In short, if you select him as your husband, you will be the mistress of those incomparable worldly happiness that women always aspire.

श्रीद्युबाच

जात्य तवं किं विज्ञानासि नारीयं काममोहिता ।

मन्दबुद्धिजलात्यर्ष भजेयं महिंश शठम ॥ ७ ॥

Hearing thus the words of Vāşkala and Durmukha, the Devi said: "O Stupid! Do you think Me as deluded by passion? Do I not possess strength and intelligence that I will worship that hypocrite Mahisa as husband?

कुलशीलगुणैसुन्यै तं भजन्ति कुलशिक्षय: ।

अधिक रूपचातुर्वैब्द्रशीलक्षमादिधि: ॥ 8 ॥

का नु कामातुरा नारी भयंेच्च यगुहविषणम् ।

पगुनामघां नूनं महिष्यं देवकपिणी ॥ 9 ॥

See! The ladies of a high family select those persons that are equal in rank as far as family and distinctions, qualifications and propriety of conduct are concerned or those who are superior in beauty, cleverness, intelligence and other qualifications. Then how can a Devi, becoming passionate, worship the worst of all beasts, the beast Mahisa?

गच्छ तं महिषं तृर्णं भूरं बाक्लतुर्मेदी ।

वदतं तद्ञचे दैत्यं गतृतुल्यं विषाणिनम् ॥ 10 ॥

भालतं गच्छ वातभ्येन संभागं कुरा वभा ।

रो जाते सहसाधो निर्भयः स्यादिति धुम्म ॥ 11 ॥

O two Asuras! Go you immediately to your King Mahiṣa resembling in his body like an elephant and having a pair of horns and tell him "Go either to Pātāla (the nether regions) or come and fight with Me; the Lord of the Devas will no doubt be happy if war ensues."

हलाई त्वा गमिष्यामि नान्यथा गमनं मम ।

इन्ध जाला सुवुरुबैद्य पेशेच्छसि तथा कुक ११ १२ १।

मामनिजीक्य धूभागे न स्थानं ते कदाचन ।

भविष्यति चतुण्यात दिवि वा गिरिकन्दरे ॥ 13 ॥

“O Stupid! My advent here cannot go in vain; I will easily slay you and then depart; knowing this do as you like.” “O Beast! Without conquering Me, you would get no shelter either in the heavens, or in this earth, or in the caves of mountains?

व्यास उवाच

इतृपुत्ती ती ताया दैत्यौ कोपालुकिलतलोचनौ ।

धनुबाँधणार्य वीरी युद्धकामी बथपुतुः ॥ 14 ॥

Vyāsa said: Hearing thus, the two powerful Daityas, with eyes reddened with anger, firmly resolved to fight and took bows and arrows in their hands.

कृत्वा सुविष्णुं नादं देसी सा निर्भाय स्थिता ।

उभी च चक्रतुस्तीजां बाणवृहिं कुरूकृद्ध ।। १५ ॥

भगवत्ययि बाणीयान्मुगेच दानवौ प्रति ।

कृत्वास्तिमधुरं नादं देवकायार्थीसिद्धदे ॥ १६ ॥

O Descent of Kuru! The Devi then made a terrible noise and fearlessly stood there. The two Dānavas then began to shoot dreadful arrows at Her. For the victory of the Devas, the Devi also began to hurl arrows after arrows on the two Dānavas, emitting a sweet sound.

तयोसु बाषकललूर्ण सम्पुखो3प्रयुगाणे।

दुर्भुखः प्रेशकस्तत्र देवीभिभयुखः स्थितः ॥ 17 ॥

तयोर्भुंद्रमभूद्धोगं देवीबाषकल्योस्तदा ।

बाणातियरियात्यारैभंदरे मंद्येतेसान्म ॥ 18 ॥

Bāskala first came forward with no delay; and Durmukha stood aloof there simply as a witness. A terrible flight then ensued between the Devi and Bāskala; arrows, swords and weapons were seen shining in the air and raised terror to those that were dull in intellect.

ततः कुन्द्र जगमाता शूद्यात ते युद्धुमंदम् ।

जयान पद्धभिवर्गी कागोईः शिलानिंतः ॥ 19 ॥

Then the Mother of the Universe seeing Bāskala growing turbulent shot at him five arrows sharpened on stone.

दानवोदी शगत्नोद्याधिष्छेद निशिणे: शरैः ।

सदाधिततादयामास देवीं सिंहोपरि सिद्धात्म। ॥ 20 ॥

The Dānava, too, cut off the arrows of the Devi and hurled seven arrows at Her, seated on a lion. साउपि तं दशामिश्तीक्षैगः सुयीतः सायकैः खलम् ।

जयान तच्छरांश्चला जहास च मुबुरुईः ॥ 21 ॥

The Devi cut off the Dānava's arrows and shot at that hypocrite, sharpened arrows and began to laugh frequently.

अधिन्द्रेश आणेन चिष्ठदं च शासनम् ।

बाक्लोर्स पि यतं गृहा देवाँ हंतुमुपायची ॥ 22 ॥

She again cut off his arrows with Ardachandra arrow; Bāskala then pursued the Devi with a club in his hands to slay Her.

आगछर्टन गदायाणि दानवं मदगर्वितम् ।

चपिड़का स्वगदाधाते: पातयामास भृत्ते ॥ 23 ॥

Seeing the arrogant Dānava with club in his hands, Candikā Devi struck him down on the ground with Her own club.

Book V Chapter XIII

The very powerful Bāskala fell down on the ground but rose up within a very short time and hurled again on the Devi his club.

बाक्कल: पतितो भूमौ मुहुर्नादुत्वितः पुनः ।

चिक्षेप च गद्य सोरीय चिष्टकां चंद्रविक्रमः ॥ 24 ॥

Seeing him again attacking Her, the Devi got angry and pierced him with Her trident; Bāskala fell down, thus pierced, and died.

तमागच्छन्तमालोक्य देवी शुलेन बक्षसि ।

जयान बाषकर्त कुन्दा पपात च ममार सः ॥ 25 ॥

पत्तेत बाषकले सैन्यं भगत तस्य दुरात्मतः ।

जयेति च मुदा देवाङ्धुकुशुपर्गाने स्थिता: ॥ 26 ॥

Bāskala falling thus dead on the field, the soldiers of the wicked demon routed; whereas the Devas became glad and repeatedly shouted aloud "Victory to the Devi."

तरिमंधु सितदे देद्ये दुर्वैज्ञोरित्वालान्ति: ।

आजगाम रणे देदी क्लोभसंरक्तलोचनः ॥ 27 ॥

On this Daitya being slain, Durmukha came forward on the battlefield, filled with anger and accompanied by a stronger army.

तिथि तिथावले सोर्धि भाषमाण: पुन: पुन: ।

धनुर्बाणघः श्रीमात्रथसः कवचावृतः ॥ 28 ॥

Mounted on a chariot, shielded all over his body with a coat of armour, Durmukha came before the Devi, shouting all along “Wait, wait, O You weak woman!” and with bows and arrows in his hands. तमागछंतमालोकय देवी रंगखमावद्यत् ।

कोपयन्ती दानवं तं ज्याओंचे च चकार है ॥ 29 ॥

The Devi blew Her conchshell and made sounds by stretching Her bow in order to make the Dānava infuriated with anger.

सोर्पि बाणामुभोद्यगृ तैश्यागशिविणमान् ।

स्वाशीरनामहामाया चिद्धेद्व च ननाव च ॥ 30 ॥

The Asura then began to shoot sharp arrows after arrows like poisonous snakes. The Mahāmāyā, by Her own arrows, cut off those of Her enemy and began to shout loudly.

तयोः परस्यं मुव्दे बभव तुमुलं नृव ।

बाणशक्तिपदाप्रातैमुसलैस्तोमरैस्तथा ॥ 31 ॥

The fight then raged furiously, when both parties began to use arrows, Śaktis, clubs, Mūsalas, and Tomaras.

राणभूमौ तदा जाता कृदीरोयवहा नदी ।

पतितानि तदा तीर शिरोसि प्रवभुस्तदा ॥ 32 ॥

यथा सन्तरणायीय यमिकंकनायकैः ।

तृबीकतानि नीतानि नविशिषाणरैभूदा ॥ 33 ॥

Blood began to flow in the battle-field in torrents like rivers and on the banks of that river of blood, were seen the severed heads of the dead bodies which looked like so many hollow shells of gourds, as if kept there by the attendant of the god of Death, for their swimming purposes.

राषभमितदा घोरा बभूवातीत दुर्गा ।

शरीरै: पतितैभूमी खाद्यानेर्कृकादिधि: ॥ 34 ॥

गोपायुसारमेवाङ्ग काकाः कंका अयोगुपातः ।

गृधा: ग्रेशगांख खाद्यान्ति शरीरागि दुरायान् ॥ 35 ॥

The battle-field, then, became very dreadful and impassable; at some places dead bodies are lying; wolves were feeding on their flesh; at other places were seen jackals, dogs, herons, crows, vultures, eagles, and other voracious birds and beasts and iron-tipped arrows, eating the dead bodies of those wicked demons.

वबी वायुश दुर्निधो मुनीनां देहसंगतः ।

अभूतिकतिकताश्दः खानां पतभक्षिणाम् ॥ 36 ॥

Air began to emit an offensive smell, because of its contact with these corpses; and there were heard the heart-rending sounds of various carnivorous birds and animals.

तदा चुकोप दुर्भाला दुर्भुंकः कालभौहितः ।

देवामुवाच गर्येंण कृत्वा चोर्यकेंरं शुभम् ॥ 37 ॥

Then the wicked Durmukha began, as if inspired by the god of Death, to address the Devi angrily and arrogantly with his right hand raised up before Her.

गच्छ चाधिड हनियाथि त्वामश्रिव सुवातिलेशे ।

दैत्य वा भग वामोक महिषं मदार्वितम् ॥ 38 ॥

"Your brain has become perverted; fly away just now or I will send you unto death, or you

Šrimaddevībhāgavatam

better accept the proud Mahiṣa, the lord of the Daityas, as your husband.

देव्युवाच

आसत्रमरण: कामं प्रलपयव्य महोहितः ।

अष्टिव त्वं हनिष्यामि ययावयं बाक्लोहतः ॥ 39 ॥

The Devi said: “O Villain! I see your death at hand this very day; therefore you are deluded and therefore raving like a mad man. I will kill you today like Bāskala.”

गच्छ वा तिछ वा मंद मरणं यदि रोचते ।

हूलां तां वैविध्यामि वालिंगं महीपीतुन्ति ॥ 40 ॥

O Stupid! Better fly away; or if you prefer death, then wait; I will slay you first; then the dull Mahisa, the son of she buffalo".

तच्छुला वचनं तस्य दुर्भुवो मतुमुक्तः ।

भुमोच बाणवहिं तु चरिष्ठकां प्रति दारुणम् ॥ 4 1 ॥

Hearing thus, Durmukha, as if prompted by Death, hurled dreadful arrows on the Devi.

सार्थि तां तस्या ईल्चा बाणवहिं श्रिं: श्रैः ।

जयान टानवं कूदा चूर्वे जूदधरे यथा ॥ 42 ॥

तयोः परस्यरं युद्धे सझातं चातिककर्णम् ।

Instantly the Devi, too, cut off all his arrows and, infuriated with anger, pierced the Dānava by sharpened arrows as Indra had pierced Vītrāsura before. The fight then turned out very dreadful.

भयदं कातागणं च शूगाणं बलवधीनम् ॥ 43 ॥

देवी चिक्छेट तस्या धनुस्य करे चित्यान् ।

तथेव पड़ाधिबाणीर्बभघ्र तथमुन्तम् ॥ 44 ॥

O King! Weak persons become very afraid and strong ones become very excited. Instantly the Devi cut off the Asura's bow and broke his chariot by five arrows.

रधे भने महाबाहुः पदातिवृभुंखस्तदा ।

गदा गृहीता दुर्भारं जगाम चधिडको प्रति ॥ 4 5 ॥

On seeing his chariot broken, the powerful Durmukha attacked on foot the Devi with his club, very hard to overcome.

चक्रास स गद्याघतं सिंहमौली महावतात् ।

न चचाल हौरे: स्थानाताविहेतप्रेम महावत: ॥ 46 ॥

He knocked at the head of the lion with that club with great force; but the powerful lion did not become unsteady, though so very hard hit.

अधिकां ते समातेकय गतृपाणी पुरः स्थितम् ।

खडूनेन शितापोरण शिराकुळेद मौलितम् ॥ 47 ॥

Seeing the demon thus standing before Her, the goddess Ambikā cut down his head by her sharpened axe.

छिडे च मतके भूमी पगत दुर्मुखो मृतः ।

जयगर्भं तदा चकुम्पित्त निजांर भृगम् ॥ 48 ॥

On his head being thus severed, Durmukha fell down dead on the field. The band of Immortals, then, loudly shouted “Victory to the Devi.”

तृषुवुस्तां तदा देवीं दुमुंखे नितर्धेमतः ।

पुष्पवृष्टि तथा चक्रवैर्याद्ध नभःस्थितः ॥ ४ १ ॥

When Durmukha was slain, the Immortals from the celestial space began to chant praises and hymns to the Devi, showered down flowers on Her head and gave shouts of “Victory to the Devi.”

त्रिष्य: सिद्धान्यर्वर्न: सिद्धायर्यकित्रनः ।

जद्धुस्तं हतं दृष्ट्वा दानवं रणमतकरः ॥ 5० ॥

इति श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणे पत्रमकन्के

त्रयोद्योअयगः ॥ 1 7 3 ॥

The Rsjs, Siddhas, Gandharvas, Vidyādharas, and Kinnaras all became very glad to see the Demon dead on the field.

Here ends the Thirteenth Chapter of the Fifth Book

on the killing of Bāskala and Durmukha in

Śrimaddevithāgavatam, the Mahāpurānam, of

18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER XIV

On the Killing of Tāmra and Ciksura

व्यास उवाच

दूरपुरं निर्त हंभात महिष: क्रोधभूषितः ।

उवाच दानवान्सर्वान्कि जातमिति चास्यकृत् ॥ । ॥

निर्हती दानवीं गृत्री रणो दुरुपुंखाक्कली ।

तन्या तत्परामाशयं पश्यंतु देखचेष्टितम् ॥ 2 ॥

Vyāsa said: Hearing the death news of

Book V Chapter XIV

Durmukha, Mahisāsura became blind with anger and began to utter repeatedly to the Dānavas “O! What is this? What is this?” Alas! That delicate woman has slain in battle the two heroes Durmukha and Bāskala! Lo! Now look at the wonderful workings of the Daiva (Fate).

कालो हि बलवान्क्ता सततं सुखदुःखये ।

नरणां परन्त्याणां पुण्यपापानुयोगतः ॥ 3 ॥

It is the acts virtuous, or otherwise that make men dependent; and the powerful Time awards pleasure or pain accordingly.

निर्ती दानवश्रेष्ठी किं कतल्यमतः परम् ।

ब्रुवंतु मिलिताः सर्वे यदुक्तं कार्यसूब्द्धे ॥ ४ ॥

The two powerful Demons are killed; what are we to do now? You all judge and say what is reasonable at this critical juncture.

एवं बुवर्ति राजेन्द्र महिषे5तिजलात्पिते ।

चिक्षुगाखस्तु सेनानीसमुजाच महारवः ॥ ५ ॥

Vyāsa said: When the powerful Mahisa said thus, his general Cikṣura, the great warrior spoke as follows:

राजर्श हनियाभि का चिन्ता क्षीविहिंसने ।

इत्युक्ता स्वबलैयुक्तः प्रययौ राथसंवृत्तः ॥ 6 ॥

“O King! Why are you so anxious as to take away the life of a delicate woman? I will kill Her, thus saying he departed for battle, mounted on his chariot and accompanied by his own army.

द्वितीयं पाणिगाई तु कृत्वा ताथं महावलम् ।

महता सैन्याधेण प्र्ययनगनं द्विगः ॥ ७ ॥

The powerful Tāmra accompanied him as his attendant; the sky and all the quarters became filled with the clamour of their vast army.

नागारछंतमालोक्य देवी भगवती शिजा ।

चकार शड़ज्याधोष घटानादं महाद्रतुम् ॥ 8 ॥

तत्रमुस्तेन शब्देन ते च सर्वे सुरारयः ।

किमेतेदिति भाषेतो दुबुकुर्षयकामिता: ॥ 9 ॥

The auspicious Devi Bhagavati saw them before Her and She made an extraordinarily wonderful sound with Her conchshell, with Her bow string and with Her great bell. The Asuras

heard that and trembled and fled, speaking amongst each other “What is this?”

चिश्रास्यस्तु तान्टूद्वा पलाययपरायणान् ।

उवाचातीव संकुढ़ः किं भयं चः समापतनम् ॥ 10 ॥

The Cikşurākṣa seeing them turning their backs, told them very angrily “O Dānavas! What fear has now overcome you?

अष्टीवाई सृत्यिषय पालीर्जालां मदोजताम् ।

तिश्छतव भयं त्यक्त्वा दैत्या: समरमूर्धीन ॥ 1 1 ॥

I will slay today this vain woman in the battle with arrows; so you should quit your fear and remain steady in battle.

श्रुत्यक्त्या दानवश्रेक्षायपाणिकार्लानित्यः ।

आपात्य संगरे देदीमित्युबान सातध्यः ॥ 1 2 ॥

Thus saying, the Dānava Chieftain Cikṣura came fearlessly before the Devi with bows and arrows in his hands and, accompanied by his army, angrily spoke thus:

किं गर्जसि विशालासिंध भीयवन्तीताश्रान् ।

नाहं विषीम तत्वांगि शुवा तेउडा विवेष्टितम् ॥ 13 ॥

सीयोदे दूषणं जाला तवैवाकीर्तिसभ्यम् ।

उपेक्षां कुन्ते विचर्भ मदीयं यामतोचने ॥ 14 ॥

"O Thou of large and broad eyes! Why are you roaring to terrify the weak persons! O the Soft-limbed One! I have heard all about your deeds but I am not a bit afraid of You." O One of beautiful eyes! It is a matter of disgrace, rather sin, to kill a woman; knowing this my heart wants to pass over this act, (does not like to do it, if my purpose be served without it).

क्रीणां युद्धं कताईशु तथा हावैशु सुन्दरि ।

न सशैर्विवहितं क्वापि त्वापुरीना कदाचन ॥ 15 ॥

O Beautiful One! The women fight with their side glances and amorous gestures, but I have never heard a woman like your coming to fight with arms and weapons.

पुर्येरपि न योद्धव्यं किं पुनर्निंशिणीतः शरैः ।

भवापुरीनां देहेषु दुर्जोग मातनीतदन् ॥ 16 ॥

Even the delicate flowers, Malati, etc., cause pain on the bodies of beautiful women like you;

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

so it is not advisable to fight against you with flowers even; what to speak of sharpened arrows!

धित्रजन मानुषे लोक क्षात्यभानुजीविनाम् ।

लतितोतः पिंयो देहः कुन्नीभः शितः शैः: ॥ 17 ॥

Fie on those who spend their lives according to the Kṣatriya Dharma! Oh! Who can praise that Dharma which allows this dear body of ours to be pierced by sharpened arrows?

तैल्यभ्यौ: पुथवातैल्णा मिहाङ्गभोजनैः ।

पोषितोज्यं प्रियो देहो घानतीय: पोर्षुभि: ॥ 18 ॥

This dear body is nourished by sweet food, by being smeared with oil, and by smelling the scents of beautiful flowers.

देईं हित्वाजिस्याराभिर्धनभुज्याते नर: ।

प्रिधानं दुःखदं पूर्वै पश्रातिक सुखदं भवेत् ॥ 19 ॥

Ought, then, one to destroy it by arrows from an enemy? Men get their bodies pierced by arrows and then become rich. Now is it possible for the riches to give pleasure afterwards when they caused such pains in the beginning?

स्वभयत्रैव सामोके युद्धमाकांक्षे सये :।

सुखं संभोगैतं व्यक्त्वा के गुणं वैलिसे संगरे ॥ 20 ॥

Even if this be so, fie on those riches! O Beautiful One! It seems you are not intelligent; why have you desired to fight instead of to enjoy sexual pleasure. O beautiful! What merits have you found in the battle that you have chosen this. खद्यात गदायाते भेदं च शिलीमुखैः ।

प्रगान्ते तु संस्कारो गोपायमुपखकर्णम् ॥ 21 ॥

Where you see the action of the axes and spears, striking each other with clubs, and hurling of sharpened arrows and weapons and where, when death comes, jackals come and feed upon the dead bodies, what merits have you been able to trace out in these things!

तस्यैव कविभियूर्ि: कृतं चारीव शंसनम् ।

रगे मृतानां स्व:गतिरत्यक्षितदोस्ति केलतः ॥ 22 ॥

It is only those cunning poets that praise these; they say that those who die in battle go to heaven! O Beautiful! Those sayings are, no doubt, mere flatteries.

तस्मादगच्छ वरातोहे यत्र ने रामते मनः ।

भज वा भूपतिं नारं हवारि सुरमदन् ॥ 23 ॥

Therefore, O Excellent One! Go away anywhere else you like; or accept this king Mahisa, the tormentor of the Devas, as your husband.

एवं बुबाणं ते दैत्यं प्रोवाच जगद्धिका ।

किं भूग भावसे मुंह बुद्दिमानिय चपिछ्तः ॥ 24 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The Dānava Cikṣura speaking thus, the Divine Mother addressed him thus: O Stupid! Why are you speaking false words, having no significance, like a literary man giving out mere words only?

नौतिशास्वं न जानासे विद्यां चावीक्षकों तथा ।

न सेवितात्मव्या वृद्धा न धर्मे मतिरित्त ते ॥ 25 ॥

मूढ़ीसेवापरो यस्मात्ममान्चं मूर्षे एल हि ।

राजधर्म न जानासि किं ब्रवीषि समात्रतः ॥ 26 ॥

You do not know anything of politics, ethics, metaphysics; you serve illiterate and stupid; therefore, you are also a first class illiterate; you do not know what are the royal duties; then what are you speaking before me?

संग्रामे महिषे हत्ता कृत्वा कधिरकर्दमम् ।

यश:सत्मयं स्थिंरं कृत्वा गतिय्यामि यवासुखम् ॥ 27 ॥

देवानां दुःखदातारं दानवं मदगवितम् ।

हनिष्येड़ं दुराचारं युद्धे कृम स्थिते भव ॥ 28 ॥

I will kill that Mahisāsura in battle, make the soil muddy with his blood, thus establish firmly My pillar or Fame and then go happily to My abode. Surely will I slay that vain vicious demon, the tormentor of the Devas. Better fight steadily.

जीवितेच्छाजित चेन्मुद महिष्य तथा तत्व ।

तदा गच्छेतु पातालं दानवाः सर्वे एव ते ॥ 29 ॥

मुभूर्ण यदि यक्षिते युद्धं कुर्वर्ति सत्वतः ।

सर्वानेव वधिष्यामि निशयोःयं माभ्यान् ॥ 30 ॥

O Stupid! Better go to Patāla with all the Dānavas, if you and Mahisa desire to live any longer. And if you like to go unto death, then be ready fight without any delay; I will slay you all; this is My firm resolve.

Book V Chapter XIV

यास उवाच

तच्छुत्वा वचनं तस्य दान्वो बलदार्यितः ।

भुभोच वाणवृष्टि तां घनवृष्टिमिवापगम् ॥ 31 ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! Hearing the Devi's words, the Dānava, proud of his own strength, began to hurl instantly on Her showers of arrows, as if another shower of rain burst upon Her.

चिच्छेट तस्य सा बाणान्वबाणर्पिशितेतत ।

ज्यान तं तथा घोरोराशिवसदीः शरैः ॥ ३२ ॥

The Devi cut off those arrows into pieces by Her sharp arrows and shot at him dreadful arrows like poisonous snakes,

युद्धे परमर्भ तत्र बभूव विस्मयप्रदम ।

गदया घातयामास तं रथाजगदीविका ॥ ३३ ॥

Then their fight became astounding to the public; the Divine Mother, then, struck him with Her club so much that he fell down from his chariot.

मूर्षे प्राप स दुहाया गद्याउभित्तो भूग्ग ।

मुह्त्रदयमात्रं तु रथोपस्य ईवाचलः ॥ 34 ॥

That vicious demon, thus struck by the club, remained senseless near to his chariot for two muhūrtas, fixed like a mountain.

तं तथा मृक्तिं दृष्ट्या तापः परवलातः ।

आजगाम रणे योद्धं चीढ़कां प्रति चापलत ॥ 35 ॥

आगच्छंतु तु तं वीक्ष्य हसनी प्राप चीढ़का ।

एषहीि दानवश्रेष्ठ यमलोकं नयाम्यहम् ॥ 36 ॥

Tāmra, the tormentor of the foes, seeing him thus, could not remain steady and came forward to fight with Candjikā. The Devi, seeing him laughed and said “O Dānava! Come, Come, I will instantly send you unto death.”

किं भवपिद्रुः समावातैरबलैषुः गतायुषेः ।

महिषः किं गृहे घुकः करोति जीवनोषधम् ॥ 37 ॥

Or, what is the use of your coming? You are so weak that you can be called lifeless. What is that stupid Mahisa doing now? Is he thinking out the way to save his life?

किं भवद्धिद्रतैर्यैर्मानपि विफकलः शमः ।

अत्ते महेषे घाये सुरुगजी दुरात्मिन ॥ 38 ॥

You all are too weak; no use in killing you, all my arrows will go in vain, if that wicked Mahisa, the enemy of the gods, be not slain.

तस्मायुं गृहं नाला महिंक्षे प्रेयचित्व ।

पश्येनां सोडपि मन्त्यात यादुर्गी ततुर्गी सिंहात्म। ॥ ३९ ॥

Therefore, do you go to your home and send here your king Mahiṣa. I am staying here in that form in which that wicked one likes very much to see Me.

तापस्तद्धनं शुत्वा बाणवृष्टि चकार है ।

चिडकां प्रति कोपेने कर्णाक्हणसानः ॥ ४ ० ॥

Hearing Her words, Tāmra became very angry and drawing his bow up to his ear, began to hurl arrows after arrows on Candjā Devī.

भगवत्य पितामक्षी समाकृष्य शासनम् ।

बाणानुभोच तरसा हन्तुकामा सुरावित् ॥ 4 1 ॥

The Bhagavatī, too, had her eyes reddened with anger and drawing Her bow began to shoot arrowing quickly at the demon, wishing to kill, as early as possible, the enemy of the gods.

चिद्याग्राओंपि बलवान्मूर्ण तयक्नोचित्य: पुनः।

गृहीत्वा सगरं चापं तस्थी ततस्ममुखः क्षणात् ॥ 4 2 ॥

In the meanwhile, Cikṣura regained his senses, and taking up again his bow in an instant, came before the Devi.

चिद्यात्यस्थ तामस्थ द्वाव्यतिवलोक्तटी ।

युयुधते महावीरी सह देया रणारिणे ॥ 4 3 ॥

Then Cikṣura and Tāmra, the two valiant warriors, began to fight dreadfully with the Devi.

कुविता च महामाया ववर्ष गरसांतिन् ।

चक्रात दानवान्सर्वानाण्डस्ततुच्छदान् ॥ 4 4 ॥

Mahā Māyā then, became very angry and began to hurl arrows after arrows so incessantly that all the armour of all the Dānavas became pierced and were cut down to pieces.

असुरा: क्रीयसम्मुजा बम्बुरु: शरताडिता: ।

चिश्विपुः शरजालानि देर्वी प्रति रुचपात्वि: ॥ 4 5 ॥

The Asuras, thus pierced by arrows, became infuriated with anger and hurled angrily a network of arrows upon the Devi.

बभूते राक्षसास्त्र किंगुका इव पुष्पिणः ।

शिलीमुखक्षतः सर्वे वसते च वने गे ॥ 46 ॥

The Dānavas, thus struck with sharp arrows and filled with cuts and wounds looked like the red Kīmśuka flowers in the spring.

बभूव तुमुलं युद्धं ताप्रेण सह संयुगे ।

विस्मयं परमं जमुदैवा ये प्रेक्षकाः स्थिताः ॥ 47 ॥

The fight then grew so severe between Tāmra and Bhagavati that the seers, the Devas, were all struck with wonder.

तापी मुसलमादाय लोहजं दारुणं दुबम् ।

जघान मस्तके सिंहं जहास च नददं च ॥ 48 ॥

नद्मानं नदा तं तु दूर्ध्वा देवी भ्वाप्तिज्ञा ।

खद्वेगेन शिलाधोग्य शिर्धिश्छेदे सदसा ॥ 49 ॥

Tāmra struck on the head of the lion with his dreadful hard Mūsala (club) made of iron, and laughed and shouted aloud. Seeing him thus vociferating, the Devi became angry and cut off his head by her sharp axes in no time.

छिडे शिरसि तामस्तु विशीर्षो मुसली बली ।

वधाम क्षणमात्रं तु परात तरणमस्तेक १ ५० ॥

The head being thus severed from the body, Tāmra, though headless, for a moment turned round his Mūsala and then fell down on the ground. परितत तामपातोल्क्य चिक्षुराख्यो महावलः ।

खडूरामादाय तस्या दुत्राव चण्डिकां प्रति ॥ 51 ॥

भगवत्यपि ते दूद्वा खडूरापाणिभुपगतात् ।

दानयं पश्चभिवर्गीर्जान तस्या रणे ॥ 52 ॥

The powerful Cikṣura, seeing Tāmra thus falling down, instantly took up his axe and ran

after Candikā. Seeing Cikṣura with axe in his hand, the Bhagavatī quickly shot at him five arrows.

एकेन पत्तितं खडूगं द्वितीयेन तु तत्कर: ।

कणठाच्य मनकं ततय कृतिनतं चापरै: སྐོད་ ། ། ། ། ། ། ། ། ། ། ། ། ། ། ། ། ། །

With one arrow, his axe was cut down, with the second arrow his hands were cut and with the remaining ones his head was severed from his body.

एवं तौ निर्तही कृरौ राक्षसौ रणदुपंदौ ।

भन मैन्ध तयोत्पूर्ण दिश्वु संरसलमानसम् ॥ 54 ॥

Thus when the two cruel! warriors were slain, their soldiers soon fled away in terror in all directions.

देवाधु मृत्रितः सर्वे दृष्टवा तो निर्तीत रोगे ।

पुष्पवृष्टि मुदा चक्रज्वगस्त्वं नभः स्थिताः ॥ 55 ॥

The Devas were exceedingly glad at their downfall and showered gladly flowers from the sky and uttered shouts of Victory to the Devi.

क्रश्यो देयान्यर्वां वैलातः सिद्धाराणाः ।

कचुसे जय देविति चाधिकेति पुनः पुनः ॥ 56 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे पड़ामकरभ्रे

वन्द्रदीशोक्षायः ॥ 14 ॥

The Rşis, Gandharvas, the Vetālas, the Siddhas and Čāranas were all very glad and began to utter repeatedly “O Goddess! Victory, victory be Yours.”

Here ends the Fourteenth Chapter of the Fifth Skandha on the killing of Tāmra and Ciksura in Śrimaddevibhāgavatam, the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsā.

[Unreadable]

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER XV

On the Slaying of Bidjalākṣa and Asilomā

यास उवाच

तौ तया निह्तौ शुत्वा महिषो विस्मयान्वतः ।

प्रेययामास दैतेयांस्तद्धार्थं महात्बलान् ॥ १ ॥

असिलोमपिब्डाताबम्पमुखान्. युद्धुदम्बित् ।

सै-येन महत युक्तान्यायुधान्यपरिच्छिदान् ॥ 2 ॥

ते तत्र ददूर्दैर्वीं सिंहस्योपरि संस्थिताम् ।

अशदाशुभुजां दिव्यां खड्गखेटकधारिणीम् ॥ 3 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing the two Demons slain by the Devi, Mahisāśura became very much amazed and sent the powerful Asilomā and Bidālāksa and the other Dānavas to the battle to kill the Devi. The Dānavas, all very skilled in the art of warfare, marched out for battle, fully equipped with weapons and clad in armour, and

Book V Chapter XV

well attended by vast army. They arrived there and saw the Divine Mother with eighteen hands taking Her stand on a lion, with axes and shields in Her hands.

असिलोमाउगतो गत्वा तामुवाच ह्स्त्रिक्ष

विन्यावन्तः शगान्तो देवीं दैत्यवधोत्तात् ॥ 4 ॥

The calm-tempered Asilomā appeared before the Devi ready to kill the Daityas, saluted Her and smilingly said:

असिलोमोवाच

देवि बूहि वच: सत्यं किमर्थसिंह सुन्दरि ।

आगतासि किम्बवं वा हिंस दैवास्त्रियगमः ॥ 5 ॥

O Devi! Why have You come here? and what for You are killing these faultless Daityas? O Beautiful One! Tell all these to me truly. We will make treaty with you.

कारण कथायदा त्वं त्वया सक्यं करोम्यस् ।

काछ्जं मणिरलातिन भाजपातिन वराशि च ॥ 6 ॥

Take gold, jewels, pearls and any other excellent things that you like and retire from the field as early as passible.

यानीच्छसि वरातोहे गृहीत्वा गच्छ माधिसम् ।

किमर्थं युद्धकामासि दुःखसन्तापवर्धनम् ॥ ७ ॥

Why do you like this warfare tending to increase misery; the wise persons say that it leads to the destruction of all happiness.

कवयवित्त महात्मानो युद्धं सर्वसुखापदम् ।

कोमपेजरीत ते देहे पुर्णापातासहे भुगम् ॥ ८ ॥

Your body is very delicate; it cannot bear the stroke of flowers even; then why are you suffering the stroke of weapons on your bodies; I am very much puzzled to think these things.

क्रियर्ष शाखसंपातानसहीति विरितिमये ।

बागुदीयर फलं शगत्ति: सततं सुखावेयनम् ॥ ९ ॥

See! The cleverness is judged when peace is the result thereof; for it leads always to happiness.

तकिषायं दुःखहुर्ने संग्रामं कर्तिमिष्ठसि ।

संसारोउ सुरुं प्राहुं दुःखं हेयमिति स्थिति: ॥ १० ॥

Then why are you liking to fight which will lead only to pain and suffering. Happiness is only

to be had and pain is to be avoided; this is the rule.

तत्सुंई त्रिविंध प्रोक्ते निव्यानित्यप्रभेदतः।

आमजानं सुखं नित्यमनित्वं भोगतं स्युतम् ॥ १ ॥ ॥

O Devi! That happiness is again of two kinds: Permanent and transitory. The pleasure that comes out of the knowledge of Ātmaṇjāna is permanent and that which is derived from enjoyments is transitory; these who know truly the Veda Śāstra, they avoid this transitory pleasure of enjoyments.

नाशामकं तु तत्यायं वेदग्राहाश्रियैतकैः ।

सौगतानां मतं चेत्वं त्वीकरोयि वरानने ॥ १२ ॥

तथापि यौवनं प्राप्य भुघ्व भोगान्तुसान् ।

पताकीकस्य सन्देहो यदि तेस्ति कुणोदिरि ॥ 13 ॥

If you follow the opinion of the Mimānśasakas and do not believe in the existence of future births, even then you ought not to fight; when you have got this youthful age, you ought to enjoy the excellent pleasures in this world. O One of lean stomach!

स्वर्गभोगपरा नित्यं भव भाभिमि भृतले ।

अनित्य यौवनं दैहे झालेति सुकृतं चोर्त ॥ 14 ॥

And if you doubt in the existence of the other worlds after death, even then you ought to desert from fighting and perform, in this life, such actions as will lead you to the attainment of Heavens.

परोपतपर्नं कार्य वर्जनीयं सदा बुद्धे: ।

असिरोधेन कतिथ्यं धर्मार्थकामसेवन् ॥ १५ ॥

This fully developed womanhood is transient; knowing this do virtuous actions always; the wise ones always avoid tormenting others; thus one ought to perform things not contradictory to Dharma, Artha and Kāma.

तस्मामपि कत्थापि माते धर्मे सदा कुः ।

अपराध विंना दैत्यान्क्मवानारयसेप्रिक्के ॥ 16 ॥

Therefore, O Auspicious One! Do You also things virtuous always. O Mother! Why are you killing these Daityas without any cause? There is, again, the feeling of mercy; the lives again of all are dependent on Truth.

दया धार्मिक्य देहोर्सित सने प्राणा: प्रकीर्तिता:।

तस्माद्या तथा सत्यं रक्षणीयं सदा बुधैः ॥ 17 ॥

कारणं चद सुश्रोगि दानवानां चधे तव ।

Therefore the sages always preserve piety, mercy and Truth. O Beautiful One! Then what is the use in Your killings these Demons? Please say explicitly on this point.

देव्यग्राव

त्वया पृष्ट महाबाहो किमर्थमित्व चागता ॥ 18 ॥

तन्दे समप्रवक्षापि हनने च प्रयोजनम् ।

The Devi said: O Powerful one! Hear why I have come here and why I am killing the Daityas? I answer your question on the above points.

विवरापि सदा दैत्य सर्वलोकेषु सर्वदा ॥ 19 ॥

व्यायायीचे च पृतानां पृथसी साक्षिक्षिपि ।

O Demon! I, though merely a spectator, always go about all over the world, seeing the justices and injustices done by the several souls there.

न मे कदापि भोपोच्छा न लोगेने न च वैहिता ॥ 20 ॥

धार्यार्थ विचाराययर संसारे साधुरक्षणम् ।

Never I possess any desire of enjoyment, nor have I greed for anything, nor have I enmity with any creature.

त्रतमितनुं नियतं पालयामि निजं सदा ॥ 21 ॥

सामुन्ता रक्षणं कार्य हन्तवा येप्रमाधावः ।

Only to preserve the virtue and religion and to keep up the righteous, I roam over the worlds. This is My vow and I always adhere to it. To preserve the good and to put down the evil doers is My duty.

देवरसंक्षणं कार्यमवतारैरेनेकशः ॥ 22 ॥

युगे युगे तानेवाहामवताराभिर्भरिं च ।

Many Avañaras are to take their incarnations, cycles after cycles, to preserve the Vedas; therefore I incarnate Myself in yugas after yugas.

महिषस्तु दुराचारो देवान्वे हृतुमुचतः ॥ 23 ॥

ज्ञात्याई नदायार्थ भोः प्राणात्रिय ताक्षसामुना ।

Now the wicked Mahisa is ready to destroy the Devas; seeing this, I have come hereto kill him.

तं हनिष्ये दुराचारं सुरग्रुं महावलम् ॥ 24 ॥

गच्छ वा तिष कामं ल्व सत्यमेदनुदाहून।

I tell you verily that I will slay that vicious powerful Mahisāsura, the enemy of the gods. Knowing this, you remain a depart, as you desire. बूहि वा ते दुरात्मानं राजानं महिषीस्तुम् ॥ 25 ॥

किमान्यान्येषयस्य स्वयं युद्धे कुरूब ह ।

Or you can go to Mahisa, that impious son of a she-buffalo, and say what is the use in sending other Asuras to the battle; he can come himself and fight.

सन्धिकृत्पमिछास्त सित्त जास्तव मया सह ॥ 26 ॥

सर्वे गछन्तु पालातं वैरं तवक्ता यथासुतम् ।

If your king likes to make a treaty, then let him avoid his enmity with the Devas and go down to the Patāla.

देवद्रव्यं तु यक्स्विवद्भुतं जित्वा रोण सुतम् ॥ 27 ॥

तदाचा यान्तु पातालं प्रकृदो वयं तिहिति ।

Let him return to the Devas whatever he has taken perforce from them and go to the Patāla, where Prahlāda is residing.

व्यास उवाच

तद्धृत्वा वचनं देख्या अस्तिलोमा पुरःस्थिता ॥ 28 ॥

विद्वालासं महावीरं पघ्छ्छ प्रीतिपुर्वेकम् ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing thus the Devi's words, Asilomā asked gladly, before the Devi, the powerful Asura Bidālākshya:

अस्तिलोगोवाच

शुतं तेउढ विडालाक्षयं भवान्यां कवितं च चत् ॥ 29 ॥

एवं गते किं कर्त्वोय विग्रहः सन्धिरेव वा ।

Well, Būjalāksya! You have heard just now all what the Devi has said; now are we to observe treaty for declare war. What are we to do under the circumstances?

विश्वालाक्य उवाच

न सन्धिकामोर्स नृयोर्भिमानी

युढ़े न मृत्युं निवर्त हि जान्न ।

दृष्टा हातान्येरत तथाउस्मादैवं

है कॉंसितक्रमितुं समर्धेः ॥ 30 ॥

Book V Chapter XV

Bidālāksya said: Our king knows full well that his death will certainly take place in the battle; knowing this, he is not willing to make peace, out of his egoism and vanity. He is seeing before him daily the deaths of the Dānavas and still he has sent us to battle. Who can overcome the destiny?

“दुः:साध्य एवारित्वर् सेवकानां

धर्मे: सदा मानविवर्लितानाम् ।

आज्ञापरणां बशवर्तिकानां

पाझ्ञालिकानामिव सूत्रभेदात् ॥"

गत्वा ककं तस्य पुरस्त्वया च

मयास्पि वक्च्यमिदं कठोरण ॥ 31 ॥

The duty of a servant is a very difficult one; he will have to be always submissive and obedient, without caring the least for his own self-respect; just as the dancing dolls are completely under the hands of the actors and their movements vary according to the pulling of the wires employed in making them dance.

गच्छन्तु पातालमितघ सर्वे

दन्वास्य रन्तानि धर्न सुराणण ।

"प्रियं हि वक्तव्यमसत्येवेन न

च प्रियं स्यादितकृतु चाणितम् ॥

सत्यं प्रियं नो भवतीह कामं

मौनं तते बुद्धिमतां प्रतिश्छिन् ।”

न फल्यावक्येः प्रतिविधानीयो

राजा तु वैरिसिति नीतिशासून् ॥ 32 ॥

How can we then go to our master and say hard words as he would give away to the Devas all the gems and jewels and go down to Pātāla with other Dānavas. One considers it one's duty to speak pleasant words though untrue; true words cannot be beneficial; true and at the same time beneficial words are very rare in this world; at such critical cases, one ought to remain silent.

न नूनं तर मतन्थं हितं वा वकुमाद्वात् ।

ग्रुं बार्थि गते राजा कोयपुको भविष्यति ॥ 33 ॥

Especially heroes ought never to excite their kings by useless words; this is the essence of politics. We should never go and advise our king

with eagerness what is best or to ask advice from him about such things; the king would then certainly be very angry.

इति सच्छित्य कल्लेय युद्ध प्राणस्य संशये ।

स्वामिकार्य परं मत्वा मरणं तृणतस्था ॥ 34 ॥

Therefore we ought to do our duties to the king, even if our lives be at stake. To consider our lives as nothing and to fight for our king are what is best for us.

व्यास उवाच

इति सर्किष्य ती वीरी संस्थिती युद्धतत्परी ।

धनुबाणधारी तत्र सत्रद्वी रथसङ्गती ॥ 35 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus thinking, the two heroes then wore their coats of armour, mounted on their chariots and, with bows and arrows in their hands, became ready for fight.

प्रधाने तु बिडालाश्म: सन दाणानुमोगेश ह ।

असिलोगा स्थितो दूरे प्रेशकः परमाखिति ॥ 36 ॥

First Bādālāksya shot seven arrows; the great warrior Asilomā stood aloof at a distance as a mere witness.

चिछ्छेते तांतधा प्राप्तानीवका खशैर: शगान ।

विद्वालायं त्रिभिबार्णीज्ञान च शिलाहिति: ॥ 37 ॥

The Divine Mother cut off those arrows to pieces with Her arrows, no sooner they reached Her, and then shot at Bādālāksya three arrows sharpened on stone.

ग्राय वाण्याध्यां दैत्य: पपात समारांगे ।

मृहितो5ध ममारगरु दानवो दैवयोगतः ॥ 38 ॥

The demon Bādālāksya fell sensless by these arrows on the battlefields and after a short while died, as if ordained by Fate.

बिडालाशूं हर्ते बृद्धवा रणे शक्तिशाल्यतः ।

असिलोमा धनुष्याणि: संश्वितो युद्धतपर: ॥ 39 ॥

Seeing Bādālakṣya thus dead, Asilomā took up his bows and arrows and came up, for fight. The hero, then, raising his left hand, said briefly, thus:

क्रधै सक्यं करं कृत्ता तानुशच चिन्ते वचः ।

देवि ज्ञानामि मरणं दानवानां दुरात्मनाम् ॥ 40 ॥

तथापि युद्धं कतैलं पराधीनेन वे मया ।

महिषो मन्दयुद्धिक्र त्र नाजाति प्रियाधिके ॥ 4 1 ॥

"O Devi! I know that death is inevitable to the Dānavas; still I am ready to fight; for I am dependent; and Mahiṣa is of very dull intellect; he cannot make any distinction between what is really good and what is merely pleasant.

तद्यो नैव कत्क्यं हितं चैवाधिरमं मा ।

मतैवं वीरधर्मेण शुभं वा.प्यशुभं भवेत ॥ 42 ॥

दैवमेव परं मये धिक्यौकसमयकम् ।

पत्नित दानवास्तूर्ण तव जाणहता भृति ॥ 43 ॥

I will never speak before him unpleasant words, though beneficial. Rather I will sacrifice my life in the battlefield than advise him anything, be that auspicious or inauspicious.

इतुल्बता शाख्यिं स चकार दानवोतमः ।

देवी चिच्छेते तान्वाणीरुपातंति निजानिति ॥ 4 4 ॥

The Dānavas are being killed no sooner they are shot at by your arrows; seeing this I consider Fate superior to all.

अन्यैर्विष्याथ तं तृणमिसलोमानगायूरैः ।

बीक्षिताप्रमरसैधुः कोपपुणान्ना तदा ॥ 4 5 ॥

गुर्णुः दानवः कामं बाणैर्वेब्रतुः किल ।

स्ववुद्धीयधारः स प्रपुस्तः किंगुको यथा ॥ 46 ॥

Prowess does not lead to any success; Fie on one's prowess! Thus saying, the Demon began to shower arrows after arrows on the Devi; the Devi, too, cut them to pieces with Her own arrows before they came to Her; and, becoming angry, soon pierced him with arrows. The Devas witnessed this sight from above.

असिलोमा गदां गुर्वीं लौहीमुचाय्य वेगतः ।

दूराव चीछकां कोपालिंस्ते भूर्टि जघान द ॥ 4७ ॥

The body of the Demon was then covered with cuts and wounds; blood began to flow from them; the Demon consequently began to shine like the jovial Kinśuka tree.

सिंहोपि नखत्थातैस्तं द्वारा भुर्जाते ।

अगणस्य गदायातं कृते तेन बलीयास ॥ 48 ॥

Asilomā then lifted aloft his heavy iron club

and ran after Candikā and hurt the lion on his head with anger.

उत्पल तत्सा दैत्यो गदायाणिः सुदारुणिः ।

सिंहमूर्दिन समाकष्ठ जायन गदायांबिकाम् ॥ 49 ॥

Not caring at all this severe stroke of the club inflicted by that powerful Demon, the lion tore asunder his arms with his claws.

कृतं तेन प्रहारं तु वढ्यस्चा विश्रापणे ।

खदुरेन शिताधोगेश गिरिश्छ्छेद कण्ठतः ॥ 50 ॥

Then that dreadful Demon leapt with club in his hand and got up the shoulder of the lion and hit the Devi very hard.

छिले शिरसि दैलेन्द्र: परपात तरसा शिता।

हाहाकारो महानासीलैन्ये तस्य दुरात्मनः ॥ 51 ॥

O King! The Devi, then, baffled the hit and cut off the Demon's head with Her sharp axe.

जय देवीति देवतानं त्रुहुर्षुर्गवाद्विकवाम ।

देवदुवुभयो नेतुर्गुश्र नृप किकराः ॥ 52 ॥

The head being thus severed, the Demon was thrown on the ground with great force; seeing this, a general cry of distress arose among his soldiers.

मित्तो दानवी वीध्य पतितो च राजांगो ।

निह्ता: सैनिका: सर्वे तत्र केसरीणा बलात् ॥ 53 ॥

The Devas shouted aloud “Victory to the Devi” and chanted hymns to Her. The drums of the Devas resounded and the Gandharvas began to dance in great joy.

भक्षिताक्ष तथा केचित्रिज्ञेशं तरुणं कृमम् ।

भन्ना: केचित्रता मन्दा महिंधं प्रति दुःखिता ॥ 54 ॥

Seeing the two Demons thus lying dead on the battlefield, the lion killed some of the remaining forces by his sheer strength and ate up others, and made the battle field void of any persons. Some fled away in great distress to Mahisāsura.

चुकुगू कनुदृष्टि जाहि जाहिति भाषणैः ।

असिलोमविद्धालाख्यौ निर्हती नृपसत्तम ॥ 5 5 ॥

अन्ये ये सैनिका राजन्सिद्धेन भक्षिताश्रु ते ।

एवं बुबन्तो राजानं तदा चक्रकु शेजम्मू ॥ 56 ॥

The fugitives began to cry aloud “Save us, save us” and said, “O King! Asilomā and Bīdalākṣya

Book V Chapter XVI

are both slain; and those soldiers that remained were eaten up by the lion." Thus they told and plunged the King in an ocean of dire distress.

तच्छुत्वा वचनं तेषां महिषो रुर्मनास्तदा ।

वधूव चित्ताकृलितो विमान दुःखसंयुतः ॥ 57 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे पश्रमकक्रचे

पडवशो3ध्याः: 11 15 11

Hearing their words, Mahisa became absent minded through pain and grief and began to think over the matter with great anxiety.

Here ends the Filteenth Chapter of the Fifth Book on the slaying of Bídalākṣa and Asilomā in

Srimaddevithbagavatam, the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter XVI

CHAPTER XVI

On the Conversation Between the Devi and Mahisa

व्यास उवाच

तेषां तद्वचनं शुत्वा क्रोधयुक्तो नराधिपः ।

दारूकं प्रह तत्सा रथमानय मेउद्धतुम्म ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing those words, the King Mahisa in anger addressed the charioteer Darūka: “Bring over my chariot quickly.

सहस्वखरसंयुक्ते पताकाध्वजभूषितम् ।

आयुधै: संसुतं शुधं सुचकं चाकुबक्रम ॥ २ ॥

That chariot is drawn by one thousand excellent horses, is bedecked with banners flags, and ensigns, is furnished with various arms and weapons, and is endowed with good wheels of a white colour, and beautiful poles in which the yoke is fixed.”

सूतोर्धि रथमानीर तमुवाच रत्यान्चितः ।

राजत्रवोउभ्यानीते हारि तिहिति भूषितः ॥ ३ ॥

The charioteer brought the chariot instantly and duly informed the king “O King! I have got the chariot ready at your door, your beautiful chariot, bedecked with beautiful carpets and various arms and weapons.”

सर्वायुधसमयुक्तो वरास्तरणसंयुक्तः ।

आनीर्ति तं रवं जात्वा दानवेंत्रे महावक्तः ॥ 4 ॥

मानुषं देहामस्थाय संग्रामं गन्तुमुढ़तः ।

विवायं मतसा चेति देवी मां प्रेक्ष्य दुर्भुंबम् ॥ 5 ॥

श्रूष्टिभं महिंचं नूनं विमता सा भविष्यत् ।

Hearing that the chariot had been brought, Mahīsa thought, the Devi might not care him, seen him ugly faced with a pair of horns and therefore

decided to assume a human shape and then go to the battle.

नारीणा च प्रिंयं रूपं तथा चातुर्यमित्यपि ॥ 6 ॥

तस्मादूर्षे च चातुर्यं कृत्वा यास्यामि तां प्रति ।

The beauty and cleverness are the delights of women; therefore I will go before Her, with a beautiful body and with all the cleverness and dexterities.

यथा मां दीक्ष्य सा बाला प्रेमयुक्ता भविष्यति ॥ 7 ॥

मामपि च तदैव स्थान्युखं नान्यस्करणतः ।

For I will never be delighted with anything but that woman looking at me with fondness and becoming passionately attached to me.

इति संचित्य मनसा दानवेत्रो महाकानः ॥ 8 ॥

त्यक्तन्त नात्राहिंरं कर्णे बधून पुरुषः शुभः ।

Thus thinking, the powerful King of the Demons quitted the buffalo appearance and assumed a beautiful human shape.

सर्वायुधधरः श्रीमांशुकापूर्णपृषितः ॥ 9 ॥

विद्यार्थरर: कान्त: पुरुषबाण इवारपर: ।

He put on beautiful ornaments, armpilates, etc., and wore divine clothes and had garlands on his neck and thus shone like a second Kandarpa, the god of Love.

रथोपथितः केयुरस्वर्यी बाणधन्धर्धः ॥ १० ॥

सेनापरिवृत्तो देईी जगाम मदागर्वितः ।

Taking, then, all the arrows and weapons, he mounted on the chariot, and, attended by his army, went to the Devi, elated with power and vanity.

मनोजं रूपमास्थाय मानिनीनां मनोहाम् ॥ १ १ ॥

तमागतं समालोक्य दैत्यनामधिरं नतं ।

बहुधिः संवृतं वीरैदेंकी शकुमवादत् ॥ १ २ ॥

The Devi blew Her conchshell when She saw Mahisāsura, the lord of the Dānavas, come before Her with a handsome appearance, tending to captivate the minds of mistresses and surrounded by many powerful and valiant warriors.

स शकुनिन्दं शूला जनविस्यवाकरम् ।

समीयमेवत देल्यातु तान्यावच हास्विक १ १ ३ १

देवि संसारचक्रेश्मवर्तीमानो जनः किल ।

नरो वास्य तथा नारी सुखं वांछिति सर्वथा ॥ १४ ॥

The King of the Demons heard the blow of the conchshell, wondrous to all, came up before the Devi and smilingly spoke to Her thus: O Devi! Whatever person there exists in this world, this wheel of Saṃsāra (the eternal round of births and deaths), be he or she a man or a woman, everyone always hankers after pleasure or happiness.

सुखं संयोगेनं नृणां नासंयोगे भवेदित्र ।

संयोगो बढुधा भिवस्तान्ब्यीनि श्युपाव है ॥ 15 ॥

And that pleasure is derived in this world by the combination of persons with each other; never is it seen where this combination is absent.

भेदानुभूगितितृत्यान्वभावोल्याननेकशः ।

तत्र प्रीतिभवानादौ कंश्वामि यथागति ॥ 16 ॥

Again this combination is of various kinds; I will mention them; Hear. Union is of various kinds according as it arises out of affection or out of natural consequences. Of these, I will now speak of unions coming out of affection, as far as my understanding goes.

भातापिक्षेस्तु पुत्रेय संयोगस्तुभम: स्मृतः ।

श्रानुभांत्र तथा योगः कारणान्याधयो मतः ॥ 17 ॥

The union that comes between father, mother and their sons arises out of affection; it is therefore good. The union between brother and brother is middling, for mutual interests of give and take are there between the two.

उत्तमस्य सुखत्यैव दातुल्यातुतः स्मृतः ।

तस्मादत्यसुखत्यैव प्रदातुराच्च मध्यमः ॥ १ ८ ॥

In fact, that union is considered as excellent which leads to happiness of the best sort and that union which leads to lesser happiness is known as mediocre.

नाविकानां तु संयोगः स्मृतः स्थापाविकी बुधैः ।

विविधायतृतिचानां प्रसङूरपितर्किनात् ॥ 19 ॥

The union amongst the sailors, coming from distant lands, is known as natural. They come on various errands concerning their varied interests.

अत्यल्युखदानुत्वाकनिष्ठोउयं स्मृते बुधैः ।

अत्युत्तमसृ संयोगः संसारे सुखुःखदः ॥ 20 ॥

This combination, because it offers the least amount of happiness, is considered as worst. The best union leads in this world to best happiness.

नारीपुरुषयोः काने समानवयसोः सदा ।

संयोगे च: समाध्यातः स एवात्युभम: सूतः ॥ 21 ॥

O Beloved! The constant union of men and women of the same age is considered as par excellence; for it gives happiness of the very best sort.

अत्यनुभमसुख्यैव दातृत्वास तथायित्यः ।

चातुर्वीरूपवेषाठीः कुलशीरिनगुणैस्वा ॥ 22 ॥

Both the parties, men and women, are elevated when they want to excel each other in their family connections, qualities, beauty, cleverness, dress, humility and propriety of conduct.

परस्परस्मुतर्कः कक्षते हि परस्यम् ।

तं चेक्कारोषि संयोगे वीरोग च मया सह ॥ 23 ॥

Therefore, O Dear! If you establish with me that conjugal relation, you will get, no doubt, all the excellent happiness.

अत्यनमसुख्यवैय प्राप्ति: स्थाने न संशय: ।

नानाविधानि रूपाणि कापीय संवेख्या दिये ॥ 24 ॥

इंत्रदाय: सुर्गा: सर्वे संभावे विजिता मया ।

रत्नानि यानि दिव्यानि भवनेत्रसिन्यामानुना ॥ 25 ॥

भूंभृव त्वं तानि सर्वांशि यथेथे देवैल ता यथा ।

Specially I will assume different forms at my mere will. All the Divine jewels and precious things that I have acquired after defeating Indra

Book V Chapter XVI

and the other Devas in battle, and others are lying in my palace; you can enjoy all of them as my queen consort or you can make a charity of them as you like.

पूरानी भवाछ त्वं सोरोप्रम तव सुवृदि ॥ 26 ॥

वैर त्वजेउई देवैस्तु तव वाक्यात्र संगयः ।

यथा त्व सुखामानोषि त्वादुः कराविणि वे ॥ 27 ॥

O Beautiful One! I am your servant; consequently, at your word, I will no doubt quit my enmity with the Devas. In short, I will do anything that leads to your pleasure and happiness.

आतापय विशालाहित्र त्वादुः प्रकटीमय ।

चिनं मे तव रूपेण मोहितं चाकभाषिधि ॥ 28 ॥

आतुरोर्सिम वरातोहे प्राप्तसे शरणं किल ।

O Sweet speaking One! O Large-eyed One! My heart is enchanted very much with your beauty; I will do, therefore, as you order me. O One having a broad hip! I am very much distressed; I now take refuge unto You.

प्रपत्रं पात्रि रंभोक कामबाणीः प्रपीडितम् ॥ 29 ॥

धर्माणामुत्तमो धर्मः शरणागतस्थानम् ।

O One having beautiful thighs! I am very much struck with the arrows of Cupid, and I am very much discomforted; therefore, save me. To protect one who has come under one's refuge is the best of all virtues.

त्वदीयोरम्यसितापरिग सेवकोउई कुशोदिर ॥ 30 ॥

मरानांतं चचः सत्वं नान्यथा प्रकतीमयहम् ।

O One of a somewhat whitish body! O One having a slender waist! I will spend the remaining portion of my life in serving you as your obedient servant. Never will I act contrary to your orders to the risk even of my life. Take this as literally true and do accordingly.

पाटी नतोउसि तत्विं गत्वका नानायुघानि ते ॥ 31 ॥

द्वां कुक विजगतालिं तत्पोत्रि काममार्गीः ।

I now throw aside all my weapons before Your feet; O Large eyed! I am very much distressed by the arrows of Cupid; dost Thou therefore shew Thy mercy on me.

जन्मप्रभृति चार्वीपि दैन्यं नाचरितं मया ॥ 32 ॥

बहादारीनीक्षागम्प्य त्वयि तिद्ध्यामवहम् ।

O Beautiful One! Never I showed my weakness to Brahmā and the other Devas; but today I acknowledge that before You. I have defeated Brahmā and others; they are fully acquainted with my prowess in the battle-fields.

चरितं मम जान्तिन रणे बहादाय: सुतः ॥ 33 ॥

सोप्रधृते तत्सोस्मि ममुचं एपय भामिनि ।

But, O Honoured Woman! Though I am so powerful, I now acknowledge myself as your servant. Better look at me and grant your mercy.

व्यास उवाच

इति बुवाणं तं दैत्वं देवी भगवती हिं सा ॥ 34 ॥

प्रहस्य सरित्यं वाक्ययुवाच करवर्गिनी ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Mahisa, the lord of the Daityas, having said so, that beautiful Bhagavati laughed loudly and spoke smiling:

देवयुवाच

नाहं पुरुविध्छामि परमं पुरुचं विजा ॥ 35 ॥

तस्य चेच्छास्महं दैत्य सुवामि सकर्तं जगत् ।

स मां पर्यति विश्वाता तस्यहं प्रकृति: शिवा ॥ 36 ॥

The Devi said: I do not desire any other body than the Supreme One! O Demon! I am His Willpower; I therefore create all these worlds. I am His Śivā (auspicious) Prakṛti (Nature); That Universal Soul is seeing Mc.

तसासीध्यवगादेव चैतन्यं मायि शाशून् ।

जड़ाई तस्य संयोगातप्रभावीम सचेतना ॥ 37 ॥

अयक्रान्तस्य सारिष्याद्वसश्रेतना यथा ।

न प्राप्यसुखवांछा मे कदाचिद्वि जायते ॥ 38 ॥

It is owing to His proximity that I am appearing as the Eternal Consciousness, manifesting Itself as this Cosmos. As irons move owing to the proximity of magents, I, too, though inert, owing to His proximity, work consciously.

मूक्रीत्यासि मन्दामन् वस्त्रीसंगं चिकीर्मिति ।

नरस्य बक्यनाथीय मृंखला कथी प्रक्रांतिति ॥ 39 ॥

I do not desire to enjoy the ordinary pleasures;

Śrimaddevibhāgavaam

you are very dull and stupid; there is no doubt in this, when you desire sexual union. For women are considered as chains to hold men in bondage.

लौहकद्धोरि मुच्येत क्रीबढ़ो नैव मुच्येत ।

किमिष्ठसि च मन्तात्मनुसागारस्य सेवनम् ॥ 40 ॥

Men bound up by iron chains can obtain freedom at any time, but when they are fastened by woman, they can never obtain freedom. O Stupid! You now want to serve the source of urine, etc.

गर्भ कुरु सुखाय त्वं शमासुख्यावयसि ।

नारीसई महुःखं जानिके त्वं विमुहासि ॥ 4 1 ॥

Take refuge under Peace; peace will lead you to happiness. Great pain arises from connection with women; you know this; then why are you deluded?

त्वज वैरं सुर्ः: सार्थं यथैंह विचारवानी ।

पातालं गच्छ वा कामं जीवितेच्छा यद्यस्ते ॥ 4 2 ॥

अथवा कुरं स्थांगं बलवत्यासि संग्रतम् ।

Better avoid your enmity with the Devas and roam over the world anywhere you like. Or, if you desire to live, go to Pātāla; or fight with Me. Know this for certain that I am stronger than you.

प्रेपितानई सुरैः सर्वस्वल नागाया दानव ~.43 ||

सत्यं वर्धायिं येनाच तस्या वचनसीहदम् ।

O Dānava! The Devas collected have sent Me here; I tell you this very truly; I am satisfied with you by your words of friendship; therefore dost thou fly away while you are living.

दर्शितं तेन तूषात्सि जीवनाल्य यथासुखम् ॥ 44 ॥

सतां सतपदीं भैत्री तेन मुंचामि जीवित･।

See! When words are uttered seven times amongst each other, friendship is established between saints.

मरणेच्छाशित चेषुद्धं कुर वीर यथासुखम् ॥ 45 ॥

हनिष्यामि महाबाहो त्यामहं नात्र संशयः ।

That has been done so amongst us; so there is friendship now between you and me; I won't take away your life. O hero! If you desire to die, fight gladly; O powerful one! I will, no doubt, kill you.

व्यास उवाच

इति तस्या वचः शुत्वा दानवः काममोहितः ॥ 4 6 ॥

उवाच शल्भाया वाचा मधुरं वचनं ततः ।

विघेभ्यं वरारोहे त्वां प्रहर्तुं वरानने ॥ 47 ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! Hearing the Bhagavati's words, the Dānava, deluded by passion, began to speak in beautiful sweet words: O Beautiful One! Your body and the several parts thereof are very delicate and beautiful. A mere sight of such a lady make one enchanted.

कोमलां चास्त्रवर्गी नारी नार्विमोहिन् ।

जित्वा हरित्याईश लोकपालांश सर्वेशः ॥ 48 ॥

किं तव्या सह युद्धं मे युक्तं कमललोचने ।

Therefore, O Beautiful faced one! I fear very much to strike against your body. O Lotus-eyed One! I have subjugated Hari, Hara, the Lokapālas and the several other Devatās; I therefore ask whether it is proper for me to fight with you!

रोचते यदि चार्विंग विवाहं कुक मां पत्र ॥ 49 ॥

नोद्रहच्छ ययेहे ते ईंगे यस्मात्समागत ।

O Fair one! If you like, you marry and worship me, or you can return to your desired place whence you have come.

नाहं त्वां ग्रहर्थियां यतो मेत्री कृता त्वया ॥ 50 ॥

हितभुक्ते शुभं वाक्यं तस्मादुपच्छ यथासुद्यम् ।

You have declared friendship with me; I therefore do not like to strike any weapons on you. I have now spoken for your good and welfare. You can gladly go away.

का शोभा मे भवेदने ह्वा तां चालोरोचान् ॥ 51 ॥

स्वीहता बालहना च बहाहता दुरत्यया ।

O beautiful one! You are a fair woman with beautiful eyes; what fame shall I earn by killing you! O One of slender waist! Murdering a woman, a child, and a Brāhmin certainly makes the murderer liable to suffer the consequences thereof.

गृहीता त्वां गृहं नूनं गच्छामध्य वरानने ॥ 52 ॥

तथापि मे फलं न स्वादुताद्वीमासुखं कुतः ।

I will certainly carry you to day to my place without killing you. If I use force to you, I will

Book V Chapter XVI

not get happiness; for, in such cases, the application of force leads to no happiness.

प्रद्वीमि सुकौरि त्वां तिवनावतन्ते यतः ॥ 53 ॥

पुरुषस्य सुखं न स्यातुते कानतामुखांबुजात् ।

O One having good hairs! I salute before you and speak that a man can not be happy without the lotus face of a woman; similarly a woman cannot be happy without a man's lotus face.

तत्थेव हि नारीगां तत्समाच पुरुधं विज्ञा ॥ 54 ॥

संयोगे सुखबम्पूतिविधोगे चुःखमम्बतः ।

Where comes off the good combination between these two, then the highest pitch of happiness is conceived and pain arises on the disjunction thereof.

कान्तात्सि रूपसम्पत्रा सर्वीभरणभूषिता ॥ 55 ॥

चातुर्व त्वयि किं नास्ति यतो मां न भवस्यहो ।

True that you are well decked with ornaments all over your body but you seem wanting in cleverness; for you are not worshipping me. Who has advised you to renounce enjoyments?

तवोपविद्धे केनेवं भोपानां परिवर्जन् ॥ 56 ॥

वढित्राउसि प्रियालये वैशिण केनिविर्चह ।

मुख्यग्रह्मियमं कानते कुरु कार्य सुशोभनम् ॥ 57 ॥

सुखं तव ममापि स्यादिषहि विहिते किल ।

O Sweet speaking One! If this be true; then surely he is your enemy; he has deceived you. O Dear! Leave your this stubbornness and marry me; both of us shall then be happy. Visnu shines well with Kamalā, Brahmā looks splendid with Savitri, Rudra is well associated with Pārvatī and Indra with Saṃi, so I will shine well with you; there is no doubt in this.

विष्यानुक्स्या सहाप्राणित सावित्रा च सहासम्भः ॥ 58 ॥

रुद्रो धाति च पार्वील्या गश्या शतमखतस्तथा ।

का नारी पतितीना च सुखं प्रानोति शाश्रन् ॥ 59 ॥

येन त्वमसियमपार्थिति न करोषि परिते शुभम् ।

कामः क्वाता गतः कान्ते यस्वां बाणी: सुकोमपेः। 60 ।

मादनै: पञ्धिः: कामं न ताउविते मंदपृधि: ।

No woman can ever be happy without any good husband. And why are you not then, acknowledging me your husband even when you have got him. O Beloved! Where is now that Cupid of dull intellect? Why is he not troubling you with his maddening delicate five arrows?

मन्येउहमिव कामोपि दरावांस्वपि सुन्दरि ॥ 6 1 ॥

अबलेतीच म मवानाने न प्रेयाति मारणान्न ।

मनोभवस्य वैरं वा किमण्यस्ति मया सह ॥ 6 2 ॥

तेन च व्यव्यातालिंक न पुब्जति शिलीमुखान् ।

अथवा मेरुहितैर्वैवरिति उसौ कृपयज्ञः ॥ 6 3 ॥

सुखविद्यर्वसिभरनेत न्वयि न प्रहत्यपि ।

O Fair one! I think that Madana (the god of Love) out of his pity to you, seeing that you are very weak is not striking his arrows on you as he has done to me. O One looking askance! Or it may be that I have got some enmity with that Cupid; else why is he not shooting arrows at you? Or my enemies the Devas have advised the God of Love not to dart his arrows on you.

त्वक्ता में मृगशाराविष्ठ पढ़ातापार कीधस्यि ॥ 64 ॥

मंदोदरीव तन्त्यिं परित्यज्य रुर्णं नृपम् ।

अनुकूलं परितं पढ़ात्या चकारा शर्डे पतिम् ।

कामातं च यदा जाता मोहेन व्याकुलान्तरा ॥ 65 ॥

इति शीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणो पड़ामकन्क्रे

देवीचिते घोडशोप्रथाय: 11 16 11

O One of slender body! As Mandodari had to marry afterwards, when she became passionate, a hypocrite, and so she had to repent thinking that she had not married before a beautiful auspicious king, so I think, O One, having eyes like the young of a deer! You, too, will have to repent like her if you decline to marry me now.

Here ends the Sixteenth Chapter of the Fifth

Book in the conversation between the Devi

and Mahiṣasura in Śrīmaddevibhāgavatam,

the Mahāpurānam, of 18,000 verses, by

Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

CHAPTER XVII

On Mandorari's Accounts

यास उवाच

इति श्रुत्वा वचस्तस्य देवी पण्ड्छ दानवम् ।

का सा मन्दोदी नारी कॉसौ सत्चो नृसत्चा ॥ १ ॥

गरः को वा गुपः पशुताने वृहि कक्षानकम् ।

वित्तरोग यथा प्राणत दुःखं विनताय पुनः ॥ 2 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing thus, the Devi asked the Dānava “Who is that Mandodari? Who is that king who was not first taken by her? And who is that king whom she married afterwards? And how did she repent afterwards? Describe all these in detail to me.

महिष उवाच

सिंहले नाम ईशोर्स्त विद्यातः पृथिवतले ।

घनपादवर्षयुक्तो धनधान्यस्पूद्धिनान् ॥ 3 ॥

Thus asked by the Devi, Mahisa began to say: "O Devi! There is a place, named Simhala, noted in this earth and decorated with various trees and prosperous with wealth and grain.

चन्द्रसेनाप्रधस्त जाग धर्मपरावणः ।

न्यायवण्डधर: शानतः प्रजायणतन्ततः ॥ 4 ॥

सत्यवादी मुदुः शूरस्तितक्ष्नान्तिसागाः ।

गगस्त्रवित्वसंधर्मौ छनुवेदेस्तिनिष्ठितः ॥ 5 ॥

A virtuous king, named Candrasena, used to reign there; he was calm, peaceful, truthful, heroic charitable, steady, forbearing, well versed in politics, ethics and morals vast as a wide ocean, learned in Sāstras, knowing all forms of religions and much skilled in archery. He was mindful in governing his subjects and he used to punish according to the laws of Justice.

तस्य भार्यां वरातोहा सुन्दरी सुभगा गृधा ।

सदाचारात्रसिपुयुची पतिभक्तिपरायणा ॥ 6 ॥

The king had a beautiful well-qualified wife, very handsome and broad-hipped. She was very much devoted to his husband and always engaged in religious acts and of good conduct.

नाना गुणवती कान्ता सर्वलक्षणसंयुता ।

सुप्रुवे प्रथमे गर्भे पूर्वी सा चातिसुन्दरीम् ॥ 7 ॥

This wife, endowed with all auspicious signs, gave birth to a beautiful daughter in her first delivery.

पिता चातीव सन्तुषः पुर्जी प्राय्य मनोरामम् ।

मन्दोर्धर्तिन मानास्या: पिता चक्रे मुद्रावितः ॥ 8 ॥

The King Candrasena, the father, was very pleased to have this beautiful daughter and gladly called her by the name of Mandodari.

इन्टोः कलैल चात्यर्थ बढ़ये सा दिति दिति ।

द्रशवर्षं यदा जाता कन्या चातिमनोहरा ॥ 9 ॥

This daughter began to grow daily like the phases of the Moon. When she grew ten years old, she became very handsome.

वारर्थं नृपतिभ्रन्तमावग च दिति दिति ।

मढ्रेलाप्राधि: शूर: सुधनाना पापाधिव: ॥ 10 ॥

तस्य पुत्रोतिभेधारी कंकुर्णीशोतिविषयुतः ।

बाहणी: कवितो गोरे संकुतोज्ञाय रा: शुभः ॥ 11 ॥

सर्वलक्षापमप्र: सर्वविद्धायधारण: ।

राज दृष्टा तदा राजी नाना गुणकती विद्या ॥ 12 ॥

The King now became anxious to have suitable bridegroom and used to think of it everyday. The Brāhmins then told the king that there was a prince named Kambugriva, the intelligent son of the powerful king Sudhanvā of Madra; this prince was endowed with all kingly qualifications and versed in all knowledge and was therefore a fit match for your daughter.

कंडुगीयार कन्यां खरा व्याजीय वरविणीम् ।

सा तु पत्युर्वचः शुचा पूर्णी पण्ड्छ सादरम् ॥ 13 ॥

विवाहं ते पिता कर्तुं कंडुगीयेण वाज्छति ।
