तस्मिड्लेने सदा देवी तिःक्षौति मया भुत्तम् ॥ 20 ॥

स्तुला सम्पूक्तिता सही वांतिज्ञानीश्रव्याति ।

निद्धां घरमं कृत्वा गब्धुर्धं वे हैत्यालयम् ॥ 21 ॥

सुतः सर्वाणि कार्याणि सा दः कार्म विधाप्यति ।

I have heard that the Devi resides always in the Himācala; if you worship and praise and chant hymns to Her, She will certainly grant you your desired boons. Therefore fully decide on this thing and go the Himālayas. O Devas! She will fulfill all your desires and carry out all your intentions.

व्यास उवाच

इति तस्य वचः श्रुत्वा देवतसे प्रव्युपिंगिणि || 22 ॥

हिमालयं महाराज देवीधानपरायणा ।

मायाकीजं हृदा नितयं जपन्तः सर्व एच हि || 23 ||

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing thus his words,

Book V Chapter XXII

the Devas departed to the Himālayas and they became all merged in the devotional worship of the Supreme Goddess and began to meditate constantly in their hearts the Seedmantra of Māyā. (Hrim.)

नमश्रकुमहायायां घबतानामभयप्रदाम् ।

गुरुधुः स्तोजमदेश्व भवत्वा ययातुः ॥ 24 ॥

They bowed down to the Goddess Mahā Māyā, the Discarder of all the fears of Her Bhaktas and began to chant hymns to Her with perfect devotion. नगे देवि विश्रेष्ठशी प्राणनाथे

सदानन्द्रके सुरानन्ददे ते ।

नगे दानवान्तप्रदे मानवानाम-

नेकाध्वे भक्तिभायमखरके ॥ 25 ॥

O Goddess! Salutation to Thee! O Thou, the Lord of the Universe! the Lord of our hearts! Thou art the Everlasting Bliss and the Giver of bliss to the Devas! Salutation to Thee! Thou art the Destroyer of the Dānavas and Thou art the Giver of all desires of human beings.

न ते नामसंख्या न ते रूपमीदृक्तया

कोप्रिय वेदादिदेवादिद्वेषो ।

त्वमेवासि सर्वेषु शक्तिस्वरूपा

प्रवास्धिसंहारकानले सदैव ॥ 26 ॥

स्पतिरलं धृतिरलयं त्वमेवासि

ब्रुद्धिजीर पृष्टिषुर्धी धृति: कान्तिनशान्ती ।

सुविधा सुलुक्ष्मीर्गति: कीतिंमेधे

त्वमेवाति विश्वस्य बीजे पुराणम् ॥ 27 ॥

Thou canst be approached with devotion. Salutation to Thee! O Thou, the Incarnate of all the Devas! Thy names are endless; Thy forms are endless; none can count them. Thou residest always as the Force Incarnate in all the actions, in the Creation, Preservation and Dissolution of Beings, O Goddess! Thou art the Memory, Constancy, Intelligence, Old Age. Thou art the nourishment, contentment; Thou upholdest all; Thou art the beauty, peace, good knowledge, prosperity and happiness, Thou art the Goal, fame, and intellect and Thou art the Eternal Seed unmanifested.

यदा यै: स्वक्षे: क्रोसौह कार्य

सुगणां च तेभ्यो नगामोउष शार्ये ।

भूमा योगनिर्द्रा दया त्व विश्वासा

स्विता सर्वभूतेषु शस्तैः स्वक्षे: ॥ 28 ॥

कृतं कार्यमोदी त्वया यत्सुगणां

हतोउसी महातिर्यांदोहा हयाति: ।

दया ते सदा सर्वदेवेषु दोव

प्रसिद्धा पुराणेषु वेदेषु गीता ॥ 29 ॥

We now bow down to those forms of Thine through which Thou dost serve the purpose of the Devas in this world as we are now in need of peace. Thou art forgiveness and I mecy; Thou art the Yoga Nidrā (a state between sleep and wakefulness); Thou art the kindness and Thou residest in all the beings in so many forms, great and grand, and so very celebrated; O Goddess! Thou hadst already served the cause of the gods in killing our great enemy Mahisāsura, puffed up with vanity. Therefore Thy mercy is well known amongst the gods; what more, Thy mercy is known, since very ancient times and it is narrated in the Vedas.

किम्यास्ति चित्र यद्याचा सुतं

स्वं मुदा पालयेल्योषयेसम्येगे ।

यतस्त्वं जनिजी सुगणां महापा

कुर्वैकचित्ने कायं समगम् ॥ 30 ॥

न वा ते गुणानामिघनां स्वकर्णे

वयं देवि जानीमहे विश्वबन्धो ।

कृपाणत्रमित्येव मत्वा तथा-

उसमान्पेधः स्वा पाहि पातुं समर्थ ॥ 31 ॥

What wonder is there that a mother nourishes gladly her own sons and preserves them carefully! For Thou art the Mother of the Devas; Thou art the great source of help to them; therefore dost Thou fulfill all their desires with Thine whole heart. O Devi! We do not know the limit of Thy qualities nor of Thy forms; O Goddess! Thou art worshipped by the whole Universe. Thou art fully competent to save all from dangers; we are objects of Thy pity; dost Thou save us from our present troubles!

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

<table><tr><td>विना बाणपातीलैंना पृथित्यातैविनाशूलखड्गैशिग शफित्वणे.:।</td></tr><tr><td>रिपूर्हनुभवासि शक्ता विनोदानधापीईलोकोपकाया लीला || 32 ||</td></tr><tr><td>दूरं शाशत नैव जानन्ति मृछान कार्य विना कारणं सम्पयेढ़ा ।</td></tr><tr><td>वयं तकैयामोउनुगानं प्रमाणं</td></tr><tr><td>त्यवेयासि कतोउस्य विश्वधर्य चेति || 33 ||</td></tr></table>

Thou art capable to kill enemies without shooting any arrows, without striking any blows, without hurling any trident, axes, Saktis, clubs, or any other weapons; merely by Thy mere will Thou canst kill; still for sport and for the good of all beings Thou incarnates and fightest for the sake of Lilā. The ignorant persons know such things as birth, death, etc., that this world is not eternal; that no actions can be without any cause; we, therefore, ascertain by reasoning and inference that Thou art the Supreme Cause of this whole Universe.

अत्र: सुधित्ती मुकुन्दोस्तितयं

होर नाशकृदा पुर्णो प्रसिद्धः ।

न किं त्वत्पुस्ताख्यन्ते युगादौ

त्वमेवासि सर्वस्य तैनैव माता ॥ 34 ॥

Brahmā is the Creator, Viṣṇu is the Preserver, and Maheśa is the Destroyer; so it is related in the Purāṇas. Thou again hast given birth to these three Gods in the respective cycles; therefore Thou art the Mother of all; there is no doubt in this.

विभिन्त्वं पुरातिधता देयि दत्ता

त्वया शक्तिज्ञा च तेयः समप्रा ।

त्वया संयुतासे प्रकृवीत

कार्मं जतायालनोत्यनिसंहारमेव ॥ 35 ॥

O Devi! In days of yore, these three Devas worshipped Thee; Thou wert pleased and gavest them all the best powers. Beings thus endowed with Thy powers, they have been able to create, preserve and destroy this Universe beautifully.

ते किं न मन्दयतो यतयो विमुळास्तवं

ये न विश्वजनर्गी समुपाश्यन्ति ।

विद्यां परां सकलकामफलप्रद्यां तां

मुक्तिप्रदां विबुधर्वंदुस्वंदिवांधि. || 3 6 ||

Art they not foolish, though they do Yatis (persons of self-controlled nature), who do not worship the Universal Mother, the Consciousness Incarnate, the Giver of liberation, or Whose feet are worshipped by the Devas, and worshipping Whom, one gets the fruits of all one's desires?

दे बैल्याना: यागपुराना क्षेरा

द्यभास्त एव प्रतिभाति नूनम् ।

ध्यायन्ति न त्वां कमलां च लज्जां

कान्ति स्थिति कौतिंमथापि पुहिष् ॥ 37 ॥

Certainly those Vaṣṇavas, Sauras (worshippers of the Sun) and Pāśupatas (worshippers of Śiva) are foolish braggarts who do not meditate Thee as the embodiment of Kamalā (prosperity), modesty, beauty, continuancy, Fame, nourishment.

हरित्रादिभयथ सैविता

त्वमिदं देववर्तेर्युर्तेल्या ।

भुवि भजन्ति न येडल्यिधो नरा

जननि ते विधिना खलु वर्जिताः ॥ 38 ॥

O Mother! The Ausras, Hari, Hara and other great Devas worship Thee in this world; therefore those mortals are certainly deceived by their Creator that do not worship Thee on the surface of this earth.

जलिङापादपंकजरझनं

जतुरसेन क्रांति ही: स्वयम् ।

त्रिनयनोत्रि भगधरजाधि-

पहूजपरागविद्यालयातयर: ॥ 3911

O Devi! Hari himself serves the lotus feet of Lakṣmī by colouring them (toes and other fingers of the feet) red with lac juice; Hara is very anxious to serve the lotus feet and take the dust thereof of Pārvatī; Lakṣmī and Pārvatī are but Thy part manifestations; therefore to serve them is, in other words to serve Thee.

किमपरस्य नरस्य कवानकैस्तव

पदान्वयुगं न वर्जान्ति के ।

विपततगगगृहाशं द्वां क्षमां

कृताधिया मुनवोर्धि भवन्ति ते ॥ 40 ॥

What to speak of other persons, even those who

Book V Chapter XXII

can discriminate between real and unreal and those who have left their worldly homes and have become dispassionate towards worldly objects, even those Munis worship forgiveness and mercy, that are but Thy parts; therefore who is there in this world that does not serve Thy lotus-feet!

दैव त्वद्धिभवने न जना रता

ये संसारकुपपतिताः पतिताः किलामी ।

ते कुशुस्पलिश्राअधितुयात भवन्ति

दारिद्वदै-यसहिता रहिताः सुखौधेः ॥ 41 ॥

O Devi! Those human begins plunge into the dreadful wells of this Samsāra, the round of birth and death, and are deprived of all pleasures, who do not serve Thy lotus feet. What more can be said than the fact that those fallen beings suffer terribly from poverty, humility, leprosy, headache, and the chronic enlargement of spleen.

वे काषभारहने यवसावहारे

कार्य भवन्ति निपुणा धनवहारीनाः ।

जानीमहुंल्यमतिभभिवर्तीयसेवा

पूर्वे भवे जननि तैनं कृता कदारुषि ॥ 4 2 ॥

O Mother! Those persons are void of any wealth and wife; they are the carriers of loads of wood and collect grass and leaves and show their skill in such acts; they are of little understanding and never they served in their previous births Thy lotus-feet. This we have to know very well within our heart of hearts.

व्यास उवाच

एवं स्तुता सुरैः सर्वैरिक्वा करणार्विता ।

प्रावृद्धिपूव तससा रूपयीवनसर्वुता ॥43 ॥

Vyāsa said: "O King! When all the Devas thus eulogised, instantly the Devi Ambikā, full of youth and beauty appeared there out of mercy.

दिव्यांवराय तेवी दिव्यभूषणभूषिता ।

दिव्यमाल्यसमायुक्ता दिव्यचन्दनचरिता ॥ 44 ॥

जगनोहनतावणया सर्वतक्षणालक्षिता ।

अहितीयवरूपा सा देवानां दर्शनं नता ॥ 45 ॥

जाछ्वयं नतुकामा सा निर्णता निरिश्याद्रात् ।

दिव्यरूपधरा देवी विश्यमोहनमोहनी ॥ 46 ॥

That extraordinary beautiful Bhagavatī, endowed with all auspicious signs, and adorned with the Divine clothing, ornaments, and garlands and sandal paste, etc., appeared before the Devas. Before Whom, even the world enchanter Cupid bows down; with such beautiful, Divine appearance, the Devi emerged from the mountain cave in order to take Her ablutions in the Ganges.

देवान्मृतीपरानाह मेघगम्भीर्या शिरा ।

प्रेमपूर्व सिमतं कृत्वा कोक्रिकलमंजुबातिवि ॥ 47 ॥

That Devi, sweet-voiced like a cuckoo, gladly smiling began to say to the Devas, singing hymns to Her, in a voice deep like that of a rumbling cloud.

देव्यवाच

भो भो: सुवरा: काऊ भविद्धः सुगते भूणम् ।

किमर्थ बूत वः कार्य चिंताविष्ठा: कुतः पुनः ॥ 48 ॥

The Devi said: O Best of Suras! Whom are you praising constantly in this place? What do you want? Why are you so anxious and seem to be so much careworn? Do please tell all this to Me in detail.

व्यास उवाच

तच्छूला भाषितं तस्य मोहिता रूपसम्पदा ।

प्रेमपूर्व हदुल्साहास्तमचुः सुस्सत्तमः ॥ ४९ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The Devas were first enchanted by Her beauty and softness; then, being encouraged by Her sweet words, began to speak with great joy.

देश ऊचुः

देवि सुमस्त्वां विश्रेष्ठि प्रणातःस कृपाणवे ।

पाहि नः सर्वदुःखेष्यः संविन्यान्दैत्यातपितान् ॥ 50 ॥

पुरा त्वया महोद्वी निर्हत्यासुरकेटकम् ।

महिषं नो वरो दन्तः स्मर्तैम्बाई सदापदि ॥ 51 ॥

O Devi! We pray to Thee, O Lord of this Universe! We bow down to Thee. O Thou, the Ocean of mercy! Protect us from all the troubles; we are very much care-worn and tormented by the Daityas. O Great Goddess! In ancient times

Thou didst kill Mahisāsura, the source of troubles to all and then told us to remember Thee whenever any difficultly would arise.

सरगाहैत्वर्जा पौढ़ा नागाधिष्णायमंशगयम् ।

तेन त्वं संस्मृता देवि नृनमस्मामित्रियपि ॥ 5 2 ॥

Then Thou wouldst undoubtedly remove all the troubles arising from the Daityas no sooner we remember Thee. O Devi! We have now remembered Thee for that very reason.

अध: शुभ्भिशुभ्भी द्वावसुरी चोरदर्शनी ।

द्वभजी विनकर्तारिहन-यी पुर्षेः किल ।। 5 3 ।।

At present the two dreadful Asuras Sumbha and Niśumbha have sprung up and are creating great disturbances; and they cannot be killed by any male beings.

रक्तबीजघ्छ बलवांघंडमुपईी तथाज़मृती ।

पूर्तेपट्रीघ देवानां हृत राजयं महावलरेः ॥ 54 ॥

The powerful Raktabrija and Caṇḍa Mūnda and other Asuras united have dispossessed the Devas of their Heavens. Thou alone art our goal and refuge; without Thee there is none other to save us.

गतिरात्य न चास्मार्कं त्वमेवास महावले ।

कुरु कार्य सुराणां वे दुःस्तितानां सुमध्यमे ॥ 55 ॥

Therefore, O Beautiful One! Thou dost do this work for the Devas who are extremely troubled and distressed.

देवास्त्वद्धिष्णजेन निरातः सदैव

ते दानवैरित्वलैर्विषयं सुनिताः ।

तां देवि दु:खराहितां कुक भक्तिपुक्तां

मातत्स्वेव सरणं भव दु:खितानाम् ॥ 56 ॥

O Powerful Devi! The Devas are always at the services of Thy lotus feet; still the very powerful Dānavas are throwing them into dangers; O Mother! Thou art the Preserver of the distressed; therefore dost Thou preserve the Devas, devoted to Thee.

सकलपुवनरशा देवे कावां लवासा प्र

स्वकृतमिति विदित्वा विश्यमेततुयातो ।

जननि जगति पीडां दानवा दपयुक्ता:

स्वकलमदस्मितासे प्रकुर्बनित मातः ॥ 57 ॥

इति श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणे पश्रमकल्भे

द्वाविंशो3थायः ॥ 22 ॥

O Mother! The Dānavas, being very much emboldened by their powers, are creating many havocs on the surface of the Earth; now remembering that, in the beginning of the Yugas, Thou didst create all this Universe, Thou dost now ought to protect all this Universe."

Here ends the Twenty second Chapter of the Fifth Book on the eulogising of the Devi by the Devas in

Śrimaddevībhāgāvatam of 18,000 verses

by Maharsi Veda Vyāsa.

Śrimaddevibbāgavatam

CHAPTER XXIII

On the Prowess of Kauśiki

व्यास उवाच

एवं सुतृता तदा देखी दैवहैः: शुज्ञात्रिकेः ।

स्वर्गरीतरवं रूपं प्रापुर्युं चकार है ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! When the tormented Devas praised thus, the Devi created from Her body another supremely beautiful form.

पार्वेत्यस्तु शरीराई नि:सृता चाधिका तदा ।

कौशिकीति समतेषु ततो कोकेषु पढ़ते ॥ 2 ॥

This created form, the Ambikā Devi, became

known in all the worlds as Kauśiki, as She came out of the physical sheath of the Devi Pārvatī.

निद्यातां तु तस्यां सा पार्वीनी तनुव्यतयात् ।

कृष्णकाप्रध सुस्तात कालिका सा प्रकर्षितिं ॥ ३ ॥

मर्षीवाणं महाधोरा दैत्यानां भयवर्धिनी ।

कालराजीति सा प्रोक्ता सर्वकामफलप्रदा ॥ 4 ॥

When Kauśiki was created out of the body of Pārvatī, the Pārvatī's body became transformed and turned out into a black colour and became

Book V Chapter XXIII

known as Kālīkā. Her terrible black appearance, when beheld, increases the terror even of the Daityas.

अधिकाया: परे हर्षे विरराज मनोहरम् ।

सर्वभूषणसैभुक्ते लावणवगुणसंगुवन् ॥ 5 ॥

O King! This Devi is now become known in this world as Kālarātri, the night of destruction, at the end of the world, identified with Durgā, the Fulfiller of all the desires.

ततोप्रविका तदा देवान्तिवल्याच हु सस्मिता ।

निहन्तु निर्भया युयं हनियाथि सिपूनि ॥ 6 ॥

The Ambikā Devi, then, began to look splendid, decked with various ornaments; Her beautiful form began to look very lovely. The Devi Ambikā then smiled a little and said, “Better be fearless; I will slay just now your enemies.

कार्य वः सर्वेथा कार्य सिद्धिर्याम्यर्थे रो ।

निरुभादी-विध्यामि युयाकं सुखहतेते ॥ 7 ॥

It is My incumbent duty to carry out your purposes; I will therefore slay in battle Nisumbha and others for the sake of your happiness."

इत्युक्त्वा सा तदा देवो सिंहारूका मदोल्कटा ।

कालिकां पार्थत: कृत्वा जगाम नगरे रिपोः ॥ 8 ॥

Thus saying, the Devi Bhagavatī, elated with pride, mounted on lion and, taking Kālākā with Her, entered into the city of Sumbha, the enemy of the gods.

सा गतोपयने तस्थाविक्यका कालिकावित्य।

जगावय कलं तत्र जगन्मोहनमोहनम् ॥ 9 ॥

Ambikā went to a garden adjoining the city accompanied Kālākā, and began to sing in such a sweet melodious tune that enchants even the God of Love, who fascinates the whole world.

भुत्वा तन्यभूरं गानो महोर्षीयुः खगा मृगाः ।

मूदं च परमामापुरमरा गाने स्थिताः ॥ १० ॥

What more can be said than the fact that, hearing that sweet melodious song, the birds and beasts became enchanted; the Devas then began to feel much pleasure from the sky.

तस्मिवसरे तत्र दानवो शूभ्मसेवकी ।

चण्डपुण्डाधिषी धौते रमागणी यदच्छया ॥ 1 1 ॥

आगती ददुपगते तु तौ तदा दिव्यस्विणीम् ।

अधिकां गानसेवुक्तां कालिकां पुतः चित्ताम् ॥ 12 ॥

In the meanwhile Canda, Munga the two dreadful Asuras, and attendants of Śumbha, came out accidentally there on their sportive excursions and saw the beautiful Ambikā Deviś singing and Kālika Deviś sitting before Her.

दृष्टा तां दिव्यल्पां च दानवी दिव्यमात्रिति ।

जमतुस्तरासा पार्शं शुपम्यय नृपस्तम ॥ 13 ॥

O best of Kings! No sooner Gandā, Munḍa saw the extraordinary beauty of the Goddess Bhagavati, than they went at once to Śumbha.

तौ गत्वा तं समासीनं दैत्यानामधिर्य गृहे ।

कंचतुर्मंडुरां वाणीं प्रणाय शिरसा नृपम् ॥ १४ ॥

राजन्हिमालयात्कामं कामिनी काममोहित्री ।

सम्याता सिंहमारूढा सर्वतैक्षणपर्वेतुा ॥ १ ५ ॥

On approaching towards the lord of the Daityas sitting in his room, they bowed down and told thus in a sweet voice:—“O King! Here has come from the Himalayas a woman accidentally, mounted on a lion; Her limbs are, shining with all good signs so much so that even the God of love would be enchanted by Her sight.

नैदुपी देवलोकेंर्जित न नायवंदुपे तथा ।

न दृष्टा न सुता कवापि पुविष्यां प्रपदोत्तमा ॥ 16 ॥

Nowhere, in the Devalokas, the Gandharva Lokas or in this earth can be found such a beautiful lady; we never saw nor heard about such a lady before.

गानं च तादृषी राजकानेति जनराजूनम् ।

भूगपित्रहान्त तत्थर्षं मनुप्रत्यवगोहिताः ॥ 17 ॥

O King! That lady is singing so beautifully and pleasingly to all that even the deer are standing motionless by Her side enchanted, as it were, by Her melodious voice.

ज्ञायतों कस्य पुत्रीयं क्रियांमिह चागत ।

गृह्धतां राजशादूल तव योग्यास्ति कामिनि ॥ 18 ॥

O King! That Lady is fit for you; therefore determine first whose daughter is this lady, what for she has come there and then marry Her.

ज्ञाला नय गृहे भार्यों कुक कल्याणलोचनाम् ।

निश्रिंत नानि संसोने नारी लेवेंबिधा कित १ १९ १ १

Know this as certain that such a beautiful lady is not to be found anywhere in this world. Therefore do you bring Her to your house and marry Her.

देवानां सर्वेल्पानि गृहीतानि त्वया यून ।

कमाओमां वरातोहां प्रगृहासि नृपोत्तम ॥ 20 ॥

O Lord of men! You have acquired all the gems and jewels of the Devas; why not, then, accept this Gem in the form of a lady?

इन्स्ट्रीरातः श्रीमान्यारिजाततक्रस्तथा ।

गृहीतेशः सचमुखास्वया नृप बलातिकल ॥ 21 ॥

O King! You have taken by force the exquisitely beautiful Airāvata elephant of Indra, the Parijāta Tree, the seven faced horse Ucaisāravā, and many other jewels.

विभानं वैधसं दिव्यं माललध्वजसंयुतम् ।

त्वासा उरं तलमृतं तढ़नेन युच चाढ़तम् ॥ 22 ॥

You have acquired by your might the Prince of Jewels, the celestial car of the Creator Brahmā, ensigned by the emblematic Swan.

कुबेरस्य निधि: पचास्वया राजनस्माहतः ।

छंज जलपते: शुभं गृहीति तत्त्वया बलात् ॥ 23 ॥

You have dispossessed Kubera of his treasure of the value of a Padma (one thousand billion) and Varuna, the God of oceans, of his white umbrella.

पाश्राशि निरुभ्यमेन भात्रा तव नृपोतम ।

गृहीतोस्ति हटाकामां वर्णायण जितवच च ॥ 24 ॥

O King! When Varuna was defeated, your brother Niśumbha took perforce his Pāśa weapon.

अलानपत्रज्ञां तृभ्ये मालां जलनिधिद्वैति ।

भयातव महाराज रन्तानि विवधातिन च ॥ 25 ॥

O King! The Great Ocean gave you, out of terror, various jewels and honoured you by presenting a garland of lotuses which never fade away.

मृत्यो: शक्तिर्यमस्यायि एण्डः परमदात्णः ।

त्वया जिल्वा हूतः कामं जिकमव्युदयोंते नृप ॥ 26 ॥

What more can be said than the fact that you have conquered the Death and took away His force and that you have easily conquered Yama, the God of Death and have taken from Him His horrible staff.

कामधेत्रगृहीतास इतंते सागतेढ़वा ।

मेनकाशा बेरो गरसंतल शिक्छित चाधसः ॥ 27 ॥

O King! You have brought that Heavenly cow which came out when then ocean churned; that cow is still with you; what more to say than that Menakā and other Apsarās are under your control. एवं सर्वांशि रन्तानि तवास3न्तानि बतावपि ।

कम्यान गृहाते कान्तारनमेधा वराङून ॥ 28 ॥

Thus you have got by your strength all the jewels. Why, then, are you not taking this exquisitely beautiful lady, the Prince of Jewels, amongst women.

सर्वाणि ते गृहस्याति रन्तानि विश्वादाय ।

अनरया सभविध्यन्ति रत्नभूताति भूपते ॥ 29 ॥

O King! All the jewels in your house, will serve their real purpose, no doubt, then and then only when they will shine with this queen of jewels—this Lady.

विषु लोकेषु दैवेन्ये नेपूर्णी वर्तेते प्रिया ।

तस्मातामानयाण्ये लं कुक भार्यों मनोहारणे ॥ 30 ॥

O Lord of the Daityas! There cannot be seen in all the Trilokas such a Beautiful Lady as this that I have now described before you. Therefore bring this Beautiful Lady quickly and accept Her as your wife.

व्यास उवाच

इति श्रुता त्योबांक्यं मयुं मयुग्राकरण ।

प्रसत्रवदनः ग्राह सुत्रीयं सरिष्ये सरिष्ये। ३ १ १

गच्छ सुग्रीव दृतत्वं कुक कार्य विश्वक्षण ।

वक्तव्यं च तथा तन ययावंधेति कुशोदरी ॥ ३ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing thus the sweet words of Caṇḍa and Munḍa, Śumbha spoke gladly to Sugrīva who was close by: “Go, Sugrīva, do my messenger’s work; you are well skilled in these things. Speak so that the Beautiful Lady of thin waist may come over to me.

Book V Chapter XXIII

उपायौ द्वी प्रयोक्त्यौ कान्तासु सुविचक्षणैः ।

सामदाने इति प्रपुः श्रुत्रसस्कोविदः ॥ ३३ ॥

Those who are well versed in the science of amorous love declare that only two methods are to be adopted by the clever persons towards the female sex: (1) conciliation and gentle words and (2) gifts and presents.

घेरे प्रयुस्थमानेउनि रसाधासस्तु जायते ।

निऋई रसभूँः: स्यातस्मातौ दृषिती बुढ़ेः ॥ 34 ॥

For if the policy of division or sowing dissensions be applied then hypocrisy is shown and that means the improper manifestation of love sentiment; whereas if chastisement be applied then the love sentiment becomes interrupted.

सामदानमुखैवार्वसैः: रतक्षौर्भैर्मधुतेलथा ।

का न याति वशे दूत कामिनी कामपीढिता ॥ 35 ॥

Therefore, the wise have condemned these as corrupt means. O Messenger! Where is that woman who does not come round excited with passion when good and sweet words are spoken to her in accordance with the Sāma and Dāna methods?

व्यास उवाच

सुगीवस्तु वचः श्रुत्वा रुष्णोर्फं कष्टिपयं पटु ।

जगाम तसा तव यमासे जगद्विक्का ॥ ३६ ॥

सोप्रथम्युपुर्षी कानां तिंहस्योपर सैंस्थिता ।

प्रणाय मधुरं वाक्यमुवाच जगद्विक्काम् ॥ 37 ॥

Vyāsa said: Sugriva, hearing the nice skilled words of Śumbha went hurriedly to the spot where existed the Mother of the Universe. He saw the Fair Lady mounted on a lion, saluted Her and spoke gently and sweetly as follows:

दूत उवाच

चरेकि चिद्गशारितः शुभ्मः सर्वानुसुन्दरः ।

त्रैतोक्याधिपतेः शूरः सर्वेचिज्ञजते सूचः ॥ 38 ॥

The messenger said: “O Beautiful One! Śumbha, the enemy of the Gods and the King of all, is beautiful in all respects, the ruler of the three Lokas, a great hero and conqueror of all.

तेनताई प्रेषितः कामं त्वत्सकार्ण महात्मना ।

त्वदुपश्वजपासक्तिश्चेनातिविद्युक्ता ॥ 39 ॥

Hearing your beauty and loveliness, that high-souled monarch is so much attached to you and has become so very passionate that he has sent me to you to express his views.

वचनं तस्य तत्विक्रं श्युणु प्रेमपुर:सर्मः प्राणपत्य यथा प्राह दैत्यानामधिपत्यत्विय ॥ 40 ॥ देवा मया वित्तः सर्वे तैलोक्याधिपतित्वसहम् ।

यजभागगाहं काने गृह्मागीह स्थितः सदा ॥ 41 ॥

O One of delicate limbs! Please hear what that Lord of the Daityas has spoken Thee, after duly saluting to Thee, words full of love and affection towards Thee: O Beloved! I have defeated all the Devas and have thus become the Lord of the three worlds; specially I partake of all the offerings made in sacrificial acts and ceremonies, without moving away from my house.

हतसारा कृता नूनं दीर्भेय स्तवर्जिता ।

यासि स्तानि देवानां तानि चाह्तवानकम् ॥ 42 ॥

I have taken away all the gems, jewels and wealth that belonged to the Devas; consequently the abode of the Gods has become now worthless, on account of all its jewels being carried away.

भोक्ताङ्गं सर्वत्नानां विषु लोकेषु याभिनि ।

वशानुगाः सुरा: सर्वे मम देत्याक्षं मानवाः ॥ 43 ॥

O Fair One! I am now enjoying all the jewels that exist in the Trilokas; so much so that all the Devas, Asuras, and human beings are passing away their times, subservient to Me.

त्वदुगुणै: कर्णमागव प्रविष्य हृदयांतम् ।

त्वद्धीनः कृतः कामे किंकरींस्म क्रोम किम् ॥ 44 ॥

त्वमाजायय रभोते तक्ररोमि वशानुगः ।

दासोड़हं तव चार्वीग रक्ष मां कामबाणत: ॥ 45 ॥

But no sooner Thy qualifications reached my ears Thou hast penetrated into my heart and hast made me completely subservient to Thee; O Fair One! What am I to do now? Whatever Thou commandest, I am ready to do that; verily I am now Thy servant; so Thou ought'st to save me

from the darts of passions. O One having swan-like eyes! I am verily made your captive.

भज मो तंच मरालाशि तवाधीने स्मारुकलम् ।

शैलोक्यसामिनी भूत्वा भूंकुव भोगानुतनाम्। 4 6 11

Specially I am extremely agitated by the arrows of Cupid; therefore dost Thou serve me when Thou wilt be made the Lord of the three worlds and thus enjoy the incomparably excellent things.

तव चात्राकर: कान्ते भवामि मरणाविधि ।

अवधोर्धासि वरारोहे सदैवातुर्मानुषैः ॥ 47 ॥

सदा सीयायसंयुक्ता भविष्यसि वरानने ।

O Beloved! I will remain ever Thy obedient servant up to the last moment of death. O Excellent One! I cannot ever be killed by the Devas, Asuras and human beings. O Fair faced One! Thou wilt be always prosperous and fortunate.

घन ते रमते चित्त तज क्रीडव्य सुन्दरि ॥ 48 ॥

इति तस्य वर्धिश्रते विमुघय मदमन्त्ये ।

वत्क्यं यद्वदेवीपणा तदूब्किं हमूयं चक्न : ॥ 49 ॥

गृष्भाय चंचलापाक्षि तदुब्बीभ्यमारु वै ।

Thou wilt be able to sport anywhere Thou likest. O Devi! Please ponder over words of the Lord of the Daityas in Thy heart and speak out Thy views gladly and with the same sweetness in reply; O Brisk One! I will go immediately to Śumbha and inform him about Thy mind.

व्यास उवाच

तदुद्वावचनं शुत्वा मित्तं कृत्वा सुप्रेणलम् ॥ 50 ॥

तं प्रह मभुतं वाचं देवी देवायंसाधिका ।

Vyāsa said: O King! The Devī, ready to serve the cause of the Gods, heard the messenger's gentle words and replied smiling and sweetly.

शीटेख्याच

जानाम्बई निषुर्णभं च गुर्णभं चातिवलं नृपम् ॥ 51 ॥

जेतारं सर्वदैवानां हेतांरं चैव विविधाम।

Śrī Devi spoke: I know fully well Śumbha and Niśumbha; the King Śumbha is very powerful, the conqueror of all the Devas, and the destroyer of enemies.

राशिं सर्वगुणानां च भोक्तारं सर्वसंयदाम् ॥ 52 ॥

दातारं चातिरुगरं च सुन्दं मन्याकृतिन् ।

He is the repository of all good qualities, the enjoyer of all pleasures, very valorous, charitable and is beautiful, in fact a second Cupid.

ब्रात्रिंशलक्षणीयुक्तमवध्ये सुरामानुषे: ॥ 53 ॥

ज्ञाना समागतान्यमय उद्धकामा महामुगम् ।

He is adorned with thirty-two auspicious signs; particularly he is a hero and cannot be killed by the Devas or human beings.

रत्नं कतनकायाति स्वर्णोपाधिकवुद्धे ॥ 54 ॥

तजाई स्वपति उहुं दूरादेवागतास्थि से ।

O Messenger! Knowing this I have come here to have a look of that great warrior Sumbha. The jewel comes in contact with gold to increase its lustre; so I have come here from afar to see my husband.

दृष्टा मया सुरा: सर्वे मानवा भृति मनादः ॥ 55 ॥

गन्धर्वां राक्षसाश्रान्ये ये चातिभिर्यदर्शनाः ।

सर्वे रुंभभयाद्धोता वेपमाना विचेतसः ॥ 56 ॥

श्रूला शुभगुणानत्र प्रणातस्यवद विद्वस्या ।

On seeing all the Devas, Gandharvas, Rāksasas and the eminent beautiful persons on the earth I have come to know that they are all terror stricken and almost unconscious and shudder at the name of Śumbha. So, on hearing about his abilities, I have now come here to see him.

गच्छ दूत महाभाग कृष्टि शुभ्यं महावलम् ॥ 57 ॥

निर्जीने शल्याग्या वाचा वचनं चवनान्माम ।

त्वां सात्वा बलितां श्रेष्ठ सुवर्णाणं च सुवर्ण ॥ 58 ॥

दातारं गृणेन गृहं सर्वविद्याशिरापदम् ।

जेतारं सर्वदेवानां दक्षे चोरं कुलोतनम् ॥ 59 ॥

भोक्तां सर्वलतानां स्वाधीनं स्वखलोजतम् ।

पतिकामान्यस्मदं सत्यं तव योग्य नाराधि ॥ 60 ॥

O Messenger! O Fortunate One! Better now go back to the great hero Sumbha and speak to him in private the following sweet words of Mine: "That you are foremost amongst the powerful; beautiful of the beautiful, skilled in all the branches of learning, well qualified, charitable, clever, born of a high noble family, energetic, and conqueror

Book V Chapter XXIV

of the Devas; especially, by the sheer force of your arms, you are so much exalted and you now enjoy all the gems and jewels.

स्वेच्छया नगरे तेउत्र समायाता महामते ।

मागसित कारणं किंचिविहावोर हास्पोसेन्म १ ६ १ ॥

Therefore, O King! Knowing your qualifications, I have come truly of my own accord to your city with the desire of getting for Me a husband.

बालभपावदृतं किंडिकृतृतं राजन्मय पुरा ।

क्रीडांत्वा च व्यस्यथापि: सहैकाते वदुच्छया ॥ 62 ॥

स्वदेहबलदर्पेण सखीनीं पुरतो रहः ।

मत्समानक्षल: गुरो रणे मां जेच्यति सुकुद्य ॥ 63 ॥

तं विराधार्यहं कर्मां जात्वा तस्य बताबलम् ।

O High! souled One I am fit for your consort. O Lord of the Daityas! There is a slight hitch in My marriage. It is this: In my early days while I was playing with My comrades, I promised before them privately partly out of childishness and partly out of vanity for bodily strength that I will certainly marry that hero who is powerful like Me and who will defeat Me in battle, thus testing his powers and weaknesses.

जहसुवंचन शुला सख्यो विमितमानसा: ॥ 64 ॥

किमेता कृतं कृरं त्रामदूतमागुं वै ।

My comrades laughed at my words and spoke with wonder “Why has this girl made such an extraordinarily difficult promise?”

तस्माचमपि राजेन्द्र जाला मे हीदुर्ग कलम् ॥ 65 ॥

बिला मां खवलेनावा वार्छितं कुरु घानतः ।

त्वं वा ततानुजो घाता समेल्य स्मरांगणे ।

बिला मां समेरणात्र विवाहं कुक सु-दर ॥ 66 ॥

इति शीमदैवीभागवतं महापुराणे घबमकन्भे

यजीवीगोर्यायाः ॥ 23 ॥

Therefore, O Monarch! Better marry Me and fulfil My desires after knowing My strength and defeating Me in a battle. O Beautiful One in all respects! Better come yourself or your younger Niśumbha and perform the marriage ceremony after defeating Me in the battle field.

Here ends the Twenty-third Chapter in the Fifth Book on the prowess of Kauśiki in Śrimaddevībhāgavatam, the Mahāpurānam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣī Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter XXIV

CHAPTER XXIV

On Dhūmralocana

व्यास उवाच

देल्यासद्धचनं श्रुत्वा से दृतः प्राह विविसतः ।

किं बूढ़े रुचिरणाष्ट्र क्षीस्वभावाद्धि साहास्ता ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The messenger was thunderstruck with Devi's words and said: "O Beautiful Lady! What art Thou speaking? It seems that Thou dost no think on this matter, owing to Thy feminine nature.

इन्हादा चिर्षिता येन देवा दैत्यलाशअउपरे ।

तं कयं समरे देँवे जेतुनिष्छ्वसि पाधिनी ॥ २ ॥

O Devi! Thou art boasting in vain; how canst Thou expect to conquer Śumbha in a battle when he was conquered Indra and other Devas and many other Dānavas?

त्रैतोक्ये तात्पूर्णो नासित यः शुर्णं समरे जयेत् ।

का तवं कमलपत्रासित तस्यागो युधि संग्रतम् ॥ ३ ॥

O Lotus-eyed One! There is no hero in the three worlds that can conquer Sumbha in battle; Thou seemest to be a mere trifle before that King of Demons in a face-to-face fight.

अविरवार्य न चत्कयं चत्वयं क्वाणि सून्दरि ।

बलं स्वपर्योजिता चत्क्यं समयोत्रितम् ॥ 4 ॥

O Fair One! Nowhere ought to be said any words without being thought over; one must weight the one's own and other's might and then speak accordingly.

त्रैतौक्यापित्रितः शृष्यस्तव रूपेण गोहितः ।

त्वां च प्रार्थयेत राजा कुक्र तस्येशितं प्रिये ॥ 5 ॥

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

The King Šumbha, the Lord of the three worlds, enchanted by Thy fascinating beauty, is desirous of Thee; therefore dost Thou fulfill his desires and become his beloved wife.

स्वक्तम मुर्षस्वभारे लं समान्य वचनं मम ।

भज सुभ्यं निर्युभ्यं वा हितमेतद् ब्बीपि ते ॥ 6 ॥

Thou better now abandonest Thy illiterate nature and worshippest Šumbha or Niśumbha; I am speaking for Thy good; so keep my words.

शूज्ञ: सर्वेथा सर्वैं: प्राणिधिः: परथा मुदा ।

सेवनीयो बुद्धिमद्धिन्नानामुनीभे ससः ॥ ७ ॥

The amorous love sentiment is the best of nine prevailing sentiments. Therefore every intelligent being ought to cherish with gladness this amorous feeling.

नागमिष्यसि चेद्राले संकूद्रः पृथिवीपति: ।

अन्यान्ताकान्योथ वलाज्येथिति साम्यान् ॥ ८ ॥

And if Thou, O Weak girl! dost not go to Sumbha, then that Lord of the Earth will become very angry and will order his servants to take Thee perforce before him.

कैशोलाकृष्य ते नूनं दानवा बलदायिताः ।

त्वां निय्थिति वामोक ततसा शुंभसब्धिधी ॥ 9 ॥

O Fair One! Those proud Demons will carry Thee by holding Thy hair before Šumbha; there is no doubt in this.

स्वतलजां रक्ष तत्वंभी साहसं सर्वेथा त्यज ।

मातिता गच्छ ततार्थे मानपात्र यंतोत्रि वे ॥ १० ॥

O thin bodied One! Bener forego Thy boldness in every way and keep Thy self-respect. Thou art the object of respect and admiration and so should go before him.

वव युद्धे निशितेबीण: वव सुखं तिलसृजम् ।

सारासारं परिच्छेध कुक मे वचनं पटु ॥ १ १ ॥

भवं शुभ्यं निषुभयं वा लब्धाजिस परमं शुभम् ।

What difference is there between the fight which makes one's body liable to cuts and wounds by sharp arrows and pleasures that arise out of sexual intercourse! These are like the two opposite poles; therefore judge what is useless and what is

useful and keep my good advice. Thou shalt be exceedingly happy if Thou servest Šumbha or Niśumbha.

देव्यवाच

सत्यं दूत महाभाग प्रवक्तुं निपुणो हासि ॥ १२ ॥

निशुभ्यभूषी जानाभि बलर्वेनीविति धुवम् ।

प्रतिजा मे कृता बाल्याद्यथा सा कर्व भवेत् ॥ १३ ॥

The Devi spoke: 'O Messenger! You are fortunate; you are well trained to speak out truth; I know full well that Śumbha and Niśumbha are strong. Still out of my childish nature, the promise that I made before I cannot undo it.

तस्मादुहि निष्यूभं च गुभ्यं वा बलवततम् ।

विता युद्धं न ने भर्ता भविता कोउपि सीघडान् ॥ 14 ॥

Therefore speak to the powerful Śumbha or Niśambha that none can be my husband simply from his beauty. Without defeating Me in battle no one marry Me.

वित्ता में तरस का कंर रंगहुतु सामघत् ।

युढ़ेच्छया समावातां विद्धि माबलतं चूर ॥ 15 ॥

So conquer Me soon and marry Me as you like. Though of a weaker sex, I have come here to fight; know this as certain.

युढ़ं दैहि समर्थोस सीतभर्म समाचर ।

विशेषि मम गुताव्येन्यातालं भवळ माधिसम् ॥ 16 ॥

Therefore if you be capable, fight and do the duty of a warrior. And if you be terrified by seeing my trident or if you want your life, quit the Heavens and this earth and go down to Pātāla without any delay.

विदिवं च धरा त्यक्वा जीवितेच्छा यद्यति ते

इति दूत बदलु चं नाला स्वपतिमादगत् ॥ 17 ॥

O Messenger! Go just now to your master and tell him sweetly these words. Then that powerful Lord of the Dānavas will judge what ought to be done.

स विचार्यं यथा युक्तं कार्त्थिति महावलः ।

संसारे दूतधार्मोंयं यत्सत्यं भाषणं किल ॥ 18 ॥

ज्ञाने पर्यो च धर्मत्र तथा लं कुम भ चित्रम् ।

O Knower of Dharma! To speak out truth before

Book V Chapter XXIV

an enemy, before one's own master is certainly the duty of a messenger in this world; therefore go quickly and tell him what are verily true.

व्यास उचाच

अथ तद्वचनं शुत्वा नीतिमढ़तसंयुतम् ॥ 19 ॥

हैतुयुक्त प्राणचं च चित्मितः प्रययी ततं ।

गत्वा दैत्यपर्ति तृता चिवाएं च पुनः पुनः ॥ 20 ॥

प्राणय पादयोः प्रद्धः प्रत्युवाच नृचं च तम् ।

राजनीतिमयं वाक्यं यदुपूर्वि प्रियं वचः ॥ 21 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The messenger was quite surprised to hear the bold words, though full of reason and morals, of the Devi, puffed up by the vanity of Her strength and departed. Coming to the Lord of Daityas, the messenger bowed down before his feet and told him sweet words, full of morals, in a very humble way after pondering over and over again what he would say.

दूत उवाच

सत्यं प्रियं च चल्यं तेन चित्तायरो हहाम ।

सत्यं प्रियं च राजेन्द्र चवनं दुर्वर्भ किल ।। 22 ॥

The messenger said: O King! Words, true and the same time sweet, ought to be spoken before one's master; but these are very rare in this world.

अधियं वदतां कामां राजा कुयतेति सर्वथा ।

सायात्कृत: समायाता कस्य वा किंकलाजवलि ॥ 23 ॥

On the other hand, if unpleasant words be spoken, the King gets very angry. So I am very anxious now. O King! Whether that lady is weak or strong, whence has She come, whose wife is She, I have not been able to ascertain all these.

न ज्ञानोगेचं किंचितिकं बलीमि विचेहितम् ।

युद्धकामा मया द्वा गर्यिता कटुपभाषिणी ॥ 24 ॥

How then can I say about Her conduct? But, on seeing that harsh speaking woman, I have come to understand this much that She is exceedingly haughty and has come to fight.

तया यत्कक्षितं स्यन्तकृष्णुव महामते ।

मया वाल्यास्त्रितियं कृता पूर्वं विज्ञोदतः ॥ 25 ॥

O King! You are very intelligent; therefore

judge what ought to be done after hearing only what that lady has told me to speak to you.

सखौनीं पुत्तः काम विद हं प्रति सर्वथा ।

यो मां युद्धे जयेद्धा दर्षे च विधुँतीते में ॥ 26 ॥

तं वरीचायम्हे कामां पर्तिं समवलं किल ।

न मे प्रतिज्ञा मिथ्या सा कर्तेल्या तृपसत्ति ॥ 27 ॥

That Lady said: “In days of childhood, while I was playing, out of my childish nature, I promised before my comrades that I would marry that valiant warrior who would defeat me thoroughly in a battle and thus curb My pride. O best of kings! You are religious; so you ought to make My word turn out false.

तस्याद्धूब्द्वास धर्मत्र जित्वा मां स्ववर्गं कृतः ।

तथेति व्याह्त्रत वाक्यं श्रुलाउंहं सपुपगततः ॥ 28 ॥

Bring Me under your control by defeating Me in a battle." O King! Hearing these words I have returned; now do whatever you like.

यथेच्छसि महाराज तथा कुक्र तव प्रियम् ।

सा युद्धर्व कृतमति: सायुधा सिंहगामिनि ॥ 29 ॥

निश्छला वर्तेत पूय यद्योग्यं तद्धिपयातम् ।

That Lady is determined to fight and is waiting there, firmly mounted on a lion, and with various weapons in Her hands. Now judge and do what is best.

व्यास उवाच

इत्याकपयं चचनस्तस्य सुप्रीयस्य नाराधिः ॥ 30 ॥

पूषच्छ घातरं शूरं समीपस्यं महावलम् ।

Vyāsa said: “O King! Hearing thus the words of Sugrīva, the king Śumbha asked his hero brother Nísumbha who was close by.

गृभ्र उवाच

घातः किमत्र कर्तेल्यं बृहि सत्यं महामते ॥ 31 ॥

नार्यका योदयुकामापत्स समाढ्यवित समानुम् ।

अहं गच्छामि संग्रामे लं वा गच्छ बलान्धितः ॥ 32 ॥

यद्रोचते निर्गुभाधु तत्कर्तेनंच मया किल ।

O Brother! You are intelligent; speak out truly what ought to be done now? The lovely woman is challenging us to fight. Shall I go to fight or you would go with forces? I will do whatever you say.

<table><tr><td>निरुभूम उवाच</td></tr><tr><td>न मया न त्वया वीर गतन्ध्यं रणामूर्पिति ॥ 3 3 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>प्रेयमव्म महागरा त्वितं धूपलोचनम् ।</td></tr><tr><td>स गत्वा तां रोय चित्वा गृहीत्वा चारक्लोतवाग्य ॥ 3 4 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>आगमिष्यति शुभ्योउत्र विवाह: संविद्यितात् ।</td></tr><tr><td>Nisūmbha said: O King! It is not proper that you or I would go to the battle field. Better send Dhūnralocana to the field quickly. Let that hero go there and defeat that beautiful Lady and bring Her here. You can then marry Her.</td></tr></table>

व्यास उवाच

<table><tr><td>तिश्रिया वचस्तस्य शुष्पो धातुः कनीयसः ॥ 35 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>कोपातसंप्रयवामास पार्थस्यं धूप्तोचनम् ।</td></tr></table>

Vyāsa said: Hearing thus his younger brother's words, Śumbhā filled with anger, instantly sent Dhūmralocana who was close by to battle.

शृष्म उवाच

<table><tr><td>धूपतोचन गछासु सैन्येन महता3प्रत्: ॥ 36 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>गृहीतास3नय तां मुर्धा स्ववीयंमदोहिताम्।</td></tr><tr><td>देवे वा दानवो वाउपि मनुष्यो वा महावल: ॥ 37 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>तत्याधिण्रातां प्राप्तो हेत्व्यतस्ता तथा ।</td></tr></table>

Śumbha said: “O Dhuñmalocana! Take a vast army and go at once to the battle-field and bring that stupid Lady, vainly boasting of Her strength. If any Deva, Dānava or any other powerful human being take Her side, kill him instantly.

तत्यार्थवर्तिनी काली हवा संगुहा तां पुन: ॥ 38 ॥

गीधमत्र समागच्छ कृत्वा कार्यमन्तुजम् ।

स्थपीया त्वया साखी मुंडतीं घुद्यागरणा ॥ 39 ॥

Slay Her companion the Goddess Kālī and bring Her too. Do all these responsible duties and return quickly. That Chaste Lady is to be protected by all means. The body of that thin Lady is very delicate; so shoot arrows at Her very carefully and see that they are not sharp.

यत्नेन महता वीर यदुदेहा कृणोदरी ।

तत्सहायश्छ हत्वय ये एंग राश्वाणयाः ॥ 40 ॥

सर्वथा सा न हन्तव्या रक्षणीया प्रयन्ततः ।

But kill those that will help Her with weapons

in their hands. Try your best to protect Her, never to kill Her.

व्यास उवाच

इत्यादिष्टस्तव त्राता तस्या ध्रुपलोचनः ॥ 4 1 ॥

प्रणय युंभं सैन्येन वृतः शीर्धं ययो रणे ।

असाधूनां महस्वाणं घघ्टा तेपां वृतस्तथा ॥ 4 2 ॥

स ददर्श ततो देर्वी रयोघवनसंशिताम् ।

Vyāsa said: “O King! No sooner ordered thus by the king, Dhūmralocana bowed down to the king, and, accompanied by sixty thousand Dānava forces, quickly went to the battlefield and saw there that the Lady was sitting in a beautiful garden.”

दृष्टा तां भुगाशाङ्गी विनयेन सम्वित्तः ॥ 4 3 ॥

उवाच च्चनं शल्बगं हेतुमद्रभूषिव्मा ।

भ्यु तैये महाभागे शुभस्वविद्धरातुः ॥ 4 4 ॥

Seeing that deer-eyed Lady, Dhūmralocana began to address Her with great humility and in sweet words full of reason and goodness. O Devi! O highly Fortunate One! Hear! Śumbha is very much distressed owing to Thy absence.

दूतं प्रेषितवान्यार्थं त्व नीतिथगारः ।

स्वभंभपवीदानः सामपूर्वं लयि स्वयम् ॥ 45 ॥

नेतासगत्य वचः प्रोक्त विषतीतं वराने ।

Lest there be any break in the love sentiments, that King, a wise statesman, sent a messenger with instructions to speak Thee in sweet and suitable terms; but, O fair One! That messenger, on arriving before the King had told all the contrary words.

वचसा तेन मे भर्ता चिन्ताविष्टमान नृप: ॥ 46 ॥

बंभूव रसमार्गे सुंभ: कामविमोहित: ।

O Knower of love sentiments! Hearing thus the messenger's words, my lord Śumbha, sick with love, has become immersed in cares an anxieties. That messenger had not been able to realise the true meaning of Thy words.

सूनेत तेन ने सात हेतुगर्भ वचस्वल ॥ 47 ॥

यो मां जयित संग्रामे यदुक्तं काटिनं वचः ।

O honourable Lady! “The sentence uttered by Thee “He who will conquer me in battle” is full

Book V Chapter XXIV

of deep meanings; he was stupid; hence he could not realise the meaning of the word “battle” intended by Thee.

न ज्ञातसेन संग्रामो ब्रिविधः खलु मानिनि ॥ 4 8 ॥

रतिजोअलासाइज प्रापत्रे देववर्भितः ।

रतिजल्यवि यामोगे शग्रोत्स्मात्र: स्मृतः ॥ 4 9 ॥

सुखदः प्रथमः कानते दुःखद्धारिजः स्मृतः ।

ज्ञानाम्बं वरात्रेे भवत्या मानसं कित् ॥ 5 0 ॥

रतिसंगमभावसे हृदये परिवर्तेत ।

इति जन्मं विदित्या मां तलत्कारां नराधिप: ॥ 51 ॥

प्रेयमायाम सुभोउघ बलेन महान3उतून ।

O Beautiful One! “Battle” means two different things according to persons for whom it is intended; it is of two kinds—One out of excitement and another out of sexual intercourse. With Thee, the sexual intercourse is intended; and with any other enemy, excitement in a real fight is meant. Out of these, the fight of sexual intercourse is full of sweetness and the fight with enemies is painful. O Beautiful One! I know Thy intentions fully. In Thy heart reigns that fight of sexual intercourse. Knowing me as expert in these affairs, the king Šumbha has sent me today to Thee with a vast army.

चतुरासि महाभागे शृणु मे वचनं मृदु ॥ 52 ॥

भज सुभयं जितोकों देवदर्पिनहाईपम् ।

पुतजी प्रिया भूत्वा भूख्व भोगननुचमम् ॥ 53 ॥

O highly Fortunate Lady! Thou art clever and shrewd; hear my gentle words; serve Śumbha, the lord of the three worlds, the destroyer of the Deva's pride. Thou wilt be the dearest queen-consort and enjoy the best pleasures.

उर्वति तां महाबाहुः शुभम्: कामवलार्थवित् ।

विविचाकृतः हावांस्वे संप्रिय भावाकार्थियों ॥ ५४ ॥

The powerful Śumbha knows the real meaning of the fight of sexual intercourse; so he will easily conquer Thee. When Thou wilt shew various amorous gestures, he will also show his feelings.

भविष्यति कारिकेकयं तव नैं नासांखिणी ।

एवं संगार्योगेन पतिमं परमार्थवित् ॥ 55 ॥

जिवा तां सुकुषायायां पशिज्ञानो कर्णिष्ठिति ।

रक्तद्रोहां नखायातैर्दैनंश खण्डताथगम् ॥ 56 ॥

स्वेदविक्त्वां प्रभनां तां संविधास्यति भूपति: ।

भविता मानसः कामो रतितंभागजनम् ॥ 57 ॥

And the Kālika Devi, your companion will remain with Thee as a helping mate in your vital pleasure. The lord of the Daityas, expert in the science of love, will certainly conquer Thee engaged in amorous fight and will lay Thee stretched on a soft bedding and will make Thee tired; he will make Thy body covered with blood by striking with nails and he will bite Thy lips to pieces; then Thou wilt perspire profusely and will cease fighting. Thus Thy mental desire for fight—sexual intercourse—will be satisfied.

दर्शनाड्रण एवाले सुभ्म: सर्वान्तम प्रिये ।

वचनं कुर मे पथ्यं हितकृष्णविष येशन्तम् ॥ 58 ॥

O Beloved! At Thy mere sight Śumbha will be completely subject to Thee. Therefore dost Thou keep my sweet and beneficial words.

जय शुभं भणाध्यधं माननीयात्मानिनि ।

मद्भाग्याश्र ते नृनं हृत्यवृद्धिणश्र शे ॥ 59 ॥

Thou art an honourable Lady; and Thou wilt be highly honoured by all if Thou merriest Śumbha. Those are certainly very unfortunate who like fighting with weapons. O Beloved! The sexual intercourse is always favourite to Thee; therefore it is not worthy of Thee to fight with weapons.

न तद्योसि कान्ते चंख सदा सुत्तवल्लभे ।

अंगोंके कुर राजानं पाद्वाताविकासिन् ।

बकुलं सीधुसेंकेन तथा कुरबक कुरु ११ ६० ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे वैदीमहान्ये

चतुर्विंशोस्थाः ॥ 24 ॥

Therefore dost Thou make the king free of sorrows by pouring on him Thy mouth nectar and by making his heart bud forth by Thy kicking, as Bakula and Kurubaka trees blossom forth when drenched with mouth nectar and Aśoka trees gets blossomed by the kicking of woman.

Here ends the Twenty-fourth Chapter of the Fifth Book on the description Dhimralocana giving the news in Śrimaddevībhāgavatam, the Mahā Purānam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER XXV

On Dhūmralocana Killed

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्खा विरारामासी वचनं धूपतोचनः ।

प्रत्युवाच तटा काली प्रदस्य ललितं वचः ॥ १ ॥

विद्युक्सोसि जात्म लयं तैगतूय इव भापसे ।

दृषा मनोराखींशते कांपि मधुरं वदन् ॥ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O Janamejaya! When Dhūmralocana ceased speaking, the Devi Kālikā made a wild laughter and began to speak sweetly thus: O Stupid! Skilled in flattery, you know only how to use jugglery of words like an actor; do you think that your ends will be served if you speak only sweet words; this can never be.

बलवाव्यलस्येषु: प्रेषितीप्स दुरामना ।

कुरु सुद्धे वृथा यादं मुंच मुक्रमतेधुना ॥ ३ ॥

O Stupid! Fight now; there is no need of useless words. You are strong and have been sent by that wicked Demon with a great army.

हत्वा रुष्यं निरुष्यं च त्वद्याव्याव वलाधिकान् ।

देवी कुंद्रा शराषातैर्जिष्यति निजालयम् ॥ 4 ॥

This Devi, out of wrath, will kill you, Šumbha, and Nisūmbha and other commanders by Her arrows and will then return to Her abode.

वसारी मंदपाति: शुभ्म: कव वा विध्यांगेति ।

असुकः खतुं संसारे विहाहित्धितेतः ॥ ५ ॥

Where is that stupid Sumbha? And where is this Devi, the Great Enchantress of the Universe! Their marriage in this world is entirely out of question and can never take place.

सिंही किं त्वितिकामाता जँबुक कुकते पतिम् ।

क्रिणी गर्दभे वापि गवयं सुरिधः क्रिपु ॥ ६ ॥

गच्छ शुभ्यं नित्यमं च वद सत्यं वयं चेम ।

कुक सुदूदं न चेघाड़ि पात्लां तस्पासंधुना ॥ ७ ॥

O Stupid! What do you think that a lioness becoming very passionate, would make an ordinary jackal her husband? or would a she elephant prefer an ass? or would a heavenly Cow

like a bison? Go to Śumbha and Niśumbha and tell truly to them: "Fight or go instantly to Pātāla."

व्यास उवाच

कालिकाया वचः शुचा स देत्यो धूपजोचना: ।

तापुवाच महाभाग क्रोधसंरक्तलोचना: ॥ ८ ॥

दूर्दैंग ल्चा निर्हलाजी सिंहं च दमवर्गितम् ।

गृहीतैनां गमियामि राजानं प्रत्यहं किल ॥ ९ ॥

रसभंभपथालाकि विषेपि निद्र साम्पतम् ।

नोचेतां निशितैर्वर्णीह्न्यय कलहप्रिये ॥ १ ० ॥

Vyāsa said: O Fortunate One! The Demon Dhimralocana, hearing thus the Kālika's words, became very angry and spoke with reddened eyes: "O Ugly One! I will slay Thee and this lion infatuated with pride in battle and take this Fair One to the king. O Kālī! I have not been able to do this simply it would break our amorous love sentiments. O Quarrelsome One! Otherwise I would have undoubtedly slain Thee just now with my sharpened arrows tipped with irons."

कालिकोवार

किं किवल्यासि मंटान्याजयं धार्यं धनुयात्मा ।

स्वासना युंच वितिष्ठान्तनाति यमवसिति ॥ १ १ ॥

Hearing thus, Kālikā said: “O Fool! Why do you boast vainly? this is not the religion of a hero with bows and arrows in their hands, Shoot your arrows with all your might; I will send you to the realm of Death.”

व्यास उवाच

तच्छूला वचनं दैत्वः संगूहा कार्मकं कुदम् ।

कालिकां तां शरासार्वेदवर्गतिलिजातिशितः ॥ १ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing the Devi's words, Dhimralocana caught hold of his very strong bow and began to shoot arrows after arrows at Kālīkā.

देवायतु प्रेक्षकास्त्र विमानवरसर्यस्थतः ।

तां स्तुवंते जयेन्युद्वेदीं शक्रपुरोगमाः ॥ 13 ॥

Book V Chapter XXV

Indra and the other Devas came out to see the fight on their best cars in the celestial space and shouted “Victory to the Devi” and thus eulogised Her.

तयोः परस्यं युद्धं प्रवृतं चातिवहागणम् ।

बायाड़बागदाशक्तिमुसलातिद्धिकलटम् ॥ १४ ॥

कालिका बाणपातैसृत हवा पूर्व खतराव ।

वंभज तद्यं व्यूंदं जहास च मुडुमुई: ॥ १५ ॥

Then a deadly fight ensued between them with arrows, axes, clubs, Šaktis, and Mūsalas and various other weapons. Kalikā cut off at the very outset all the asses that carried the chariot by Her arrows and next broke his chariot and began to laugh repeatedly.

न चान्यं रथमाकडः कोपेन प्रज्वलम्रिति ।

बाणवृष्टि चकारागां कालिकोपर भारत ॥ 16 ॥

सारपि चिष्ठदेल तरसा तथ्य बाणानसंगतान् ।

मुगोचान्यगुवेगान्दानोपरि कालिका ॥ 17 ॥

O Bharata! Then Dhūmralocana becoming angry mounted on another chariot and began to shoot deadly arrows at Kālika. Kālika Devi, too, cut off those arrows into pieces before they reached Her and shot arrows after arrows on the Dānava in quick succession.

तैवाँणिन्हितास्तस्य पाणिण्याहः सहचशः ।

बर्षत्र च रर्थ वेगात्युतं हत्वा खरानपि ॥ 18 ॥

चिच्छेद निद्वुः सद्यो बाणैकरासद्रिभे: ।

मूंद चक्र सुराणों सा संखावटा तथाप्रकेतो ॥ 19 ॥

Thousands of his soldiers near to him were killed; the asses and the charioteer were killed and the cariot was broken. She cut off his arrows by Her swift serpent-like arrows and blew Her conchshell.

विद्य: परिधं गृछा सर्वलोहमयं दृछम् ।

आजगाम त्योपस्थ कुपितो धूखलेचनः ॥ 20 ॥

वाचा निर्भसैन्यकाली काललः कालसबिभः ।

अर्शैव त्वां हनिष्यामि कुरूपे पिंगलोचने ॥ 21 ॥

The Devas seeing this became very glad. Dhūmralocana, seeing himself displaced from his chariot, took up with anger his very strong Parigha

weapon and came near to the chariot of the Devi. Then the Dānava, terrific like death, began to abuse the Devi and said: "O Ugly tawny-eyed Kālī! I will kill Thee just now."

इत्युक्त्वा सहसा उपान्य परिवं प्रिषयते यदा ।

हूँद्रपेणैव तं भम्म चकार तरसांविका ॥ 2 2 ॥

Thus saying, he suddenly went near to Her and when he was about to throw his Parigha weapon on Her, the Ambikā Devi burnt him to ashes simply by Her loud shout (of defiance).

दृष्ट्वा भर्मपौहृतं दैत्वं सैनिका भयविक्रताः ।

चकुः पलायनं सढो हा तातैत्यबुवन्यथ ॥ २३ ॥

दैवास्तं निहतं बूद्या दानवं धूपलोचनम् ।

मुपुचुः पुच्यष्टिं ते मुदिता गगाने स्थिताः ॥ 2 4 ॥

Seeing Dhūmralocana burnt to ashes, his soldiers became panic stricken, and fled away immediately, crying aloud “O Father!” “O Father!” The Devas saw this and gladly showered from high heaps of flowers on the Devi.

राथूमितसदा राजन्द्रकाण सम्यघत ।

निरहतैनवरश्रेः खरेक्ष वाणैतशा ॥ 25 ॥

O King! The battle ground then assumed a dreadful appearance; at some places the slain Dānavas; at others, the horses; at other places elephants and at some other place the asses lay scattered on the field.

गृधा: काका वटा: ग्रेशेना वरभा जयुकासता ।

ननुद्धुकुगु: प्रेतान्यतितान् रणभूमिषु ॥ 26 ॥

The herons, crows, vultures, the Piśācas of the class Vatā, Varaphās and jackals and other carnivorous animals, began to dance wildly and clamor hideously at the sight of the dead bodies, lying on the field.

अधिका तरणाश्यानं त्यकवा दूर्यलान्ते ।

गत्वा चकार चायदूगं संश्वानं भयप्रद्ध ॥ 27 ॥

The Ambikā Devi then quitting the field, went to a distant place and blew Her conchshell so furiously and terribly that Śumbha heard that terrific noise, while he was sitting in his own residence.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

तं श्रुता दरगबंदु तु शुभ: सचानि संस्थितः ।

दृष्टावश दानवाभ्यमानानातन्कीरोक्षितान् ॥ 28 ॥

छित्यपादकाश्रांश मझकारोपितानपि ।

भन्पुक्रकटिधीवाक्नन्द्यानाननेकशः ॥ 29 ॥

At the next moment, he saw that the Dānava forces had retreated, and they were coming there crying. Some of them were besmeared with blood all over the bodies; some had got their feet, some their arms, cut asunder, some were devoid of eyes, some had got their backs broken; some had their waists broken; some got their necks broken and some were going on bedsteads.

बीक्ष्य युष्पो निरुर्णेषु कव गतो घुप्तोषतः ।

कर्थे भन्या: समायाता ननीति किं वरानना ॥ 30 ॥

सैन्यं कुत्र गत मन्दा: कथवन्तु यथोचितम् ।

कयायं शुब्नानोडा श्रूयते भयवर्धनः ॥ 31 ॥

Seeing them thus, Sumbha and Niśambha asked them: “Where is Dhūmralocana? Why have you all retreated? And why have you not brought that Lady? Where are the other forces? Who has blown this horrible conchshell? O Fools! Inform me quickly and truly all these things.

गणा ऊचुः

बलं च पातितं सर्व निहतो धूपलोचनः ।

कृतं कालिकया कर्म रणभूमावमानुप्रम् ॥ 32 ॥

गंखवार्डपिंककायासु गगनं व्याय प्राते ।

हर्षद: सुरसंधानां दानवानां च शोककृत् ॥ 33 ॥

The soldiers said: “O King! Dhūmralocana has been slain by Kālikā; She has destroyed all the soldiers and has done extraordinary deeds. O King! Know the blowing of the conchshell that has caused terror in the hearts of the Dānavas and has enhanced the joy of the Devas and is being resounded in the celestial space, is that done by the Ambikā Devi.*

*Note: In the Mārkandeya Purāna, Ambikā killed Dhūmra.

यदा निपातिता: सर्वे तेन केसरणि विषो ।

रथा भवना ह्याधीक्ष बाणपातिर्विनारिताः ॥ 34 ॥

गगानस्या सुराधुकुः पुण्यवृष्टि मुदावित्वः ।

दूर्खा भवने वर्स सर्वे पातितं धूमलोचनम् ॥ 35 ॥

“O Lord! When the Devi broke the chariot of Dhimralocana by the multitude of Her arrows and killed the horses and at last slew Dhimralocana himself, when all the forces were slain by Her who appeared like a lion and when the rest of the army retreated, the Devas seeing all these were very much gladdened and showered flowers from the celestial sky.

निश्यस्तु कृतोउसामिभजीये नैव भवेदिति ।

विवारं कुरु राजेन्द्र मनिशिमर्शवितमेः ॥ 36 ॥

O King! We have come to a perfect conclusion that we will not get the victory; now consult with your expert ministers and do what is needful.

विस्योउं महाराज यदेतका जनद्विकला ।

भवाद्ध: सह युद्धाय संशिता सै-व्यावर्जिता ॥ 37 ॥

O King! The Supreme Goddess of the Universe is waiting there alone to fight with you without any help of any other forces; this is a great wonder to us.

निर्भयैकाकिनी बाला सिंहासुक्रा मटोल्कता ।

चिचमेनतमहाराज भासतेउद्धुमझसा ॥ 38 ॥

O King! Intoxicated with Her power, that Girl, fearless, is reigning there taking Her stand on the lion. All these seem wonderful to us.

सचिवा विश्राटो वारण खर्चा निवर्णमेव च ।

मन्त्रयिता महाराज कुरु कार्य व्यासचि ॥ 39 ॥

O King! Consult with your councillors and out of the four policies peace, fight, retreat or remaining neutral, accept what is best.

तस्त्रिधी बलं नास्ति त्वाधि श्रुतापन ।

पांशिग्राहाः सुराः सर्वे पंधिष्यन्ति किलापति ॥ 40 ॥

O Tormentor of the foes! True! There are no forces with the Devi, but the whole host of the Devas will take up Her cause in crisis, there is no doubt.

समये तस्मीपस्थौ जाती च हरिशुक्टी ।

लोकपाला: समीपेपेड्य वर्जने गनने स्थितः ॥ 4। 1 ॥

In due time, Hari and Hara both will come and assist Her, now the guardians of the several

Book V Chapter XXV

quarters, the Lokapālas are waiting by Her side in the celestial space.

स्वोगणाकु गन्थवीं: किसरा मानुशास्तथा ।

तत्सहायोष् मन्त्वा: समये सुत्तापन ॥ 42 ॥

O Tormentor of the Gods! Know that the Gandharvas, Kinnaras, and human beings all will come timely and help Her. O King! We guess all these.

अस्माकं मितिमानेन झालते सर्ववैधानम् ।

अंबिकाया: सहायाशा तत्कायीशा न काचन ॥ 43 ॥

But that Lady does not want the assistance of anyone nor does She expect that any other body would do the work for Her.

एका नाशयितुं शक्ता जानसर्वं चराचम् ।

का कथा दानवानां तु संवैषधपिति निश्रयः ॥ 44 ॥

इति ज्ञाला महाभागा यथा कचि तथा कुल ।

हितं सर्व मिति वाक्यं कतव्यमनुयाधिभि: ॥ 45 ॥

You must know this certainly, that She alone can destroy this whole Universe. What to speak of the Dānavas only! O Highly Fortunate One! Knowing all these, do as you like. It is the duty of the servants to speak beneficial and at the same time true words with moderation.

व्यास उवाच

तच्छुत्वा वचनं तेषां गुभम: पवलतादनः ।

कनीर्वांसं समानीय पप्रच्छ रहसि सिधतः ॥ 46 ॥

भातः कालिकापडोद निरहतो धूपतोषतः ।

बतं च शगतितं सर्वे गणा भवना: समापततः ॥ 47 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Śumbha, the tormentor of others, hearing their words asked his younger brother in private: "O Brother! This Kālika has slain today Dhūmralocana with his forces; the few retreated and came over to me.

अधिका शंखतादं वै कोतित मन्दार्थित ।

जानितं चैव दुर्जीया गति: कालस्य सर्वेधि ॥ 48 ॥

दूर्णं वज्ञायते दूर्तं वज्जं चैव तृणायते ।

बलवान् बलहीन: स्याहैवस्य गतिरीयुगी ॥ 49 ॥

Now the Ambikā Devī, puffed up with pride is blowing Her conchshell. Brother! The ways of Time are knowable even to the wise. The grass

becomes a thunderbolt and the thunderbolt becomes like a grass and powerless.

पृष्ठधीय तां महाभाग किं कतन्यमतः परम् ।

अभोग्या चाधिक्वा नूनं कारणादत्र चागता ॥ 50 ॥

युक्ते पलायनं वीर युद्धे वा वद सल्वम् ।

लघुं ज्येष्ठे चित्रज्ञापिति त्वामई कार्यसंकटे ॥ 51 ॥

Know thus the course of Destiny. O Fortunate One! Now I ask you, what are we to do now? Are we to entertain yet the desire of enjoying Ambikā, or are we to fly away from here or are we to fight on? Say quickly. Though younger, in times of difficulty, I consider you as my elder.

निरुभ्य उवाच

न वा पलायनं युक्त न वुर्ग्रिद्धगं तथा ।

युद्धमेव परं श्रेय: सर्वेश्वरायताउनच ॥ 52 ॥

Hearing thus the Sumbha's words, Niśumbha said: "O Sinless One! Flight or taking refuge in a fort is not reasonable.

सरी-योर्ड गयिष्यायि रणे तु प्रवराशितः ।

हत्वा तामागयिष्यायि तसा त्ववलायीयम् ॥ 53 ॥

To fight with this Lady is the best course. I will take the best generals and soldiers with me and will slay that Lady and quickly return.

अथवा बलवदैवाद्यथा चेढ़ाविध्यति ।

मृते माय त्वया कार्य विमुग्य च पुनः पुनः ॥ 54 ॥

And if Fate be strong and prove it otherwise, then, after my death, think out again and again and do what is best."

इति तस्य वधः शूला शुभः प्रोवा चानुजम् ।

तिह तं चचडमुपडी द्वी गच्छेतां बलसंयुतो ॥ 55 ॥

Hearing thus the younger brother's words, Śumbha said "You better wait; let Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa go to the battle, surrounded with forces."

शशक्रामृद्धायात्र न सुक्तं गतयोचनम् ।

चण्डपुरुषी महावीरी तां हनुं सर्वेथा क्षमो ॥ ५६ ॥

To kill a hare it is not necessary to send an elephant. This is a very trifling matter; the two great warriors Candā and Mundā will be freely able to slay Her.

शूलुक्ला प्रातरं शुभ्यः सम्भाय च महावली ।

उवाच चचनं राजा चणदुम्ृद्धी पुरःस्थिति ॥ 57 ॥

गच्छतं चण्डपुण्डी हौ स्वसैन्यपरिवारिति ।

हन्तु तामवलों शीधि निर्लेक्जां मदार्थवितान् ॥ 58 ॥

Thus saying his younger brother, the King Śumbha addressed Caṇḍa Muṇḍa, who were waiting before him, thus: O Canda! O Muṇḍa! Take your forces and go quickly to kill that shameless Lady, puffed up with pride.

गृहीत्वाद निहत्याजी कालिकां पिंगलोचनाम् ।

आगम्यतां महापारी कृत्वा कार्य महत्ततम् ॥ 591 ||

O Pair of warriors! Kill that tawny-eyed Kālika in the battle and bring that Arnbikā Devi here quickly.

सा नायाति गृहीताउपि गर्हिता चाधिक्या यदि ।

तदा बाणीम्हातीक्षेणीर्नल्याउउद्वमपिछ्ठा ॥ 60 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे पत्रमकर्ये

पडविंशोउद्यानः ॥ 25 ॥

Do this Great Service. And if that haughty Ambikā be unwilling to come here, though taken as a captive, then kill that Durgā, the ornament of the battle, too, by sharp arrows.

Here ends the Twenty-fifth Chapter of the Fifth Book on the killing of Dhūmralocana in Śrimaddevībhāgavatam, the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

CHAPTER XXVI

On Caṇḍa Muṇḍa Killed

व्यास उवाच

इत्याज्त्ती तदा वीरी घण्डयुपडौ महावर्ती ।

जममुत्स्तरीबाबी सैन्येन महात्रान्विती ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus ordered, the two strong warriors Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa hurriedly went to the battle, accompanied by a vast army.

सूद्या तत स्थिता देईी देवानां हिततारिणीम् ।

ऊचतुस्ती महावीर्णी तदा सामान्तितं वचः ॥ 2 ॥

There they saw the Devi, intent on doing good to the Gods. Then they began to address Her in conciliatory words.

बाले तवं किं न जानासे शुभ्म सुचलादनम् ।

निरुभयं च महीवीर्य तृताषड्विजयोढ़तम् ॥ 3 ॥

O Lady! Dost Thou not know that the extraordinarily strong Sumbha and Niśumbha, the Lords of the Daityas have crushed down the Devas and vanquished Indra and have become intoxicated with their victory?

ल्यमेकासि वरारोहे कालिका सिंहसंयुता ।

जेतुपिछ्छसि दुर्बदैवं सुभ्यं सर्वबलात्विन् ॥ ४।।

O Fair One! Thou art alone! Only Kālikā and Thy Lion are with Thee! It is Thy foolishness that Thou art desirous to conquer Śumbha, who is endowed with all power.

मतिहः कोप्रिय ते नास्ति नारी वार्थि नरोप्रिय वा ।

देवात्मां प्रियल्येव विनागाय तवैव ते ॥ ५ ॥

I think there is no adviser to Thee, man nor woman; the Devas have sent Thee here simply for Thy destruction.

विषय्य कुर्त त्वर्धिके कार्य स्वपरयोबल् ।

अष्टादशभुजत्वाच्चं गर्व च कुक्रे मृषा ॥ 6 ॥

Think, O Delicate One over the power of Thine as well as those of Thy enemy. Vainly dost Thou boast of Thy having eighteen hands.

किं घुरौबैहुभिव्यंशैरियुः किं शमगदैः ।

गृष्मचयो सुराणां वे जेतु: स्मारगालिनः ॥ 7 ॥

Before the great warrior Šumbha, the conqueror of Devas, many hands and many weapons will be useless; they will prove mere burden.

ऐरावतकरच्छेनृदितन्तरणाकारिणः ।

जयितः सुरसंघानां कार्य कुक्र मनोत्तम् ॥ 8 ॥

So dost Thou fulfil what reigns in the heart of Śumbha, the destroyer of the legs and the uprooter of the teeth of Airāvata elephant.

वृथा गवायसे कान्ते कुक्र मे वर्चनं विध्वम् ।

हितं तव विशालासिं सुखदं दुःखनागनम् ॥ ९ ॥

Vain is Thy boast, O Beloved! Follow my sweet words; they will do good to Thee, O Large-eyed One! They will destroy Thy pains and give Thee bliss.

Book V Chapter XXVI

दुःखदानि च कार्याणि त्यास्यानि दूततो बुधेः ।

सुखदानि च सेव्यानि शास्त्रत्वशिगारदेः ॥ 10 ॥

Those actions that lead to pain are to be avoided by the wise; and those that bring in happiness are to be served by the Pandits, versed in the Śāstras.

चतुराति चिकालापे पश्य शुभ्यबलं महत ।

प्रत्यक्षं सुरपेक्षायां मदौनेन महोदयम् ॥ ११ ॥

प्रत्यक्षे च परित्यज्य वृषेवानुभिति: ब्रिक्त ।

सन्देहसिते कार्ये न विषधित्यवर्ते ॥ १२ ॥

O Sweet speaking one! Thou art clever. Look at the great strength of Śumbha with Thy eyes. He has enhanced his glory by crushing the Devas. And if Thou thinkest the gods superior, that is false, for the wise men do not rely on mere guess, full of doubts; they believe what they actually see.

शुः सुगणं परमः शुभः समरुद्धिनः ।

तस्मात्तं प्रेर्यल्य देवा दैत्येयपीर्तितः ॥ 13 ॥

Šumbha, hard to be conquered in battles, is the great enemy of the Gods; they have been crushed down by him, and have therefore sent Thee here.

तस्मात्तद्वनै: सिनर्धैवैचितासि सुचिसिते ।

दुःखाय तव तेवानां शिक्षा स्वार्थस्य साक्षित्य ॥ 14 ॥

O Sweet smiling One! Thou hast been deceived by their sweet words; they, prompted by their selfish ends, have sent Thee here simply to give Thee trouble.

कार्यमिति परिशिष्य धर्ममिति समाश्रयेत् ।

देवा: स्वार्थपरा: कामं त्वामहं सत्यमवर्णम ॥ 15 ॥

The friends that come with certain business and selfish ends are to be rejected. Friends out of religious motives are only to be sought for refuge. Verily I tell Thee that the gods are terribly selfish.

भत्र शुर्णमं सुरेशानं जेतारं भुवनेक्षरम् ।

चतुरं सुन्दरं शूरं कामाशाखियशाराद् ॥ 16 ॥

Therefore dost Thou serve Sumbha, the conqueror of Indra and the lord of the three worlds; he is a hero, beautiful, lovely, cunning and thoroughly expert in the science of coronous love.

ऐभूयं सर्वतोकानां प्रायस्ये शुभाशासनान ।

निश्रयं परमं कृत्वा भतरिं भव जांशेभनम् ॥ 17 ॥

Thou wilt get the prosperity of all the worlds by the mere command of Sumbha; therefore make a firm resolve and serve that splendid husband Sumbha."

व्यास उवाच

इति तस्य वचः श्रुत्वा चंडस्य जगद्धिक्वा ।

मेधामार्थीनिरन्ति जगर्जं पुनरबहित् ॥ 18 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The Universal Mother, hearing the words of Canda spoke with a voice deep as thunder. "O Boor! Why do you use false deceitful words?

गच्छ जात्म युषा किं त्वं भाषसे वंचकं वचः ।

त्यक्ता हरिदात्रादीक्ष शुभ्मं कस्माद्वजे पतिम् ॥ १९ ॥

Fly away just now. Why shall I make Śumbha my husband, disregarding Hari, Hara and the other Devas?

न मे कक्रित्यति: कार्यं न कार्य पतिना सह ।

स्वामिनी सर्वभृतानामहमेव निजामय ॥ 20 ॥

गृभा मे बह्वो दृषा निशुप्भाशु सहस्वण: ।

घातिताश मया पूर्वे शतगो देत्यदानतः ॥ 21 ॥

O You, a veritable Fool! I have no necessity for My lord; I have got nothing to do with my lord. I Myself am the Lord of all the beings; and I preserve this whole Universe with all the lord and beings therein. Note this. In ancient times I saw thousands and thousands of Śumbha and Niśumbha and I slew them all.

ममगो देवेबंदान विन्णधिन चुगे चुगे ।

नाशं यास्यसिं दैत्यानां युधानि पुनरब है ॥ 22 ॥

I sent hundreds and hundreds of Daityas and Demons to the realm of Death. Before Me the hosts of Devas were destroyed in yugas after yugas.

काल एवागतोउस्यत्र दैत्यसंहाराकारकः ।

वृथा त्वं कुर्ये चर्न स्थणायामसर्तेनैः ॥ 23 ॥

Today the Daityas again will go unto destruction. The Time has come to destroy the Daityas; why, then, are you struggling in vain with your followers for your lives?

कुर्यु पूर्वे वीरधर्मश्रव्ये त्वं महामते ।

मरणे भावि दुस्त्याज्यं योगे रक्ष्यं महात्माधि: ॥ 24 ॥

Fight now and keep the Dharma of the warriors; death is inevitable; thinking thus, the high-soiled ones should keep their name, fame, and respect.

क्रि ते कार्व निर्गुभ्येन रुष्येने च दुर्जान्ता ।

वीराधर्म परं प्राया गछ्छ स्वर्ग सुरालयम् ॥ 25 ॥

What business have you to do with Sumbha and Niśumbha? Follow the warrior's Dharma and go to Heavens, the abode of the gods.

गुष्पो निरुष्णश्रवीये चे चात्र तव बांधावः ।

सर्वे तवानुगा: पशुततापीम्यन्ति साम्यान् ॥ 26 ॥

Sumbha, Niśumbha and your other friends and followers, all will follow you and will come here no doubt.

क्रभशः सर्वेटैल्यानां कारीध्यायघु संक्षयम् ।

विवादं त्वज मन्दात्मकुन कुवूदं विश्रापिते ॥ 27 ॥

O Stupid One! I will put an end to all the Dānavas today. Therefore cast aside your weakness and go on, fight.

त्वामहं निर्हायाम विभारंत तव साम्यात् ।

ततः शुभ्यं निर्गुभं च रत्कबीजं मदोत्कटम् ॥ 28 ॥

अन्योक्षा दानवासर्वन्हल्वाउई समरोगेण ।

I will slay you and your brother just now; next I will kill the proud Rakta Bija, Nisumbha and Śumbha and the other Dānavas in the battlefield and will then go to My desired place.

गमिथामि यथास्थानं तिष्ठ वा गच्छ वा हुतम् ॥ 29 ॥

गृहाणालं च्या पुरु कुरु युद्धे मावअरुना ।

कि जल्यसि मुषा वाक्यं सर्वेण कातरिप्रियम् ॥ 30 ॥

Now remain here if you like or fly away quickly. You have been fed in vain because you fear to fight. What use is there now in using sweet words like a weak and distressed man. Well! Take up your arms now and fight."

व्यास उवाच

त्येवं प्रेति दैवी चडपुण्डी कृपास्तिति ।

न्याशर्धं तसा घोरं चक्रतुर्बतद्विती ।। ३। ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Candā and Munḍa, elated with pride, got excited at the Devi's words, became angry and made a violent noise with their bow strings.

सापि शंख्यवनं चके पुरवन्ती दिशो दग ।

सिंहोर्पि कुपितस्तावजारं समकारेहली ॥ ३ ३ ॥

The Devi, too, blew Her conchshell so loudly that the ten quarters of the sky reverberated; in the meanwhile, the powerful lion became very angry and roared loudly.

तेन नानेव शक्राधजा जहरुंपरमरस्तव ।

मुनयो यक्षान्यावीः सिद्धाः साधायश क्रिकराः ॥ 33 ॥

Hearing that sound Indra and other Devas, the Munis, Yakṣas, Siddhas, and Kinnaras became all very glad.

युद्धे परस्परं तत्र जातं कातरभीतिदम् ।

चिण्डकावणद्योस्तीयं बाणाखुरणादिषिः ॥ 34 ॥

A dreadful fight then ensued between Caṇḍikā and Caṇḍa with arrows, axes and other weapons, causing terror to the weak.

चण्डपुकाळप्रत्यदेवी चित्रद्रेर निशितः शरैः ।

युमोच पुनस्त्याजाराचाङ्गिडिका पत्रगानिव ॥ 35 ॥

The Caṇḍikā Devi became very wrathful and cut off to pieces all the arrows shot by Caṇḍa and then hurled arrows serpent-like on him.

गगनं छात्रितं तत्र संग्रामे विगिर्छेस्तदा ।

शलभैरिव मेघान्ते कर्षिकाणां घयप्रदेः ॥ 36 ॥

Then the sky over the battle ground seemed to be overcast with arrows just as the clouds get covered over with locusts, dreadful to the cultivators.

मुण्डोरि सैनिकः सार्थ प्रपात तरसा रणे ।

मुभोच बाणवृहिं है कूढ़ः परमदाकणः ॥ 37 ॥

In the meanwhile Munda, exceedingly terrible, came up to the field, taking with him his army and becoming impatient with anger began to shoot arrows.

वाणजलं महदुपृथवा कुढ़ा तर्याविका भुग्ग।

कोपेन वदर्त नत्या बभूव घनपात्रिभम् ॥ 38 ॥

कदलीषुधपनें च भुकुटीकुटिलं ततां ।

निष्काना च तदा काती ललाद्धपलकादु हुणम् ॥ 39 ॥

Seeing that multitude of arrows, Ambikā got very angry; out of Her frowny look, Her eyebrows became crooked, Her face became black, and Her

Book V Chapter XXVI

eyes turned red like Kadalī flowers; at this time suddenly came out of Her forehead Kālī.

బాహ్వమన్నా క్రు నాక్రమన్నాన్నా ।

मुड़समालाधर थोरा शुक्वावासीमोदरा ॥ 40 ॥

खदुपाणप्रधान्डतीव बीषणा भयदाविनी ।

खद्वोगायारिणी रैत्रा कालगात्रियराया ॥ 41 ॥

वित्तीणिद्वाना चिज्ञां चालयन्ती मुहुर्दुः ।

वित्तारजघन वेगाजघायानुरसैरिकान् ॥ 42 ॥

Wearing the tiger's skin, cruel, covering Her body with elephant's skin, wearing a garland of skulls, terrible, with a belly like a well dried up, mouth wide open, with a wide waist, lip hanging loosely, with axe, noose, Śiva's weapon, in Her hands, She looked very terrible like the Night of Dissolution. She began to lick frequently and forcibly dashed into the Dānava army and began to destroy it.

करे कृत्वा महावीरांतसरा सा रुचान्यिता ।

मुखे चिश्वेप देतेतायिपेष दूरनैः रानैः ॥ 43 ॥

She angrily began to take the powerful Dānavas by Her arms and pouring them into Her mouth crushed them with Her teeth.

गजान्यंदायितान्हसे गृहीला निदधे मुखे ।

सारोहान्यक्षयित्वाजी सहुहासं चकार ह ॥ 44 ॥

Taking the elephants with bells by Her own power in Her hands She put them all into Her mouth and swallowed them all with their riders and began to laugh hoarsely.

तथैव तुरागदुर्गलक्षा साराधिभः सह ।

निश्रिय वक्रेः दूरनैश्वैल्यंतिभेदस्य ॥ 4 5 ॥

Thus camels, horses and charioteers with chariots all She put into Her mouth and began to chew them all grimly.

हन्यमानं बलं ग्रेक्ष्य चणद्युपही महासुरी ।

छाव्यामासतुहैंवी बाणासारित्नन्ते: ॥ 4 6 ॥

चणड्छश्डकन्छायं चकं चक्धभाग्युम् ।

चिश्वेग तसा देहीं नानद च मुहुंमुँः ॥ 47 ॥

नन्तं वीक्ष्य तं काली रथांगं च रविष्यम् ।

बाणौनैकेन चिष्छेद सुप्रभं तल्युदर्शनम् ॥ 48 ॥

O King! Seeing that the forces were being thus destroyed, the two great warriors Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa began to shoot arrows after arrows without intermission and covered the Devi with them. Chaṇḍa hurled the Sudarśana-like disc, lustrous like the Sun, with great force against the Devi, and frequently shouted thundering cries.

तं जयाने शरैस्तीक्षीशुष्टं चेषडी शिलाशिति: ।

मूँछितोउसी यपातोल्यों देवीबाणादिती० भृणम् ॥ ४ १ ॥

Seeing him roaring and the lustrous disc coming towards Her like another sun, She shot at him arrows sharpened on stones so that the warrior Caṇḍa became overpowered by them and lay down senseless on the ground.

पतितं भारतं बीक्ष्य मुण्डो दुःखादितस्तदा ।

चकार शरबृष्टि च कारिकोषपि कोपतः ॥ ५० ॥

The powerful Muṇḍa seeing his brother unconscious became very much distressed with grief, but he got angry and began to shoot arrows immediately on the Devi.

चण्डिका मुण्डविन्मुक्तां शरबृष्टि सुदारुकाणम् ।

ईयिकाखैर्वीन्यृकैश्यकार तिलशः क्षणगत् ॥ 51 ॥

अधिन्द्रेण बाणेन ताउवामास तं पुनः ।

पतितोउसी महावीर्वों मेहिन्यां मदवर्जितः ॥ 52 ॥

Candjikā Devi hurled the weapon named Iṣikā and thus cut off to pieces all the dreadful arrows of Muṇḍa in a moment and shot Ardha Candra (semi-circular) arrow at him. With this arrows the powerful Asura was deprived of his pride and made to lie down unconscious on the earth.

हाहाकारो महानासीहानवांग केले तदा ।

जहरुप्रमरा: सर्वे गतपन्स्वा गतपत्यतः ॥ 53 ॥

Mundra thus lying on the ground, a great uproar arose amidst the army of the Dānavas; and the Devas became delighted in the sky.

बिहाय मृहां चण्डसु मंग्रहा महर्ती गदाम ।

तरसा ताड्यामास कालिकां दक्षिणे घुजे ॥ 54 ॥

In the meanwhile Caṇda became conscious and taking a very heavy club hurled it violently on the right hand of Kālīkā.

वंचियत्वा गद्यापाते तं बबंध महासुरम् ।

तस्या बाणपाणेन मन्युक्तेन कांकिका ॥ 55 ॥

Kālīkā rendered that blow useless and instantly tied down that Asura by Her Pāśa weapon, purified by Mantras.

उदयित्रु तदा मुण्डो बबंदं दूध्यानुर्ज बलात् ।

आजगाम सुस्मद्धः शफिंक कृत्वा कें दूबम् ॥ 56 ॥

आपच्छन्तं तदा काली दानवं वीथ्य सत्यम् ।

बबंध तरसा तं तु द्वितीयं घातरं भृणम् ॥ 57 ॥

Mundā again rose up, and, seeing his brother in that fastened condition, came to the front well armoured and with an exceedingly strong weapon called Śakti. Seeing the Asura coming, She instantly fastened him down like his brother.

गृहीता ती महावौं चण्डपुर्णी शशगवित्र ।

कुकंती विपुर्णे हासमाजगमाधिकां प्रति ॥ 58 ॥

आयात्य तमाध्योवाच गृणोगेमी पशु प्रिये ।

राण्यज्ञाथीमांती दानवी रणुदुर्जी ॥ 59 ॥

Taking the powerful Caṇḍa and Munḍa like hares and laughing wildly, Kālī went to Ambikā, and said: "I have brought the two beasts very auspicious as offerings in this sacrificial war. Kindly accept these."

तावानीती तत वीक्ष चफिड का तेचुकावि ।

अंबिका कालिकां प्राड़ माधुर्गस्वरुं वचः ॥ 6० ॥

Seeing the two Dānavas brought, as if they were the two wolves, Ambikā told her sweetly: "O Thou, fond of war!

वर्ध मा कुक मा मुंच चतुरासि रणाधिये ।

देवानां कार्यसंभितिः कर्सेवा तरसा तवा ॥ 6 1 ॥

Thou art very wise; so dost not commit the act of envy nor dost leave them; think over the purport

of my words and know that it is Thy duty to bring the Deva's work to a successful issue."

व्यास उवाच

इति तथा वचः शुत्वा कालिका प्रह तां पुनः ।

यूद्वम्येज्ञतिविज्ञायते खड्यो यूये प्रतिक्ति ॥ 6 2 ॥

आलम्भे च करिष्यासि यथा हिंसा न जायते ।

इन्युक्त्वा सा तत देवी छखुर्जन ऐगरसी तयोः ॥ 6 3 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing thus the words of Ambikā, Kālikā spoke to Her again: "In this warsacrifice there is this axe which is like a sacrificial post; I will offer two as victims to Thy sacrifice. Thy no act of envy will be committed (i.e., killing in a sacrifice is not considered as envy)."

चकर्ते तरसा काली पपी च रधिरं मुदा ।

एवं देयो हनौ दृष्टवा मुदतोगाच चाधिक्व ॥ 64 ॥

कृतं कार्य सुराणां ते ददाय्यघ वरं शुभम् ।

चण्डपुरुषी हर्ती यसात्स्याने नाम कालिके ।

चामुपडेति सुविख्यातं भविष्यति धरातले ॥ 65 ॥

इति श्रीद्वीभागवते महापुराणे पत्रमकन्दे

चंचदुपुरुवर्षे चद्विरोगेअवारः ॥ 26 ॥

Thus saying, the Kālikā Devi cut off their heads with great force and gladly drank their blood. Thus seeing the two Asuras killed, Ambikā said gladly: "Thou hast done the service to the gods; so I will give Thee an excellent boon. O Kālikā! As Thou hast killed Çanda and Muṇda, henceforth Thou wilt be renowned in this world as Cāmuṇḍa."

Here ends the Twenty-sixth Chapter of the Fifth Book on the killing of Çanda and Muṇḍa in Śrimaddevirbhāgavatam, the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER XXVII

On the Fight of Raktabija

व्यास उवाच

हतौ तौ दानवी नुद्ध्या हतोगेभ्यो श्वैनिकाः ।

पलायनं ततः कृत्वा जम्पुः सर्वे गृप प्रति ॥ १ ॥

भिनांगा विशिष्टः केचित्केवचिष्छकरास्तथा ।

कौधरस्वावदेहाश्र चतंतेगभिवयुः परे ॥ 2 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Seeing the two Dānavas killed in the battle, the remnant soldiers all fled away back to Śumbha. Some of them were cut and wounded in many places by arrows, some had their arms severed, some were bleeding; thus they entered crying into the city.

Book V Chapter XXVII

गत्या दैत्यपति सर्वे चकुर्बुबारवे मुङुः ।

रक्ष रक्ष महाराज भक्षत्यच्छ कालिका ॥ 3 ॥

On reaching the lord of the Daityas, they began to make frequently the noise indicative of danger and exclaimed “O King! Save us, Save us; Kālika is devouring everything today.”

तया हर्ते महावीरो चपड़पुण्डी सुरादनी ।

भक्षिताः सैनिकाः सर्वे वर्ष भन्पा भयतुतः ॥ 4 ॥

The two great warriors Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, the tormentors of the Devas, were slain by Her; all the soldiers were devoured by Her; we have fled away panic stricken.

भीतिदं च रणस्वानं कृतं कालिकया प्रभो ।

पातितैर्गवीराधेदिंदिसरक्पदातिभि: ॥ 5 ॥

O Lord! Kālika has rendered the battle-field horrible by the dead bodies of elephants, horses, camels, warriors, and foot soldiers.

शोणतोषवहा कुल्या कृता मोसातिकदर्भा ।

कैगशैलिनी भन्नरधघक्रविराता ॥ 6 ॥

छिडबाढ़ापिमल्याड्या शीर्भेत्रीजलान्धिता ।

भयदा कातताणां वै सुगणां मोदखर्दिनि ॥ 7 ॥

A river of blood is flowing there is which the flesh of the soldiers is sufficient mud, their hairs are like aquatic plants, the broken chariot wheels are like whirlpools, the severed arms and feet are like fishes and their heads look like Tumbi fruits (long gourds).

कुलं रक्ष महाराज पातालं गच्छ सत्यम् ।

कूद्धा देवी क्षयं सचः कार्चिति न संगया ॥ 8 ॥

O king! Save your line; go quickly to Pātāla. The Devi has become angry and will, no doubt, destroy our race.

सिंहोरपि भक्षत्याजी दानवाद्नुनेक्षर ।

तथैव कालिका देवी हाँत बाणैरनेकथा ॥ 9 ॥

Even the lion is eating away the Dānavas; and the Kālīkā Devi is killing innumerable Dānavas by Her arrows. Therefore, O king! What intentions are you cherishing in your mind?

तस्माचवमी राजेन्द्र मरणाय मृषा मतिम् ।

कंरोष सहितो धाता शुभ्येन कुपिताशय: ॥ 10 ॥

किं कंचिति नारैघा कृता कुलिवनाशिन।

यस्या हेतोर्महाराज हेतुमिष्ठसि बोधयान् ॥ १ १ ॥

Is it that you have desired to be merely slain with your younger brother Niśumbha! And what good purpose will this cruel woman, destroying your race, serve, for Whose sake, you have desired to kill all your friends?

दैवधाती महाराज लोके जयपराजये ।

अल्याधीय महदुःखं बुद्धिमात्र प्रकल्यन्ते ॥ 12 ॥

O king! Victory or defeat in this world are under the Daiva. The wise never risk to meet with great difficulties for the gratification of an ordinary whim.

चित्रं पश्य विधेः कर्म यद्धीनं जगत् प्रभो ।

निह्ता ताक्षसा: सर्वे तिक्वा पश्येकाउन्पाय ॥ 13 ॥

O Lord! Look at the wonderful deeds of that Great Creator? What more wonder can there be than this that a woman alone killed all the Dānavas. जेता त्वं लोकपालानां वैन्ययुक्ता हि संधानम् ।

एका प्रार्थयते बाला युद्धायेति सुसंधभः ॥ 14 ॥

O King! You have conquered by the help of your army all the Lokapālas (guardians of the quarters of the sky); but now that Lady, though alone and unsupported by anybody, is challenging you to fight.

पुरा त्वया तपस्तपं पुक्रते देवतायने ।

वद्दानाय संप्राणो बहा लोकपितामहः ॥ 15 ॥

ध्योक्तिस्तर्व महाराज वर्ग वर्य सूत्रत ।

तदा त्वयाभरन्त च प्रार्थितं ब्रहणा: किन ।। 16 ॥

O King! In ancient times, in the holy pilgrimage of Puskara, the sacred place of the Devas, you performed austerities when Brahmā, the Grandsire of all the worlds, came to you to grant a boon. Then you asked the boon and wanted to become to be immortal.

देवदैत्यमनुषेधनो न भवेन्मरणं मम ।

सर्पिक्रतयक्षेध्च: पुंलिङ्गवाचकादि १। १७ ॥

But when Brahmā refused to grant it you wanted from him and were granted that you would not be killed by any male being, be he a Deva,

Dānava, a man, Nāga, Kinnara, Yakṣa, or any other person.

तस्मारां हनुकामीय प्राप्ता योषित्वा प्रभो ।

यूद्म म कुर फजेन्द्र विषार्विं हियाउंगून ॥ 18 ॥

O Lord! For that very reason we think now that this Lady has timely come to kill you. Think over it seriously and cease fighting.

देवी होया महामाया प्रकृति: परमा मता ।

कल्प्यतकाले राजेन्र सर्वसंहारकारिणी ॥ 19 ॥

O King! This Devi is the great Mahā-Maÿā, the Highest Prakṛti; It is She that devours everything at the end of a Kalpa.

उत्पादयिती लोकानां देवनामीक्षी शुभा ।

विरुणा तामसी देवी सर्वशक्तिसमवित्ति ॥ 20 ॥

This auspicious Devi is the Creatrix of all the worlds and the Devas. She is the embodiment of the three qualities, endowed with all the powers.

जज्यच चाक्षया निवा सर्वज्ञा च सदोदिता ।

वेदमाता च गाययी संध्या सर्वसुगतया ॥ 21 ॥

She is Tāmasi, i.e., is the Destructrix of the whole world. This Devi can never be conquered, Imperishable, Eternal, She is the Sandhyā and the Refuge of the Devas.

निर्णीग सुगण सिद्धा सर्वसिद्धप्रदास्थ्या ।

आनन्दोद्ध उनन्ददा गौरी देवानामभयप्रदा ॥ 22 ॥

She is Gāyatrī, the Mother of the Vedas. She is All-knowing and always manifested. This Undecaying Lady is void of any Prākṛtic attributes, though She at times possesses attributes.

एवं जाला महाराज वैरभावं स्वाज्ञाय ।

गरणं जज राजेन्द्र देवी त्वां पतायिष्यति ॥ 23 ॥

She is Success Incarnate and bestows success to all; She is Bliss Herself and gives bliss to all. This Gauri bids all the Devas discard all their fears.

आज्ञाको भवेतावा: संजीवय निजं कुलम् ।

हतोगेश्ठा ये दैत्यासे भवन्तु चितयपुष: ॥ 24 ॥

She is Śuddha Sattva. Thus knowing, O king! Quit thy inimical feelings to Her; seek refuge unto Her; the Devi would then certainly protect you. Be obedient to Her and save your race. Then the

remnant Dānavas will be able to live for a very long time."

व्यास उवाच

इति तेषां वचः श्रुवा शुभमः सुरबलादिनः ।
