उवाच बचनं तव्यं वीरवर्गे गृणान्वितम् ॥ 25 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing thus, Šumbha, the conqueror of the Devas, told them truly in words becoming of a hero.

शुभ्य उवाच

मानं कुर्वर्तु भो मेदा यूयं भगना रणाधिजित् ।

शीधं गछ्ठत पातालं जीविताशा बतीयसी ॥ २ ६ ॥

Śumbha said: “O Fools! Hold your tongue. You have fled because your desire to live is very strong. So you better go to Pātāla without any delay.

दैवाधीनं जानसर्वं का चिन्तानज जये मम ।

देवास्तबैव ब्रहादा देवाधीना चयं यथा ॥ 27 ॥

This world is under the control of Fate; so I need not think about Victory. I am under this Fate just as Brahmā and other Devas are under it.

ब्रह्मा विष्युध मृत्रोयं यमोर्जनवैरभत्सता ।

सूर्यश्रुस्त्वाण शक्र: सर्वे दिवशग: किति ॥ 28 ॥

Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Rudra, Yama, Agni, Varuna, Sūrya, Candra, and Indra are all under the sway of this Destiny.

का चिता ताई्धे मे मंदा यद्धावि तद्धाविषिति ।

उड्यमस्ताङ्गो भूयादूर्गी भवित्यन्ता ॥ 29 ॥

O Fools! Whatever is inevitable will certainly come to pass. What need I think over it then? The effort also comes to be of such a nature as will lead to that ordained by Fate.

सर्वेवैव विचार्यैव न शेणैस्ति वुभा: क्वचित् ।

स्वधर्ष न त्वजेनीतु जानिनो मरणाद्वान् ॥ 30 ॥

Thus thinking, the wise never grieve; especially the wise ones never leaves their own Dharma for fear of death.

सुखं दुःखं तथेवायुर्जीवितं मरणं नृगाण ।

क्राने घवाति संग्रामे सर्वेश दैवनिर्मितम् ॥ 31 ॥

बह्ञा पतिति काले सेव विद्युध पार्वतीपति: ।

नाशुब्धल्यायुगोंने सर्वे देवा: सवासया: ॥ 32 ॥

Book V Chapter XXVII

तथाड़मपि कालस्य वर्गगः सर्वेधार्धुना ।

नाश जयं वां नानासि स्वधर्मपरिणतन्ति । 33 ।।

The happiness, pain, longevity, birth and death of all the embodied souls are all determined by Fate when their proper time arrives. See! When the time is over, Brahmā, Viṣṇuṇad Mahādeva, the lord of Pārvati die away; on the expiration of their terms of lives, Indra and other Devas go to destruction.

आहृतोंप्रयनया कामं युद्धायावलया किल ।

कर्च पत्नायपनरो जीवेये शरादों शगन् ॥ 34 ॥

करिष्यायद्य संगाय यद्धावि तद्वारवित्व ।

जयो वा मरणं वाउपि स्वीकृतोमि यथा तथा ॥ 35 ॥

Similarly I am also completely under the sway of time; so what doubt is there that I, too, will go to destruction when I have observed my own Dharma! This Lady is challenging me to fight of Her own will; how can I fly away and live hundreds of years.

दैवं पिथ्येति विह्जोसो वदन्युधामविन्दि: ।

युनिक्षुर्फं बचसेवो थे जानंत्यिभाषपितुम् ॥ 36 ॥

I will fight today, Let the result come whatever it may. I will gladly take the victory or defeat whatever the case may be.

उद्यमेन विना कामं न सियत्थिन मनोराशः ।

काता एव जल्पनिति यद्धान्यं तद्धाविषयति ॥ 37 ॥

अनुर्धे बलवन्यूहा: प्रवदन्ति न पशिडता: ।

प्रमाणं तस्य सचो क्रिममुळयं दूरयते कर्ण ॥ 38 ॥

The learned approving of the cause of efforts declare Fate as fictitious; those who realise their saying know that they are full of reason. Without exertion no end can be achieved; weak persons depend on the destiny.

अनुहें स्वार्थ पूर्वे स्यादेषा मूर्खिधिभिका ।

अललब्यं विवेर्वेया दुःः येचितस्य धाणा ॥ 39 ॥

Foolish persons say that Fate is strong; but the wise do not say so. There is no proof whether Fate exists or not; in fact what is called Fate is invisible; how can it then be seen? Has anybody seen Fate?

चक्रीममीपे संश्रिय संश्रित्यापीटकाशिणी ।

उद्यमेन विना पिछे न भवत्वेव सर्वथा ॥ 40 ॥

It is simply a scare for the illiterate; remedy only to console one's mind in times of distress. Simply proximity to a grindmill without any man's effort cannot grind a material.

उद्यो च कृते कार्य सिंद्धि यायेव सर्वथा ।

कदाचित्तस्य न्यूनले कार्य नैव भवेद्येच ॥ 41 ॥

Therefore if exertion be made in proportion to the gravity of the work, success is sure to ensue; if exertion be made less in proportion, the work does not come to a successful issue.

देशं कालं च विज्ञाय स्ववलं शुज्जं बलम् ।

कृतं कार्य भवत्येव बृहस्यतिचरो यथा ॥ 42 ॥

If time, place and one's enemies' forces be correctly taken into account and then if the proper attempts be made, success follows; thus Brhaspati has said."

व्यास उवाच

इति निश्रय दैत्येन्द्रो रक्तवीजं महासुरम् ।

प्रेयामास संग्रामे सैयेन मतहातुम् ॥ 43 ॥

Vyāsa said: O (King! Thus making a firm resolve to send the powerful Raktabija to the battle with a vast army.

गृष्म उवाच

रक्तबीज महाबाहो गच्छ लं व समारंगोगे ।

कुक युद्धं महाभाग यथा ते बतलाथहितम् ॥ 44 ॥

Śumbha said: “O Raktabjā! You are a very powerful hero; therefore do you go to the battle. O Fortunate One! Fight as you are the strength of your forces.

त्कर्जित ज्ञाच

महाराज न ते कार्या चित्ता स्वत्यतारीय वा ।

अहमंनी हनिष्यापि करिष्यापि वरते तब ॥ ४५ ॥

पश्य मे युद्धचातुर्यं केवयं बाला सुराया ।

दार्ती तेर्ह करिष्यापि तिजेपांा समेर वतत् ॥ ४ ६ ॥

Raktabīja said: “O king! You need not be a bit anxious for this work. Certainly I will either slay Her or I will bring Her under your control. Please see my skill in this warfare; that Lady, favourite of the gods, is worth nothing; I will just now conquer Her and make Her your slave.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

<table><tr><td>व्यास उवाच</td></tr><tr><td>जूल्याध्य कुरक्रशे त्क्त्रीजो महापुरः ।</td></tr><tr><td>जगाम रथामक्हा स्वसैन्यपरिवारितः ॥ 47 ॥</td></tr><tr><td>हस्वयश्रयपादान्त्वन्देश परिवहितः ।</td></tr><tr><td>निर्वर्णाम रथाकडो देवीं शैलोपरि स्थितगम ॥ 48 ॥</td></tr></table>

Vyāsa said: O Best of Kurus! Thus saying, the powerful Raktabijā mounted on his chariot and went to the battle accompanied by his forces. The battalion consisted of cavalry, infantry, chariots and elephants. Thus surrounded he departed from the city for that Devi, seated on a mountain top.

तमागतं समालोक्य देवौशुकुमवादयत् ।

भवदं सर्वदैत्यानां देवानां मोदवधान् ॥ 49 ॥

शुत्वा शुबुस्वनं चोघं रक्तकीज्ञोतिवेगान् ।

गत्वा समीपे चामुपडां बभावे चवनं मृदु ॥ 50 ॥

Then the Devi, seeing him coming, blew Her conchshell; the Dānavas were terrified at that sound and the joy of the Devas increased. Hearing that sound Ratkatbija came very hurriedly to Cāmupāda and began to speak to Her sweetly.

रत्कारीज उवाच

वाले कि मां भीषधसि मत्वा तं व कातरं हिये ।

शढ़ान्देत नत्विकु वैसि किं धूपलोचनम् ॥ 51 ॥

"O Girl! Do you think me weak and thus want to terrify me with the sound of a conchshell? O Lean One! Have you taken me to be a Dhimralocana?

रक्तबीजीसि नामाद्रई तलसकाशिमहागत: ।

युद्धेष्ठ योत्सिकालापे सज्ञा भव भयं ने ॥ 52 ॥

O Sweet speaking one! My name is Raktabjja; I have come here for Thy sake. If Thou desirest to fight, be prepared; I am not a bit afraid of that.

पश्यादि मे वर्लं काने सूदा ये कातारत्स्वया ।

नाहं पैंकितानतेर्षा कुक युद्धं ययेच्छसि ॥ 53 ॥

युद्धाश सेविता: पूर्व नीतिशाखं श्रुत त्वया ।

पठितं चार्यविज्ञानं विद्वूर्तोही कृतास्ववा ॥ 54 ॥

साहित्यतन्त्रविज्ञानं चेदिस्त तव सून्ति ।

शुणु पे चचनं पध्य तत्थं प्रमितिबृहितम् ॥ 55 ॥

रसानां च नवानां वे हुावेव मुख्यतां गते ।

शुज्ञाकः शान्तिसो विद्वज्ञनसभासु च ॥ 56 ॥

O Dear! You saw those who were weak; I do not belong to that class. Therefore dost Thou fight as Thou likest and then Thou wilt be able to ascertain my strength. O Beautiful! If Thou didst serve the old persons before, if Thou hadst heard the science of politics and morals, if thou hadst studied the political economy, joined the assemblage of the Pandits or if Thou hast been well versed in literature and Tantras, then hear this my good counsel which will serve as a medicinal diet to Thee.

त्योः शूक्रात एवाटी नृपभावे प्रतिहितः ।

विष्णुर्वंक्षया सहातेवे स वासिव्या चतुरानः ॥ 57 ॥

Out of the nine sentiments, the Śrīgāra (Amorous love sentiments) and Śānti (Peace) are considered as the chief by the assemblage of the Paṇdits. Again out of these two, the love sentiment is the king.

शब्येत्र: शैलमुतया शक्रूर: सह श्रेते ।

वल्या युक्षो मुगो मृया ककोलाया च कोपोकाः ॥ 58 ॥

एवं सर्वे प्राणभूत: संयोगारसिका भृगम् ।

अप्राप्तभोगाविषया च यान्ये कतत नगः ॥ 59 ॥

Drenched with this sentiment, Vishnu lives with Kamalā; Brahmā, the four-faced, lives with Śāvitrī; Indra with Śacī and Śaṅkara resides with his wife Umā. The tree stands with creepers surrounding it, the deer lives with his female deer, the pigeon lives with the female pigeon; thus all the beings are very attached to this sentiment of remaining in couples.

भवन्ति यतयते वे मुका देवेन वक्षिताः ।

असंसाररसत्राते वक्षिता वज्चनापरेः ॥ 60 ॥

Those who cannot enjoy owing to certain disease or illness, they are deprived by Fate of such enjoyments.

मधुरतालपितुर्णे ततः शान्तितरें है ते ।

क्व तातं क्व च वैरायं वर्तमाने मनोभये ॥ 6 ॥ ॥

Those who are ignorant of this love sentiment in couples, they are deprived of it by the sweet

Book V Chapter XXVIII

juggler of words of the cheat and yet attached to the Peace sentiment.

तोपे कोटोच व तुर्धर्ष मोहे सति विनाशके ।

तस्माचमपि कल्याणि कुरक कानतं मनोहारूम ॥ 62 ॥

गुध्मं सुराणों जेतातं निर्गुध्मं चा महाजतम् ।

When delusion, the destroyer of Buddhi, the common sense, when the violent indomitable anger, greed, and lust arise, where, then, is the place for knowledge and dispassion? Therefore, O Auspicious One! Dost Thou marry the beautiful Sumbha or the powerful Niśumbha."

व्यास उवाच

इन्युक्चा रक्तबीजोर्सी विरास मुःश्रितः ॥ 6 3 ॥

श्रुत्वा जहास चामुखा कार्षिका चाधिक्का तथा ।

इति श्रीमद्वीभागवते महापुराणे पड्यकस्क्ये

सप्तिश्रेणीअवयः ॥ 27 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! When Raktabīja spoke all these words, standing before the Devi Kalikā, Ambikā and Cāmuṇda began to laugh.

Here ends the Twenty-seventh Chapter of the Fifth Book on the description of the war of Raktabīja in Srimaddevibhāgavatam, the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter XXVIII

CHAPTER XXVIII

On the Devi's Fighting

व्यास उवाच

कृत्वा हास्यं ततो देवी तमुवाच विश्यांपते ।

मेघाभ्यामीरा वाचा युक्तिपुर्णमिदं वचः ॥ १ ॥

पूर्वमेव मवा प्रोक्ते मन्दात्मार्कि विकलध्यसे ।

दूतस्यो यथायोगं वचनं हितसंयुतम् ॥ २ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The Devi, hearing thus, laughed and spoke the following reasonable words, in a voice deep as a rumbling cloud: "O Dull brained one! Already I told to that messenger before in reply to you; why then do you boast in vain?

सदृगो मम रूपेण बलेन विषवेन च ।

त्रितोक्यां यदि कोउपि स्यानं पर्ति प्रयुणोभ्यम् ॥ ३ ॥

If there be any such in the three worlds who can stand equal to Me in appearance, strength, and prosperity, I will marry him.

ब्रहि शुभ्यं निशुभ्यं च प्रतित्रा मे पुरा कृता ।

तस्मातुद्रव तिथा मां विहाई विप्रित्वलुक ॥ 4 ॥

Go to Sumbha and Niśumbha and inform them that thus I promised before; therefore let any of them conquer Me in battle and then marry Me according to the prescribed rules.

तं वै तदात्राया प्राप्तस्तस्य कार्यांविसिद्धये ।

संग्रामं कुक पातालं गच्छ वा पतिना सह ॥ 5 ॥

You have come here to execute the order of Śumbha; so either stand up and fight or fly to Pātala with your King.

व्यास उवाच

तच्छूला वचनं देया: स दैत्योउमर्षपूरितः ।

मुभोच तरसा बाणान्सिद्वल्योपरि दारुणान् ॥ 6 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Hearing the Devi's words, the Dānava was filled with anger and began to shoot dreadful arrows at once on the lion.

अधिका ताउछानीवीक्ष्य गमाने प्रजोगोपाम्न ।

चिच्छेद सायकैस्तीक्ष्मौनैवुड्स्तनाव क्षणात् ॥ 7 ॥

Ambikā, then, ready-handed, seeing the multitude of arrows coming in the sky like serpents, cut them off in a moment to pieces by Her sharp arrows.

अर्थैर्जयान विशिष्टे रक्तबीजं महातुर्मुम् ।

अधिका चारभिनुर्फै: कर्णाङ्टैः शिलाशिति: ॥ 8 ॥

The Devi, then, drawing Her bow, shot arrows sharpened on stone at the great Asura Raktabija.

देवीबाणघतः पायो भूछ्ठीमाय एथोपरि ।

पत्तिते रक्तबीजे तु हाहाकारो महानभूत् ॥ 9 ॥

Then that wicked Demon, thus shot at by the arrows, fell unconscious on the chariot. When he lay thus senseless, a great uproar arose amidst his army and the soldiers began to cry aloud saying "Alas! We all are killed."

सैनिकाशुकुगुः सर्वे हताः स्म इति चावुवन् ।

ततो बुबारवं शुत्वा शुम्भः परमदातगणम् ॥ 10 ॥

उद्योगं सर्वैसैन्यानां दैत्यानामादिदेश है ।

Then Šumbha, the king of the Asuras, hearing the sound of Boombā (a danger cry by hands and mouth) ordered all the Dānavas to be ready for the battle.

यृष्म उवाच

निवान्तु दानावा: सर्वे कांबोजा स्वबलैवृता:। 11 1 ॥

अन्येध्यातिकावः शृगः कालकेवा विशेषतः ।

Then Šumbha said: “Let all Kāmbojas* go to the battle with all their forces; let other powerful heroes, especially the Kālakeyas, too, who are very strong heroes, go up for the battle.

Note: Kamboja, name of a people and their country. They inhabited the Hindukusā mountain which separates the Giljit valley from Balkh, and probably extended upto little. Tibet and Ladakh. The Kālakeyas may be the Afridis.

व्यास उवाच

इत्याजदां बलं सर्वे रुष्मेचन च घतुलीर्वम् ॥ 12 ॥

निर्जीगम मद्यादिष्ट देवी समरमण्डले ।

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus ordered, all the fourfold army of Sumbha, viz., cavalry, infantry, elephants and chariots, went out, intoxicated for war, to the battle ground where the Devi existed.

तमागतं समालोक्य चधिडका दानवं बलम् ॥ 13 ॥

घण्टनादं चकाराणु भीषणं भयंदं मुड़ुः ।

The Devi Çandikā, seeing the Dānava forces coming near, made at once terrible sounds frequently.

ज्यास्वनं गढ़ुनादं च चकार जगद्विष्का ॥ 14 ॥

नेन नादेन सा जाता काली विस्तारितानना ।

The Ambikā Devi also made the sound with Her bowstring and blew Her conchshell. Kālī, then, shouted aloud opening Her mouth widely.

शुत्वा तिभिनंदो घोर सिंहो देल्याश्र वाहनम् ॥ 15 ॥

ज्ञानं सोउपि बलवाझ्ञनयभ्यमद्धुतम् ।

The powerful Lion, the Devi's Vāhana, hearing the terrible sounds, roared so loudly that the Dānavas were struck with strange terror.

तत्रिनादमुपश्रुच्य दानवा: क्रोधमूर्हिता: ॥ 16 ॥

सर्वे चिश्रिपुरस्त्राणि देर्वीं प्रति महावासतः ।

The powerful Dānavas, then, hearing that sound became impatient with anger and shot arrows after arrows on the Devi.

तस्मिनेवायते युद्धे टारुणे लोमड़र्णे ॥ 17 ॥

बहाद्यीनां च वेदानां गन्धाक्षीष्टिकां ययुः ।

The wonderful horrible battle, then, ensued, causing horripilation and the Śaktis of Brahmā and the other Devas began to come to Caṇdikā Devī.

यस्य देवस्य यदूर्य यथा भूषणवाहनम् ॥ 18 ॥

तादुपूरसदा देख्न: प्रययुः समराजूणे ।

The Devīs, the wives of the several Devas, then went, to the battle-field in their respective forms with ornaments and Vāhanas as generally on such occasions.

बहाणी वराटक्वा साक्षसूकमण्डलुः ॥ 19 ॥

अगगता बहाणा: शनिकन्द्रवाणीति प्रतिश्छिता ।

The Śakti (wife) of Brahmā named Brahmānī, mounting on the back of Her Swan came there with a string of beads and Kamandalu (a wooden waterpot used by ascetics).

वैष्णी गरुकाड़ा शुबुचक्रान्धरा ॥ 20 ॥

पचहस्ता समायात पीतावर्गविष्यिता ।

The Vaṣṇavī with Her yellow robes came there mounted on Garudā (the sacred bird of Viṣṇu, the carrier of Him) with conch, discus, club, and lotus in Her hands.

शाङ्गरी तु वृष्णकाठा निरुणलवधारिणी ॥ 21 ॥

अधिन्द्रप्राय देवी तथार्हिवलया शिका ।

The Devi Śaṅkari, the wife of Śiva, the Auspicious One, arrived on the back of Her Bull. The emblem of half-moon was on Her forehead while in Her hands She held snake, bracelet, and trident (Triśūla) and the sign of fearlessness for Her devotees.

कौभागी शिकाससंरका शफिरूस्ता वरान्ना ॥ 22 ॥

यूद्धकामा समयाता कार्तिकेयस्वलिधेण ।

The beautiful wife of Kártika, Kaumārī Devī,

Book V Chapter XXVIII

looking like Kärtika, came to fight there mounted on a peacock.

इन्द्रगी सुसुवदना सुश्रेतगजाछ्ना ॥ 23 ॥

वज्डस्तास्तिरोगबखा संग्रामिभूगुंची ययी ।

The fairfaced Indrānī, decked with ornaments on Her several limbs, came there to fight, with thunderbolt in Her hand, mounted on the elephant Airāvata.

वाराही सुकराकारा प्रौढ़ोतासना मता ॥ 24 ॥

नारसिंही नृसिंहस्य विष्नती सुर्गं वपुः ।

The Vārāhi Devi looking like a female boar, came also, seated on an elevated seat of departed souls (Preta). The Nārasinhi, resembling Nṛsirīha (the Man-Lion Incarnation) came there.

भाया च महिषाङ्ठा दण्डहन्ता भयप्रदा ॥ 25 ॥

सभ्यातात्य संग्रामे यमस्पा गृचिविस्ता ।

The wife of Yama, looking fearful like Yama arrived there on the battle-field smiling and with staff in Her hand and mounted on the back of a buffalo.

तर्वेय वाक्णी गस्ति: कौवेरी च मदोल्कटा ॥ 26 ॥

एर्वेशियालयाकाया ययु: स्वस्ववर्षैईता ।

Thus the wives of Kubera, Varuna, and other Devas came there with proper forms, Vahanas, ornaments, accompanied by their forces and all excited.

आगतातला: समालोक्य देवी मुदमवाप च ॥ 27 ॥

स्वस्था मुप्रदेरे देवा दैत्याक्ष भयमाययुः ।

ताथि: परिवृतसत्र ऋष्टो लोकशुक: ॥ 28 ॥

समागत्व च संग्रामे चधिडकामित्युवारा है ।

हन्यन्तामुर्ग: शीर्घे देवानां कार्यसिद्धये ॥ 29 ॥

निरुभधे वैद्युभचे च ये चान्ये दानवा: स्थितः।

Seeing them all, the Devi Ambikā became glad; the Devas, too, became peaceful and expressed their great joy the Dānavas were afraid at the sight of them, Śaṅkara, auspicious to all the beings, came there to the battle-field, surrounded by these goddesses and thus said to Capdikā: “Slay quickly all these Asuras, Śumbha, Niśumbha and all other Dānavas to serve the cause of the Devas.

हत्वा देत्यबलं सर्व कृत्वा च निर्भयं जगत् ॥ 30 ॥

स्वानि स्वानि च चिष्याप्नि समापच्छुं जनकः ।

Let all the goddesses destroy the Dānava race and thus free the world from dangers; they may, then, return to their own abodes.

देवा यजभुज: सन्तु ब्राह्मण यजने रतः ॥ 31 ॥

प्राणिनः सन्तु सन्तुतः सर्वे स्वावरजनाः ।

Let the Devas get their shares of sacrifices, let the Brāhmaṇas perform the sacrificial acts, and let all the creatures, moving or not moving, be happy.

गर्भ यान्तु तथोल्पात ईत्थक्ष तथा पुनः ॥ 32 ॥

यना: काले प्रवर्धन्तु कृधिवढ़मूला तथा ।

Let all calamities such as proceed from drought, excessive rain, rats, foreign invasion, locusts and birds, bats, etc., come to an end. Let the clouds bestow rains regularly and let the cultivation yield ample harvests. Note here that rats bring in plague."

व्यास उवाच

एवं बुवति देवेगे शड़हे लोकशड़हे ॥ 133 ॥

चिडकाया: शरीगतु निर्मात शक्तिच्छुता ।

भीषणाउतिषुचछा च शिवास्ताननितिदि ॥ 34 ॥

घोरकाणशय पडास्यमित्युवाच सिमतानना ।

Vyāsa said: O King! When Śaṅkara, the Lord of the gods and Auspicious to all, said thus, a wonderful female ensued out of the body of Candikā, very furious, horrible, with hundreds of jackals surrounding and yelling; then that Śakti of dreadful appearance, said to Śiva, smiling:

देवदेव द्वजायु एवं दैत्यनामियं प्रति ॥ 35 ॥

दूतत्वं कुरु कामारे बृहि सुभ्यं समत्कुलम् ।

निरुभयं च महोत्सवं चवनामम शक्र ३ १ ३ ६ ॥

मुक्चा त्रिविष्ट्यं यात यूयं पातालमागुं चै ।

O Deva of the Devas! Go quickly to the lord of the Daityas; do the duty of a messenger for us, O Destroyer of lust! O Śānkara! Tell that haughty Iustful Śumbha and Niśumbha to leave the Heavens and go to Pātāla.

देवा: स्वर्गे सुसंयातु तुगाष्ट् स्वासनं गुभम् ॥ 37 ॥

तपन्ति विविद्वे स्थानं यद्भागांश्र देवता: ।

जीवितेच्छा च युभाकं यदि ययानु महत्ता ॥ 38 ॥

तहिं गच्छत पाताल तरसा यत्र दानवाः ।

Let the Devas reign in the Heavens; Indra go to his own beautiful throne; let all the Devas go to their appropriate places in Heaven and receive their sacrificial offerings duly. And if the Demons desire to live, let them go quickly to the city of Pātāla where other Dānavas are dwelling.

अथवा बतनास्थाय युद्धेच्छा मरणाय चेत ॥ 39 ॥

तदा.उगच्छनु रुवन्यु मधिच्वा: विशितेन वः ।

Else, if they like to die, let them go quickly come to the battle-field and let their flesh be eaten up by Her jackals.

व्यास उवाच

तच्छ्वा वचने तथा शूलपाणिर्वल्यापिताः ॥ 40 ॥

गत्वाउं दैत्यजानं शुभ्मं सदस संस्थितम् ।

Vyāsa said: O king! Hearing Her words, Sūlapāni went quickly to Sumbha, the lord of the Dānavas, seated in the assembly, and said thus:

शिव उवाच

राजन्नृतींहर्मंबायासिपुर्जपत्नकारो हर: ॥ 4 1 ॥

स्वलसकाशमिहायाते हितं कर्तुं त्वाविडलम् ।

त्वल्बा सर्ग तथा घूमिं यूयं गच्छत सलवम् ॥ 4 2 ॥

याताले यत्र प्रक्रादो बलिश्छ खलिनां बरः ।

अथवा मरणेच्छा चेनह्रागच्छत सलवम् ॥ 4 3 ॥

संग्रामे को हनिष्यामि सर्वनिवाहमागुः वै ।

इत्युवाच महात्रजी युष्याकल्याणहतेव ॥ 44 ॥

"O king! I am Hara, the Destroyer of the Asura Tripura; now I have come to you for your good as a messenger of Ambikā Devī. Quit the Heavens and Earth and go quickly to Pātāla where reside the powerful Bali and Prahlāda, or if you like to court Death, come to fight; I will slay you all in a moment. O king! The Great Queen Ambikā Devī has sent me with these instructions for your welfare and information."

व्यास उवाच

इति दैत्यवरान्देवी वाक्यममुत्स्त्रकिंपम् ।

हितकृष्णव्यवित्या स प्रत्यवातश्छ शूलभृत् ॥ 45 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus conveying the nectar-like, beneficial words of the Devi to the chief Daityas, Śiva, the Holder of the trident, returned to his own place.

यवाउसौ प्रेरितः शम्भूर्तन्चे दानवान्यप्रति ।

शिववृतीति विद्यताना जाता विभुवनेत्रखले ॥ 46 ॥

The Śakti that sent Śambhu as a messenger to the Dānavas is known in three worlds as Śiva Dūti. तेजी क्षुवा वचो देखाः शकुंगेनें तु उक्रमः ।

युद्धाय निर्वयुः शीर्धे दंशिताः शाखणायाः ॥ 47 ॥

The Daityas, thus hearing the rigorous words of the Devi, put on their coats of armour and taking their bows and arrows quickly went out to fight.

ततसा रणागान्य चधिछकां ग्रात दानावाः ।

निजन्धुघ शरैसतीधैः: कर्णकुँधै: शिलापिति: ॥ 48 ॥

They came with great force to the battle field, stretched their bows to their ears and shot piercing arrows, sharpened on stone and tipped with iron, at the Caṇḍikā Devi.

कालिकामुपलितन्तून गदाशक्तिविद्यारितान् ।

कुर्वीतुं व्यचरत्त्र भक्षयन्ती च दान्यान् ॥ 49 ॥

The Kālika Devi, too began to strike some with the trident, some with Śakti weapons, and some with clubs and rent them asunder and devoured them all, and began to roam in the field.

कमपडतुलनाधेपनताणान् महावलान् ।

ब्रह्माणीं चाकोत्तित्र दानवान्यमरांगणे ॥ ५० ॥

Brahmānī began to pour water from Her Kamandalu on the powerful Dānavas in the battlefield and thus destroyed them.

माहेश्वी वृबाहस्त दिशूलेनारितरंस्ता ।

जशन दानवासंख्ये पातयामास भूतते ॥ ५१ ॥

Māheśvari, mounting on Her Bull gave violent blows by Her trident and thus lay them dead on the ground.

वैथावी चक्रपातेन गदाधातेन दानवान् ।

गतप्राणांशकागयु चोतमांगविवर्जित् ॥ 52 ॥

Book V Chapter XXIX

Vaisņavi, with the blow of Her club, took away the lives of many Daityas and striking them with Her Discus cut off the heads of several others.

पैरीविप्रभूरोग पातयामास भूतले ।

रेशालकरायातपीक्षितान्दैत्यपुगवान् ॥ 53 ॥

Indrānī hurled Her thunderbolt on the chief Dānavas, already struck by the feet of the elephant and lay them dead on the field.

वाराष्टीतुपड्योतेन दंद्याप्रापतेनेच च ।

जयान कोधयंसुक्ता शतशो देह्वदावन्त्य ॥ 54 ॥

Vārāhi, angrily killed thousands of Daityas with her sharp teeth and trunk.

तारसिंही नखैसौदीर्वेरितान्दैत्यपुंगवान् ।

प्रश्यन्ती चचाराजी ननाद च मुडुबुईः ॥ 55 ॥

Nārasinhi tore the strongest Dānavas with the sharp nails and, devouring them, walked to and fro and made dreadful sounds.

शिवदूतीं सगुहासं पातयामास भूतले ।

नोंख्यादाय वाणुका कालिका च त्वर्यात्मा ॥ 56 ॥

Śiva Dūti began to laugh hoarsely and laid the Dānavas flat on the field, when they were atonce devoured by Kalikā and Candikā.

शिखसंस्या च कोमी काणंहुःः शिलाशिनः।

निज्ञान रंगे शत्रुदेवानां च हितार वे ॥ 57 ॥

Kaumārī, seated on a peacock, by drawing the bowstring to Her ears hurled arrows sharpened on stone on the enemies and killed them to serve the cause of the gods.

वाश्णीपाशसम्बद्धान्दैत्यान्समयमस्तके ।

पातयामास तत्पृष्टे भूर्द्धितानातवेतनान् ॥ 58 ॥

एवं मातृगणेनाजातिर्वीर्यपराक्रमम् ।

मदितं दानवं सैन्यं पलायनपरं हृषभूत् ॥ 59 ॥

Vārṇuī tied down the Dānavas by Her Pāśa weapons in a face to face fighting; thus they lay down senseless on the ground. O King! Thus the Mātrkās, the goddesses, crushed the forces. Then the other powerful soldiers fled away terrified.

बुंबारबस्तु सुमहानभूतत्र बल्णवे ।

पुष्पबिहं तदा देवाश्रकुंदेव्या गणेशपरि ॥ 60 ॥

The “Boombā” danger cry rose then loudly; on the other hand, the Devas began to shower flowers on the Devís.

तच्छ्वा निवन्दे घोरं जयगब्द च दानावाः ।

रक्त्वीजशुकोपाणु इव्हा दैत्यान्यतायितान् ॥ 61 ॥

Hearing the distressful agonies of the Asuras and the shouts of victory of the Devas, Raktabīja, the chief of the Dānavas became very angry.

गर्जमान्तिस्वा देेवान्बीक्ष्व दैत्यो महाकलः ।

त्क्तीजसु तेजस्वी राणाभ्याययी ताद ॥ 6 2 ॥

Seeing specially the Dānavas flying away and the Devas shouting, that powerful Demon came hurriedly to the battle-field in anger.

सावुधी रक्षसिंहिः: कुर्वेक्राणास्वधदुतम् ।

उजाजगाम तदा देर्वीं क्रोधरनैक्षणीघातः ॥ 63 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे

पद्रभकन्देगेडणिविंगोअधाय: 11 28 11

Then with eyes reddened with anger, and with various weapons came before the Devi, Raktabija mounting on a chariot, and making sounds unusual with his bowstring.

Here ends the Twenty-eighth Chapter of the Fifth

Book on the description of the fighting of the

goddesses in Śrīmaddevibhāgavatam the Mahā

Purānam, of 18,000 verses by Maharsī Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter XXIX

CHAPTER XXIX

On the Killing of Raktabija

यास उचाच

वरदानिभंदं तस्य दानवस्य शिवार्थितम् ।

अत्यब्धुततरं राज्यबुणु ततप्रबंधीयह्म् ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Please hear attentively about the extraordinary boon that was given by

Mahādeva, the God of gods, to the great warrior Raktabhja.

तस्य देहात्कर्विदुर्घदा पतति भूतते ।

सभ्यतन्ति दैत्यास्तदुपास्ततयात्राकमा: ॥ 2 ॥

असंख्याता महावीर्यां दानवा रक्तस्म्यवा: ।

प्रभर्विविति क्रंण दानोउसत्यबढ़तो वर: ॥ ३ ॥

Whenever a drop of blood from the body of that great warrior will drop on the surface of the earth, immediately will arise innumerable Dānavas, equal in form and power to him; thus the Deva Rudra granted the Demon the wonderful boon.

स तेन वरादानेन दर्पितः क्रोधसंयुतः ।

अध्यमानातसा संखले हन्तु देवीं सकालिकाम् ॥ 4 ॥

Thus elated with the boon, he entered into the battle-field with great force in order to kill Kālika with Ambikā Devi.

स दूर्वा वैषाणीं शक्ति गरुकीपरि संश्विताम् ।

गल्क्या जघान दैश्येन्द्रस्तां वै कमललोचनाम् ॥ 5 ॥

Seeing the Vaisṇavi Śakti, lotus-eyed seated on the bird Garuda, the Demon struck Her with a violent weapon (named Śakti).

गद्या वारयामास गर्फिकः सा शफिरसंयुता ।

आतडयच्च चक्रेण रक्तबीजं महापुरम् ॥ 6 ॥

She then baffled the weapon by Her club and hurled Sudarśana disc on the great Asura Raktabīja.

रधागतदेहानु बहु सुसाव शोणितम् ।

वज्राहतिरिः: श्रृगाधिरांरा इव गैरिका: ॥ 7 ॥

Thus struck by the disc, blood began to ooze out from his body as the red stream of soft red sandstone comes out of a mountain-top.

यत्र यत्र यदा भूमौ पतत्ति तर्कविद्वः ।

समुतस्थुस्तदाकारः पुरुषाश्र सहस्रशः ॥ 8 ॥

Wherever on the surface of the earth drops of blood fell from his body, then and there sprang out thousands and thousands of demons like him.

ऐ.झी तमसुं घोरं घजेशाणिबजघन च ।

रत्कबीजं कृधारिखा नि:ससार च शोगिणा ॥ 9 ॥

Indrāni, the wife of Indra, became very angry and struck the terrible Raktabīja with his thunderbolt.

ततस्तल्लजाज्ञाता रत्कीजा हानेकशः ।

तद्वीयोर्षच तदाकारा: सायुधा युद्धुदर्भेदः ॥ 10 ॥

Streams of blood then began to ooze out form his body. No sooner the drops of blood fell from the Demon's body, than were instantaneously born from the blood many powerful Asuras, of similar forms, having weapons and hard to be conquered in battle.

बह्वाणी बह्वायईन कृपिता हहनदभूभूम ।

माहेश्री त्रिगूलेने दाययामास दानवम् ॥ १ १ ॥

Brahmānī then becoming enraged stuck at him with the staff of Brahmā with greater force. Mahesvarī rent the Dānava asunder by striking him with Her trident.

नारसिंही नखाघातैरत विद्याथ महासुरम् ।

अहन्नुपडयातेन कुंद्रा तं गछासाधम् ॥ 12 ॥

कौभारी च त्वा शक्यता वसुस्येनमताडयत् ।

सोप्रि कुब्दः शगसारित्विंभेद निजीर्शेतु ता: ॥ 13 ॥

गदाशक्तिग्रहरितु मानु: सर्वो: पृथवसुयक् ।

शक्तयवं तशयातेर्विथ्युस्तत्यकोपितः ॥ 14 ॥

Nārasinjhi pierced the Asura with Her nails; Vārāhī struck at him with Her teeth. Then the Dānava becoming angry shot at them all with sharpened arrows and pierced them all. Thus when the Mātrkā Devīs were pierced by the club and other various weapons of that great Asura, they got very angry and pierced the Dānavas in return with shots of arrows. Kaumārī, too, struck at his breast with Her weapon, named Śakti. The Dānava then got angry and hurled on them multitude of arrows and began to pierce them.

तस्य सश्वाणि चित्रद्रेच चधिडका स्वशैर: जितः ।

जवानान्यैक्ष विशिष्ठेयं देवी कुविता भुगणम् ॥ 15 ॥

O King! The Caṇḍika Devi, getting angry, cut off his weapons into pieces and shot violently at him other arrows.

तस्व देहाच्च सुसाव कधिर बहुधा तु यत् ।

तस्मात्तस्वरुगाः शूरा: प्रगुतासन्सहस्वः ॥ १ ६ ॥

O King! Thus struck by severe blows, when blood began to flow in profuser quantities from his body, thousands and thousands of Dānavas resembling Raktabijā sprang out instantly from it.

Book V Chapter XXIX

रत्कबीजैर्गेब्ज्ञापतं द्वितीयसमुद्धेः ।

सत्रादै: सायुधैः कामं कुर्वैद्धरुद्धमद्धुन् ॥ 17 ॥

So much so, that the heavens were all covered over with Raktabijtas that sprang up from the blood. They all covered all over their bodies with coats of armour, began to fight terribly with weapons in their hands.

प्रहरंतक्ष तान्ट्यहा रक्तबीजान्नेकशः ।

यथभीतः सुगतेसंविधणणा: शोककर्षितः ॥ 18 ॥

Then the Devas, seeing that innumerable Raktabijas were striking the Devi, became very much frightened and were distressed with sorrow.

कंयमदा क्षयं दैत्या गमिध्यन्ति सहस्रणः ।

महाकाया महावीयीं दानवा तक्सम्बवाः ॥ 19 ॥

They began to talk with each other with sorrowful countenances that thousands and thousands of huge bodied warriors were springing from the blood. These were all very powerful; so how could they be destroyed!

एकैय चौड़काउग्रानित तथा काली च माततः ।

तृगाभिर्वानतः संबैं जेतलाः कटभेव तत् ॥ 20 ॥

In this battle-field there were now left only the Mātrikās, Kālikā, and Caṇḍikā. It would be certainly extremely difficult for them to conquer all these Dānavas.

निरूपभो वारश रूपभो वा सहसा बनसंकृतः ।

आगामधिति संग्रामे ततोउनर्थ महान्यचेत् ॥ 21 ॥

And if at that moment, Sumbha and Niśumbha were to join them with his army, certainly a great catastrophe would occur.

व्यास उवाच

एवं देवा भयोद्विनाड़ितातामापुर्महन्ताम।

यदा तदाधिक्वां प्राक काली कमललोचनाम ॥ 22 ॥

वायण्डे कृक विस्तीर्णे वदर्थे वलिता भृम्गे ।

मच्छसपातस्मघृतं कर्धिरं पिब सत्वा ॥ 23 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! When the Devas were thus extremely anxious, out of terror, Ambikā Devi said to the lotus-eyed Kālī: “O Cāmuṇḍā! Open out your mouth quickly, and no sooner I strike

Raktabija with weapons, you would drink off the blood as fast as it runs out of his body.

भक्षयन्ती चर रणे दानवानघ कामतः ।

ह्निथ्वायि शरैस्तीक्षौर्णद्विसमुसलैस्तथा ॥ 24 ॥

Instantly I will kill those Dānavas sprung form the blood with sharpened arrows, clubs, swords and Mūsalas; and you would then be able to devour them all at your will, and, then, roam in this field as you like.

तथा कुरु विशालाश्रि यान तद्धिपरस्य च ।

बिदुर्मारं यथा भूयां न पतेदीप सम्पतन् ॥ 25 ॥

O Large-eyed One! You would drink off all the jets of blood in such a way that not a drop of it escapes and falls on the ground.

भक्ष्यमाणास्तद दैत्य व नोचल्यन्ति चापरे ।

एवमेभां क्षयो नूनं भविष्यति न चान्यथा ॥ 26 ॥

And then when they will all be devoured, no more Dānavas would be able to spring. Thus they will surely be extirpated; otherwise they will never be destroyed.

घातयिष्याम्यं दैत्यं लयं भक्षय च सत्वरा ।

विचन्ती क्षततं सर्वे चत्मानानर्शिसंक्षये ॥ 27 ॥

Let me begin to strike blows after blows on Raktabija and you better drink off quickly all the blood, being intent on destroying the forces.

इत्यं दैत्यमयं कृत्वा दत्ता राज्यं सुरातयम् ।

इज्ञाय सुसिंत्यं सर्वे गायिष्यायो यथासुखम् ॥ 28 ॥

O Cāmunḍaī Thus, the Dānavas being all exterminated, we will hand over to the Indra, the lord of the Devas, his Heavens without any enemy; and, thus, we can go peacefully and happily to our own places.

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्ताप्रिक्वया देवी चामुपडा चण्डविक्रम।

पपी च हातर्ज सर्वे रक्तवीज्ञगरीरजम् ॥ 29 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! The Cāmunā Devī, of furious strength, hearing thus the Devi's words began to drink the jets of blood coming out of the body of Raktabija.

अधिका ते जयानागु खडूगेन मुस्लेन च ।

चखाद देशगकलांशुपुखा तान्कूगेशेरी ॥ 30 ॥

Srimaddevibhāgavatam

The Devi Ambikā began to cut the Demon's body into pieces and Cāmuṇḍā, of thin belly, went on devouring them.

सोरी कुब्द्री यद्याश्रीमुपुर्भां सम्पातयन् ।

तथापि सो परवायुः झलर्न तमपश्वसन् ॥ 31 ॥

Then Raktabīja, becoming angry, struck Cāmuṇḍā with her club. But though She was thus hurt severely, She drank off the blood and then devoured all the limbs thereof.

येऊ्ये कदिरवा: कूरा तर्फजीया महावलाः ।

तेपि निष्पातिताः सर्वे भक्षिता गतशोणिताः ॥ 32 ॥

O King! Thus Kālīkā Devi drank off the blood of all other powerful wicked Dānava Raktabīja that sprang out of the blood. Ambikā thus destroyed them.

कृत्रिमा भक्षिताः सर्वे सत्य स्थापाविकोसुरः ।

सोर्पि प्रपातितो हत्या खड्योनातिविखणिताः ॥ 33 ॥

रक्तबीजे हते रौद्र ये चान्ये दानवा रणे ।

पलायनं ततः कृत्वा गतातसे भयकमित्या: ॥ 34 ॥

Thus, all the Dānavas, created out of the blood were devoured; then, there was left, lastly, the real Raktabīja Ambikā Devi then cut him asunder into pieces by Her exe and thus killed him. Thus, when the dreadful Raktabīja was slain in the battle, the Dānavas fled away trembling with fear.

हाहिति विबुवंतसे रुभ्यं प्रोचुः सुविहृतः ।

स्टिरात्कन्देश्व श्वितानखा विवेलसः ॥ 35 ॥

Without any weapons, covered all over their bodies with blood, and void of consciousness they uttered dumb confounded “Alas! Alas! What has happened, what has happened.”

राजत्रविक्वा रक्तक्रीजोसी विनिपातितः ।

वापुपडा तस्य देहानु घपी सर्वे न गोशिताम् ॥ 36 ॥

Thus crying, they told their King Śumbha thus: "O King of Kings! Ambikā Devi has killed Raktabīja and Cāmuṇḍā has drunk off all their blood.

ये चान्ये दानवाः शूरा वाहनेनातिरेहास ।

सिंहेन नित्रतः सर्वे काल्या च भक्षिताः परे ॥ 37 ॥

The carrier (Vāhana) of Devi, the powerful

ferocious Lion killed other powerful warriors and Kālī devoured the remaining soldiers.

वयं त्वां कथितुं राजजगता युद्धरेषितम् ।

चारितं च तथा देव्या: संगमे परमाद्धान् ॥ 38 ॥

O Lord of the Dānavas! We have fled and come to you to give the news of the battle and to describe the wonderful doings of that Caṇḍikā Devi in the battle-field.

अजेवेयं महाराज सर्वथा देत्यन्दानेः ।

गन्धर्वसुग्यक्षेध उपगोरागसर्षे: ॥ ३ ९ ॥

O King! In our opinion, no one will be able to conquer that Lady, be he a Daitya, Gandharva, Asura, Yakṣa, Pannaga, Cāraṇa, Rāksāsa, or an Uraga.

अन्यास्त्रियाना तेव्य इन्द्राणीप्रमुखा धृगम् ।

व्यध्याना महाराज वदिर्विपद्यैताः ॥ ४ ० ॥

O King of Kings! The other Goddesses, Indrānī and others, have come to the battle, on their own carriers respectively and are fighting with various weapons.

ताथि: सर्व हृत सेन्यं दानवानां वरायुधैः ।

रक्तबीजोंपि राजेन्द्र तस्या दिविनपिततः ॥ 4 1 ॥

O Lord of the Dānavas! The Dānava forces are all slain by them with the excellent weapons in their hands. Even Raktabīja has been slain in no time.

एकाजपि दुःसहा देवी किं पुनस्ताधिर्यिता ।

सिंहोरिप हिन्द संग्रामे राक्षसान्यितमध्यः ॥ 42 ॥

That Lion, of indomitable prowess, killed the Rāksaṣas in the battle; The Devī alone is hard to conquer; how much more would it be impossible to conquer Her, when She has been joined with other goddesses.

अतो विचार्यं सचिवैर्विषुक्त नित्रधीयताम् ।

न वैरमवना युक्ते सन्धिरेव सुखप्रद: ॥ 43 ॥

So consult with the ministers and do what is reasonable. In our opinion it is better to make treaty with Her and quit your cinnity.

आध्रधीमेदतद्धिलं यवगी हन्न त्रास्तान् ।

रक्त्रीजोरिपि निहत: पौर्त तस्यापि शोगितिण् ॥ 44 ॥

Book V Chapter XXIX

O King! Think over the fact that Lady destroyed all the Dānavas and at last drank off the blood of Raktabīja and at last killed him.

अन्य निपातिता द्विण: संग्रामेप्रिक्वका नृप ।

चानुंदया च मांसं वै भवितं सकलं रणे ॥ 4 5 ॥

What on earth can be more wonderful than this? O King! The Devi Ambikā killed all the other Daityas and Cāmunḍā devoured their blood, flesh, and all.

वरं पातालगमनं तस्या: सेवा5थावा चरा ।

न तु युद्धं महाराज कार्यमंविकया सह ॥ 4 6 ॥

Considering all this, it is now better for us to serve the Devi Ambikā or fly away to Patāla. No more fighting is desirable.

न नारी प्राकृतता होया देकावार्यैसाधिनी ।

मार्येय प्रवला देरी क्षयन्तीयमुपचिता ॥ 47 ॥

She is not an ordinary woman; she is Mahā Māyā, there is not the least doubt in this. Only to serve the cause of the Gods, She has manifested Herself and is now destroying the Rāksasas' race.

व्यास उवाच

इति तेषां वचतल्यं श्रुचा कालविधोहितः ।

पुयुषुः प्रत्युवाचेदं शुचपः प्रसूक्रितात्पर: ॥ 48 ॥

Vyāsa said: Hearing thus, Śumbha got confounded by Kāla (Death), as his end was coming nigh, and said the following words, his lips quivering with anger.

शुभ्म उवाच

यूयं गच्छत पातालं शरणं वा भयातुतः ।

हनिष्यान्यहमदैय तां च तांश सपुवतः ॥ 49 ॥

"You are struck with fear; so you all take the refuge of Candikā or fly down to Pātāla; but I will kill Her with all my exertion and effort.

वित्ता सर्वानुसानाजी कृतावार्ज्यं सुयुकलम् ।

कथं नारीभदोघिनः पातालं प्रविश्याम्बून ॥ 50 ॥

I conquered all the hosts of Devas and I have enjoyed their kingdoms; shall I now, out of the fear of one Lady, fly and enter into the Pātāla.

निहत्य पार्वदानस्वान् रत्क्रीजमुखान् रणे ।

प्राणाजाणाय गच्छामि हिला किं विपुलं यशः ॥ 51 ॥

All my attendants, Raktabija and other heroes, are now slain in the battle and is it possible that I will now fly away out of the sake of preserving my life only.

मरणे त्विनिवार्ये श्राणिनां कालकल्पित्यम् ।

तद्यं जन्मोपोषन्तं त्यजेनको तुर्वर्णे यशः ॥ 52 ॥

See! The death of all the beings is ordained by Kāla and it is unavoidable. No sooner a being is born, he is liable to the fear of death.

निरूपभर्ते गतिय्यामि यशाकडो रणातिरे ।

हत्वा तामागतिथ्यामि नागतिथ्यमि चान्यथा ॥ 53 ॥

How can a man, then, out of fear of death, quit all his name and fame? O Niśumbha! I will now go immediately to the battle, mounting on my chariot and will return after slaying Her in battle. त्वं तु सेनायुतो वीर पाणिण्याहो भवस्य मे ।

तरसा तां गरेस्तीक्षौर्निरीं नय यमालये ॥ 54 ॥

And if I cannot kill Her, I will not then return any more. O Best of warriors! Better stand on my side with all your forces and kill that Lady in no time, with sharp arrows.

निरुभ्म सजाच

अहमदा इनिष्यामी पाला दुर्धी च कालिकाम् ।

आगमिष्याम्बट शीर्घे गृहीला तामथीविकाम् ॥ 55 ॥

Nisūmbha said: "Today I will go to the battle and slaying that Kālīkā, will shortly return here with Ambikā.

मा चित्तां कुक राजेन्द्र वसाकायस्तु कारणे ।

क्वेषा बाला क्व मे बाहुर्वीर विश्वशंकाम् ॥ 56 ॥

O King! Do not think at all for that Lady; see my world-conquering strength and look at that weak woman; there is a vast difference.

त्वक्वातिं विश्वां धानभूश्व योगानुभात्मान ।

आनयिष्यान्छे कामं मानिर्मी मानसंयुताम् ॥ 57 ॥

Cast aside your this great mental anxiety and trouble. Enjoy, O Brother, excellent things. I will bring that dignified Lady with all honours before you.

मायि तिश्रित ते राजन युर्फे गमनं रणे ।

गत्वाड्मानयिष्यामि तवार्थे वै जयधियम् ॥ 58 ॥

O King! You ought not to go to the battle when I am alive. I will presently go to the fight and bring for you that Lady as a sign of our victory.

ध्यास उवाच

इत्युक्त्वा भारतं ज्येषुं कनीयावलपहितः ।

रथाभास्थाय विपुलं सनझदः स्वबलावृतः ॥ 59 ॥

जगाम तस्मा तूर्णं संगरे कृतमंगलः ।

संस्तुतो बन्स्युतैंध सायुधः सपरिक्रतः ॥ 60 ॥

इति शीमद्वीभाग्यतो महापुराणो प्रह्मस्कन्धे

एकानोविशोर्धया: 11 29 || 1

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus saying, the younger brother, proud of his own strength went hurriedly to the battle-field, mounting on his big chariot. He was protected all over is body his coat of armour and he was well provided with various weapons and all other accoutrements of war. The bards began to sing hymns to him and various other propitious ceremonies were being performed.

Here ends the Twenty-ninth Chapter of the Fifth

Book on the killing of Raktabíja in

Śrimaddevibhāgavatam, the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

CHAPTER XXX

On the Killing of Niśumbha

यास उवाच

निरूपभो निद्धयं कृत्वा मरणाय जयाय वा ।

सोहाम: सबल: शूरी रोणे देहीपुणयची ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus making a firm resolve that there would be either victory or death, the great warrior Niśumbha went to fight with the Devi, with great excitement and with all his forces.

नामाजाग सृष्मोर्धि स्वबलेन समावृतः ।

प्रेशकोंअप्रूर्णे राजा संग्रामसमपिडतः ॥ २ ॥

Šumbha, too the Lord of the Daityas, accompanied by his forces, went after Niśumbha; Šumbha knew full well the rules of warfare; therefore he remained a witness there.

नागने संस्थिता देयास्तदाउप्रभटलातूनः ।

दिदूक्ष्वस्तु संगमे सेन्द्रा यक्षनगातताः ॥ ३ ॥

Indra and the other Devas and Yakṣas, all stationed themselves in the celestial space, eager to see that fight, covered with clouds.

निरुभ्योद्ध रणे गत्या धनुराद्य शार्डैक्म ।

चकार शरबृष्टि स भीयवझगद्धिकम ।। ४ ॥

Nisūmbha came to the field, and, taking the strong bow made of horns, began to shoot arrow after arrows at the Divine Mother with the object of frightening Her.

मुब्जतं शरबालातिन सिगूभ्यं चधिडका राणे ।

वैश्यादाय धनु:श्रेडं जहास सुखरं गुड़ुः ॥ 5 ॥

Seeing Niśumbha with his excellent bow, shooting arrows, Candikā began to laugh frequently.

उवाच कालिका देवी पश्य मुत्बल्मेपतेयो: ।

मरणायगती कालि मत्समीयमिहाछ्ना ॥ 6 ॥

दूर्शा देत्यवधी घोरं रक्तबीजाययं तथा ।

जयागां कृतस्त्वेती मोहिती मम मायया ॥ 7 ॥

With a soft slow voice She spoke to Kalikā: "O Kāli! See their foolishness! They have come before me, courting death. They are so much deluded by My Māyā, that they yet expect victory when they have already witnessed the death of Raktabjā and many Dānavas.

आणा बलवती होना न जाहित नरं क्वचित् ।

भर्न हतं बलं नहं गतपक्षे चित्रनेनम् ॥ 8 ॥

आशापाशनिबद्धौ ही युद्धाय समुपगतो ।

निह्नल्यो मया कालि रणे शुभ्यनिरुभ्यकी ॥ 9 ॥

Hope is so very that it never quits a man. How wonderful is this that some of their armies are destroyed, some are wounded, some are rendered senseless, some made powerless, some have fled; seeing all these, yet, they have come to fight, as it were, fastened by the cord of hope of victory. O

Book V Chapter XXX

Kāli! Today I will certainly slay Niśumbha and Śumbha.

आसम्रमणावेति संप्राती देवभोहिती ।

पश्यतां सर्वदैवानां हनिष्याम्यमहम तो ॥ 10 ॥

Their death is nigh; deluded by the Daivi Maya, they have come to Me. Therefore, in the face of all the Devas, I will kill them to day.

व्यास उवाच

इतुसुक्वा कालिकां चण्डी कणाकृशगेतकरैः ।

छाव्यामास तस्या निशुभ्यं पुरतः स्थितम् ॥ 1 1 ॥

द्रानवोदी प्रशंसल्याधिछ्छेद्व निशितः शरैः ।

तयोः परस्यं युद्धं बध्वातिसंचायकम् ॥ 12 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus saying, and suddenly drawing Her bow, Candi covered Niśumbha, in front, all over with a multitude of arrows. Niśumbha, too, cut off those arrows into pieces by his sharp arrows; thus the fight became more and more dreadful between them.

कैसरी कैगलानिति धुवानः कैन्यसागम् ।

गह्यामास बलवान्सरर्गे वाणीय चथा ॥ 13 ॥

At this time, the lion of Bhagavati, came down upon the forces, quivering his manes, like a powerful elephant going down into a lake.

नरईदैन्तप्रहीरसु दानवान्युतः स्थितान् ।

चयाद च विशीणाईन्न गवानिव मोत्केवान् ॥ १४ ॥

By his nails and teeth, he tore asunder the bodies of the Dānavas that fell before him and devoured them, as if they were infatuated elephants.

एवं विद्यमयाने तु सैन्ये केसरीणा तव ।

अभ्यधाविज्ञाम्बो 5व कृष्टवराकामुकः ॥ 1 5 ॥

अन्येपि कृष्टा देलेन्द्रा देवी हनुपायवयुः ।

सन्द्धदानसना रक्तेना झनेकाः ॥ 16 ॥

That lion thus crushing down the soldiers, Nüsumbha came forward hurriedly, drawing his excellent bow. Hundreds of other generals of the Dānavas came up there to kill the Devi, biting their lips and with their eyes reddened with anger.

ततजगान तस्या गुष्पः सैन्यस्मापुतः ।

नित्य कालिकां कोपादुरुहीतुं जगदधिकवम् ॥ 17 ॥

In the meantime Śumbha killed Kālīkā and

came very hurriedly there with his forces to capture the Divine Mother.

तत्राग्य ददुराजावंविका च पुरःस्थिताम् ।

गैरससुतां कानतां श्रंगारससंयुताम् ॥ 18 ॥

Coming to the battle field Sumbha saw that the Divine Mother was standing before him; though She was looking very beautiful, fit for love sentiment, yet She was filled also with the sentiment of fiery wrath.

तां वीक्ष्य विमुतपाणीं शैतैकव्यसरुन्दीम् ।

सुरत्कनयं तस्यां ठोधोरात्क्षणां तथा ॥ १९ ॥

At that time the large eyes of Bhagavatī, the Beautiful in the three worlds, though naturally red, looked more red due to wrath.

विवाहेच्छो परित्यज्य जयाणां दूततस्तथा ।

मरणे निश्रयं कृत्वा तत्थावाहित्कामुईकः ॥ 20 ॥

When Śumbha saw Her lovely feature, the desire to marry Her and the hope of victory all vanished away from his mind; and he stood there with bow in his hand, firmly holding in his mind that he would die.

तं तथा दानवं देवी सिमन्तुर्धिंदः चवः ।

वधापे श्रुण्वतां तेषां द्वैत्यानां रणमस्तके ॥ 2 1 ॥

Seeing the Dānava in that state, She smiled and began to say, so that all the Dānavas could hear. गच्छधं पामता युंपालतां वा जलाणविम ।

जीतिज्ञां स्थितं कृता व्यक्तात्रजेवापुधानि च ॥ 2 2 ।

“O Wretched Fools! If you all want to live, quite all your weapons here, go to the Pātāla or to the middle of the ocean.

अथवा मच्छागतहतमुणा रणाशि ।

प्राय स्वर्गसुखं सर्वे क्रीईतु विनतज्ञतः ॥ 23 ॥

Or be slain in the battle by My arrows and go to heavens and enjoy there without any fear all the enjoyments and sports there.

काततरंचे च शूरत्वं न भववेय वरवंथा ।

द्वाद्यभयदानं वै यानु सर्वे यथासुपुखम् ॥ 24 ॥

Weakness and heroism, both cannot be expected at one and the same time and at the same individual; therefore I am ordering you to dispel

your fears. Now go wherever you find your ease and happiness.

व्यास उवाच

इत्याकर्णं वचस्तस्यां नित्यमो मटगर्वितः ।

नित्रितं खद्गमादाय चर्मे चैवाहचन-ऋकम् ॥ 25 ॥

धावामान्तु तसास्त्रसिना सिंहं मदोक्कटम् ।

जयानातिवलान्मृदिन धाययझादिवक्षाम। 26 ॥

Vyāsa said: O king! Hearing these words of the Devi, that haughty Niśumbha ran forward, holding in his hand the sharpened axe and shield adorned with eight Candras (embossed) and firstly struck with sword violently on the proud lion; then, whirling with great force that sword, hurled it upon the Divine Mother.

ततो देवी खरवाद्या वंचवित्याउसिपातनम् ।

ताड्यामास तं बाहोभूले पर्युना तदा ॥ 27 ॥

The Devi, then, thwarted off the blow of the sword by Her club and struck at his arm by Paraśu.

खडेरोन निर्हत: सोडपि बाहुमूले महामदः ।

संलम्य वेदनां घुवो जाघान चोषड़का तदा ॥ 28 ॥

साउपि घणटावनं घोरे चकार घायदं नृणाम् ।

The warrior Niśumbha, thus struck at his arm, patiently bore that hurt and struck at Candikā by his axe. The Devi then made such a terrible noise of bells that all the Daityas were struck with terror.

परी पुनः पुनः पानं नियुर्णमं हनुनीच्छती ॥ 29 ॥

पूर्व परसरं युद्धं बभूवातिभप्रमुद्रम् ।

देवानां दानवानां च परस्पराजवीर्णम् ॥ 30 ॥

Then She, desiring to kill Niśumbha, began to drink nectar frequently. O King! Thus the terrible fight went on between the Devas and Dānavas both trying to defeat the other party.

पलादा: पक्षिणः कृतः सामेवाश्र जयकुाः ।

ननुशुशासित्नुशा गृधा: केनाका शयासः ॥ 31 ॥

राण्यपणिति भृथिष्यतितानुरवर्षकैः ।

कौरास्वावसंयुवर्तैर्गजाश्वेदेसंकुला ॥ 32 ॥

पतितान्दावनावन्दूखा निर्गुभ्योतिरुकाश्विततः ।

प्रयथी चणिकडा तृर्णबुद्याबाद्य द्रकाणाम ॥ 33 ॥

Then began to dance ir the battle-field, the cruel varacious dogs, jackals, vultures, herons, crows

and other birds, very much gladdened. The battlefield was drenched in blood and the dead carcasses of innumerable Dānavas, elephants, and horses were strewn around. Nisūmbha, then, seeing the Dānavas dead on the field, became very angry and ran forward with his terrible club before the Devi.

सिंहं जघान गटया मरतके मदगवितैः ।

प्रहृत्य च सितं कृत्वा पुनर्वैभिवाडवत् ॥ 34 ॥

सापि ते कुपिताऊतील निषुभं पुतः स्थितम् ।

प्रहन्तं समीक्षाय देवी वचनमवर्षित् ॥ 35 ॥

That proud Asura struck first at the head of the lion with that club and laughed again and again and struck the Devi with that same club. The Devi, too, got very angry seeing Niśumbha before Her and striking at Her. She then spoke thus:

देव्यवाच

सिट्ठ मंदमते ताव्हायावलड्वारमिदं तव ।

ग्रीवायां ग्रेरायाम्यसाद्र-तासिर यमसदनम् ॥ 36 ॥

O You Stupid! Wait till I sever your head from your body by this axe. Soon you will be sent unto death with your head severed off your body.

व्यास उचाच

इत्युक्चना तरसा देवी कृपाणेन समाहिता ।

चिच्छेद मस्तकं तस्य निजुम्भस्याच चिष्ठकां ॥ 37 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus saying, the Caṇḍikā Devī instantly cut off the head of Niśumbha by Her axe with great caution.

स हिजमस्तको देया कबन्धोर्जीव दारूण: ।

वधाम च गदापाणिशासयदेवतागणान् ॥ 38 ॥

The head thus severed from the body by the blow of the Devī, the headless Demon began to roam there with great force with club in his hand. The Devas then got very much frightened.

देवी तस्य त्रिरितांणीशिक्छेद्व चरणी करी ।

पपातोल्यात ततः पपी नातानुः पवर्तेनोपः ॥ ३९ ॥

The Devī, then, cut off the hands and feet of that headless Demon with sharpened arrows.

तत्मिभिपतिते दैतेः निर्गुभे भीभिविक्रोः ।

हाहाकारो महानासीतलै-वे भयकमीयते ॥ 40 ॥

Book V Chapter XXX

That vicious wretch fell down lifeless, on the ground like a mountain. The powerful Daityas Niśumbha being thus killed, a great uproar arose amidst his panic stricken forces.

व्यक्त्वासप्रधानि सर्वांगी सैनिका: क्षताज्यतुतः ।

जगयुर्धावरां सर्वे कुबीणा राजमन्त्रिय् ॥ 41 ॥

The soldiers, covered all over their bodies with blood, left all their weapons in the field, began to make Boombā sound (a piteous cry with mouth and hands as sign of danger) and fled away to the king Śumbha.

तानागतानुसंप्रेक्ष्य शुभ्यः शज्ञिपृद्व्नः ।

प्रच्छ क्व निशुमोजसी कवं भग्न: पलायिता: ॥ 4 2 1

He, the tormentor of the foes, then asked them coming: "Where is Niśumbha now? Why have you fled away from the field?"

तच्छुत्वा बचनं ग्रासते प्रोषुः प्रगता भ्रुगम् ।

राजसेन निद्रातो भाता शेते समस्यूर्दिन ॥ 4 3 ॥

Thus hearing the King's words, they bowed down and said: "O King! Your brother Niśumbha is lying dead on the battle-field.

तया निपातिता: शूगा ये च तेध्यज्ञानुगा: ।

वय त्वां कवितुं सर्वे वृत्तानं समुपगाता: ॥ 4 4 ॥

O King The Devi killed all the Dānava warriors that attended your brother; only we are left and have come here to give you the information.

निरुभामो निद्रास्तरस तथा चण्डिकवासुघान ।

न हि युद्धस्य कालो3घ तत राजजगंगोगे ॥ 4 5 ॥

O King! Niśumbha has been killed by the weapons of the Devi. So we think you ought not to go to the battle.

देवकार्य समुहिष्य कारपीय परमांगना ।

हन्तु देत्यकुलं नूनं प्रापतीत परित्थित्य ॥ 4 6 ॥

Know this as certain that the Lady, the Highest Cause of this Universe has come here to destroy the Dānavas, the object being to serve the cause of the Gods.

नैया प्राकृतयोषिव देवीशक्तिरन्तमा ।

अर्विव्यचरिता क्वापि दुर्जेया द्वैतरीपि ॥47 ॥

नानारूपधरातील मायामृत्यिशारत ।

विश्वभूषणा देवी सर्वायुधरा शुभा ॥ 48 ॥

This Lady is not an ordinary woman; She is the Supreme Force; Her doings are inconceivable; what more can be said than the fact that the Devas never can know Her! This Devi can assume various forms; She is the origin of Māyā; She is very clever; She is adorned with various ornaments and is holding various weapons in Her hands.

गह्ना गृहवर्ति कालरात्रिविपाया ।

अपारपारगा पूर्णी सर्वलक्षणसंयुता ॥ 49 ॥

Her doings are incomprehensible; She is like a Second Night of Dissolution (at the end of the world); She is Perfect, endowed with all auspicious signs, capable to go beyond the insurmountable.

अन्तर्रक्षित्या द्विवासी सुवंकृतुकोभावः ।

देवकार्य च कुवर्णी श्रीदेवी परमादुताम ॥ 50 ॥

This wonderful Devi is serving the cause of the gods and the Devas from the sky are singing hymns to Her.

पलायनं पोर धर्मः सर्वथा देहराक्षणम् ।

रक्षिते कित देशैस्म्य-कालो5स्मसुखताक्षते ॥ 51 ॥

O King! It is now your paramount duty to fly away and save your life; if you live, you may have the chance for gaining the victory when time will turn out favourable; there is no doubt in this.

संग्रामे विजयो राजन्यविता ते न संग्रतः ।

कालः कंपोत बलिनं समये निर्विवतं कवित् ॥ 52 ॥

It is Time that makes a strong man weak; and it is that very Time that makes that weak man strong again and stimulates him for victory.

तं पुनः सकलं कृत्वा जयायोपद्धाति हि ।

दातारं याचकं कालः करोति समये कवित् ॥ 53 ॥

भिश्छूकं धनदातारं करोति समयान्तरे ।

विषयु: कालवजो नृनं बढ़ा वा पर्वतीपति: ॥ 54 ॥

Time makes a generous donor a beggar and it is Time that makes the same beggar again a generous donor. Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Maheśa, Indra and other Devas are all under the sway of this Time; so Time is the Sovereign of all.

इन्हादा निजीरा: सर्वे काल एव प्रभु: स्वयम् ।

तस्माकालं प्रतीक्षस्य विपरीतं ततायुना ॥ 5 5 ॥

Therefore, O King! Wait for this Time. Now Time is favourable to the Gods and inimical to you.

सम्पुखो देवतानां च दैत्यानां नाशहेतुकः ।

एकैव च गतिनिति कालस्य किल भृपते ॥ 5 6 ॥

Therefore Time is destroying now the Daityas. But the course of Time is not the same throughout. O King! The actions of Time are various no doubt.

- नानास्पधाराध्यपति झात्म्यं तस्य चेषितम् ।

कदाचित्सभवो नृणां कदाचित्त्वलयतथा ॥ 57 ॥

Time creates men and Time destroys them. The time of creation is different from the time of destruction, this is evident to you before your eyes.

उत्पत्तिहुः कालोज्ञ्यः क्षयहुतुलसाधपः ।

प्रत्यक्षे ते महाराज देल्या: सर्वे सवासना: ॥ 58 ॥

करताले कृतः पूर्व कालेन समुद्धेन च ।

तेनैव विमुचेनाठ्य बलिनोजलवग्यसुराः ॥ 59 ॥

नित्ता नितरां कालः करोति च सुभागुभम् ।

न चात्र कारणं काली नैव देवा: सनातना: ॥ 60 ॥

See! When Time was favourable to you, you subjected Indra and all other Devas and made them pay taxes to you; and now Time is unfavourable to you; so an ordinary weak woman is killing the powerful Dānavas; Time, therefore, is doing favourable things and also unfavourable things.

The host of Devas or the woman Kālī is not the cause thereof.

यथा ते रोचते राजसंखा कुक विमुश्य च ।

कालोउं यान हैतुले दानवानां तथा पुनः ॥ 6 1 ॥

त्वद्याते गतः शको भपनः संख्ये निरगुधः ।

तथा विद्युस्तथा रुडो करणो धनदो यमः ॥ 6 2 ॥

O King! The present Time is not favourable to you and the Daityas; knowing this, do as you like. See! Indra, Viṣṇu, Varuna, Yama and other prominent Devas all fled before in battle, quitting their weapons.

तथा त्वमिप राजेन्द्र वीक्ष्य कालवर्गे जगत् ।

पातालं गच्छ तरसा जीवन्मुद्रमाव्यसि ॥ 63 ॥

मृते त्वयि महाराज शगवस्ते मुदाधिततः ।

मंगलानि प्रगायंती विचारिधानि सर्वतः ॥ 64 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे पत्रमकन्के

शिरोअधाय: 11 30 ॥

So, knowing this world as subject to the control of Time, you can now fly away and go quickly to the Pātāla. For if you live, you will get in future all the pleasures; and if you be killed, you enemies will all be very glad and roam everywhere fearlessly, singing propitious songs.

Here ends the Thirtieth Chapter of the Fifth Book on the killing of Niśumbha in Śrimaddevibhāgavatam,

the Mahā Purānam, of 18,000 verses

by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Šrimaddevibhāgavatam

CHAPTER XXXI

On the Death of Šumbha

व्यास उवाच

इति तेषां वचः शुत्वा शुभ्यो दैत्यपतिस्तदा ।

उवाच सैनिकानाशु कोपाकृतिलतलोचनः ॥ १ ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Śumbha, the Lord of the Daityas, hearing the words of the soldiers, began to say, then, with eyes rolling with anger.

शुभ्म उवाच

जात्या: किं दूत तुवाँर्थे कृत्वा जीवितुयुले ।

नित्य सचिवाय्प्रात्रिलैंकजो विचारामि किम् ॥ 2 ॥

Śumbha said: "O Fools! What are you saying

all this? How can I do this unspeakably mean act and then hope to live? How shall I be able to roam in this world when I have become the cause in the slaying of my brothers and ministers?

कालः कर्ता शुभानो राजभुभानो बलवततः ।

का चित्ता मम दुवरि तस्मिहोगोउध्यकपके ॥ ३ ॥

Time is the more powerful cause of all that takes place, good or bad; so when this formless Time is the Supreme Ruler, what use is there in my brooding over the result?

Book V Chapter XXXI

यद्वयिति तद्वयत् यत्कोतिति करोतु तत् ।

न मे चिन्ताजसित कुजापि यमणाजीविनताया ॥ 4 ॥

Let whatever come that is inevitable, let whatever be done that is destined to take place; death or life, I do not think of either.

स कालोउपन्यथा कर्तु भावितो नेशते कवचित् ।

न कर्षिति च पर्नयः श्रावणे मासि सर्वदा ॥ 5 ॥

The more so when Time is never able, even when worshipped, to thwart off death or life when their proper moment arrives.

कदापिभागीर्णिरें वा पोरे माघेउध फालुने ।

अकाले वर्षतीवाशु तस्मामुख्यो न चास्तवम् ॥ 6 ॥

See! The God of rain gives up rain in the rainy season; but, it is seen that sometimes it does not rain in the month of Śrāvaṇa (the rainy season); whereas it rains sometimes in the month of Agrahāyaṇa, Pausa, Māgha, or Phālguṇa (not the rainy season).

कालो निमित्मात्रां तु दैवं हि बलवतस्म।

दैवेन निर्मितं सर्वे नान्यथा घबतीत्वः ॥ 7 ॥

Therefore it is evident that Time is not the chief factor. Fate is stronger than Time; Time is merely the instrumental cause.

दैवमेव परं मन्ये चित्यभीरमवर्गकम् ।

जेता यः सर्वदेवानां निर्याम्योउपन्यथा हतः ॥ 8 ॥

It is this Fate that has created all this universe; it cannot be rendered otherwise. I consider Fate as Supreme; Fie on this one's own exertion! For, Lo! Niśumbha, who had before conquered all the Devas, is slain today by an ordinary woman!

त्काबीजो महापुरः सोर्धि नागं गतो यदा ।

तदाई कौतिंमुचूज्य जीवितागां कोमीय किम् ॥ 9 ॥

Alas! When Raktabīja, too, had been slain, how can I desire to hold on to my life, foregoing all my name and fame!

प्राप्ते काले स्वयं बढ़ा पराध्रियसामिति ।

निधयं चाति तरसा जागक्तां स्वयं प्रभुः ॥ 10 ॥

Even Brahmā, who has created all this universe, will not sooner come to an end than his longevity expires.

चतुरुंपासहरं तु बढ़ाणो विद्ये किल ।

पतन्ति भवानतरंच नव चेज्ञतला युन: ॥ १ १ ॥

तथैव द्विगुणो विद्यूर्मराणायोपकल्पते ।

तथैव द्विगुणो काले शड़ूः शान्तिमेति च ॥ १ २ ॥

Four thousand Yugas constitute one day of Brahmā; and in that one day fourteen Indras perished; so twice the life of Brahmā constitute the life of Viṣnū; similarly twice the life period of Viṣnū constitutes the life period of Maheśa; and when their longevity expire, they come to an end.

का चित्ता मरणे मुहा निश्छले दैर्विनिति ।

मही महीधाराणां च नाशः सूर्यशगाङ्गवोः ॥ 13 ॥

This visible earth, mountains, sun and moon all will perish; so it has been specially ordained by the Destiny; therefore, O Fools! I do not care a bit for death.

जातस्य हि धुवं मृत्युधुवं जन्म मृतस्य च ।

अधुउेस्मिज्ञगीरे तु स्थणीयं यथा: स्थितम् ॥ 14 ॥

When a being is born, he must die; and when anyone dies, he will be born again, there is no doubt in this.

रथो मे कल्यन्ता शीर्भ गमिष्यामि रणाजिरः ।

जयो वा मरणं वारि भवत्वशैद वैतः ॥ 15 ॥

So one ought to preserve one's name and fame which is more permanent in this transitory body. Prepare my chariot; I will go today to the battlefield; let victory or defeat come as it may, as Fate has ordained. I will soon go to fight."

इत्युक्त्वा सैनिकाऊयुंपो यद्यमाध्यय सत्यतः ।

प्रयवादाधिक्वा यत्र संश्रिता तु हिमाभलने ॥ १ ६ ॥

Thus saying, Śumbha mounted on the chariot quickly and went where the Devi Ambika was staying. Then the four-fold army, cavalry, infantry, chariots, horses and elephants and innumerable soldiers, followed him with weapons in their hands.

सैन्य प्रचलितं तस्य संगे तत्र चतुविधम् ।

हल्पस्थायपादातिरमंयुतं सायुधं बड़ु ॥ 17 ॥

Going there to the Himalaya mountain, he saw the Divine Mother sitting on Her Lion. She

appeared so very lovely as to enchant the three worlds.

तत्र गलाड़चेल शुभ्मः संशितां जगद्धिककाम् ।

शैलैकवमोहीनी कान्तापय्यत्सिंह्वाहिनीम् ॥ 18 ॥

सर्वापरणभूषात्घां सर्वलक्षणासंरुताम् ।

स्तूयमातं सुरैः ख्वर्थीन्धर्वेयक्षिकरोः ॥ 19 ॥

Her body was decorated with various ornaments, all the auspicious gems were manifest; the Devas, Gandharvas, Yakṣas and Kinnaras in the heavens were all worshipping Her with hymns and Pārijāta flowers, and the Devi was making beautiful sounds with bells and conches, indicative of Her victory.

पुर्थेध पूच्यमानां च मन्दारपादपोद्बरैः ।

कुर्णिणां गंखिनन्दे चफटनादं मनोहस्म् ॥ 20 ॥

दूद्वा तां मोहस्मामद्वृष्मः कामविवाहितः ।

पडबाणाहतः कामं मनसा समर्पितयद् ॥ 21 ॥

Seeing Her Śumbha was very much enchanted with passionate love and struck with, the five arrows of cupid, thought thus: How wonderful is Her lovely countenance! See! How wonderful and amazing is Her skilfulness! Delicacy and capability to endure the hardships of war, though quite contrary to each other, are both in Her.

अंही रूपमिदं स्यमगहो चातुर्धयेबुतम् ।

सौकुमार्य च धैर्य च परस्यरिवीध यत् ॥ 22 ॥

What a wonder is this! Her bodies are extremely delicate and limbs are lean and thin; besides She is lately blooming into womanhood; still She does not feel any passion; this is undoubtedly very wonderful!

सुकुमाराशतितन्यंगी सदा: प्रकटयीवना ।

चित्रप्रेतदसी बाला कामभावविर्वित्ति ॥ 23 ॥

कामकान्तासमा रूपे सर्वलक्षणलक्षिता ।

जचिकेकं पिकित्त हौत सर्वान्महावल्न् ॥ 24 ॥

She is exquisitely beautiful that can be desired of in one's mind; and though She is endowed with all the auspicious signs, yet She has no inclinations for all the pleasure and allurements of the world and is now slaying the powerful Asuras; this is wonderful indeed!

उपायः को3त्र कर्तेल्यो येन मे वर्गगा भयेत् ।

न मन्त्रा वा मरालाड़ीरामायेन सत्रिषी मम ॥ 25 ॥

Now what steps are to be taken so that this Lady comes under my control? All the Mantrams also are not with me now to bring over this Swan-eyed Lady unto me.

सर्वेनमयी होषा मोहिनी मदार्षिति ।

सुन्दरीयं कवं मे स्याहजगना वरवर्गिनी ॥ 26 ॥

पातालगमनं मेउत्र न युक्तं समरांगणत् ।

सामदानविषयेक्ष्य तेयं साधया महावला ॥ 27 ॥

This proud lovely Lady is the incarnate of all Mantrams; how will She come under my control? This heroic Lady cannot be controlled by conciliatory words, allurements, dissensions; it is not advisable, too, to fly away from the battle-field and to go to Pātāla.

किं कतवैल्व चान्तव्य विषये समुपस्थिते ।

मरणं नोतमं चात्र स्कीकृतं तु यशोप्रदात् ॥ 28 ॥

मरणं श्रक्रिधितः प्रोक्ते संगरे मेगलास्यदात् ।

यत्तस्मानवलयोर्धियोर्धियते। किल ॥ 29 ॥

What am I to do? Where shall I go in this critical moment? And if I die at the hands of this Lady, that death is not a glorious one; it will take away my fame. The death in a battle-field is conducive to one's well being, so the sages say, when both the parties are equally strong.

प्रातयेरे दैर्वत्तिता नारी नरशतोतमा ।

नाशायास्कुलस्येह सर्वधात्रतिकाराजकला ॥ 30 ॥

दृषा किं सामवाक्यानि मय योज्यानि साम्यान् ।

हननायागत होषा किं तु सामना प्रसीदिति ॥ 31 ॥

The Devas have created this Lady stronger than even hundred strong men; She is a woman merely in name. This Lady is very powerful and has come here to destroy the Dānavas; there is no doubt in this. What effect will conciliatory words now produce on Her; She has come to slay us; Will She be appeased with good words?

न दानैश्रातिर्तुं योग्य नानागस्विध्यपिता ।

येवसूत विफलः कामं सर्वदेववशानुगा ॥ 32 ॥

Neither will allurements of precious things be

Book V Chapter XXXI

of any avail, for She is decked with various arms and weapons; nor will it be of any use to sow dissensions between the Devas and Her. Further all the Devas are under Her control.

तस्मातु मरणो श्रेयो न संग्रामे पतयवन् ।

जयो वा मरणं वाउँ घावलेवे यथाविधि ॥ 33 ॥

Therefore it is far better to die than to fly; victory or death would come unto me today as Fate has ordained.

व्यास उवाच

इति संवैल्यानः स गुणः सचाशितोभवत् ।

युद्धाय सुश्रियो भूत्वा तापुवावा पुरः स्थितम् ॥ 34 ॥

देवि युध्यव कान्तेउ ह्यावाउं ने परिघमः ।

मूर्जा5स किल नारीणां नायं धर्मः कदाचन ॥ 35 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! Thus thinking in his mind, Sumbha became ready to shew his strength; and firmly resolved to fight, he said to the Devi before him: "O Devi! Fight. But, O One of delicate limbs! Thy so much toil is in vain. Thou hast no sense at all; for Thou art doing contrary to the doings of woman-kind.

नारीर्णा लोचने बाणा घुवावेव शरासनम् ।

हावभावासु शस्त्राणि पुर्णिलल्यं विवश्रण: ॥ 36 ॥

सत्राहुशागगोजंत्र रथधारी पनोरथः ।

मन्दप्राजित्यर्ने भेरीश्वदो नान्यः कदाचन ॥ 37 ॥

The pair of eyes of women are their arrows; the eyebrows are their bows; their gestures and postures are their weapons and their hits are those persons who are skilled in amorous love sentiments. The dyes used in painting the bodies are their amorous, their mental desires are their chariots, so sweet soft words and conversations are their trumpet sounds; women have no other things for their war preparations.

अन्याख्याणरी स्त्रीण विडवनमसंगयम् ।

तरजेदे भूषणं काने न च धार्थे कदाचन ॥ 38 ॥

Therefore, O Beloved! Any other weapons are mere mockeries and ridiculous; their modesty is their ornament; impudence can never grace them.

युध्यमाना वरा नारी कक्रीश्वामिपूषयते ।

स्तनी संगोपनीदी वा धनुषः कर्णी कयम् ॥ 39 ॥

An exquisitely beautiful woman, if engaged in a fight will look harsh; especially when Thou wilt draw Thy bow, how wilt Thou be able to hide Thy breasts?

केव मन्दामाने कुरा नदामादाय धावनम् ।

बुद्धिदा कालिका तेत्र चाणुषा परानीक्षा ॥ 40 ॥

When Thou wilt run with Thy club, where will Thy gentle treadings go? O Beautiful! Thy councillors are this Kalikā and the stupid Cāmundā.

चिषुकामान्यमध्यसा लालनेउपुस्वारा शिका ।

वाहनं मृगराडासे सर्वसत्तचयंकरः ॥ 41 ॥

Candikā is Thy adviser her voice is very hoarse; how can then she be able to nurse Thee? Again this Lion, the terror of all the beings, is Thy carrier. Therefore.

वीणानादं परित्यज प्रधटानादं करोषि यत् ।

रूपयौवियोः सर्वे विरोधि चरावर्धिनि ॥ 42 ॥

O Dear! Leave aside all these and come over unto me. O Beautiful One! That Thou art ringing Thy bells and dost not sound Thy lute goes quite against Thy beauty and youth.

यदि ते संगरेळधारित कुकराण भव भाभिनी ।

लप्योदी कुनखी कृतः ध्यक्षरणां विलेत्वाचे ॥ 43 ॥

लम्बयात इकुन्ची च माजार्चवनाकृति: ।

ईपुर्णं कल्पामाध्याय तिछा सुखे चिक्षा भव ॥ 44 ॥

O Sensitive One! If Thou likest to fight, better assume an ugly appearance, let Thy nature be ferocious and cruel; let Thy colour be black like a crow; lips elongated, legs long, nails ugly, teeth horrible, and let Thy eyes be ugly or yellow like those of a cat.

कर्कर्णं वचनं बृहि ततो सुद्धं करोम्यहम् ।

इंडूर्णीं सुदतीं दृष्टवा न मे पाणिगः प्रसीदिति ॥ 4 5 ॥

हनुं त्वां मृणागावासिंक कामकानोयेमे मृढे ।

O Devi! Assume such an ugly appearance and stand firmly for the fight. O Deer-eyed One! Speak first harsh words unto me; then I will fight with

Thee; my hand does not get up to strike Thee with handsome teeth, in the battle-field, Who art like a second Rati."

व्यास उवाच

इति बुवाणं कामार्तं बीक्ष्य तं जगद्धिष्का ॥ 4 6 ॥

सितपूर्विमिंदं वावस्यमुवाच भरतोतम ।

Vyāsa said: O Best of the descendants of Bharata! When Śumbha said thus, the Divine Mother, seeing him passionate, smiled and said:

देव्यवाच

किं विधीदसि मन्दात्मकामबाणवियोहितः ॥ 47 ॥

प्रेक्षाकार्ड स्थिता मूद कुरु कालिकया मृधम् ।

चामुपडया वा कुवेते तव योग्ये राणांगणे ॥ 48 ॥

प्रहरव यथाकामं नाई त्वां योद्मूलसहै ।

“O Stupid One! Why are you so much distressed with passion? O Fool! If your haud does not come forward to strike weapons at Me, then fight with this ugly Kālikā or Cāmuṇḍā; they are your best compeers in the battle-field; they will fight with you; I will stand as a mere Witness.

इन्युसना कालिकां प्राद देवी मयुर्या सिरा ॥ 49 ॥

जहोनं कालिके कृरे कुरूपप्रियमाहये ।

Thus saying, the Devi Bhagavati said to Kālika in sweet words: "O Kālika! Your nature is fierce; this Śumbha likes also the fierce; so kill him."

व्यास उवाच

इत्युक्ता कालिका कालप्रेति कालकपिणी ॥ 50 ॥

गदा प्रगृहा तस्या तस्यावाजी कृतोघाम ।

Vyāsa said: O King! That Kālika, the incarnate of Death, thus ordered, took up Her club immediately and became ready to fight, as if sent there direct by the God of Death.

तयोः परस्यं युद्धे बभूवातिभयानकम् ॥ 51 ॥

पश्यां सर्वैद्वानां मुनिनो च महात्यनाम् ।

A dreadful fight then ensued between the two; and the high-souled Munis and the Devas were present there and witnessed the great event.

गदामुहाय गृभभो3द जघान कालिकां रो ॥ 52 ॥

कालिका दैत्यसाजानं गदया न्यहनद्भुगम् ।

बर्भजास्य रथं चएडी गदाय कनकोज्ज्वलम् ॥ 53 ॥

खान्द्वता जघनागुं दाफकं दाफाग्वसना ।

Śumbha first struck at Kalikā, raising his club. Kalikā, then, struck Śumbha in return with her club violently. Instantly she made a dreadful sound, broke down his chariot, glittering like gold, into pieces, killed the horse of the chariot and slew the charioteer.

स पदातिर्वादो गुर्वीं समादाय कुधान्वितः ॥ 54 ॥

कालिकापुभवोमीये प्रहसान्तनता ।

Walking, then, on foot with a very heavy club in his hand, Śumbha struck with great anger on the breast of Kalikā and began to laugh.

वड्यचिता गदादारां खड्यामादाय सच्चा ॥ 55 ॥

चिच्छेदायभुंजस्वंसायुंचन्द्रान्धितम् ।

Kālikā, in the meanwhile, rendering his stroke useless, soon took up Her axe and cut off his left hand, pasted with sandal and decked with arms and weapons.

स छिड़बाहुविंशयो गदाणाणिः पारित्तुः ॥ 56 ॥

अधिरेष समानार्थ कालिकामहानत्तदा ।

His left hand thus cut off, his whole body was drenched with torrents of blood; yet he came up with club in his hand and struck Kalikā with it.

काली च कवालेत पुर्ज नस्याव दक्षिणम् ॥ 57 ॥

चिछ्छेउ प्रहस्नी सा सगदं किल सांगदम् ।

Kālika, too, laughed and with Her scimitar cut off his right arm holding the club and ornamented with armlet.

कर्तुं पादपूहरां स कुषितः प्रययौ जवात् ॥ 58 ॥

काली चिच्छेच्चे चरणी खड़ोगनाय त्वारीकता ।

Śumbha became angry and came up violently to kick Her when Kālīkā quickly cut off his two legs.

सच्छिकरणपाठ़ेपि सिंह तिक्षिति च बुच्न ॥ 59 ॥

धायमानो ययावाणु कालिका भीषयाश्रि ।

His arms and legs thus severed from his body, the Demon frightened Kālīkā and told Her. "Wait, wait." And soon he came up before Her.

तमापण्ड्तमालीकरण कालिका कमलोषमम् ॥ 60 ॥

चकर्त मलकं कंठादुघिरीयचहं घुगम् ।

Book V Chapter XXXII

Seeing the Demon coming, Kálikā severed his neck from his body like a lotus; blood began to gush out in continuous streams.

छिडेउसी मस्तके भूपी पपात गिरिसझिभः ॥ 63 ॥

प्राणा विनिर्यमुक्तस्य देहादुकन्मय सत्वम् ।

O King! The head of Šumbha, thus severed from his body, fell on the ground like a mountain. Immediately the life left the body.

तातपुं पत्ति देत्यं चूद्या देया: सवासना: ॥ 62 ॥

गुडुकुर्नां तदा वैधी वापुपां कालिकां नवा ।

Seeing the Dānava fell down lifeless, Indra and the other hosts of Devas began to worship the Devi Bhagavatī, Cāmunḍā, and Kālīkā and chanted lovely hymns to them.

ववुर्वाता: शिवास्तत्र दिशिक्ष विमला भूषणम् ॥ 63 ॥

वेपूवुक्षान्यो होमे प्रदक्षिणशिक्षा: शुभाः ।

The winds then began to blow pleasantly; all the quarters looked very clear and Fire in sacrificial altars, being circumambulated, became very propitious.

इतनेशेषा थे दैत्याः प्रणाय जगद्धिकवम् ॥ 64 ॥

त्वक्तानउदयुपानि ते सर्वे पातालं प्रययुँच ।

On the other hand, those Daityas that remained alive quitted their arms and weapons, bowed down to the Divine Mother, and fled away one and all to the Pātāla.

तृत्ते सर्वमाख्यानं देत्याश्रितमुत्तमम् ॥ 65 ॥

गृभाष्टीर्नां वधं चैव सुराणां रक्षणं तथा ।

O King! I have now described in regular order to you how the Devi protected the Devas and destroyed Sumbha and other Asuras.

एतदाख्यानकं सर्वे पठतिनं भुवि मानवाः ॥ 66 ॥

शुणतिने च सहा भवनया ते कृतावर्ण भवतिन हि ।

Those human beings on the surface of the earth that read this anecdote from the beginning to the very end or hear it constantly, get all their desires fulfilled; there is no doubt in this.

अपुजे तपते पुजार्धिंग्रंघ धर्न बहु ॥ 67 ॥

रोगी च मुचले रोगास्वी-कामानवायुगत ।

O King! Verily he gets a son who has not got any son; he gets abundance of wealth who is without any wealth; the diseased become cured of their diseases; what more can be said than the fact that he who hears this glorious deed of the Devi in its entirety, gets all that he desires.

शकुत्तो न भयं तत्य य इदं चरितं शुभम् ।

भूणीति पदते नित्यं मृतिमाज्ञावते नः ॥ 68 ॥

इति श्रीमदेवीभागवते महापुराणे पड्यसकन्धे

शुभ्यधौ नाम एकविंशोउधायः ॥ 31 ॥ 11

O King! That man who reads daily this holy anecdote or hears it, has never to fear from his enemies; in addition he gets liberation after leaving his this body.

Here ends the Thirty-first Chapter of the Fifth Book

on the death of Sumbha in the Mahā Purānam

Srimaddevibhāgavatam of 18,000 verses by Maharṣi Veda Vyāsa.

Book V Chapter XXXII

CHAPTER XXXII

On the King Surahta's Going to the Forest

जनमेजर उवाच

महिमा वर्णते सम्बन्धिकृतकायास्तव्या मुने ।

कैन चारात्रिता पूर्वं चरित्रजयोगतः ॥ १ ॥

Janamejaya said: O Best of Munis! The glory of Caṇḍikā has been fully described by you. By whom was She worshipped in the ancient times after the reading and hearing of Her three glorious deeds (the killing of Madhu Kaṭṭabha, etc.)?

प्रसत्रा कस्य बरतृ देकेन प्राप्तं फलं महत् ।

आगाध्य कामादों देर्वीं कथायस्य कृपानिधे ॥ 2 ॥

उपासनाविधि बहास्तथा पूजाविधि वह ।

वित्तरेण महाभाग होमम्य च वित्तिं पुनः ॥ ३ ॥

Who was it that derived the best effects by worshipping the Devi, the Bestower of all desires? When and with whom was She pleased and then offered boons? O Ocean of mercy! Kindly narrate fully all these things to me. O Brāhmana! Describe

to me also the rules how the meditation, worship and Homa of the Great Devi are conducted.

सूत जतच

इति भूपवचः शुत्वा प्रीतः सत्वातीसुतः ।

प्रत्युवाच नृपं कृष्णो महामायापूजनम् ॥ 4 ॥

Sūta said: “O Rsis! Kṛṣna Dvaipāyana, the son of Satyavati, was very glad to hear these questions of Janamejaya and began to describe how the worship, etc., of the Mahā Māyā, the Devi Bhagavati are to be done.

व्यास उवाच

स्वातोचिष्ठन्तरे पूर्व सुर्यो नाम पाधिद्धः ।

बधूव परमोदारः प्रजापालनततरः ॥ 5 ॥

Vyāsa said: O King! In days of yore in Svārocīṣa Manvantara there was a king, named Suratha, very liberal-minded and devoted to govern well his subjects.

सत्यवादी कर्मपोरो ब्राह्मणानां च पुत्रकः ।

गुरुभस्तितो नित्यं स्वदारयमने रतः ॥ 6 ॥

He was truthful, active and energetic, and devoted to his Guru; he always served the twice-born and he never used to hold any sexual intercourse except with his legal wife.

दानशीलोत्रविरोधी च धनुर्वैदकयागः ।

एवं पालयती राज्यं मेलछा: पर्वतावासिनः ॥ 7 ॥

He was generous, not liking to quarrel with anybody, and expert in the science of archery. While he was thus governing his kingdom, the Mleechas, the hill tribes, turned out his enemies.

बलाच्छुल्यमाप्रा: सैन्यं कृत्वा चतुर्विधम् ।

हृस्यध्रयपादातसिहातसे मदोकटा: ॥ 8 ॥

कोलाविद्यसिनः प्रापा: पृथ्वीमहणततया ।

सुध: सैन्यमादाय सम्पुखः सम्पघत ॥ 9 ॥

They destroyed the city of Kolā, became very haughty and turbulent and desired to conquer the whole earth by their sheer force. Thus accompanied by the great four-fold army elephants, chariots, cavalry and infantry they came to conquer the dominion of the King Suratha.

यूद्धं समभवद्धोरं तस्य तैरतिदाफणी: ।

मलैच्छानां तु बलं स्वल्यं ग्रासनुद्वलम्बुद्धूनि ॥ १० ॥

तथापि तैर्जितो युद्धे देवहात्राव गरपात्रः ।

भानश्छ स्वारुं प्राप्तः सुरक्षं तुर्मपिडतम् ॥ १ १ १ ॥

A dreadful fight then ensued between the King and the dreadful Mlecchas. O King! The Mleccha forces were not at all numerous whereas the king were large; still the Mlecchas were lucky to win the battle. The King, defeated, fled to his own city which was a strongly fortified place.

चितयामास मेधावी राजा नीतिविश्वधानः ।

प्रधानान्विमना दूष्ठा श्रुवृष्ठसमाधिशान् ॥ १ २ ॥

स्थानं गृहीता विमुले परीखतुर्विगटजत् ।

कालप्रतीक्षा कतैम्वा किं वा युद्धं वरं मतम् ॥ १ ३ ॥

The good King, wise in statesmanship when he saw that his ministers had gone over to the enemies' party, became very anxious and thought whether it was advisable for him to wait for a better opportunity, remaining within his own extensive city, well guarded by a strong wall and ditch or it would be better to fight on.

मक्रिणः श्रत्रवाणा मन्योयाना न ते किल ।

कि करोमौति मनसा भूपति: समचितयत् ॥ 14 ॥

The King thought also that it would not at all be advisable to consult with his ministers who were, then, under the control of his enemies; what then would he do under the circumstances?

कदाचिते गृहीत्वा मां पाणाचारा: पराशिता: ।

सुबुभो3प्र प्रदास्यन्ति तदा किं वा भविष्यति ॥ 15 ॥

Those vicious ministers could at any time deliver him to the hands of his enemies; what would then happen to him!

पापबुद्धिरु विश्वासो न कतंब्यः कदाचन ।

कि ते वै प्रकुर्बिनि चे लोभवगगना ना: ॥ 16 ॥

Those men, that are avaricious, can do anything in this world; therefore it would never be advisable to trust them.

घातरं पितरं मित्रं सुहृदं बांधवं तथा ।

गुरुं पूर्यं द्वितं ऋषि लोपाधिद्ध: सदा नर: ॥ 17 ॥

The people under the sway of greed commit

Book V Chapter XXXII

injury to their fathers, brothers, friends, acquaintances, their Gurus and the adored Brāhmanas.

तस्मायमा न कर्तव्यो विश्वासः सर्वेधाउपूना ।

मंत्रिवर्गेस्तिपापिषे त्रगुष्वसमाप्रशिने ॥ 181 ॥

When the ministers had joined with his enemies, they could well be classed with the vicious; no doubt in this. Never could they be trusted under the above circumstances.

इति सच्छित्य मनसा राजा परयातुर्भानः ।

एकाकी ह्वयामहा निर्जीगम युगत्ततः ॥ 19 ॥

असहायो३य निर्णय ल्हानं वनमाश्रितः ।

चिन्त्यामास मेधावी कव रोल्व्यं मया पुतः ॥ 20 ॥

Thus pondering over the matter, the King became absentminded, and, finding no remedy, went out of the city alone, mounted on a horse. The intelligent King, helpless, entered into a dense forest and thought where would he go now?

योजनप्रयमाते तु घुनेराभ्रमपुत्तमम् ।

जात्वा जगाम भूपालस्तापसस्य सुयेधसः ॥ 21 ॥

Knowing, then, that there was, at a distance of three Yojanas from that place, a hermitage of the great ascetic, the Sumedhā Rṣi, the King went there.

Note: A Yojana is a distance measuring four Krōsas or eight or nine miles.

बहुद्धसयायुक्ते नदीपुलिनसंश्रितम् ।

निर्वेलाशयादकौणी कोकिलतारवमिडतम् ॥ 22 ॥

शिणायाध्यवनगश्दाको भृगायुध्यतावत् ।

नीवातारभुपव्यकावों सुपुधफलपादायम् ॥ 23 ॥

O King! That hermitage was more beautiful than even the Heavens; it was on the bank of a river; various kinds of trees were there; it was frequented with wild animals having no enmity with each other; the whole place was echoed with the sounds of cuckoos.

होमधूमसुनघेन प्रीतिंदं प्राणिनां सदा ।

वेट्यविनस्माकान्तं स्वगाँदपि मनोहरम् ॥ 24 ॥

दूद्वा तामश्रयं नारा बभावसी मुवात्वितः ।

भयं त्यक्त्वा मिति चक्रे विश्रायाव ग्रिजान्ये ॥ 25 ॥

The students were studying and reverberating

the atmosphere with their Vedic chants; hundreds of herds of deer were running there; rice trees had grown there wildly at places and their harvests were collected at places; good flowery trees and others with delicious fruits were seen there; at places fragrant smells of oblations of ghee, etc., were coming; all these were delighting to any man who went or stayed there. The King Suratha was very glad to see that Śārama; he became fearless and wanted to stay there in the hermitage of the Brāhmin.

आसाज्य पादधे३ हुं जगाम दिव्यान्वितः ।

दूढ़ता ते पुनिभासीने सातच्छायसु संश्रितम् ॥ 26 ॥

Fastening his horse at the root of a tree, the King approached humbly to the Rşi, and saw that the Muni was seated on a deer skin under the shade of dense Sal trees.

भूगतिज्ञानसं शान्तं तस्माजितकृशं ऋतुम् ।

अधयापनं तिणाध्यो ऋदेशागक्षार्विहिनम् ॥ 27 ॥

He was peaceful, lean and thin by tapasyā. His stature was straight; and he was teaching his disciples and explaining to them the meaning of the Veda Śāstras.

रहितं क्रोधलोभाषदैद्धातीतं विमत्सम् ।

आत्मान्तारं सत्यवादिनं ग्रामसंयुतम् ॥ 28 ॥

He was void of anger, greed, etc., beyond all the dualities, without any jealousy, always devoted to the contemplation of his Self, truthful and full of peace.

तं वीक्ष्य भूपतिभूभी पपात् दण्डवन्ता ।

तद्यो3धुजालापूर्णनयनः प्रेमसंयुतः ॥ 29 ॥

जतिष्ठातिष भई ते तयुबाच तदा मृति: ।

शिष्यो ददो बूर्गी तस्ये गुरुणा नोदितस्तदा ॥ 30 ॥

Seeing him the King was filled with tears and prostrated before him and fell like a stick before him. The Muni, seeing him thus, asked him to get up and enquired about his welfare. A disciple then, at the sign of the Guru, gave him a kuśāsana, to take his seat.

उत्थाय नृपतिस्तस्यां समासीनस्तद्वाना ।

अर्धयपाढ़ाईं चक्रे सुभेय विधिपूर्वकम् ॥ 31 ॥

The King got up and at his permission took his seat on that kuśāsana; then the Muni worshipped the King duly by offering to him water to wash his feet, and Arghya (an offer of green grass, rice, etc).

पूच्छात्र कुतः प्रातः कस्वं चिंतापः कवम् ।

कवयवय यथाकामा संवृत्तं कारणं चिक्छ् ॥ 32 ॥

Then the Muni asked him “Who are you? What for are you come here? Why are you so anxious? Tell frankly all these that are not yet known to me.

किमागमनकृत्वं ते बृहि कार्य मनोगतम् ।

कनिषेय वार्तिड़न काममसाध्यमपि यतच ॥ 33 ॥

What do you want? Speak out your mind. Even if that be impracticable, I will no doubt try my best to accomplish your desired ends."

राजोबाब

सुरयो नाम राजाड़े शुभ्रभिश्र पश्रजितः ।

स्वक्त्वा राज्यं गृहं भाघामिंहे ते शरणं ततः ॥ 34 ॥

The King said: "O Muni! I am the King Suraha; defeated by my enemy, I have left my kingdom, palace, and wife and wife and have come to your refuge."

यदाझापयसे ब्रह्मस्तदं घन्सितपर: ।

कन्धियामि न मे जाता स्वद्यः: पृथिवीतले ॥ 35 ॥

O Brāhmaṇa! I am ready to do whatever you order me; on this surface of the earth there is no one but you who can protect me.

गुव्यो मे भयं घोरं प्राप्तोउम्यहु त्वतितकम् ।

ग्रायस् मुनिशादूलं शरणागततस्वल १३ ३ १ ॥
